# Changelog

All notable changes to **Tadah.HPO** (hyper-parameter optimisation) are documented here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

## [Unreleased]

## [1.6.0] — unreleased

### Added
- **`declared_element_set(train_ctx)`** (`startup_validation.h`): the element set of a run without a training `DBFILE`, the elements `ATOM` declares, refused without `ATOM` (`No training DBFILE: the training configuration declares the element set with the ATOM key, such as ATOM Ta. ...`). `HPOEngine::run` takes the element set from it, and `validate_hpo_setup()` applies it, so a program that validates a setup without data refuses what a run refuses.
- **The balance table of the best potential**: the evaluation that may become the new best computes the balance table of its own fit (MLIP `fit_balance`, one O(n p²) pass, only for an evaluation whose loss may make it the best, not for the others), and `best_pot.tadah` (with its rank-local copies) has it at the end of its training record, so a run killed after its first best still has it; the `DUMP` and `BDUMP` archives have none. The decision of a new best (`detail::BestDecision`) is counted before the table is computed, with a ticket of its own, so the stagnation check of a parallel run (`AFTER_STAGNATION`) waits for every decision counted before it began, one still computing its table included, in whatever order they end; a table that fails ends its decision all the same. A run without training data records `Fit balance: none` with the reason, and a run whose `FIXINDEX` pins every coefficient records the line `tadah train` writes for such a fit, `Fit balance: none (FIXINDEX pins every coefficient: none was fitted)`. When the run ends, `tadah hpo` prints the table of `best_pot.tadah` once, from its training record (MPI rank 0, `VERBOSE` 1 and above).
- **`OPTIM ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` and `WDBFILE` search the error scales and the dataset priorities of the fit, which every evaluation applies** (MLIP `RowWeights`: a fit minimises priority × (residual/error scale)²). The value of `ESIGMA`, of the absolute parts `FSIGMA (1)` and `SSIGMA (1)` and of each `WDBFILE` value is > 0: its `AUTO` box is geometric (`RATIO 2`, [x0/2, 2 x0]), and a box whose low bound is not > 0 is refused at its line (`OPTIM ESIGMA (1): the low bound 0 is not > 0, but ESIGMA is the energy error scale, a number > 0 (meV/atom, or meV per sqrt(atom) under ENORM SQRT), and every fit refuses another, so the low bound of its box is > 0`); the relative parts `FSIGMA (2)` and `SSIGMA (2)` are >= 0: an `AUTO` box is linear and clamped at 0, a negative low bound is refused, and so is an `AUTO` box around a negative value of the training configuration (`OPTIM FSIGMA (2): the seed -0.5 is negative, but the relative part b of FSIGMA is a number >= 0, and every fit refuses a negative one`). The training record of each potential an evaluation writes (`best_pot.tadah`, its mirrors, `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives) lists the `WDBFILE` it was fitted with (`HPOTargets::record_of`). Refused when the run starts, without a line: `OPTIM` of the error scale of a quantity the fit does not have (`OPTIM FSIGMA: FORCE is false: the fit has no forces, so FSIGMA would change nothing ...`), of the error scale of the only quantity of the fit (its absolute part alone) with `LAMBDA` 0 or -1, which scales every row alike (`OPTIM ESIGMA: the energies are the only quantity of the fit and LAMBDA is 0: ...`), of `WDBFILE` in a run of one training dataset with `LAMBDA` 0 or -1, whose value multiplies the priority of every row alike (`OPTIM WDBFILE: the run has one training dataset and LAMBDA is 0: ...`), of an error scale or `WDBFILE` in a run whose evaluations fit no coefficient (`FIXINDEX` pinning every weight, no training data), under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` (`OPTIM WDBFILE searches a priority of the fit, which the analytical gradient does not differentiate`; `HYBRID` takes finite differences for them) and, under `M_KRR` implementation 2, of `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` and `WDBFILE` (its solve weighs the energies per atom over `ESIGMA`, which `OPTIM ESIGMA` searches). A `WARNING` when every error scale of the fit is searched with `LAMBDA` 0 or -1, or with `OPTIM LAMBDA` (`the error scales times c with LAMBDA divided by c^2 give the same fit`), and when the `WDBFILE` value of every training dataset is (`the WDBFILE values times c with LAMBDA times c give the same fit`): one direction of the search changes nothing. A `WARM` seed without the line of a searched key does not have its value, and `WARM_ON_MISMATCH` applies (a seed of an earlier version has no `ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA` or `SSIGMA` line; the value of its `WDBFILE` is in its training record, which is not read). `OPTIM ENORM` and `OPTIM ENERGY` (a choice and a switch) are refused at their line. The analytical gradient takes the row factors of the fit from `RowWeights` (it read `EWEIGHT` and `FWEIGHT`). Tests: `test_hpo_error_scales.cpp` (with the refusal of `OPTIM WDBFILE` of one dataset, the two `WARNING`s, `ANALYTICAL` with `OPTIM WDBFILE` and the `WARM` seed without the line), `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (`Each evaluation applies its ESIGMA, FSIGMA and SSIGMA`, `Each evaluation applies its WDBFILE`), `test_hpo_analytical.cpp` (the finite-difference guard with error scales and `WDBFILE`), `test_fit_settings_startup.cpp`.
- **`PC_SRMSE_GPA`**: a loss term on the stress RMSE of the validation datasets in GPa, the root mean square of the nine entries of each residual stress tensor (MLIP `Analytics::calc_s_rmse_gpa`: the Frobenius norm a fit with `STRESS true` minimises, the same for a rotated cell), with the global and the per-dataset form of `PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE` (`PC_SRMSE_GPA (1,3) 0.5 4`) and their `--skip-above` and `--skip-rel` options, a cap in GPa. Its columns are `SRMSE_GPA[i]`, `SRMSE_GPA[avg]` (`stats.tadah`, `outvar.tadah`) and `L(SRMSE_GPA)[i]` (`loss.tadah`), each title whole: the value columns of a run are as wide as its longest metric title and a blank (18 characters with `L(SRMSE_GPA)[avg]`, at least `DIGITS` + 9), so a header splits on blanks into one title per value; the journal, the `--check` summary and the `best_pot.tadah` header label it `SRMSE (GPa)`. Tests: `test_hpo_error_scales.cpp`, `test_validation_metric.cpp`, `test_metric_unit_labels.cpp`, `test_validation_follows_metric_e2e.cpp`, `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`Every metric column title is whole and keeps a blank before it at every DIGITS`).
- **Startup WARNINGs for an `INIT` or `RESTART` block that a search which does not start at its point never reads** (`start_point_warnings`, `backend_rules.h`; `OptimizerCaps::random_draws`; `InitStrategySpec::line`, `RestartSpec::line`). NLopt's StoGO, DIRECT and AGS never read the point they are given (`OptimizerCaps::starts_at_x0`); where such a search is accepted -- the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser under `PLAIN`, the `PRESTAGE` exploration, an `AUGLAG` whose `INNER` search is one -- `validate_hpo_setup` warns once per block, on standard error and in the journal, naming the line that opens it:
  - an `INIT` block with a `METHOD`, whose points the search never evaluates (`the INIT block on line 5: the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L does not start at the point it is given (DIRECT evaluates the centre of the OPTIM box first and then divides the whole box; NLopt never reads that point), so the search never evaluates the block's INIT points. To start the search at them, use an optimiser that starts at the point it is given.`; under `NOISE CALIBRATION` it adds that the calibration evaluates the first of them; an `OPTIMIZER`-level block that an exploration without one of its own takes: `the refinement starts from them only when no evaluation of the exploration has a usable loss`);
  - a `RESTART` block when the search draws no random numbers either (`GD_STOGO`, `GN_DIRECT`, `GN_DIRECT_L`, `GN_DIRECT_L_NOSCAL`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*`, `GN_AGS`, and `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` in one `OPTIM` dimension), whose rounds ask for round 0's points again, whatever points they start from (`the RESTART block on line 10: ... and draws no random numbers, so every RESTART round, whatever points it starts from, asks for round 0's points again, which the run's record answers: no round after round 0 evaluates a new point, and the RESTART block's only effect is to end the run once round 0 has had no new best in AFTER_STAGNATION 3 evaluations. For rounds that search anew, use an optimiser that starts at the point it is given.`). `GD_STOGO` obtains gradients, which a round forms itself, so for it the warning says what the rounds evaluate: `... asks for round 0's points again, and their gradients: the run's record answers their losses, but a round forms its gradients itself and evaluates their finite-difference stencils again (GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL): no round after round 0 evaluates a new point, and each round evaluates up to AFTER_STAGNATION 3 of round 0's points again before it ends without a new best.` (under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` each point at which a round asks for a gradient is evaluated again). With a loss the run evaluates again, such as a `--skip-rel` filter, `as long as their losses are those of round 0 (the run evaluates them again: ...)`; under `PRESTAGE`, the exploration's points;
  - a `RESTART` block on `GD_STOGO_RAND` below `POPULATION` 2n + 1 (NLopt's default is 2n), whose first trial point is the box centre and whose first local search, from it, draws nothing (`OptimizerCaps::RandomDraws::TrialPoints`): `... and, with POPULATION 4 (NLopt's default, 2n for n = 2 OPTIM dimensions), less than 2n + 1 = 5, takes the centre of the box as its first trial point and runs a local search from it before any of its random trial points, so every RESTART round, whatever points it starts from, asks first for the points of round 0's first local search again, and their gradients: ...: until that search ends a round evaluates no new point, and it reaches its random trial points only if the search ends before the round has had AFTER_STAGNATION 3 evaluations without a new best. For rounds that search anew, use an optimiser that starts at the point it is given, or POPULATION 5 (2n + 1) or more, whose trial points are all random.`;
  - a `RESTART` block with `METHOD` lines on such a search that draws random numbers (`GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` in two or more dimensions, `GD_STOGO_RAND` at `POPULATION` 2n + 1 or more), whose points no round evaluates (`the RESTART block on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L_RAND does not start at the point it is given (...), so no RESTART round evaluates the points of the block's METHOD lines: a round's search differs from round 0's only in the random numbers it draws. To start each round at the block's points, use an optimiser that starts at the point it is given.`; for a `PRESTAGE` exploration that takes the round's points, its `EXPLORE` block having no `INIT` block, the sentence adds that a round's refinement starts from them only when no evaluation of its exploration has a usable loss).

  Such configurations ran without a word (a `RESTART` round of `GN_DIRECT_L` asked for round 0's points, answered from the record, until its stagnation window ended it; one of `GD_STOGO` evaluated round 0's stencils again). The WARNING about the starting points a search does not use (`the INIT block on line N gives K starting points, and the STRATEGY-level optimiser LIB:ALGO starts from the first only: ...`) is not given for such a search, which uses none. Tests: `test_start_point_warnings.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (what a round of `GD_STOGO` and of `GD_STOGO_RAND` evaluates).
- `repeat_points.h`: `RunRecord` and `RecordedEval`, the run's usable evaluations by the point evaluated (bit for bit), `RepeatPoints`, a search's use of it with the gradients the search formed, and `repeat_policy<Vec>(host)`, and `repeat_use(reuse, strategy, with_gradient, has_gradient)`, the one rule by which a call at a recorded point is answered (`RepeatUse`), which `RepeatPoints::use` and `gradient_budget_warnings` apply; `HPO_Host::recorded(point)` (the run's evaluation of the point an evaluation of `point` would make: after the snap onto the box; a point outside the box, which is not evaluated, has none; cleared by `prepare_best_publication`); `HPOTargets::repeat_reuse_blocker()` and `HPO_Host::repeat_reuse_blocker()`, why two evaluations of one point can give different losses in the run, or `""`; `Observable::carried_state()` (what an observable carries from one evaluation to the next; `cohesive_energy` its lattice constant; one that does not say carries something); `ValidationMetricTargets::has_relative_cap()`; `run_dlib_bfgs_impl` (DLIB:BFGS on any host), `detail::bfgs_box_search` (an overload with an evaluation count: `BfgsBoxEnd::Revisit`, `BfgsBoxResult::revisit_from`), `detail::stalled_reach` and `detail::BfgsObjective` (`settle()`: counts a loss answered from the record that no call followed, when the search ends); `EndReason::NoStep` (`no step within the OPTIM box`) and `EndReason::Revisit` (`back at an earlier point`); `HPOLog::record_repeat_reused`, `record_repeat_evaluated` and `record_line_search_not_run`; `HPO_Host::count_reused_repeat(const RecordedEval&)` (a reused repeat in the RESTART window and among a PRESTAGE exploration's candidates), and `detail::throw_if_stagnated` takes the count of reused calls since the last new best (a new parameter); `describe_point` takes the digits to print (10 by default). `InitStream` and `InitContext::stream` (the block a METHOD chain draws for: an `INIT` block or a `RESTART` round), `InitStrategy::draws_from_seed()`, `seed_streams::kRestartPoints`.
- **The ALGO help reaches the CLI** (`optimizer_help.h`): `explain_optimiser(name, out)`, which tadah sets as MLIP's explain hook, prints for `ALGO` or `LIB` (any letter case) the table of the optimisers (`print_optimiser_table`: by library, sorted, whether each is global or local, gradient-based or derivative-free -- MLSL and AUGLAG: the local search they run --, needs a stop or ignores the box; then the refused backends and how to see one), for `LIB:ALGO` or an `ALGO` alone that backend's ALGO help (`OptimizerSpec::print_caps`, a refused one its refusal; for an annealer each default of a `PARAM` key as a value the annealer takes, `PARAM CORANA (default OFF)`, or in words, `PARAM T_INIT (default: calibrated with probes)`, and no key the annealer refuses, such as `RESET_TO_BEST` of `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA`), for a `LIB` its section of the table, and throws `tadah explain: unknown optimiser ...` for a `LIB:ALGO` that names no backend; it returns false for any other name. `OptimizerSpec::print_caps(std::ostream&, lib, algo)` (the `std::cout` overload stays). `optimizer_libraries()`, `refused_backend_names()` and `require_registered_backend(lib, algo, where)` (`optimizer_caps.h`; below). `print_caps` was called only from HPO's tests. Tests: `test_optimizer_help.cpp`.
- **The optimiser list, written for the documentation** (`blueprint/optimizers_gen.toml`): every `LIB:ALGO` an `OPTIMIZER` block can name, registered or refused, with its capabilities (among them how a run reads `SEED`: read, in two `OPTIM` dimensions or more, through its local search, or refused), its row of `tadah explain ALGO` and its `tadah explain LIB:ALGO` text, the same in every build of one NLopt version. `blueprint/README.md` describes the file and how to regenerate it.
- **The target-file registry, written for the documentation** (`blueprint/hpotarget.toml` and the part files it lists). It describes every block, key, option, `OPTIM` form, loss function and `PARAM` key of the target file (`--hpotarget`) as `tadah hpo` reads it. Each entry gives the values, the default, the range, the blocks the key may appear in, its repeat rule and the messages of its refusals; behaviour a reader would not expect is recorded with the entry. CTest compares the registry with what `tadah hpo` does: every example, in a complete target file, is accepted, and every invalid line is refused with exactly its message; every message of the registry has an invalid line or a stated reason; every name the parser recognises has an entry, for each block, line and `STRATEGY` `TYPE` apart. Cases generated from the entries compare the blocks of each key, its repeat rule, the ranges and words of its arguments, the ranges of the `INIT` methods and `PARAM` keys (the largest value of a range through the startup checks of `tadah hpo`), the `PARAM` keys of each optimiser, the options of each line and the defaults with the parser; each word of an argument has an example that writes it, and an example writes only the words of its argument. `blueprint/hpotarget_registry.py` loads and checks the files, and `blueprint/README.md` describes them. A rule has a title and says in words what its message holds; every example after the first of an entry has a caption; a range can leave out its minimum or take whole numbers only; `[lammps_script]` says how the script of a `PC_LAMMPS` line takes the candidate potential (`pair_style tadah`, `pair_coeff * * EXTERN <element> ...`), and a test runs its script with the candidate potentials of two evaluations; `blueprint/optimizers_doc.toml` says what each library and each registered optimiser is, and `[abbreviations]` spells out the abbreviations of the text. `blueprint/gen_hpotarget.py` writes the registry's data and messages as C++ tables, `include/tadah/hpo/hpotarget_registry_gen.h` and `hpo_param_registry_gen.h`, which are checked in and installed with the other headers (`blueprint/README.md`, "The generated tables", says how they are regenerated); no source of the program includes them, and test cases compare generated tables with the ones the parser and the startup checks hold by hand. Tests (label `docs`): `test_hpotarget_registry_conformance.cpp`, `test_hpotarget_script_contract.cpp`, `test_hpotarget_registry.py`, `check_hpotarget_coverage.py`, `check_hpotarget_generated.py` (with `tests/tools/hpotarget_case_runner.cpp`), `test_check_hpotarget_generated.py`, `gen_hpotarget.py --check` (`hpo_hpotarget_registry_sync`), `test_gen_hpotarget.py`; without a label, the Catch2 cases of `[hpotarget_gen]` (`test_hpotarget_registry_bindings.cpp`), which the build compiles again when the generated headers appear, change or go (`hpo_has_include_rebuild`, label `cmake`).
- `gradient_cost(spec)` and `gradient_budget_warnings(root, reuse)` (`backend_rules.h`): what one gradient of a search costs, and the startup WARNINGs of the phases whose budget cannot pay for one step (below), in a run that answers a point a search asks for again from its record when `reuse` (`validate_hpo_setup` passes whether `HPOTargets::repeat_reuse_blocker()`, which the host forwards, is `""`).
- `PcTaskResult::hotvar_errors` and `failvar_errors` (`pc_task_exec.h`): the LAMMPS message of each `--hotvar` and `--failvar` whose read-back raised an error, one per variable ("" for one that was read; empty when every one was), as `outvar_errors` is for the outvars; the PC worker protocol is version 4 (its `RESULT` frame carries them).
- **`OPTIM MPARAMS` is checked against the model's function at startup** (`Function_Base::mparams()`, Tadah.MODELS): `OPTIM MPARAMS (i)` is refused on a function that reads fewer than i values (`BF_Linear`, `BF_Polynomial2`, `Kern_Linear`, `Kern_Quadratic` and `Kern_LQ` read none: the search changed nothing), on an integer value (`MPARAMS (1)` of `Kern_Polynomial`, the degree; a candidate was truncated) and, for a value that must be positive (`Kern_RBF`'s sigma), on a box whose low bound is not > 0. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **`OPTIM` on a value its key takes only as an integer, or on an internal key, is refused at its line.** A value every format (blueprint `value_type`) of the key gives `INT` or `UINT` (`SBASIS`; a candidate such as 14.47 was trained as 14): `OPTIM SBASIS (1) is not a valid search target: value 1 of SBASIS is an integer (SBASIS takes UINT), ...`; `OPTIM OALGO (1)` keeps its own message. An internal key (`DSIZE`, `RCUT2BMAX`, `ESTDEV`, ..., computed by tadah; `OPTIM ESTDEV` failed every evaluation with "Attempt to add internal key"): `OPTIM ESTDEV is not a valid search target: ESTDEV is an internal key ...`. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **`OPTIM RCUT2B` / `RCUTMB` on a cutoff that a descriptor takes from its setfl file is refused at startup** (`D_Base::cutoff_fixed_by`, Tadah.MODELS): the cutoff of D2_EAM and DM_EAM, also D2_EAM's or DM_EAM's own value inside a D2_mJoin or DM_mJoin (the other joined descriptors' values stay searchable): `OPTIM RCUT2B (1) is not a valid search target: D2_EAM takes the cutoff 5.58190742024 of its setfl file 'Ta.eam.alloy' (SETFL) and refuses another value, so every candidate but the value of the training configuration would fail.` These descriptors refuse a cutoff other than the file's (Tadah.MODELS 1.6.0). In 1.5.0, whose D2_EAM replaced a differing cutoff with the file's after a WARNING, a thread's reused trainer did not build its D2_EAM again, so the later evaluations of a thread did not use their candidate cutoff, and `best_pot.tadah` recorded a candidate cutoff, which `tadah predict` now refuses. Test: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- `PcWorkerPool::worker_pid(slot)`: the pid of the worker process the pool holds for a slot, -1 when it holds none (after a reaped death until the next task respawns it, after `shutdown()`); `std::out_of_range` for a slot outside the pool. It waits for a task in flight on the slot.
- Tests: `tadah.hpo_headers` (`ctest -L headers`, cmake-tools' `tadah_add_header_check`) compiles every header of `include/` on its own and twice in a row, with what a user of `tadah.hpo` gets and the module's `TADAH_USE_DLIB`.
- Tests: `tadah.hpo_text` (`ctest -L text`, cmake-tools' `tadah_add_text_check`) reads the text files of the repository (those git tracks or would track, in a clone) and fails on a line that carries a person's initials, a reference to co-workers, a decision attributed to the code's owner, the name of an AI tool, a note kept outside the repository or filler wording.
- Tests: `hpo_configure_without_ceres` (label `cmake`) configures HPO once more, with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` OFF and without tests, from the cache of the build that runs it and the source directories of its dependencies (`FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED`: nothing is downloaded), and checks through CMake's file API that `tadah.hpo` has an include directory with `Eigen/Dense` and no Ceres: every HPO test build checks the configuration without Ceres. The initial cache of that configure (`hpo_write_no_ceres_preset()`, `tests/configure_without_ceres/no_ceres_preset.cmake`) holds every cache entry of the build that is not `INTERNAL` or `STATIC` and names no path in its build tree (`CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`, a toolchain file, the libraries it found, its options), the source directory of every dependency, given as `FETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_<NAME>` or populated under `FETCHCONTENT_BASE_DIR`, and `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES` OFF, `BUILD_TESTING` OFF; the environment is CTest's. `hpo_configure_without_ceres_preset` (label `cmake`) checks on a throw-away pair of projects that a configure from it finds a package found only through `-DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`, and gets the build's options, both kinds of dependency source and a `FETCHCONTENT_BASE_DIR` of its own. In a build without Ceres the test cases that use `LIB CERES` as an example backend expect its refusal for their phase (`CERES support is not built; reconfigure with -DTADAH_ENABLE_CERES=ON`; `tests/ceres_build.h`), where seven of them failed expecting the Ceres validators' messages; `RHOFORM NONNEG/MONOTONE: no projection WARNING when an optimiser can leave the box: such a point is not evaluated`, whose optimiser is `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, is Skipped there, naming it, and its sections that need no Ceres are a test case of their own (`RHOFORM NONNEG/MONOTONE: no box WARNING when every phase keeps to the box`).
- Tests: every hidden LAMMPS-driven case has a CTest entry and runs in the suite (`ctest -L bench`, four entries, about 8 s together): `hpo_lammps_hessian` (the LAMMPS cases of `test_lammps_hessian.cpp`: the 4-atom bcc Ta Hessian and its vflag=1 microbench), `hpo_adjoint_pilot` (`test_hpo_adjoint_pilot.cpp`: the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint chain on real relaxations, `dvirial_dW` and `dlx/dW` against finite differences), `hpo_bench_pair_tadah` (`bench_pair_tadah.cpp`) and `hpo_host_e2e` (`test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`: `HPO_Host`'s analytical gradient without a `PC_LAMMPS` line, with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` and with a `PC_LAMMPS` script run in process; the last pins that its provider, which gives no direct θ term, sends every θ to finite differences). The first three run on a Ta potential shipped with the tests, `tests/data/bench_ta.tadah`: a copy of Tadah.MLIP's Quick Start potential (`docs/source/quickstart/pot.tadah` at Tadah.MLIP c91e8ef), whose header gives its provenance and why it is used (a linear model with 9 weights, C11 = 212 GPa and C12 = 91 GPa in the cases' cell); `hpo_host_e2e` trains its own model. `TADAH_BENCH_POT` still names another potential. These 19 cases are hidden Catch cases (`[.][!benchmark]`; the bump-gate case, the twentieth, runs in `hpo_bumpgate_e2e`) and had no CTest entry. Without `TADAH_BENCH_POT` the first 16 passed, 11 with a warning and 5 without one: the default `$TADAH_PATH/HPO_test/pot.tadah`, an HPO-trained M_BLR Ta potential of one developer's workspace, is shipped by no repository. A potential that is missing, or cannot be read, parsed or run, is now a failure. With the Tadah.LAMMPS test potentials the two lattice-constant cases failed their precondition of at least 4 weights (they perturb `WEIGHTS[2]`); with the energies-only model of the Ta test data (`tests/data/ta`, C12 = -103 GPa) the `box_dofs=6` Hessian case failed its sign checks; with the `HPO_test` potential the iso lattice-constant case failed its 1e-4 tolerance, 1.1e-4 (the box Hessian, Fixed below). The `box_dofs=6` case now pins that the Voigt shear rows of the Hessian vanish on its cell (|H| < 1e-2: exactly 0, and 9.95e-4 while the diagonal was the energy's second difference; 9.154 eV on the same cell made triclinic). The cell is an orthogonal box, whose tilt factors LAMMPS ignores, so the assembler's shear strains move nothing: this is the recorded box-convention issue of the analytic-gradient path, not fixed here. The case had asserted that the entries are > 0, which held or failed on the sign of the round-off.
- Tests: what NLopt's MLSL does next to a failure region with each LD_* local search (`test_fd_bounds.cpp`, `No usable gradient: MLSL moves on from a rejected local start ...` and `... MLSL ends when an LD_LBFGS, LD_TNEWTON* or LD_VAR* local search fails at the edge of the failure region ...`). On a one-dimensional stub whose loss fails below a wall, under `G_MLSL_LDS` with an `INNER` block of each of the 12 LD_* algorithms, and `GD_MLSL_LDS` (NLopt's default `LD_MMA`, or the same `INNER` blocks):
  - at a rejected local start (a sample whose stencil point fails), the local search makes 1 request (2 for `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and the `LD_AUGLAG` inners), does not come back to it, and MLSL goes on (60 evaluations, `MAXEVAL_REACHED`);
  - a local search converges on the edge of the failure region, where the stencil of every point below it reaches the wall. With `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR1` or `LD_VAR2` its line search finds no usable point there and returns an NLopt failure, and NLopt's MLSL ends its whole search with it (`mlsl.c` ends on any error of a local search): `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: ... NLopt ended right after it (nlopt failure)` after 141 to 185 of 400 evaluations. With `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_SLSQP` and the `LD_AUGLAG` inners MLSL reaches `MAXEVAL`. HPO cannot end one local search without ending MLSL (a forced stop is an error to MLSL as well), so no code changed; whether HPO should keep MLSL going there is an open question.
  `AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG` with each LD_* `INNER` block stop at the rejected start, as documented.
- `shutdown_script_executor()` (`script_executor.h`): stops the script executor, joining its workers; `IterHealthRecord::layout_matches`, `n_weights` and `layout_cols`: whether the column layout describes the weights (as many columns as weights), and both counts; `IterHealthRecord::block_has_nan_inf`: which blocks hold a NaN or infinite weight (they get no aggregates).
- **Tests of the MPI build (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`), and the suite runs in it.** The registered suite is the serial build's and runs no MPI code; in a `TADAH_HPO_MPI` build `ctest -L mpi` now runs, through CMake's `MPIEXEC_EXECUTABLE` at 1, 2 and 4 ranks, `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix` and: `hpo_mpi_signal_<scenario>` (`tests/mpi/test_signal_mpi.cpp`: a signal one rank receives during rank 0's seed, T0 or PT pass-2 calibration, the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration, a population initialization, a replica rung or a walker sweep ends every rank `SHUTDOWN`, 128 + the signal; see Fixed) and `hpo_mpi_e2e_<case>` (`tests/mpi/hpo_mpi_e2e.py`, the built `tadah` on 1 and 2 ranks: `RESTART` refused on several ranks before any evaluation, tested at spec level only so far; a gradient search (DLIB BFGS) refused on several ranks and its one-sided differences counted on one -- finite differences run only in gradient searches, which a multi-rank launch refuses, so their counters cannot be run on several ranks; a D2_Knot5 + DM_REAM seed under ANNEAL_PT on the LAMMPS EAM-collapse fast path on every rank, the evaluated set and `best_pot.tadah` those of the 1-rank run, at an `Ecoh` target the seed misses so that the global best is a search evaluation rank 1 makes and publishes; the `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE` counts adding up to the 1-rank run's, every rank's density report of the `-c` potential the 1-rank run's, and rank 1's report of the global best the 1-rank run's report of its best; Skipped in a build without the `tadah` executable). The rest of the suite runs in the MPI build too: test_hpo initialises MPI as `tadah` does (a one-rank job; 106 cases aborted there, "MPI_Comm_rank() function was called before MPI_INIT") and every LAMMPS instance its cases create is on `hpo_lammps_comm()` (`LammpsRunner::create_instance` takes no communicator, see Changed); every case that builds a worker pool or asks for `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` -- the twelve `pc pool:` cases that build a pool (two of them on test_main.cpp's stand-in workers, one the width check, whose pools throw before they spawn) and the two `Two runs in one process` cases under `PROCESS` -- calls `SKIP_WITHOUT_PC_PROCESS_WORKERS()` (`tests/pc_process_workers.h`) first and is Skipped in that build, which has no PROCESS mode and refuses every explicit PROCESS (`pc pool: in the MPI build the worker refuses to start`). Two checks keep it so, in every build. In the serial build that call allows the PROCESS workers of its own test case, and a worker of test_hpo that a case starts without it refuses to start, as every worker of the MPI build does, so the case fails there too, whatever code built the pool (`pc pool: in the serial build the worker of a case that did not call SKIP_WITHOUT_PC_PROCESS_WORKERS() refuses to start`); the `tadah` executable's workers, which the e2e cases start, are not test_hpo's. `hpo_mpi_build_tests_lint` (`tests/check_mpi_build_tests.py`) reads HPO's test sources, headers and subdirectories as the MPI build compiles them and fails a case that, without that call as its first statement, uses `PcWorkerPool` other than as a pointer, a reference or a smart-pointer holder, or writes a PROCESS request -- the configuration line `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS`, `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION=PROCESS`, or the mode `"PROCESS"` itself (a `setenv` value, a mode list, a helper's argument), with or without `PC_LAMMPS` lines -- outside a block that checks the MPI build's refusal; such a pool or request outside every case body (a helper takes the mode or the pool from its caller); and, in every build's view, any use of the LAMMPS class (`LAMMPS_NS::LAMMPS`, `LammpsRunner::LMP`) other than as a pointer, a reference or a smart-pointer holder: an instance comes from `create_instance`. It does not see a value assembled from pieces, a request in a file a case reads, or a case that relies on the serial build's default PROCESS through the `tadah` executable (its docstring; its own unit test: `hpo_mpi_build_tests_lint_tool`). The startup-strategy case with `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` checks in the MPI build that its strategy is refused first; and the PC_ISOLATION journal and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` cases assert its THREAD-only behaviour (the latter runs `tadah` under the launcher of one rank, `TADAH_MPIEXEC`: started without it, Open MPI 5 makes its session directory under the case's `TMPDIR`, a regular file, reports the failure but returns `MPI_SUCCESS` from `MPI_Init_thread`, and the first communicator creation crashes).

Nothing but a note protected them: under `core.autocrlf=input` or `true` `git add --renormalize` wrote them with LF endings, and with a global attributes file `* text eol=lf` so did `git add` of an edit, rewriting every line of `asa.c`. Upstream's README documents 0 as "`Gener_Block` is not changed thereafter" and `asa()` resizes only when the window is above 0, but its option check rejected every value below 1, so no setting kept the block size fixed: with `Gener_Block = Gener_Block_Max` the moving average could still shrink it to 1. TADAH-PATCH 7 in `external/asa/asa.c` (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`). Opt-in: the default window (3) and the golden-neutrality gate are unchanged. The block-run banner prints `Gener_Mov_Avr`. Tests: `test_asa.cpp` (every block of a `Gener_Block = Gener_Block_Max = 4`, `Gener_Mov_Avr 0` run holds 4 states; the default window shrinks them).
