The data tests (`test_ta_data_cells.cpp`, `test_shipped_data_stress_sign.cpp`) check the two Ta datasets of that set.
- `GradientProvider::failure` (`GradientFailure`, `GradientFailureInfo`, `gradient_provider.h`): why `gradient()` returned false — `Budget` (MAXEVAL spent), `Stencil` (a failed evaluation the gradient reads: the loss at the point, under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`, or a finite-difference stencil point, see *Fixed*) or `NotFinite` (another cause: an analytical gradient that cannot be formed or is not finite, a quotient that overflows between usable losses) — with the point and the stencil point for the journal. `gradient_stop_status()` and `report_gradient_stop()` give a search that a point without a usable gradient ended its status and journal line. The DLIB, NLopt and Ceres adapters read it (*Fixed*).
- `OptimizerSpec::failscore_grading` and `HPOTargets::set_failscore_grading()`: the `FAILSCORE_GRADING` the finite differences read (a graded failure at the point is differenced, *Fixed*). `HPOTargets` sets it in the outer spec, its `INNER` block, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and that one's `INNER` block; `set_failscore_grading()`, which the `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING` override of `hpo_run()` uses, sets `FAILSCORE_GRADING` and all of them; the hand-written copy constructor keeps it, so the `MULTISTART` starts, the `PRESTAGE` refinement and each `RESTART` round carry it.
- `single_local_search()` (`phase_specs.h`): the local search a run is, when it is one — the spec itself for a local backend, and for `AUGLAG`, which HPO runs without constraints, so it runs its local search once from the starting point, that search — and `nullopt` for a global one (`MLSL`, `GD_STOGO`, …). The NLopt adapter stops a gradient-based one at a starting point without a usable gradient and reads the `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME` of `AUGLAG`'s local search (*Fixed*).
- `seed_stream.h`: `seed_stream(seed, salt, idx)`, the `std::mt19937_64` an optimiser's random consumer draws for its `SEED` (seeded through a `std::seed_seq` of the seed, the consumer's salt and an index; `anneal::make_stream` forwards to it), and `seed_streams`, the registry of the consumers' salts (the `ANNEAL` family's `kAnnealChain`, `kAnnealProbes`, `kAnnealPtSwap`, `kAnnealPaResample`, `kAnnealWarmup`; `kRandomSearch` for `TADAH:RANDOM`, `kAsa` for `ASA`, `kRefinementPhase` for the key of a `PRESTAGE` refinement), checked pairwise distinct at compile time (*Fixed*); `StreamPhase` (`Search`, `Refinement`) and `phase_stream_seed(seed, phase)`, the seed every random stream of an optimiser run is drawn from: the `SEED` itself in a search, a key derived from it (the first number of `seed_stream(SEED, kRefinementPhase)`) in a `PRESTAGE` refinement (*Fixed*).
- `OptimizerSpec::stream_phase`: the `StreamPhase` of the spec; `prestage_refinement_spec()` sets `Refinement` (the copy constructor keeps it, so the refinement's `MULTISTART` starts have it too).
- `testing::ScopedAnalyticalHelperFailure` (`hpo_host.h`): a test seam; while an object lives, `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` fails its gradient helper with `std::runtime_error(what)` at every pass that reaches it, as the helper throws where it cannot form the gradient at a point whose fit trained (*Fixed*).
- Tests: `test_ream_fastpath.cpp` (DM_REAM through the real pair style, EAMFAST vs generic, EMBF0 ZERO/FREE: isolated atom 0.0, forces/virial bitwise equal across references; the EAMFAST and generic kernels agree under either reference in energy, fnorm, virial pressure and forces), `test_hpo_embf0_e2e.cpp` (hidden `[embf0_e2e]`: CEMBFUNC/SEMBFUNC-only search, every candidate's isolated atom exactly 0), `test_hpo_embf0_journal.cpp`, EMBF0 cases in the startup-validation, init-strategy and compact-fitter tests.
- `ctest -L e2e`: the hidden host end-to-end groups run under CTest, one entry (process) each: `hpo_fixweight_e2e` (`[fixweight_e2e]`: pinned and `OPTIM`'d weights, exact potential files, seed anchors, LAMMPS read-backs), `hpo_durable_e2e` (`[durable_e2e]`: best-potential persistence, transient I/O and run endings; it spawns `test_hpo` children), `hpo_embf0_e2e` (`[embf0_e2e]`), `hpo_rcut_e2e` (`[rcut_e2e]`), `hpo_rhoform_e2e` (`[rhoform_e2e]`: `RHOFORM` through `hpo_run`; an amplitude below 0, outside its box, is not evaluated), `hpo_rhogate_e2e` (`[rhogate_e2e]`), `hpo_rhobargate_e2e` (`[rhobargate_e2e]`) and `hpo_bumpgate_e2e` (`[bumpgate_e2e]`: the feasibility gates reject before training). They never ran under CTest. `[rcut_e2e]`'s `OPTIM` box now contains its `CONFIG` seed, which the seed-in-box rule (1.5.0) requires; it failed on that rule before reaching its check. A `[durable_e2e]` case checks that a `MULTISTART` setup that would fail every start (an analytical gradient without a training `DBFILE`) is refused at startup, before any start; `run_starts`' unit test covers a failure confined to the starts. The three cases that run the built `tadah` executable, which `TADAH_BINARY` names (`[tadah_cli]`: two WARM seed anchors and the exit status of a persistence failure), have entries of their own, `hpo_tadah_cli_seed_anchor_warm`, `hpo_tadah_cli_seed_anchor_nlopt` and `hpo_tadah_cli_durable_exit`: in a build with the `tadah` target the entry sets `TADAH_BINARY` to it, and without `TADAH_BINARY` the case calls `TADAH_SKIP`, so CTest reports it as Skipped instead of passing it unchecked. `archive writer never deletes a file it could not open` (`test_append_log.cpp`) is reported as Skipped when it runs as root, where permissions are not enforced.
- `test_strict_parse.cpp`: the text `core::format_roundtrip` writes for every zero or normal double (the bounds of `hpo_optim_resolved.hpo`, the hpotargets `tadah refit` writes) reads back through the hpotarget number grammar (`parse_number`) bit for bit. A subnormal is the one value the grammar refuses, with its too-small note: the grammar stays decimal-only with no hex, `inf`, `nan` or subnormal, while the CORE readers of potential, configuration and dataset files (`core::to_double`) accept subnormals.
- **Transient filesystem errors are retried before a write fails.** On
  network filesystems a lock, write, fsync or rename can fail for a moment
  (EBUSY, EAGAIN, ESTALE, ETXTBSY, EIO, ENOLCK, ETIMEDOUT, EDEADLK; EACCES
  only when the directory is on a network filesystem -- on a local disk it
  is a permission error and fails at once) and succeed when repeated. The
  durable best potential retries its whole lock → read → compare → replace
  transaction with exponential backoff (10 ms doubling to 2 s, ±20 %
  jitter), releasing its locks between attempts, until 30 s have passed
  since the first failure (the failing calls' own time counts; a call that
  blocks by itself can add its duration at either end). A retry that finds
  its own earlier rename in place (the directory fsync failed, or an NFS
  rename reported ENOENT after landing) completes that commit. Other
  permanent errors (ENOSPC, EROFS, ENOENT, ...) are not retried. The
  per-evaluation logs (`loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah`, `params.tadah`,
  `stats.tadah` and their `best_*` twins), whose write errors were never
  checked, are appended one batch per writer drain
  (`io::RetryingAppendFile`): a failed batch -- a partial write included --
  is truncated back and rewritten, with ONE 10 s budget per drain shared by
  every file and `DUMP`/`BDUMP` archive (each still makes its own attempt,
  plus one immediate re-attempt after a permanent errno). A batch that
  still fails is dropped whole and reported with the rows lost, and that
  file gets single attempts until a write succeeds (repeats summarised, an
  INFO line when writes resume); the run goes on. Archives are written
  whole or not at all. After a shutdown signal retrying goes on for at most
  2 s (`BackoffPolicy::stop_grace`, per durable publication and per drain),
  so one blip after a scheduler's time-limit warning does not lose the
  in-flight best or the last rows. Every retry is a WARNING; DIAGNOSTICS
  gains `Transient I/O retries : N (best-potential a, per-eval
  logs/archives b); rows lost c; archives lost d` only when one of them is
  non-zero, so clean runs write byte-identical journals and files. New API:
  `durable_best::SysError` (errno, operation, path, `may_have_landed()`),
  `durable_best::Options` with `default_backoff()` and 4-argument overloads
  (the 3-argument forms keep the default policy), `io::BackoffPolicy`,
  `io::retry`. Test-only knobs: `TADAH_HPO_TEST_IO_FAULT="site,ERRNO
  [,skip=K][,count=N|always][,partial=B][,land][,latency=MS][;...]"` and
  `TADAH_HPO_TEST_IO_BACKOFF_MS="initial,cap,budget[,jitter]"`
  (`io::testing::ScopedFaults` in-process); with no faults installed an I/O
  call costs one relaxed atomic load. Tests: `test_durable_best.cpp`,
  `test_append_log.cpp`, `[durable_e2e]` in `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp`.
- **Density gates `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE`** (root keys; `rho_gates.h` / `rho_gates.cpp`), for a model whose many-body term is one DM_REAM term or one DM_Embed component (DM_mJoin, or several components, are refused). Both reject a candidate before training, right after its context is built and before any MPI traffic, as a graded band-D failure with the `BUMPGATE` bookkeeping (evaluation number, rows, stagnation, `record_fail_band`; `DUMP` potential header-only).
  - `RHOGATE <tol> [R_LO <r>] [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (`RHOFORM SIGNED` only; the positive forms guarantee rho >= 0): scans rho_raw(r) = f_c sum a_n psi_n with `ReamDensity::scan_grid` on NPTS points (default 20001) of [R_LO, RCUTMB] (default R_LO = 0.15 RCUTMB) and fails when rho_min < -tol rho_max, with v = min(1e8, v_peak + v_int) (the worst dip plus its mean depth, relative to rho_max).
  - `RHOBARGATE <bcc|fcc|hcp> <a0> <f_lo> <f_hi> [NPTS <n>] [SEED ERROR|WARN]` (every form): fails when the monatomic lattice density rhobar(a) = sum_s m_s rho_raw(d_s a) is not strictly decreasing on a = a0 [f_lo, f_hi] (NPTS points, default 1001; shells enumerated once for the largest reachable RCUTMB), with v = min(1e8, sum of the rises / max |rhobar|); a flat step fails at v = 0.
  - Saturated measures (DBL_MAX, the top of band D): a non-finite density, rho_max <= 0 or rhobar = 0, a pair weight <= 0, an empty window. A density DM_REAM would refuse gets no verdict (it fails in training as before).
  - Strict grammar: the numbers follow the number grammar of every hpotarget key (`strict_parse.h`: `tol`, `R_LO`, `a0`, `f_lo` and `f_hi` are single finite decimal tokens, so no hexadecimal float, inf, nan or subnormal; `NPTS` an integer in decimal digits, so no sign, fraction or exponent; errors name the line); keywords and values case-insensitive, duplicates, unknown or repeated keywords and missing values refused; no environment override. Startup checks (`validate_hpo_setup`): one DM_REAM term or one DM_Embed component, `RHOGATE` under `SIGNED` only, `R_LO` and the first `RHOBARGATE` shell inside the smallest reachable RCUTMB (knot-aware). Their refusals name the `RHOGATE` or `RHOBARGATE` line, except the refusal of a starting point and the `f_lo` limit of `RHOBARGATE`.
  - Arming: a gate is evaluated per candidate, its pair weights included; it is armed by an OPTIM line (in any case, anywhere in the file) on `AMPGRIDMB`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB` or `RCUTMB` of a DM_REAM term, or on a `<ID>.DENSITY.<parameter>` path of a DM_Embed component (`HPOTargets::density_gate_keys`). A gate without one cannot act (every candidate has the `-c` density and its one verdict; an OPTIM `WATOM` only scales it) and is an error at its line naming those keys (it was accepted with a "not armed" INFO line and the `-c` verdict reused). A pair weight no candidate can make positive is a startup error under either SEED mode.
  - Seeds: the `-c` potential and every CONFIG/WARM point of the resolved INIT chains and of a RESTART block's METHOD chain (`RestartSpec::seed_points`; `RESTART WARM <file>` in the messages) are evaluated as candidates at startup; `SEED ERROR` (default) stops the run with one message naming every failing seed, `SEED WARN` warns. One INFO state line per gate.
  - Combined grading: when any gate fails every configured gate is evaluated, the candidate is graded by min(DBL_MAX, the sum of the failing gates' v) and counted for the first failing gate (BUMPGATE, RHOGATE, RHOBARGATE); its reject line names the others. Reject lines (`%.4e`) are rate-limited per gate (first 5, then powers of two). SUMMARY gains ` rhogate=N` / ` rhobargate=N` next to ` bumpgate=N`, DIAGNOSTICS `of which rho-sign gate rejects: N` / `of which lattice-rhobar gate rejects: N`.
  - Verdicts are pure functions of the candidate and the settings; the gates read the candidate context through stored values only, so the context and every written potential are the same with and without them. Measured cost per candidate: 0.7 ms (RHOGATE, 20001 points, 20 Blip5 bumps), 0.3 ms (RHOBARGATE, 1001 points).
- **Density report** (`HPOLog::write_density_report`, `record_density_best`), for every run with a bare DM_REAM many-body term, gated or not: POTENTIAL SETUP rows `Density form`, `Density rho(r), -c pot` (window, extremes and their r, rel = rho_min/rho_max, the largest forward-difference slope, "non-increasing on window: yes/no" with a tolerance of 8 eps times the largest term sum, the pair weights W, the RHOGATE verdict) and `Density rhobar` under RHOBARGATE, one set per distinct WARM seed; RESULT repeats them for the best (`n/a (not derivable)` when the density could not be built at startup). A summary that cannot be computed prints `not derivable: <reason>`; the report never throws.
- **A `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` density is never evaluated with a negative amplitude.** Under `RHOFORM NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` an `AMPGRIDMB` box does not reach below 0 (*Changed*, non-negative boxes), finite differences keep to the box with either step rule (*Changed*, the bounded stencil), a coordinate within rounding below 0 is evaluated on the bound, 0, and a point an optimiser that ignores the bounds proposes below it is not evaluated (*Changed*, the BREAKING entry on points outside the OPTIM box), so every evaluated and recorded point is a valid potential. No change under `SIGNED` or inside the box.
- **Hard lower bounds in the bound-occupancy audit** (`BoundOccupancy::set_hard_lo`): an `AMPGRIDMB` lower bound of exactly 0 under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE` is marked `LO*` with a note that 0 is an admissible optimum, and the widen-that-side advice and HINT are printed only for other marks.
- INIT provenance: `InitOrigin` and `OptimizerSpec::optim_init_origin` (parallel to `optim_init`), filled through `InitStrategy::generate_tagged`; a chain keeps the first origin of a de-duplicated point. `detail::update_candidate_context` (`candidate_context.h`) is the extracted body of `HPO_Host::update_context` (now a one-line call), so startup seed checks build a seed's context exactly as the host builds a candidate's.
- Tests: `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_rho_gates.cpp` (+ the frozen references in `rho_gate_fixtures.h`: extremes bitwise, verdicts at five tolerances and every v bitwise on the nine campaign fixtures, ALL_E rising at 1307 of 3501 / 373 of 1001 steps), `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp`, `compact_fitter_signed_goldens.h` (the SIGNED compact fitter bitwise against the fitter before this change), RHOFORM cases in `test_compact_fitter.cpp`, `test_bound_audit.cpp` and `test_ream_fastpath.cpp` (through `pair_style tadah`: the `RHOFORM` banner line on file and `EXTERN` loads, the migration text in the error of a key-less potential, `NONNEG` equal to `SIGNED`, and `MONOTONE` EAMFAST matching the generic kernel); hidden tags `[rhoform_e2e]`, `[rhoform_projection_e2e]`, `[rhogate_e2e]`, `[rhobargate_e2e]`, `[rho_gates_bench]`. Every DM_REAM fixture declares `RHOFORM`.
- **Named component OPTIM paths** with selected-coordinate bounds, AUTO rules, candidate updates, warm starts (WARM reads a saved potential's blocks through its configuration parser) and exact checkpoint persistence. Component spatial radii determine neighbor ranges; envelope matching densities do not. A legacy many-body key (`CGRIDMB`, `SGRIDMB`, `AMPGRIDMB`, `CEMBFUNC`, `SEMBFUNC`, `RCUTMB`) on a potential whose many-body term is DM_Embed, which has none, is refused naming the component path (`OPTIM CGRIDMB: the potential has no CGRIDMB key: ... the named path TA.DENSITY.CENTRES; ...`; `HPOTargets::optim_component_key_error`). Test: `DM_Embed OPTIM of a legacy many-body key names the component path`.
- Component-aware NONE support gates and single-component density gates (armed by an `OPTIM` line on a `<ID>.DENSITY.*` path); local zero-reference diagnostics and CLI energy-reference advice for components.
- **Compact component refitting** by variable projection over independent density/embedding centres and scales, retaining selected physical-width coordinates. Pair shapes use the configured pair grid; legacy ladder settings are refused for this path. The configured grids are evaluated before the solve, so a precondition refusal reports itself (`the density component CU's FORM NONNEG cannot fit this table ...`); during the solve a trial point the model refuses (`std::runtime_error`) is infeasible, and any other exception stops the refit after the solve. The reachability invariant `r_reach >= dr` is checked as for the compact ladder. The Ceres solve is silent (`ceres::SILENT`), its iteration limit is `REFIT_COMPONENT_ITERATIONS` (default 60), and a solve that stops at the limit before converging is written with a WARNING naming the stop and the key. Tests: `test_embed_compact_refit.cpp`.
- LAMMPS generic, accelerated and legacy parity coverage for `DM_Embed`.
- **`tadah hpo --check`: the startup checks of a run, without the run** (`hpo_run()` with `CHECK true`). With `CHECK true` in its context (`tadah hpo --check`, or `CHECK true` in a training configuration without `WEIGHTS`) `hpo_run()` makes the checks the run makes before its first evaluation, in the run's order -- the working directory and the journal, result files, best potentials and locks the run writes there (`HPOTargets::check_run_outputs()`), the target file, its `DUMP` and `BDUMP` directories and the LAMMPS scripts of its `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_FAILPROBE` lines, the search-space files the run writes (`HPOTargets::check_search_space_files()`), `validate_hpo_setup()`, `PC_ISOLATION` and its environment overrides, `THREADS`, the training and validation datasets (loaded as the run loads them), `HEALTH_LOG`, the pre-flight audit and the `STRATEGY TYPE` and `RESTART` settings -- then writes a summary to standard output (the search parameters with their boxes and scales, the optimiser of each block with its stops, the loss terms with their targets, weights and loss functions (`Loss terms:` and rows such as `PC_ERMSE  target 0, weight 1, loss L2`), the numbers of training and validation structures, and the WARNING and HINT lines the run writes to its journal at startup, which the first line counts: `tadah hpo --check: config.hpo passed the startup checks, with 2 warnings.`) and returns 0. It evaluates nothing, installs no signal handler, starts no worker (a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker that cannot start stops the run only), does not take the lock of `best_pot.tadah` (a lock file that exists and cannot be opened to read and write stops it, as it stops the run; a lock another run holds stops the run only) and writes no file: no journal, no search-space sidecar, no result file, no `DUMP` or `BDUMP` directory. An error is thrown as the run throws it, with the run's message; the WARNING and HINT lines of the startup before it go to standard error first. `CHECK` is taken out of the context (`CHECK false` too) before the training record is taken, so a potential of `tadah hpo` records no `CHECK`; it is a setting of the run, so no potential has a `CHECK` key line and no parse reads one (Tadah.CORE's `run_setting_keys()`, Tadah.MLIP's `training_record_keys()`). `HPO_Host` takes a `HostPurpose`: `Run` (the default) or `Check`, which loads and checks the data, writes no file, and evaluates nothing (`operator()` and `prepare_best_publication()` throw `std::logic_error`). `HPOLog::begin_without_journal()` closes a journal an earlier run in the process left open and starts the run state afresh without a file, and keeps the WARNING and HINT events (`HPOLog::notes_without_journal()`). Tests: `test_hpo_check.cpp`.
- `cutoff_channels.h`: `CutoffChannel`, `knot_channels(ctx, sfx)`, `bump_channels(ctx, sfx)`, `has_knot_channel()` and `set_channel_rcut()`, the knot and bump channels of a `TYPE2B` or `TYPEMB` line, a line of one descriptor or each descriptor of a joined line with its own context and the first entry of each of its keys (*Fixed*, joined lines). `derive_binding_rcut(ctx, trg, sfx, first)` reads the `RCUT` values of a descriptor's own context as entries `first + i` of the whole key. `OptimizerSpec::key_lines` (`KeyLine`: the line of `LIB`, `ALGO`, `STEP` and `GRAD_STRATEGY`) and `StepOverride::line`, `with_key_line()` and `with_line()` (`phase_specs.h`); `reads_seed(spec)` and `seed_unread_reason(spec)`; `OptimizerSpec::seed_drawn` and `InitStrategySpec::seed_drawn`; `HPOTargets::warn_warm_of_best_pot()`.

### Changed
- **Many messages of `tadah hpo` say what is refused and why, and what each setting does, without instructions to the reader**; where 1.5.0 named no key, line or file, they name them. Among them:
  - the lines of the target file: a last line that ends with `\` (`Error on line 8: the line ends with \, which continues it on the next line, and the file has no next line`, with the line as written; 1.5.0: `unfinished line 8`); a target file that cannot be opened (`the target file run.hpo (--hpotarget) cannot be opened`; 1.5.0: `HPO file run.hpo not found`); an unknown key inside a block, which names the block, the line that opened it and its END line (`unknown key 'FOO' inside the STRATEGY block opened on line 2 (ENDSTRATEGY closes it)`), and a top-level key inside a block that is still open (`LOSS is a top-level key, and the STRATEGY block opened on line 2 is still open (ENDSTRATEGY closes it)`); a key or an option given twice (`OPTIM_SCALE is set twice (first on line 1); each key is given once`);
  - `OPTIM` lines: a key no search can take (`WEIGHTS`, `EMBF0`, structure keys, the choices and switches of the fit) says what the key holds; a key that is no key of the training configuration is named with the closest key (`SGRIDMB2 is not a key of the training configuration (closest key: SGRIDMB)`), and a key without a line in it is named so (`the training configuration has no AMPGRIDMB line, and OPTIM AMPGRIDMB searches the values the training configuration gives`); the `AUTO` options (`'X' is not an AUTO option (RATIO, FRAC, FLOOR or GROUP_FLOOR)`, an option given twice, `RATIO` beside `FRAC`), a box without width or not finite, the `LOG` and `ASINH` boxes, and an index past the values of a key name the key, the bound and the value; a line without indices names its key (`OPTIM CGRID2B: the key is followed by its indices in parentheses, such as OPTIM CGRID2B (1)`; 1.5.0: `No parentheses found up to: 2 1.5 2.5`); a starting point outside its `OPTIM` box names its `INIT` block and the optimiser (`the starting point 1 of the INIT block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser: CGRID2B[2] = 0.583333 lies outside its OPTIM box [9, 10]`; 1.5.0: `INIT[OUTER] starting point 1: ...; move the seed inside the box or widen the box`);
  - `PC_LAMMPS`, `PC_FAILPROBE` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` lines: an option without its value (`PC_LAMMPS --script takes one value, the LAMMPS script file`; 1.5.0: `Invalid number of arguments for --script`), a line without `--script` or `--varloss` (1.5.0: `LammpsSim object must have a script specified.`), a script without a command, an unknown option (`(closest option: '--hotvar')`), the reserved LAMMPS variable `maxtime`, `--ncpu`, an unknown loss function (`LOSS: unknown loss function 'FOO'; the loss functions are L1, L2, LOG_COSH, MAPE, HUBER, RMSLE and TUKEY, in any case`; 1.5.0: `LossFunction::from_string: unknown type 'FOO'`), and the options of `PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` (`--weight must be >= 0 (got -1)`, `--hotvar a0 <hi> must be greater than <lo> (got <lo> 2.5, <hi> 1.5)`, `--lattice takes bcc, the one lattice of the term (got 'fcc')`); `--varloss` without its values (`PC_LAMMPS --varloss takes <name> <target> <weight> (E has no weight)`; 1.5.0: `--varloss E: missing <weight>`); a file with `PC_OBSERVABLE` and `PC_LAMMPS` lines is refused at the first line of the group that comes later in the file, naming the lines of the other (`PC_LAMMPS: this target file also has 1 PC_OBSERVABLE line (line 7); PC_OBSERVABLE lines do not share a target file with PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE lines`; 1.5.0 named no line and said `these paths are mutually exclusive in the current MVP`), and one `--hotvar` name on two lines at the later of the two lines;
  - the `OPTIMIZER` block and the blocks inside it: `METHOD` lines (`METHOD FOO: unknown method; the methods are CONFIG, RANDOM, LHS, SOBOL and WARM, in upper case`; `METHOD NOISE_AUTO` and `GRAD_STRATEGY AUTODIFF` get the messages of an unknown word), `GRAD_STRATEGY` without a value (`GRAD_STRATEGY takes one value: NUMERICAL, ANALYTICAL or HYBRID`; 1.5.0: `GRAD_STRATEGY must be one of NUMERICAL, ANALYTICAL, HYBRID (got '')`), a `STRATEGY` or `NOISE` block outside the `OPTIMIZER` block (`STRATEGY block must appear directly inside OPTIMIZER`; 1.5.0: `... inside OPTIMIZER (OUTER level)`), `INNER` blocks (`INNER block given twice for one optimiser (the first on line 6); STRATEGY and REFINE configure the same optimiser, and so do two EXPLORE blocks`; 1.5.0: `INNER block specified more than once in this scope`), an optimiser that does not use `STEP`, named as the other startup checks name it (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser: DLIB:BFGS does not use STEP; ...`; 1.5.0: `DLIB:BFGS (OUTER) does not use STEP; ...`), the refusals of the Ceres optimisers and the startup WARNING of `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, which name Ceres' line-search solver and the DIAGNOSTICS section of `hpo_run.log` (1.5.0: `Ceres GradientProblemSolver enforces no box constraints: ...`), keys of another `STRATEGY TYPE`, the `PARAM` keys of `ASA:ASA` (an ASA option `tadah hpo` does not take is named so, with its reason; an index of a per-dimension option past the dimensions; a value out of range with the value), of the annealers (`TADAH:ANNEAL_PT: PARAM REANNEAL is a key of TADAH:ANNEAL`) and of `TADAH:GRID` (`PARAM GRID_POINTS must be an integer from 2 to 4294967295, got '1' (the points of each dimension include both of its bounds)`), and a launch on several MPI ranks (`a run on 2 MPI ranks takes STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN with TADAH:ANNEAL_PT or TADAH:ANNEAL_PA only, ...`);
  - `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` without a usable `FAILSCORE` (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON grades failures in bands below FAILSCORE, which needs a FAILSCORE above 0 and below 1e300 (without a FAILSCORE line, FAILSCORE is the largest double)`; 1.5.0: ``FAILSCORE_GRADING ON requires a finite positive FAILSCORE (e.g. `FAILSCORE 1e9`); the default DBL_MAX sentinel cannot be banded.``), the hints of the removed keys `STAGE_GLOBAL`, `INIT_INCLUDE_CONFIG` and `INCLUDE_CONFIG`, which state the rule (``'INCLUDE_CONFIG' was removed from the grammar: the values of the training configuration for the search parameters are a starting point only when the INIT block has a `METHOD CONFIG` line.``), and the stage blocks of `tadah refit` (`EXPLORE: LIB DLIB does not match ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS, an NLopt algorithm, whose LIB is NLOPT; LIB DLIB takes ALGO GLOBAL`).
  Lines of `hpo_run.log` that a script may read changed: one line for each `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_FAILPROBE` line on its script limit, where the limit comes from and the deadline of its worker tasks (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT of the PC_LAMMPS line on line 12: 30 s (MAXTIME on line 11), set as LAMMPS \`timer timeout\` at the start of the script and after each clear; worker task deadline: 150 s, max(3t, t + 120 s) of the limit t`; 1.5.0 wrote one `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT: <limit> (from <key>) -- ...` line for the run), with a note that a top-level `MAXTIME` is `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` under another name; `THREADS: <n> workers run the work of the evaluations ...` (1.5.0: `ScriptExecutor: <n> workers = THREADS ...`); `OpenMP: at most <n> threads ...`; `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS: ...` and `PC_ISOLATION THREAD ...`; `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON: ...`, which names the four bands; the calibration of the starting temperature of the annealers (`T0=... from <n> probes: median rise of the loss ...`); for `ANNEAL_PT`, the step warm-up and the second calibration pass; and the WARNING of `ANNEAL_PT` and `ANNEAL_PA` without `THREADS` (`ANNEAL_PT: THREADS is not set, so the 2 replicas run on 1 worker thread; THREADS <n> in the STRATEGY block evaluates up to n of them at a time`; 1.5.0: `ANNEAL_PT: THREADS not set -- running 2 replicas on 1 worker thread. ...`). A step warm-up of `ANNEAL_PT` that stops at its budget, a quarter of `MAXEVAL`, is a WARNING that gives the evaluations a complete warm-up takes, and a journal that cannot be opened is a WARNING that names it.
- **BREAKING — `DLIB:BFGS` is refused with a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line**, as every other gradient-based optimiser is, at its `ALGO` line, naming the line of the first `PC_LAMMPS` line (`Error on line 4: --hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS on line 11 but OPTIMIZER's optimiser DLIB:BFGS is gradient-based. A PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE line requires a gradient-free optimiser (ASA:ASA, DLIB:GFS, ...); an NLopt MLSL or AUGLAG algorithm is accepted with a derivative-free INNER block.`). The refusals of a gradient with such a line name the `PC_FAILPROBE` line in a file without a `PC_LAMMPS` line (`--hpotarget defines PC_FAILPROBE on line 12 but ...`). The results of a LAMMPS script have no gradient: 1.5.0 accepted `DLIB:BFGS` and differentiated the term by finite differences, two runs of every script per search parameter for each gradient. A target file converts by naming a derivative-free optimiser, such as `LIB NLOPT` with `ALGO LN_BOBYQA`.
- **BREAKING — a failure signal fails its evaluation under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` too.** A `PC_LAMMPS --failvar` value above 0, a `PC_FAILPROBE` probe value above `--gate`, and a probe value that is negative, not finite or at or above the probe's `--failscore` fail the evaluation: under `OFF`, the default, it scores `FAILSCORE`, and under `ON` the failure is graded, as in 1.5.0. In both modes `hpo_run.log` has a rate-limited `WARNING` for a `--failvar` (`PC_LAMMPS --failvar above 0 (fail.in, occurrence 1): bad = 1: the simulation reports a failure, and its evaluation fails`) and for such a probe value (`eval 1 FAILPROBE: S=inf is not a probe value (one is at least 0 and below the line's --failscore, 1e+16) -- the evaluation fails (total=1)`), which also stops the evaluation's other scripts, as a probe value above `--gate` does. Under `OFF`, 1.5.0 ignored all three: the evaluation kept the loss of the failed simulation's variables and could become the best potential; under `ON` it graded them without a word, and the other scripts of an evaluation whose probe value was no probe value ran on. The settings that only grade a failure are refused under `OFF` when the run starts, at their line: `PC_FAILPROBE --s0` (`PC_FAILPROBE --s0 grades failed evaluations, which FAILSCORE_GRADING ON does; ...`) and an explicit `--failvar` scale (`--failvar bad 2: the scale grades the failure, which FAILSCORE_GRADING ON does; ...`). The warning about a `PC_FAILPROBE` line under `OFF` is given for a line without `--gate` only.
- **BREAKING — a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term takes the `LOSS` line read above it**, as a `PC_LAMMPS --varloss` term does, and `--loss <name> [<parameter>]` takes the parameter of `HUBER` and `TUKEY` (`--loss HUBER 5`). In 1.5.0 the term was `L2`, whatever `LOSS` said, unless `--loss` named another, and a parameter after `--loss` was refused as an unknown flag. A `LOSS` line is read by the `--varloss` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms after it that name no loss function, so a second `LOSS` line is refused unless such a term between reads the first (`LOSS is set twice (first on line 1) and no line between reads the first (a --varloss or PC_OBSERVABLE term that names no loss function); each key is given once`); a `PC_FAILPROBE` line, which has no loss term, reads none. A term under `MAPE` or `RMSLE` has an analytical derivative with respect to its value, the one the metric lines take, so `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` accepts it; 1.5.0 differentiated such a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term by finite differences, which `HYBRID` takes and which stopped an `ANALYTICAL` run at its first gradient.
- **BREAKING — `LOSS MAPE` divides by the target**, |m − t| / |t|; 1.5.0 divided by the absolute measured value plus 1e-12. A target that the loss function of its term cannot measure is refused at its line: 0 under `MAPE`, and one at or below -1e-12 under `RMSLE`, whose logarithm failed every evaluation. The check applies to a metric line under the last `LOSS` line (`PC_ERMSE 0: the metric lines take the loss function of the last LOSS line (line 1): MAPE divides by the target, and the target is 0`), a `--varloss` term (`--varloss E -1: RMSLE takes ln(target + 1e-12), and the target -1 is at or below -1e-12`) and a `PC_OBSERVABLE` term (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy --target 0: ...`).
- **BREAKING — an option that takes one value, given twice on its line, is refused**, as a key given twice is: `--script`, `--failscore` and `--maxtime` of `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_FAILPROBE`, `--gate` and `--s0` of `PC_FAILPROBE`, every option of `PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy`, and `FLOOR` and `GROUP_FLOOR` of an `OPTIM ... AUTO` line (`PC_LAMMPS --script is given twice on this line; each option is given once`). 1.5.0 took the last one and dropped the first without a word: of two `--script` options, the first script never ran. A line declares a variable once with `--hotvar` or `--failvar` (`--hotvar a is declared twice on this line; each variable is declared once`) and defines it once with `--invar`, `-var` or `-v` (`--invar a: the variable a is defined twice on this line (first by --invar a); each variable is defined once`).
- **BREAKING — a `PC_LAMMPS` line that defines a hot value's name is refused when the file is read**: a name that `--invar`, `-var` or `-v` defines and that is a `--hotvar` of the line (`--hotvar a: the variable a is defined on this line by --invar a too; a --hotvar variable is injected as `variable a equal <value>`, which LAMMPS refuses for a variable of another style`), or, on a line without `--hotvar`, the `--hotvar` of another line when the line's script reads it (`PC_LAMMPS: the script cold.in reads a, the --hotvar of line 3, and this line defines a (--invar a): ...`). In 1.5.0 the refused injection passed without a word, and the script ran on the value the line defined (a usable loss, nothing in the journal). A script with a `--hotvar` is injected its own hot values only, so its line may define another line's hot name, whose value it then reads.
- **BREAKING — a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` script with a `quit` command is refused** when the file is read, also a `quit` that an `if` command runs (`PC_LAMMPS: the script run.in has a quit command, command 12 of the script with its include files read in (quit): quit ends the process that runs the script (tadah hpo itself under PC_ISOLATION THREAD)`). Under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD`, `quit` ended `tadah hpo`; under `PROCESS` it ended the worker, and every evaluation failed with a closed channel. A worker whose script ends its process is journalled with the script and the evaluation (`...; the script run.in of evaluation 7 ended the worker process (a quit command, or another command that ends the process)`).
- **BREAKING — a target file with a `PC_FAILPROBE` line and no validation metric, `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_OBSERVABLE` line has no loss source** and is refused when the run starts (`No loss source: a loss term comes from a PC_EMAE, PC_ERMSE, PC_ErRMSE, PC_ERSQ, PC_FRMSE or PC_SRMSE_GPA line (with --validation), a PC_OBSERVABLE line or a PC_LAMMPS line, and the target file has none (a PC_FAILPROBE line gives no loss term)`). 1.5.0 accepted it, although no line of it gave a loss term.
- **BREAKING — a `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` line that no line after it reads, and in a file without a metric line such a `LOSS` line, is refused at its line.** A line reads the value of a `FAILSCORE`, `LOSS` or `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` line above it when it uses it: a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line without `--failscore` the `FAILSCORE`, one without `--maxtime` the `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`, and a `--varloss` or `PC_OBSERVABLE` term that names no loss function the `LOSS`. A `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` (`MAXTIME`) line that no such line follows is refused (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT sets the script timeout of the PC_LAMMPS and PC_FAILPROBE lines after it that give no --maxtime, and no such line follows it; it would have no effect`), and so is, in a file without a metric line, a `LOSS` line that no such term follows (`LOSS sets the loss function of the --varloss and PC_OBSERVABLE terms after it that name none, ...`), and a second line of the three keys with no line between that reads the first. 1.5.0 read these lines and used none of them.
- **BREAKING — `PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` refuses settings it cannot use.** `--species` is the symbol of an element, in its case (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy: --species ta is not the symbol of an element`), and, when the run starts, an element of the potential (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy --species W: the potential has no element W (its elements: Ta)`): 1.5.0 failed every evaluation on another word, and gave a constant term for an element the potential lacks. A `--hotvar a0` start outside its window is refused (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy: --hotvar a0 <init> 3 is outside its window [1.5, 2.5]`); 1.5.0 moved it to the nearer bound. `--fmax-converged` is a number above 0.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy --fmax-converged` is the force tolerance of the relaxation**: the relaxation stops when the largest atomic force, in eV/Å, and the strain force of the cell, |dE/dε| / (3V) for its isotropic strain ε and volume V, the magnitude of the cell's pressure in eV/Å³, both fall below it; the default 0.1 allows 0.1 eV/Å³, about 16 GPa. One that stops at `--maxiter`, `--maxeval` or `--max-wall-time` before then, or when no step lowers its energy, reports the `--unconverged` value (one that `--etol` stops counts as converged when its largest atomic force is below it). 1.5.0 relaxed to a fixed tolerance of 0.1 and compared only the largest atomic force with the value: a value above 0.1 changed nothing, and a smaller one made a relaxation unconverged only when the atomic forces, which a bcc cell has only from numerical error, were above it.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy --unconverged force-scaled <slope>` reports the target plus the slope times the larger of the largest atomic force and the strain force of the cell at the end of the relaxation**, the two forces `--fmax-converged` bounds; in the bcc cell the atomic forces vanish by symmetry, so the strain force decides. 1.5.0 took the largest atomic force alone, which the bcc cell had only from an error of the relaxer's neighbour lists (fixed in Tadah.MLIP): the value grew with that error, not with the distance from convergence.
- **BREAKING — `tadah hpo` refuses a training configuration with a key that Tadah! 1.6.0 retired** (`EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT`, `SWEIGHT`, `WDBFILE_AUTO`, `EWEIGHT_TEMP`; Tadah.CORE's retired keys), its own line or one of a file it includes with `CONFIG`, when it starts, by the run and by `--check`, naming each line and its conversion (`tadah hpo: the configuration has keys retired in Tadah! 1.6.0, and a fit refuses them: EWEIGHT 1.0 (line 18 of outer.train) and FWEIGHT 100 (line 1 of inner.train; FORCE false here: converted by deleting the line). ...`; under M_KRR implementation 2, whose solve of 1.5.0 read none of them, every line is converted by its deletion, with `ESIGMA 1000`), and the error scales every fit refuses (`tadah hpo: ESIGMA -1: the energy error scale is a number > 0 ...`; MLIP `require_fit_settings`). `OPTIM` of a retired key is refused at its line with its conversion. The validation configuration (`--validation`) refuses `ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA`, `ENORM`, `ENERGY` and the retired keys at their line, as it refuses `FORCE` and `STRESS` (`Key ESIGMA on line 2 of config.val is not accepted here: the validation set is not fitted; ...`). A `WARM` seed with lines of retired keys (a potential of an earlier version) is read for its `OPTIM` values with one notice naming them. In 1.5.0 these keys weighted the fit; the error scales replace them (the conversion of each is in its refusal). Tests: `test_hpo_error_scales.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — the stress metric is `PC_SRMSE_GPA`, in GPa, and `PC_SRMSE` is refused** at its line with the conversion (`'PC_SRMSE' was removed from the grammar: replaced by PC_SRMSE_GPA, the root mean square of the nine entries of each residual stress tensor in GPa (PC_SRMSE was that of the six entries x <= y in eV/A^3). PC_SRMSE_GPA of a residual is 160.2 times its PC_SRMSE when the six entries have equal mean squares, 130.8 times for a residual on the diagonal only (a pressure) and 185.0 times for a shear residual only: ...`). A target or `--skip-above` cap of `PC_SRMSE` times 160.2 is about that of `PC_SRMSE_GPA`, and a weight under `LOSS L2` divided by about 25 670. The columns `SRMSE` and `L(SRMSE)` are `SRMSE_GPA` and `L(SRMSE_GPA)`.
- **BREAKING — `tadah hpo` refuses when it starts what every evaluation would refuse**, with the message of the fit, before any evaluation. When its evaluations fit the model (training data, and `FIXINDEX` not pinning every weight): a `LAMBDA` of the training configuration that every fit refuses (a negative value other than -1, a second value, -1 under an `OALGO` other than 3 or under `M_KRR` implementation 2: `LAMBDA -2: only LAMBDA -1 requests the evidence approximation, which OALGO 3 runs; ...`), `OALGO 3` with a `LAMBDA` other than 0 when the fit has an intercept column that `FIXINDEX` does not pin (`BIAS true`: `LAMBDA -1 requests the evidence approximation, which only OALGO 3 runs; OALGO 3 penalises every weight alike, and the model has 1 intercept column(s) ...`), and so `OPTIM LAMBDA` under `OALGO 3` with such a column, every candidate of which but 0 the fit would refuse (`OPTIM LAMBDA: OALGO 3 penalises every weight alike, and the fit has 1 intercept column(s) ...`), and, under `M_KRR` implementation 2, `OPTIM` of `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` or `WDBFILE` (`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2 fits the energies per atom of whole structures over ESIGMA, without forces, stresses or priorities: OPTIM WDBFILE would be ignored. ...`) and `OPTIM OALGO`, which its Cholesky solve does not read (`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2 solves (K + LAMBDA sigma^2 I) w = t by Cholesky and reads no OALGO: OPTIM OALGO would be ignored. ...`). Whatever the evaluations fit, an `M_KRR` implementation other than 1 and 2 (`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 3: M_KRR has implementation 1, ...; 3 is neither.`), as the `MODEL` line is the potential's. Once the training data are read, by `tadah hpo --check` too: a priority below 0 (a `WDBFILE` value: `WDBFILE -1: the priority of dataset 1 of 1 ...`) and what implementation 2 ignores (`FORCE true`, `STRESS true`, `ENERGY false`, `ENORM SQRT`, an `FSIGMA` or `SSIGMA` other than its default, an `OALGO` other than the default, a `WDBFILE` value other than 1, a training structure whose `eweight` is not 1). (The error scales a fit refuses, and the keys Tadah! 1.6.0 retired, are refused before; see the entries of the error scales.) A run whose evaluations fit nothing reads no `LAMBDA` and is not refused for it. In 1.5.0 `LAMBDA -1` under `OALGO 1`, 2 or 4, a negative `LAMBDA` under implementation 2, an implementation other than 1 and 2, `OALGO 3` with `BIAS true` and a `LAMBDA` other than 0, a negative `EWEIGHT`, and a negative `FWEIGHT` or `SWEIGHT` with `FORCE true` or `STRESS true`, failed every evaluation, which scored `FAILSCORE`, and the run ended without a potential; with `FIXINDEX` pinning every weight, `best_pot.tadah` carried the implementation other than 1 and 2. Under `OPTIM LAMBDA` with `OALGO 3` and `BIAS true`, every candidate but 0 failed its evaluation. Each of the other cases ran to its end: a negative `LAMBDA` other than -1 was fitted as `LAMBDA 0` under `OALGO 1`, 2 and 4 and ran the evidence approximation under `OALGO 3`, a second value was ignored, so was a negative `FWEIGHT` or `SWEIGHT` of a quantity the fit leaves out, and implementation 2 fitted without the `OPTIM` candidates, settings and data it ignores. Test: `test_fit_settings_startup.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — an `OPTIM OALGO (2)` box that is not inside (0, 1) is refused at its line**, and the startup checks refuse an `OALGO` of the training configuration that every fit refuses (Tadah.MODELS: an `M` of 0 or of 1 or more under `OALGO 1`, one of 1 or more under `OALGO 3`, a second value under `OALGO 2` and 4, a first value other than 1 to 4). `OALGO`'s second value is the rank threshold `M` of `OALGO 1` and 3, which only 0 < `M` < 1 sets (`OPTIM OALGO (2): the box [0.001, 2] is not inside (0, 1), but the second value of OALGO is the rank threshold M of OALGO 1 and 3, which only a value 0 < M < 1 sets: ...`). 1.5.0 accepted such a box: every candidate below 0 under `OALGO 1` fitted with the machine precision, and every candidate of 1 or more fitted with the machine precision under `OALGO 1` and with every coefficient 0 under `OALGO 3`. Tests: the registry's invalid lines of `RULE.OPTIM_OALGO_threshold_box`, `RULE.fit_OALGO1_threshold`, `RULE.fit_OALGO3_threshold`, `RULE.fit_OALGO_one_value` and `RULE.fit_OALGO_option`.
- **BREAKING — an `OPTIM` box of `LAMBDA` that reaches below 0 is refused at its line**, explicit or made by `AUTO` (`OPTIM LAMBDA (1): the low bound -2 is negative, but LAMBDA is the ridge penalty, a number >= 0: ...`; -1, which requests the evidence approximation, is no value of a box; an `AUTO` box around a negative `LAMBDA` of the training configuration names the seed, `OPTIM LAMBDA (1): the seed -2 is negative, but LAMBDA is the ridge penalty, ...`), and so is `OPTIM LAMBDA` when the training configuration has `LAMBDA -1` (`OPTIM LAMBDA: the training configuration has LAMBDA -1, which requests the evidence approximation: ...`). A box from 0 is accepted. 1.5.0 accepted them: a candidate `LAMBDA` below 0 was fitted as `LAMBDA 0` under `OALGO 1`, 2 and 4 and ran the evidence approximation under `OALGO 3`, and with `LAMBDA -1` the run stopped when it started, at its starting point outside the box. (The boxes of the error scales and of `WDBFILE`: the entry of `OPTIM ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA` and `WDBFILE`.) Test: `test_fit_settings_startup.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — `OPTIM` of `LSCALE`, `ESHIFT`, `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `EFILTER`, `FFILTER` and `ZERO_COM_FORCE` is refused at its line**, under every model: these keys prepare the data, once, when the run starts and reads them, so no evaluation takes a candidate value of them (`OPTIM LSCALE is not a valid search target: LSCALE prepares the data and is applied once, when the run starts and reads them, so no evaluation would take a candidate value of it: its value is the training configuration's.`). 1.5.0 accepted `OPTIM LSCALE`, `ESHIFT`, `EFILTER` and `FFILTER`, and every evaluation trained on the data as the run had prepared them, whatever the candidate value; it refused the others as `Out of bound index: 1`. (1.5.0 accepted `OPTIM WDBFILE`, `WDBFILE_AUTO` and `EWEIGHT_TEMP` the same way, with the same loss for every candidate: `WDBFILE` now applies in every evaluation, and the other two are retired; see the entries of the error scales.) Test: `test_fit_settings_startup.cpp`.
- **The evidence approximation (`LAMBDA -1`, `OALGO 3`) is reported once per run.** A fit that stops at its iteration limit gives its WARNING once, in the journal (`hpo_run.log`), naming the first evaluation (`eval 1: LAMBDA -1 (evidence approximation): stopped at its iteration limit, 41 iterations, ... This WARNING is said once per run: a later evaluation whose evidence approximation stops at its limit has none.`), and an evaluation writes nothing of its evidence approximation on standard output: its iterations are one INFO message of the log, printed at `VERBOSE 2` (Tadah.MODELS). 1.5.0 gave no WARNING, and at a `VERBOSE` other than 0 (the default is 1) every evaluation printed the table of its iterations and `Number of steps for convergence` or `Max number of steps reached` on standard output. A candidate whose evidence approximation is refused (alpha or beta not finite, or no more observations than effective weights) fails: its evaluation scores `FAILSCORE` (the top of band D under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`) and counts as a failed evaluation, and the journal gives the refusal once. In 1.5.0 targets that made alpha and beta infinite (all zero) gave weights that were all nan, and each such evaluation scored `FAILSCORE` as a non-finite loss (`evaluation produced NaN/Inf -- loss clamped to FAIL_SCORE`). Test: `test_fit_settings_startup.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — a count of starting points is at most 1000000, and `NOISE CALIBRATION` at most 2147483647 evaluations**, refused at the line: the `K` of a `METHOD RANDOM`, `LHS` or `SOBOL` line, and `STRATEGY TOPK`, the candidates a `PRESTAGE` refinement starts from (`Error on line 4: METHOD RANDOM: K must be an integer from 1 to 1000000, got '1000001'`; `TOPK must be an integer from 1 to 1000000, got '0'`). The points of a block are drawn when the target file is read and kept for the run, and each is a start of a search; the evaluations of the calibration have the limit of `MAXEVAL`. `K_TOTAL`, which caps the points of the `METHOD` lines (below), is bounded by them. 1.5.0 read each count up to 18446744073709551615: a large `K`, `K_TOTAL` or `TOPK` stopped `tadah hpo` with `HPOEngine error: vector::reserve` (or `cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()`), and a large `NOISE CALIBRATION` stopped the run that way when it started, as the calibration reserved room for every loss before its first evaluation (16 GiB for 2147483647); it keeps the losses of the evaluations made. Every block of `K` points a `SOBOL` seed selects lies within the Sobol sequence, so its refusal of a block past the end is no message of a target file any more. Tests: `test_init_strategies.cpp`, `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`hpo_shutdown_noise_max`).
- **BREAKING — every key of the target file that takes one value is set once for what it configures.** A key given twice for one optimiser (in one block, in a repeated block, or in `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE`, which configure the same optimiser), for its starting points (also across the `INIT` blocks inside `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE`), or in the `NOISE`, `RESTART` or `STRATEGY` settings, a second `LOG_HP`, a `STEP <KEY>` override given twice for the same indices, and a top-level key given twice (`QFLUSH`, `OUTPUT`, `BOUTPUT`, `DIGITS`, `DUMP`, `BDUMP`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, `BUMPGATE`, `PC_ISOLATION`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`) are refused at the second line, naming the first: `ALGO is set on line 7 in the STRATEGY block and again here in the REFINE block; both blocks configure the same optimiser, so each key is given once`; `SEED is set on line 5 in the INIT block inside OPTIMIZER and again here in the INIT block inside STRATEGY; both blocks configure the same starting points, so each key is given once`; `QFLUSH is set twice (first on line 3); each key is given once`. Lines that add are read as before: `METHOD` lines add to their list, `OPTIM`, `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` lines add, `PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE` lines of other datasets add, and `STEP <KEY>` overrides of other indices refine the block-wide `STEP`. `FAILSCORE`, `LOSS` and `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` (one key with its alias `MAXTIME`) are set again only after a line has read the first value, and refused otherwise (`LOSS is set twice (first on line 3) and no line between reads the first (a --varloss or PC_OBSERVABLE term that names no loss function); each key is given once`): a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line without `--failscore` (`--maxtime`) reads the `FAILSCORE` (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`) given above it, and a `--varloss` or `PC_OBSERVABLE` term that names no loss function the `LOSS`; the run-wide uses take the last line (the validation metrics' loss, the ceiling of every loss), and each line's `PROCESS` worker deadline derives from its own limit. A `tadah refit` stage takes one `OPTIMIZER` block with at most one `INNER` block and sets each of their keys once too, naming the key as written, `MAXEVAL` and its alias `MAXITER` being one key: `REFINE: MAXEVAL is set twice in the OPTIMIZER block (as MAXEVAL and as MAXITER, its alias); each key is given once`; a second `OPTIMIZER` or `INNER` block and a repeated `ENABLED` or `MAX_ADD` are refused. 1.5.0 used the later value without a message (for `LOG_HP`, with a warning; a second `OPTIMIZER` block of a `tadah refit` stage set again the keys it gave, and a second `INNER` block replaced the first), so keeping the later line of each key gives a 1.5.0 file the settings it ran with.
- **BREAKING — a repeated top-level `PC_EMAE`, `PC_ErRMSE` or `PC_ERSQ` line is refused** at its line, naming the line of the first (`Error on line 7: PC_EMAE: repeated declaration (first declared on line 6)`), as a repeated global `PC_ERMSE`, `PC_FRMSE` or `PC_SRMSE_GPA` line is; the message of a repeated global line of those names the first line too (it said `repeated global declaration or mixed global/indexed forms`). 1.5.0 used the later line, so keeping it alone gives a 1.5.0 file the loss it ran with.
- **BREAKING — `OPTIM ... AUTO` refuses `FLOOR` and `GROUP_FLOOR` on a geometric box**, given as `RATIO <f>` or the default `RATIO 2` of a positive scale key such as `SGRID2B` (`OPTIM WATOM: FLOOR and GROUP_FLOOR set the smallest half-width of an AUTO FRAC box; AUTO RATIO 2 makes the geometric box [seed/2, seed*2], which they do not change, so they are given with FRAC <p> only`). 1.5.0 accepted them and ignored them (`AUTO RATIO 2 FLOOR 5` gave the box of `RATIO 2` alone), so a line without them keeps a 1.5.0 search space.
- **BREAKING — `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` refuses `PARAM CHAIN_LENGTH AUTO` and `PARAM NT AUTO`** at startup (`TADAH:ANNEAL_PA: PARAM CHAIN_LENGTH must be a number (got 'AUTO'; AUTO is accepted by TADAH:ANNEAL only)`). `AUTO` derives these values from `MAXEVAL` in the serial `TADAH:ANNEAL` only; without the line `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` takes its defaults, 50 and 5. In 1.5.0 `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` accepted `AUTO` and gave every temperature step 0 evaluations, so the run stopped after its initialisation. The help text and the key tables offer `AUTO` for `TADAH:ANNEAL` only.
- **The help of `tadah hpo` in full words.** `tadah explain` describes how an optimiser uses `STEP` without shorthand (`the first simplex has an edge of STEP in each dimension, and the coordinates are not rescaled; ...` for `LN_SBPLX` and `LN_NELDERMEAD`; `STEP rescales the coordinates, so that the first trust-region radius is STEP in each dimension; ...` for `LN_BOBYQA` and `LN_COBYLA`; `passed to the local optimiser of the INNER block; a STEP of the enclosing block replaces the STEP of the INNER block` for the MLSL and AUGLAG forms), the options of `ASA:ASA` and the `PARAM` keys of the annealers (`anneal_keys.toml`: each key in one or two sentences, with its detail after them, its range in words where the code checks it by hand, and no shorthand or literature note); `LADDER_ADAPT`'s help says that it needs 3 replicas or more. Tests: `test_optimizer_help.cpp` (the help holds no developer shorthand and names no class of a library).
- **BREAKING — the blocks of the target file must nest, and a line that opens or closes a block holds its key alone.** `tadah hpo` refuses every other form at the line concerned:
  - a second `OPTIMIZER` block: `Error on line 7: OPTIMIZER block given more than once (the first opens on line 3); one OPTIMIZER block holds every optimiser setting`;
  - an `OPTIMIZER` line inside a block: `Error on line 5: OPTIMIZER block must appear at the top level of the file (it is inside the STRATEGY block opened on line 4)`;
  - an `END` line that does not close the innermost open block: `Error on line 7: ENDOPTIMIZER does not close the innermost open block, the STRATEGY block opened on line 4 (ENDSTRATEGY closes it)`. With no block open, `ENDOPTIMIZER` is refused as `ENDOPTIMIZER without matching OPTIMIZER`, like the other `END` lines;
  - a block the file leaves open: `Error on line 3: OPTIMIZER block not closed with ENDOPTIMIZER`, at the line that opens the block. When several blocks are open, the innermost is named. This refusal comes before the checks of the block's settings;
  - a word after the key of a line that opens or closes a block: `Error on line 3: OPTIMIZER opens a block and takes no value (unexpected 'LIB'); each key stands on its own line inside the block`, or `ENDOPTIMIZER closes the OPTIMIZER block and takes no value (unexpected 'junk')`.

  In 1.5.0 a second `OPTIMIZER` block reopened the first and replaced the settings both gave. An `OPTIMIZER` line inside a block went back to the `OPTIMIZER` level. `ENDOPTIMIZER` closed the `OPTIMIZER` block from inside any block in it, and was ignored with no block open. A file that ended inside a block was read as if the block were closed. A line that opens or closes a block dropped the words after its key: `OPTIMIZER LIB NLOPT` set no `LIB`. The other `END` lines were refused inside another block, as `ENDSTRATEGY without matching STRATEGY`, which named no open block.

  A file converts with every optimiser setting in one `OPTIMIZER` block at the top level, each key on a line of its own, and each block closed with its `END` line before the block around it. A repeated `INIT`, `NOISE`, `EXPLORE`, `REFINE` or `RESTART` block continues the first, as in 1.5.0. A key set in both is refused (*Changed*: a key of the target file that takes one value is set once). The `METHOD` lines of a repeated `INIT` or `RESTART` block add to the same list of starting-point methods.

ENDINNER: the local search it runs, from the points it samples in the OPTIM box: LIB NLOPT and a local ALGO; a global one (`tadah explain ALGO` marks each) is refused, and so is one that needs an INNER block of its own. - **BREAKING — no point outside the OPTIM box is evaluated, and none can be the best** (`box_penalty.h`, `hpo_host_impl.hpp`, `gradient_provider.h`). Of the registered optimisers only `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes such points (Ceres' line-search solver has no bounds); every other one keeps to the box, finite differences keep to it, and a coordinate proposed outside its box by no more than rounding is evaluated on the bound (8 units in the last place; the bounded-stencil entry below). `HPO_Host` evaluates no point further out -- no context, gate, training, LAMMPS or `DUMP` archive -- and scores it as a failure graded by its distance to the box, `out_of_box_score(FS, d) = 0.999 FS + 0.001 FS (0.75 + 0.25 d / (1 + d))`, the range of band D whatever `FAILSCORE_GRADING` says, d the Euclidean distance in the search's coordinates (under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` a fraction of each box's width, under `NONE` the raw value, ln x on a `LOG` dimension; infinite for x <= 0 there). The score is at least 0.99975 `FAILSCORE`, a failure for every `FAILSCORE` (the default, the largest double, included), so such a point is never the best, a `WARM` seed, a `PRESTAGE` candidate or an entry of the run's record (`HPO_Host::recorded`); it grows with the distance, below `FAILSCORE` itself, and the gradient there, whatever `GRAD_STRATEGY`, is its slope, 0.25e-3 FS e_k / ((1 + d)^2 d) for the signed excess e (positive above the box), which points away from the box, so that a descent step leads back into it, with no evaluation. The point gets its `loss.tadah` and `params.tadah` rows (the point proposed) and counts against `MAXEVAL` and in the census as a failed evaluation; the first is a WARNING, `eval 4: CGRID2B[2] = 0.9 lies outside its OPTIM box [1, 2]: it is not evaluated, and is scored 1.797284568240756e+308, a failure graded by its distance to the box, 0.09999999999999998 in the search's coordinates (the box is the configuration's contract: no point outside it is trained or can be the best) ...`, the next INFO lines (the first 5, then powers of two), and DIAGNOSTICS count them (`Out-of-box points : N`); the startup WARNING of `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` and its `ALGO` help say so. 1.5.0 evaluated such a point where it was proposed, with the one-time neighbour lists, short of a cutoff beyond the box, and it could become the best. A CERES run whose line searches leave the box takes another path: on the 1-D straddle problem of the tests (`CGRID2B[2]` in [1, 2], `FAILSCORE 1e9`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`, from 1.4) `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes 3 points outside the box, evaluates none of them, and ends `FTOL_REL reached` after 30 evaluations with its best at 1.9854. Tests: `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (`box penalty: ...`; the slope under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`), `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (the upper bound; a 2-D run, in a build with Ceres; the straddle problem from 1.4), `test_nn_exact_e2e.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_repeat_record.cpp`; Tadah.MLIP's `tadah_hpo_failed_evaluations_ceres_box`.
- **BREAKING — `CERES:LBFGS`, `CERES:WOLFE` and `CERES:NLCG` are refused**, in every phase, at the line that closes their block: `the STRATEGY-level optimiser: CERES:LBFGS is refused: Ceres' line-search solver has no bounds, and a point it proposes outside the OPTIM box is not evaluated: it is scored as a failure graded by its distance to the box, in [0.99975, 1) x FAILSCORE, far above every usable loss. Ceres' Wolfe line search interpolates that value and shrinks its step to almost nothing (a 2-D run stopped at its start after 16 evaluations, 'Parameter tolerance reached' with a step of 0; a 1-D run moved by 2.5e-7 per iteration until MAXEVAL). The recommended replacements are NLOPT:LD_LBFGS (L-BFGS within the box), DLIB:BFGS and CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, whose Armijo line search steps back from such a point` (measured with the three under that score: in 2-D on the Ta test model, `OPTIM RCUT2B` [4, 6] and `CGRID2B (2)` [0.1, 2], `FAILSCORE 1e16`, `MAXEVAL 25`, they ended at their start, loss 13.84; in 1-D on the straddle problem of the tests from 1.4, `FAILSCORE 1e9`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`, they ran to `MAXEVAL 60` with their best 95.39 at 1.968). `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`, whose Armijo line search contracts a step by at most 1e-3, is the Ceres solver HPO runs; `PARAM max_lbfgs_rank` and `nlcg_type`, which only the refused three read, are refused on it (`CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT does not support PARAM max_lbfgs_rank ...`). The refusals of Ceres' least-squares solvers name the same alternatives. Tests: `test_hpo.cpp`, `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_optimizer_help.cpp`; every test that ran a refused driver runs `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`. In an `INNER` block, whose search is a local NLopt algorithm, one of them (any optimiser of another `LIB`) is refused by that rule instead, at startup: `... names CERES:LBFGS, but NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS runs its INNER search with NLopt: the INNER block takes LIB NLOPT and an NLopt ALGO, such as LN_BOBYQA` (tests: `startup validation: an INNER block needs an optimiser that runs one and an NLopt algorithm`, and with `PC_LAMMPS` in `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp`).
- **BREAKING for gradient searches next to failures — a finite difference never mixes a usable loss with a failure** (`gradient_provider.h`, `fd_component`; `NUMERICAL`, and the parameters `HYBRID` differences). A stencil's losses are of three kinds: usable, a graded failure (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`), a failure (`FAILSCORE`, or not finite); each component is differenced with points of the kind of the loss at the point only. A central stencil point of another kind gives way to the one-sided 3-point difference on the other side, (-3 f(u) + 4 f(u+s) - f(u+2s))/2s with s pointing away from it (one evaluation more, its far point), or the 2-point one, (f(u+s) - f(u))/s, when that far point lies outside the box or is of another kind too; a forward step of another kind steps backward; a one-sided stencil next to a bound whose far point is of another kind takes its near point alone. With no such side the point has no usable gradient, as before: `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil` (`at X[1] = 0, the stencil points X[1] = 0.2 and X[1] = -0.2 failed: losses 1.797693135e+308 and 1.797693135e+308`), at a graded failure `a graded failure without a side of graded failures in its finite-difference stencil`, and a flat graded region still fails (`... and every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`). The first such difference of each dimension is an INFO line (`finite differences: at CGRID2B[2] = 1.6 (a usable loss), the stencil point CGRID2B[2] = 1.4 (loss 1.797693135e+308) is of another kind, so the difference is taken without it: one-sided 3-point difference, at CGRID2B[2] = 1.8 and CGRID2B[2] = 2 with the loss at the point itself; a difference never mixes a usable loss with a failure; ...`), and DIAGNOSTICS count them per dimension (`Mixed-kind stencils`). Every backend passes the loss it has just evaluated with each gradient it asks for (`gradient_at`), so the kind of the point is always known; `HYBRID`'s `gradient()` without it, which no backend calls, evaluates the loss at the point first. 1.5.0 took the difference of a usable loss and a failure: with the default `FAILSCORE` it overflowed, with a finite one it gave a slope as steep as the jump to `FAILSCORE`, which the searches followed (the *Fixed* entry on failed evaluations in a gradient). A search next to the failure region now goes on from a point whose stencil reaches into it: on the straddle problem from 1.6 (its stencil point 1.4 fails) every finite-difference backend differences 1.8 and 2.0 and reaches the bound 2, the best (`DLIB:BFGS` `FTOL_ABS reached` after 6 evaluations; NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND_RESTART`, `LD_VAR2` `SUCCESS` after 6, `LD_TNEWTON` after 9, `LD_SLSQP` roundoff-limited after 9, `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` `MAXEVAL_REACHED` after 9 and 6, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` `FTOL_REL reached` after 35), under `FAILSCORE 1e9` too; with the ERMSE target 10.5 `DLIB:BFGS` from 1.8 goes on to the edge of the failure region, 1.4813, `FTOL_ABS reached` after 54 evaluations. Tests: `test_gradient_failure.cpp` (a forward point of another kind, a graded failure without a graded side), `test_fd_bounds.cpp`, `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (the straddle problem from 1.4 under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`), `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`, `test_hpo.cpp`; Tadah.MLIP's `tadah_hpo_failed_evaluations_fd_straddle`.
- **BREAKING — a PRESTAGE refinement on a search that does not start at the point it is given is refused at startup** (`require_prestage_refinement`, `strategy_rules.h`; `OptimizerCaps::start_note`). NLopt's StoGO (`GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`), DIRECT (`GN_DIRECT`, `GN_DIRECT_L`, `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND`, `GN_DIRECT_L_NOSCAL`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L`) and AGS (`GN_AGS`) never read the point they are given (`OptimizerCaps::starts_at_x0` false), so as the refinement they searched the whole box from its centre and never used the exploration's candidates: in a 2-D run the six refinement evaluations of `GN_DIRECT_L`, `GD_STOGO` and `GN_AGS` were the centre of the box and their own points after it, none at the candidate. Such a refinement, at the `STRATEGY` level or in a `REFINE` block, with any `TOPK`, is refused at the `ENDOPTIMIZER` line: `Error on line 14: STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE cannot refine with NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L: it does not start at the point it is given (DIRECT evaluates the centre of the OPTIM box first and then divides the whole box; NLopt never reads that point), so the refinement would never use the exploration's candidates, for any TOPK. A local optimiser starts at each candidate (CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, DLIB:BFGS, ..., NLOPT:LN_SBPLX); NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L runs as the exploration (EXPLORE block) or once, as STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN.` (every local optimiser of the caps registry whose first call is at its point; an `AUGLAG` without NLopt's default local optimiser, which starts where its `INNER` block does, is not listed). With a `TOPK` above 1 it comes before the `TOPK` rule, whose `TOPK 1` would not help. 1.5.0 accepted such a refinement with `TOPK 1`. An `INNER` search on one of them, a global search, is refused for that (the entry above), in every phase. A `PLAIN` run and an exploration on these optimisers are accepted as before: their start is not what they are for. `StagedPolicy::validate()` applies the same rule. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_gradient_budget.cpp` and `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`hpo_run` refuses a StoGO refinement and evaluates nothing; the StoGO gradient-budget cases run a `PLAIN` search under `NOISE CALIBRATION`; the end-status sweep leaves the refused refinements out), Tadah.MLIP's `test_tadah_cli_exit_status.cpp`.
- **`tadah refit` compact mode: a reachability analysis whose `r_reach` is below the table's `dr` is an internal error** (`HPORefitEngine::compact_fit`): it throws `std::logic_error` (`tadah refit[compact]: internal error: the reachability analysis gave r_reach = 0 A (REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0 x the nearest-neighbour distance of the fcc lattice of a0 = 3.6 A), less than the step of the table's radial grid, dr = 0.0125313 A, although the crystal gate of tadah refit (eam_lattice::require_crystal_on_grid) refuses every such table; this is a bug, please report it`), where an `r_reach` <= 0 logged a WARNING and `RefitEngine::run()` fitted the table in dense mode. The hook returns nothing: it fits the table or throws, and `run()` has no dense fit to fall back on (MLIP's `RefitEngine::compact_fit`). No configuration reaches it: `run()`'s crystal gate (MLIP) refuses an unknown lattice, a0 <= 0, a crystal whose nearest neighbours at a0 are at or beyond the table's cutoff, and one whose nearest neighbours at a/a0 = `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` are less than `dr` apart, computed by the function that gives `r_reach` (MODELS' `eam_lattice::reach_distance`). In 1.5.0, whose gate asked for a positive a0 only and which read `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` without a range, `REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0`, or a subnormal a0 (`1e-323`) with `REFIT_REACH_SMIN 0.1`, fell back to the dense fit there with a WARNING. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp` (the hook with `REFIT_REACH_SMIN` 0; compact refits of a0 = `1e-323` and of a0 = `361`, a lattice constant in picometres whose fit windows began above the cutoff, refused by the gate).
- **The refusal of `GRAD_STRATEGY` beside `PC_LAMMPS` says that `PC_LAMMPS` does not support those gradients** (`HPOTargets`), at the `GRAD_STRATEGY` line: `--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS on line 11 but <block>'s GRAD_STRATEGY is set. A PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE line does not support analytical/numerical/hybrid gradients. Drop the GRAD_STRATEGY line.` 1.5.0's said that `PC_LAMMPS` "no longer" supported them, with a date, though no release had supported them (v1.3.0-beta.1 refused them as 1.5.0 did). Test: `HPOTargets rejects PC_LAMMPS with gradient-based optimisers` (`test_pc_observable_parser.cpp`).
- **BREAKING — `best_pot.tadah`, its rank-local copies and the `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives have no key line of the keys that prepared the training data; their training record lists them, and a potential's own are not applied to the run** (Tadah.MLIP's dataset-keys entry). `EFILTER`, `FFILTER`, `ESHIFT_ATOM`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM`, `ZERO_COM_FORCE` and `WDBFILE` are training record keys (`mlip::training_record_keys`): the record of every potential an evaluation writes lists those the run was given after its `DBFILE` (the blueprint defaults of `tadah hpo`, `ESHIFT_ATOM false`, `ESHIFT_DBATOM false`, `ZERO_COM_FORCE false`, which were key lines of every `best_pot.tadah`, are left out), with the `WDBFILE` the evaluation was fitted with under `OPTIM WDBFILE` (`HPOTargets::record_of`), and the resolved `ESHIFT` and `LSCALE` stay key lines. A potential's lines of them (a file with `WEIGHTS`: a `-c` seed such as a `best_pot.tadah`, or a potential that the configuration file includes with `CONFIG`) are not read (`TaskFactory`'s parse skips them, the `ESHIFT` toggles with or without the potential's `ESHIFT` line, so a seed's toggle derives no shift from the run's data), and `tadah hpo` names those the run does not give itself (a toggle neither when the run sets the other toggle true) in a `WARNING` on the console and in the journal before `HPOTargets` takes the record (`mlip::ignore_configuration_potential_lines`, for `tadah hpo`); the run's own (the command line, or a line of the configuration file that includes the potential) apply and are recorded. In the released code a seed's applied to the run's training data, so a seed trained with `WDBFILE` of two values was refused with one training dataset (`HPOEngine error: WDBFILE size (2) does not match number of datasets (1).`). The validation set and the startup checks are unchanged. Test: `The potentials of an evaluation keep the keys that prepared the training data in their training record` (`test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`); the record's opening line is `# --- training record (no key is read from it) ---` (Tadah.MLIP).
- **`best_pot.tadah`, its rank-local copies and the `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives keep the keys a potential does not use in their training record**, the comment block right above their key lines that Tadah.MLIP's `write_potential_with_record` writes for every potential: the run's training `DBFILE`, its `--validation` and `--hpotarget` files, and its `-v`, `NUMERIC`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` and `HEALTH_LOG` when it set them, as written (`HPOTargets::training_record`, taken first thing in the constructor, before anything inserts a default; `hpo_run` passes the task's `KeyProvenance`, so the blueprint defaults no one wrote, `VERBOSE 1` among them, are left out). `PotHeaderWriter::write_potential_body(os, pot, record, digits)` takes the record. The DATA section of `best_pot.tadah`'s header names the training datasets no more: each dataset's line gives its statistics and `(DBFILE i of the training record)`, so one block names them; the validation datasets keep their paths. No loader of a potential reads the block: `tadah hpo -c best_pot.tadah`, `tadah predict` and `pair_style tadah` behave as without it, and the durable-best key (`# HPO_BEST_*`) and the `WARM` hot values (`# HOT`) are read as before. In 1.5.0 `best_pot.tadah` had the run's `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` and `VERBOSE` (and an `M_BLR` run's `DBFILE`) as key lines, and its DATA section named every training dataset's path. Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`The potentials of an evaluation keep the inputs of the run in their training record, not as key lines`), and Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo leaves the VERBOSE nobody wrote out of best_pot.tadah's training record` (the `KeyProvenance` rule: a run without `VERBOSE` and `-v` records no `VERBOSE 1`).
- **`LammpsRunner::create_instance` takes no communicator** (`runners.h`, an installed header): `create_instance(lmp_tokens, error = nullptr)` creates the instance on `tadah::hpo::hpo_lammps_comm()`, the communicator every LAMMPS instance of the build runs on -- the LAMMPS STUBS' 0 in the serial build, `MPI_COMM_SELF` in a `TADAH_HPO_MPI` build. It took the communicator from the caller (`create_instance(MPI_Comm sub_comm, lmp_tokens)`), whose one right value differs between the builds: a literal 0, the serial build's, compiles in both and is a null communicator under Open MPI, on which LAMMPS aborts the process (`MPI_ERR_COMM` in `MPI_Comm_rank`); nine test cases did so in the MPI build's suite while the serial suite passed them. Every call `tadah hpo` makes passed `hpo_lammps_comm()`, so runs were not affected. A call that passes a communicator no longer compiles, in either build: drop the argument. Tests: `test_lammps_runner.cpp`.
- **A point the run has evaluated is answered from its evaluation when a search asks for it again, if every evaluation of the run is a function of its point** (`nlopt_adapter.h`, `dlib_adapter.h`, `repeat_points.h`, `hpo_host_impl.hpp`). `HPO_Host` records each evaluation of the run that gave a usable loss, whichever backend asked for it, by the point it evaluated (after the snap onto the box, bit for bit; `HPO_Host::recorded`; one entry per usable evaluation, the point twice and the loss, a few hundred bytes for a few dimensions), and NLopt's objective and DLIB:BFGS answer a call at such a point that asks for nothing the record lacks with the recorded loss (and the gradient, when the search formed one there): no host call, no row, not counted in HPO's count of evaluations, which counts each search's own evaluations and is what DLIB:BFGS's `MAXEVAL` (a MULTISTART start's `BUDGET_PER_START`, a PRESTAGE phase's `MAXEVAL`) bounds. NLopt counts every call against its own `MAXEVAL`, a call answered from an earlier search's evaluation too, so an NLopt phase's `MAXEVAL` or start's `BUDGET_PER_START` is charged for it (an `LN_SBPLX` refinement of `MAXEVAL 4` whose first call is the exploration's best: 3 evaluations, `NLopt counts its calls, and 1 of them asked for points the run had evaluated ...`). That holds for a point an earlier search of the run evaluated too: a PRESTAGE refinement starts from the exploration's best point (an LN_SBPLX exploration and a DLIB:BFGS refinement on the termination tests: 10 evaluations; 11 while the refinement evaluated its start again), a MULTISTART start reaches a point an earlier start evaluated (two DLIB:BFGS starts that end at a bound: the bound evaluated once), and a RESTART round from `METHOD CONFIG` takes round 0's path (LN_SBPLX: 9 evaluations, 14 before, 5 of them repeats); the search's first such reuse names it (`DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 4 (SGRID2B[2] = 4), which an earlier search of the run evaluated: the loss of eval 4 is reused, without an evaluation ...`). The gradients stay each search's own (they depend on its coordinates and finite-difference settings): a search that asks for a gradient at a point another search evaluated evaluates the stencil (NUMERICAL, below) or the point (ANALYTICAL, HYBRID), so MULTISTART starts that end at one bound each evaluate its stencil. TADAH:RANDOM, TADAH:GRID, the TADAH annealers, ASA, CERES and DLIB:GFS evaluate every point they ask for; their evaluations are recorded for the searches that answer from the record. A PRESTAGE exploration's call answered from the record offers its point to the refinement's candidates, as the evaluation would have (`count_reused_repeat`): a RESTART round's exploration that takes round 0's path makes no evaluation and still hands on its candidates (`PRESTAGE: 3 usable candidate(s) for TOPK 5 (the exploration made 0 evaluation(s), 0 without a usable loss, and answered 3 call(s) from the run's record of earlier evaluations); refining 3`); an exploration that asked for no point at all is still an error.
