Under `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` a call that asks for a gradient the record lacks takes the recorded loss as the loss at the point of its finite differences and evaluates only their stencil (`RepeatUse::Centre`): MLSL's local search asks for the gradient at a sample it evaluated without one, and each local start was evaluated again (3 of 60 evaluations of `G_MLSL_LDS` with `INNER LD_MMA` on the tests' bowl). The RESTART stagnation window counts each reused call as one evaluation without a new best (`HPO_Host::count_reused_repeat`): a search that only asks for points the run has makes no evaluation, and without it `LD_MMA` held at a bound (u = 0) with RESTART as its only stop would spin without end, and with MAXEVAL would never restart, NLopt's count of calls ending it (`StagnationStop fired at a repeat of eval 16 answered from the run's record, after eval 18; short-circuiting optimiser (threshold=5 evals without improvement, 3 of them call(s) answered from the run's record)`, and a window that ends at an evaluation counts its reused calls the same way; the termination tests: 23 evaluations and one restart either way, 26 and one restart when every repeat was evaluated). DLIB:BFGS asks for the gradient of the point its line search ended on right after its loss; when that loss was answered from the record the gradient takes it as an evaluation's and is no second reuse (in DIAGNOSTICS or the window), so a reused call stands for exactly the evaluation it replaces. Under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` that gradient evaluates the point (below), and the evaluation answers the loss call too: the two calls are one evaluation, counted once in the window, a repeat evaluated again and no reuse (a PRESTAGE refinement's start: `DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 4 (SGRID2B[2] = 4) and its gradient, which an earlier search of the run evaluated; it is evaluated again: ... GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL ...`); a reused loss that no such gradient follows is a reuse, counted at the next call or when the search ends (`BfgsObjective::settle`). `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` re-solve their subproblem with a larger rho and land on the same clamped corner (mma.c), then ask for it at their next iteration; `LN_SBPLX`, `LN_NELDERMEAD`, `GN_ESCH` and `GN_CRS2_LM` reflect or mutate onto a point they had; `LD_SLSQP` and `LN_COBYLA` step onto a bound point they had (the termination tests' 1-D straddle: 5 and 1 such calls); `AUGLAG` evaluates the point its local search returned once more; DLIB:BFGS's backtracking trials are clamped onto one point. Each repeat was a fit (and every `PC_LAMMPS` script). The host says when a reuse is exact (`repeat_reuse_blocker()`): not with a `PC_LAMMPS` line (its scripts run on LAMMPS instances kept from one evaluation to the next, and a `--hotvar` starts from the best evaluation's value), a `PC_OBSERVABLE` that carries state (`cohesive_energy`), or a `--skip-rel` filter (it compares with the best value so far); such a run evaluates every repeat again and journals the reason once (`... asked again for the point of eval 34 (RCUT2B = 4, SGRIDMB = 0.4); it is evaluated again: PC_LAMMPS: ...`). The run's record holds its usable evaluations under a blocker too, so a point an earlier search of the run evaluated -- a PRESTAGE refinement's start, a point an earlier MULTISTART start or RESTART round evaluated -- is a repeat evaluated again as well: counted in DIAGNOSTICS `Repeats evaluated again` and named so when it is the run's first (a PRESTAGE refinement's start on the Ta test data with a `PC_LAMMPS` line: `DLIB:BFGS asked for the point of eval 5 (RCUT2B = 5.376917644, SGRIDMB = 0.1563088886), which an earlier search of the run evaluated; it is evaluated again: PC_LAMMPS: ...`). A failed evaluation is evaluated again, and so is a point whose gradient is asked for and was not formed there under `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID`: `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` differentiates the thread's last evaluation without checking its point, so DLIB:BFGS evaluates a point first before such a gradient there that does not follow its evaluation (the provider's `gradient(x)` used to form such a gradient without one; dlib never asked for it). The journal names each search's first reuse and counts its reuses at 10, 100, 1000, ... (a search that only repeats makes no row: `LD_MMA` held at u = 0, where NLopt's XTOL_REL test is vacuous); DIAGNOSTICS has `Repeated points reused` and `Repeats evaluated again`. NLopt counts a reused call in its own MAXEVAL: a derivative-free search still ends after MAXEVAL calls with fewer evaluations, and its line says so (`NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 45 of MAXEVAL 60 evaluation(s); NLopt counts its calls, and 15 of them asked for points the run had evaluated and were answered without an evaluation`); a gradient search usually spends HPO's budget first (a loss and its stencil per call), so a reuse leaves the budget to new points and the search can get further (`LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 on the termination tests' straddle: 60 evaluations ending at 1.8659506446, 1.8678643276 before; `LD_MMA` at MAXEVAL 20 on a corner ends XTOL_REACHED, MAXEVAL_REACHED before), but one that mostly asks again ends on NLopt's count (`LD_MMA` held at a bound: 18 evaluations of MAXEVAL 60, 54 of its 60 calls answered). The first-reuse journal line says how the backend's MAXEVAL takes a reuse (NLopt: `a reuse is no evaluation: HPO's MAXEVAL does not count it, but NLopt counts it among the calls its own MAXEVAL bounds`; DLIB:BFGS: `a reuse is no evaluation, and MAXEVAL counts evaluations`), and so does DIAGNOSTICS, with the RESTART window. A repeat after the budget is spent is answered too. On a 2-D search on the Ta test data (`PC_ERMSE` and `PC_FRMSE`, `OPTIM RCUT2B (1) 4 6` and `CGRID2B (2) 0.1 2.0`, the optimum on a corner) `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` at MAXEVAL 40 make 10 evaluations (20 before), the same XTOL_REACHED; `LN_SBPLX` 45 in its 60 calls. `LD_SLSQP`'s steps onto that corner are distinct points (3.8e-13 apart) and are evaluated, as before. Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp`, `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`HPO_Host::recorded`), `test_policy_host_exceptions.cpp` (the window's count), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`[repeat_points]`, the `Run record:` cases, the RESTART cases, and nine pins whose counts or path change: LN_SBPLX 10 rows for its 12 calls, AUGLAG 8, COBYLA 59, the bump-gate refinement 18, LD_SLSQP from 1.8, G_MLSL_LDS's first local start not evaluated again).
- **A `RESTART` round's `RANDOM` and `LHS` methods draw a stream of their own, and a `RESTART` block without `SEED` journals the seed its restart points are drawn with** (`init_strategies.cpp`, `run_policy.h`, `seed_stream.h`). Round r draws `seed_stream(SEED + r, kRestartPoints)` (`InitStream::Restart`), not the generator an `INIT` block's `RANDOM` and `LHS` draw for that seed, so a round whose `RESTART SEED + r` is an `INIT` block's `SEED` does not start where round 0 did. `SOBOL` draws no random numbers: its seed still selects block `SEED + r`, which an `INIT` block's `SOBOL` with that seed draws too. Without a `SEED` each round drew `std::random_device() + r` and the journal named no seed; a run now draws one 64-bit seed S from `std::random_device` at its first restart, round r draws with S + r as a block with `SEED S` does, and the journal says so once (`RESTART: the RESTART block has no SEED, so its restart points are drawn from std::random_device: restart r draws with S + r (SEED S in the RESTART block draws the same points again)`) and names the round's seed in its line (`RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED <S + 1>, SEED 6`). A chain of `CONFIG` or `WARM` alone draws no seed (with a `SEED` its round lines still name `RESTART SEED <SEED + r>`, which draws nothing, as the startup warning about such a `SEED` says). Tests: `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`Run record: a RESTART round's RANDOM and LHS draw a stream of their own`), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Termination: a RESTART SEED draws restart round r's points with SEED + r`, `Termination: without a RESTART SEED the restart points come from std::random_device, and the journal names the seed`, and the round lines of four `RESTART` cases without a `SEED`).
- **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA` is refused** in every phase (the `OPTIM` box is the configuration's contract), an entry of the refusal table of the caps registry as `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is: the parser refuses it at the line that closes its block (`ENDOPTIMIZER`, `ENDEXPLORE`, `ENDINNER`), in 1-D and 2-D, naming the phase and `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA` (`Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA is refused: NLopt 2.10 runs LN_NEWUOA without the OPTIM box: it passes NEWUOA no bounds, so its evaluations leave the box and the best potential can lie outside the bounds the configuration sets (a 2-D run made 20 of its 25 evaluations outside the box, and its best was one of them). It also needs two OPTIM dimensions or more. The recommended replacement is NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, Powell's bound-constrained successor of NEWUOA (its STEP rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius)`); no list of alternatives offers it, and the ALGO help prints the refusal. It was registered with `ignores_bounds` and a startup WARNING: a 2-D run of `MAXEVAL 25` made 20 evaluations outside the box and its best, `RCUT2B` 8.11 for a [4, 6] box, became `best_pot.tadah`; a 1-D run ended `FAILED -- dimension 1 must be >= 2` after no evaluation. `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`'s refusal names `LN_BOBYQA` alone (it offered `LN_NEWUOA` where the search may leave the box). `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` is the only bounds-ignoring backend now, and the WARNING at the first point outside the box names `CERES:*` (it named `NLopt LN_NEWUOA` too). `docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_ignores_bounds.md`. Tests: `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_scale_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`.
- **A gradient search whose budget cannot pay for one step is warned about at startup** (`validate_hpo_setup`, `gradient_budget_warnings`): a gradient search evaluates the loss at its start and the gradient there (`gradient_cost`: `NUMERICAL` 2n central, n forward, in n dimensions; `ANALYTICAL` 1, the counted analytical gradient; `HYBRID` at least 1) before any point it steps to, so a `MAXEVAL` below that + 2 ends it at its start (`MAXEVAL_REACHED`; measured: LD_LBFGS and DLIB:BFGS in 2-D take no step with `MAXEVAL` 4 or 5, a first one with 6). An MLSL whose local search takes gradients first evaluates its starting point and its first N samples without a gradient (N its `POPULATION`, NLopt's default 4), and its first local search starts at one of these points, a point the run has evaluated, whose loss the warning counts as the run spends it (`repeat_use`, the rule by which NLopt's objective and DLIB:BFGS answer a call from the run's record of its usable evaluations): where every evaluation of the run is a function of its point and `GRAD_STRATEGY` is `NUMERICAL`, the recorded loss is the centre of the finite differences and only their stencil is evaluated, so it needs N more evaluations (measured on the Ta test data: `GD_MLSL` with `INNER LD_LBFGS` in 2-D takes no local step with `MAXEVAL` 9, a first one with 10; `POPULATION 3`: 8 and 9): `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_MLSL: MAXEVAL 9 takes no step of a local search: MLSL evaluates its starting point and its first 4 samples without a gradient (5 evaluations; POPULATION 4, NLopt's default), then its first local search, from one of them, takes the loss there from the run's record (no evaluation) and evaluates the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 10 or more`; with a `--skip-rel` filter, whose run evaluates every repeat again, the start's loss is evaluated again and it needs N + 1 more (`... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 11 or more`; measured: no local step with 10, one with 11) -- under `NUMERICAL` the one reuse blocker such an MLSL can meet, as `PC_LAMMPS` refuses a gradient MLSL and `PC_OBSERVABLE` refuses `NUMERICAL`. Under `ANALYTICAL` or `HYBRID`, whose gradient follows an evaluation of its point (a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run is one of them), the start's loss is evaluated again too, then the analytical gradient (1 evaluation; `HYBRID`: and the stencil of each parameter it differences), N + 1 more than a local search: `... evaluates the loss there again (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (1 evaluation: one analytical gradient, GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 8 or more` (measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture: `ANALYTICAL` takes no local step with `MAXEVAL` 7, one with 8, and so does `HYBRID`, which differences no parameter there). AUGLAG goes straight into its local search. A search on NLopt or DLIB:BFGS that starts at a point the run has evaluated, and asks for that point first, takes the loss there the same way (`start_is_recorded`): a PRESTAGE refinement starts at an exploration candidate, a usable evaluation (`the PRESTAGE refinement NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search takes the loss at its starting point from the run's record (no evaluation) and evaluates the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 5 or more`; measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture: `MAXEVAL 5` steps at its fifth evaluation, with `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` and `DLIB:BFGS`), and so does the run's first search under `NOISE_CALIBRATION` (`PLAIN`, the first `MULTISTART` start, a `PRESTAGE` exploration), whose seed point the calibration evaluated; evaluated again under `ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID` or a reuse blocker (`... evaluates the loss at its starting point again (1 evaluation) ...`). The later `MULTISTART` starts begin at `INIT` points the run has not evaluated, so where the first start's budget differs from theirs the two are warned apart (`the first MULTISTART start NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: BUDGET_PER_START 4 takes no step: its gradient search takes the loss at its starting point from the run's record ..., which needs BUDGET_PER_START 5 or more` and `each MULTISTART start after the first NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: BUDGET_PER_START 4 takes no step: its gradient search evaluates the loss at a starting point ..., which needs BUDGET_PER_START 6 or more`; measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture with `NOISE CALIBRATION 2`: at `BUDGET_PER_START 5` the first start steps and the second does not). NLopt's StoGO (`GD_STOGO`, `GD_STOGO_RAND`) does not start at the point it is given: its first trial is the centre of its box (stogo/global.cc; `OptimizerCaps::starts_at_x0`, false for StoGO, DIRECT and AGS, which take no starting point), so a StoGO search spends the loss there, the gradient and one step wherever its phase starts (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_STOGO: MAXEVAL 5 takes no step: its gradient search, which does not start at the point it is given, evaluates the loss at a point of its own (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (4 evaluations: ...) ..., which needs MAXEVAL 6 or more`; measured on the termination tests' Ta fixture under `NOISE CALIBRATION 2`: the search evaluates the centre of the box and its stencil, not the seed point the calibration evaluated, and steps at `MAXEVAL 6`; as the PRESTAGE refinement it is refused, and so is a StoGO `INNER` search, a global one: the entries at the top of *Changed*). Not covered, as the warning cannot know before any evaluation: a start whose evaluation was not usable, the refinement from INIT points that a PRESTAGE whose exploration has no usable candidate falls back on, and whether the point StoGO starts at is one the run has evaluated (the configuration's point at the centre of the box, evaluated by a calibration or an exploration, is), which the warning counts as an evaluation. One WARNING per phase with a budget of its own that obtains gradients -- the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, each `MULTISTART` start (its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL`), the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement (its `TOPK` share) -- and per `INNER` block whose local search takes gradients and whose own `MAXEVAL`, in NLopt calls, is 1 (under an MLSL whose run answers its local starts' loss from the record, a call costs the gradient alone: `(4 evaluations: ...; MLSL's local searches start at points it evaluated, whose loss is taken from the run's record)`; so under an AUGLAG refinement, whose local search starts at the candidate: `its local search starts at a point the run has evaluated, whose loss is taken from the run's record`), on stderr and in the journal: `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: MAXEVAL 4 takes no step: its gradient search evaluates the loss at a starting point (1 evaluation) and the gradient there (4 evaluations: central differences in 2 dimensions, GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL) before it evaluates a point it steps to, which needs MAXEVAL 6 or more`. A warning, not a refusal: such a probe of the start can be deliberate. Nothing said so. Tests: `test_gradient_budget.cpp`, `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`, which checks on a test function that the warning is given at exactly the budgets whose run takes no step (StoGO among them), on hosts that reuse a repeated point, evaluate it again, or say neither, and `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Gradient budget: ...` and `Run record: the startup budget warning ...`, the same on `HPO_Host` through `hpo_run`, under `NUMERICAL`, `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID`, for a PRESTAGE refinement, a calibrated PLAIN run, a calibrated PLAIN StoGO search and the MULTISTART starts under `NOISE CALIBRATION`).
- **At `VERBOSE` > 0 a run prints a kernel's settings line once** (`Kern_RBF | sigma: 1 | gamma: 1`; `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Sigmoid`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ`): the line of the first model it builds, the `-c` model's, at startup. `hpo_run()` holds a Tadah.MODELS `KernelSettingsLineScope` for the run, and a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker one in which no kernel prints. A second run in the process, such as a program's second `hpo_run()`, prints the line of its own `-c` model. Every model a run builds printed it, the startup builds, the model of every evaluation and the potential each `PC_LAMMPS` script of an evaluation runs: 9 lines for 4 evaluations with one script, 7 for 4 evaluations without a script and with `BIAS true` (1.5.0 built the trainer once per worker thread, and the `PC_LAMMPS` potential once per evaluation). With `OPTIM MPARAMS` the line gives the `-c` model's settings, not each evaluation's (`params.tadah` records those). At `VERBOSE 0` no line is printed, as before. Tests: `hpo_tadah_cli_kernel_line_once`, `hpo_kernel_line_e2e`.
- `init_script_executor(width)` builds the script executor of `width` workers in place of the one there is (it stored a width that only the first `script_executor()` call of the process read); `script_executor()` builds a one-worker executor at its first use outside a run, as before. `detail::script_executor_width()` is gone. The journal's `THREADS: <n> workers ...` line and its fan-out width are the executor's own width.
- **The tests need Python 3.11 or later** (`cmake/hpo_test_python.cmake`, `find_package(Python3 3.11 ...)`): the registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` runs `blueprint/gen_anneal_keys.py`, which reads the registry with `tomllib`. An older interpreter is refused at the configure step (`Could NOT find Python3: Found unsuitable version ..., but required is at least "3.11"`), and an older one first on `PATH` is passed over for a newer one; before, any Python 3 was accepted and the gate failed at test time (`No module named 'tomllib'`). Tests: `hpo_test_python_minimum_old` and `_current` (label `cmake`).
- The journal warning for a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker that died names its pid: `pc-worker 2 (pid 41237) killed/died: ...` (it was `pc-worker 2 killed/died: ...`), so it can be matched with the kernel's out-of-memory line.
- The pool checks the pid a worker reports in `HELLO_ACK` against the pid it forked; another pid fails the handshake (`HELLO_ACK names pid ..., but the worker was forked as pid ...`). It was read and discarded.
- **A candidate whose fit has a target that is not finite is an evaluation that threw, refused before the fit**: Tadah.MODELS' `LinearRegressor::train` refuses a design matrix or target with NaN or an infinity under every `OALGO`, as an intercept pinned by `FIXINDEX` (its `FIXWEIGHT` or `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` value) so large that its share of an energy overflows makes the target the free weights are fitted to. The evaluation scores `FAIL_SCORE`, `hpo_run.log` has `eval <n>: eval-thread caught throw, submitting FAIL_SCORE to keep writer queue contiguous (type=...): LinearRegressor::train: the target vector, less the part of the FIXINDEX weights, holds a value that is not finite (nan or inf) at row 1 (of 1 data row, then 6 ridge rows); no weights were fitted` and counts it under `Evaluations that threw`, and the health monitor records no weights for it. It was fitted, its weights were NaN or infinite and its loss was clamped to `FAIL_SCORE` (a `NaN/Inf clampings` count). Test: `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`[fixweight_e2e]`); its two cases of a fit with weights that are not finite pin the intercept at 2e307, which leaves the target finite and makes the `OALGO 4` weights overflow (they pinned 1e308).
- **BREAKING** — `best_pot.tadah` and every potential of an evaluation have no `DBFILE` key line, whichever the model (their training record names the training datasets, the training-record entry under *Changed*); an `M_BLR` run's had the training `DBFILE` lines. The header of `best_pot.tadah` gives the statistics of each training dataset, the record names them. With `LAMBDA -1` the potentials of an `M_KRR` run record `ALPHA` and `BETA` of the evaluation's fit, as an `M_BLR` run's did. A potential used as the `-c` configuration has no training data: add its `DBFILE` lines (without them the run is in the no-training-`DBFILE` mode, which needs every weight pinned, and is refused at startup otherwise).
- **BREAKING — `best_pot.tadah` and the `BDUMP` and `DUMP` archives have no `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` or `VERBOSE` key line; their training record lists them** (the training-record entry under *Changed*): every potential file of an evaluation (the durable best too) is written through `PotHeaderWriter::write_potential_body`, which writes no key line of the keys of Tadah.MLIP `training_record_keys` (`NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE` and `AUDIT_SAMPLE` too, and `AUDIT` and `HEALTH_LOG` as before); every other key is written as before. The files carried the run's `--hpotarget`, `--validation` and `-v` values (`HPOTARGET config.hpo`, `VALIDATION config.val`, `VERBOSE 1`), so such a potential used as the `-c` configuration without `--validation` validated on the file its line named, which nothing on the command line asked for, and `VERBOSE` set the log level of whatever read it (a retrain, `pair_style tadah`). The evaluation's context keeps the run's settings, so the potential the `PC_LAMMPS` scripts run (`PC_ISOLATION` `THREAD` and `PROCESS`) and the gradient fan-out's are built at the run's `VERBOSE`, as before (built from a context without `VERBOSE`, at the blueprint default 1, a kernel model of a `-v 0` run would print its settings line: under `THREAD` once per run, with the run's kernel-line scope of the entry above, and in a `PROCESS` worker never, as a worker prints no kernel line at any `VERBOSE`; before that scope, once per evaluation in both modes). The journal still names the hpotarget and validation files (FILES) and the header of `best_pot.tadah` the validation datasets. A potential that has the key lines (every 1.5.x `best_pot.tadah` has `HPOTARGET` and `VERBOSE`, and `VALIDATION` when its run had a validation file: 1.5.0 took `--validation` as optional) still loads, and a run seeded with it -- the `-c` configuration, or a potential the configuration file includes with `CONFIG` -- reads none of its `VALIDATION`, `HPOTARGET`, `VERBOSE`, `NUMERIC`, `OUTFILE`, `AUDIT`, `AUDIT_SAMPLE` or `HEALTH_LOG` lines (Tadah.MLIP's `potential_line_not_read`; no parse stores a run setting of a potential, Tadah.CORE): it validates on its own `--validation` file alone (a validation metric without one is refused, `... no --validation file was given`), runs at its own `-v` and `NUMERIC`, and the WARNING of `tadah hpo` names the lines it reads not at their default (`its VALIDATION config.val, VERBOSE 2 and NUMERIC 12 were settings of the run that wrote it, not of this run, and are ignored ...`). A run whose configuration has no `HPOTARGET` but the line of a potential it includes (`hpo_run()` of a program's context: `tadah hpo -c` requires `--hpotarget`) is refused at startup, saying where the run's own goes and naming the potential's line that was not read (Tadah.MLIP's `missing_run_setting`): `tadah hpo: no HPOTARGET: name the hpotarget file with --hpotarget, or with an HPOTARGET line in a configuration file without WEIGHTS. old.tadah is a potential (it has WEIGHTS): its HPOTARGET config.hpo line was a setting of the run that wrote it and is not read.`; without a potential the first sentence alone (it threw `Key not found in the Context object: HPOTARGET`). In 1.5.0 the potential's lines were the run's: `tadah hpo -c best_pot.tadah --hpotarget config.hpo` validated on the file its `VALIDATION` line named, at its `VERBOSE` and `NUMERIC`. Tests: `hpo_run without HPOTARGET is refused, naming where the run's own goes` (`test_hpo_termination.cpp`), and Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo -c of an older best_pot.tadah does not validate on its VALIDATION file unless --validation is given`. Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`The potentials of an evaluation keep the inputs of the run in their training record, not as key lines`), `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_lammps_verbose` (new CTest entry: `-v 0` with `M_KRR Kern_RBF` prints no kernel line in either mode; its `THREAD` leg pins the evaluation's context, which both modes take the potential from, and its `PROCESS` leg only that the workers print no kernel line, which they do not at any `VERBOSE`).
- **BIAS true with two or more species (BREAKING, see Tadah.MLIP): the trainer's descriptor has one intercept column per ATOM species**, as pair_style tadah's does, so the PC_LAMMPS potentials and the analytical gradient of a multi-species BIAS configuration use the layout the weights were trained in. Before, from 1.3.0-beta.1 (when pair_style tadah, the ridge and the analytical gradient took one intercept column per species while training kept one shared column), every PC_LAMMPS candidate of such a configuration gave wrong LAMMPS energies and forces, and the analytical gradient built its design matrix with the DC_Selector width (N + D columns) from descriptors of 1 + D entries, reading past each atom's descriptor: results of such runs from 1.3.0-beta.1 until this version are invalid. With 1.2.0 and earlier, pair_style tadah used one shared intercept column too, and PC_LAMMPS evaluated such potentials consistently. The analytical gradient refuses (`std::logic_error`) a DC_Selector width other than the trainer's DSIZE. Tests: `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`.
- **Startup applies the NORM/BIAS rules of `tadah train`** (`validate_hpo_setup` calls `mlip::check_bias_constraints`: NORM true with BF_Linear or Kern_Linear needs BIAS true, BIAS true needs ATOM), which every evaluation of such a configuration would now fail, and prints a WARNING when FIXINDEX is used with BIAS true and two or more species, naming the model's intercept weights (a linear model's weights 1 to N, its descriptor weights from N + 1, where they started at 2 before 1.6.0; `BF_Polynomial2`'s products (k,k); a non-linear kernel's, those of the basis its fit draws, which starts with the intercept vectors: a dot-product kernel's weights 1 to N), for FIXINDEX/FIXWEIGHT and `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` alike. The host refuses, when it loads the training data, BIAS true data whose energy-weighted compositions cannot determine the intercepts of the model's function (`mlip::require_intercepts_determined`), a non-linear kernel's too: those of the basis its fit draws, not of a basis the `-c` seed holds (an M_KRR implementation 2 seed, not checked, was refused when it was a potential, and a dot-product kernel passed startup and failed every evaluation). Startup refuses an `SBASIS` below the intercept vectors plus one drawn vector (N + 1 with BIAS true on N species; it took 2), which every evaluation's fit would refuse. The probe `Trainer` reads no `WEIGHTS` of the `-c` potential, so the EMBF0 journal takes them from the configuration (`derive_raw_embedding_f0`). The EMBF0 ZERO hint says intercept columns, one per ATOM species. Tests: `test_multispecies_bias.cpp`, `test_startup_validation.cpp` (its NORM true cases set BIAS true, so they still test the rule they name).
- `make_nlopt` (`validate_nlopt_spec`) refuses an INNER spec with an INIT chain or an INIT `K_TOTAL` (`NLopt INNER spec with an INIT chain: MLSL and AUGLAG choose the INNER search's starting points; an INIT block belongs to the enclosing block`), as it refuses an INNER `SEED` and `GRAD_STRATEGY`: only a spec built outside the parser can carry one, and nothing resolved it. Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **The journal counts an evaluation that threw as one, not as a NaN/Inf clamping** (`EvalLoss::Threw`). An evaluation whose training or prediction raised an exception scores `FAILSCORE`, as before, and DIAGNOSTICS count it under `Evaluations that threw : N (x%)`, with `[!] N evaluation(s) raised an exception and scored FAIL_SCORE; each has a WARNING "eval <n>: eval-thread caught ..." with its message.` when N > 0; the milestone `SUMMARY` adds `threw=N`, and `NaN rate` counts NaN/Inf losses only. It still counts as an unusable loss. Such an evaluation was counted as a NaN/Inf clamping: a run whose every training threw (`OALGO 3` with `BIAS true` and `LAMBDA` > 0) read `NaN/Inf clampings : 3 (100.0%)` and `[!] 100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss that was clamped.`, though no loss was computed. The `LOSS FUNCTION` section's `Fail score` line says what scores `FAILSCORE` under the run's `FAILSCORE_GRADING`: under `OFF` `(the loss of a failed evaluation: a NaN/Inf loss, an exception, a skip cap, a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term or a gate reject; the ceiling of every loss)` (for a run with `PC_OBSERVABLE` lines; `a failed PC_LAMMPS simulation` for one with `PC_LAMMPS` lines, neither for a run with neither: the failed terms the run can have, `HPO_Host::assemble_loss`), under `ON` `(the loss of an evaluation with a NaN/Inf loss or an exception; the ceiling of every loss)` and a `Fail grading` line, `ON -- a skip cap, a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term or a gate reject scores a graded loss in [0.999, 1) x Fail score` (the same failures); 1.5.0's line said `(returned when an evaluation produces NaN/Inf)` whatever the grading, and it had no `Fail grading` line. (Under `OFF` a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation scores `FAILSCORE` whatever loss its variables are measured with: see the *Fixed* entry on an evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation.) Tests: `test_hpolog_new_best_guard.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`No usable loss: evaluations that throw count as unusable losses`, which asserted the NaN/Inf wording).
- **Every optimiser records why its search ended, and the run's trailer says so: `COMPLETED` is no longer written.** The TADAH (`RANDOM`, `GRID`, `ANNEAL`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`), DLIB (`GFS`, `BFGS`) and CERES (`STEEPEST_DESCENT`) drivers record `<LIB>:<ALGO>: <reason>` with a fixed reason (`run_end.h`, *Added*: `MAXEVAL reached`, `MAXTIME exceeded`, `STOPVAL reached`, `FTOL_ABS reached`, `FTOL_REL reached`, `XTOL_REL reached`, `tol_gradinf reached`, `max_iter reached`, `T_MIN reached`, `FTOL plateau`, `all grid points evaluated`, `no optimisable dimensions`); the journal's stop line gives the numbers, a tolerance's value with `(default)` when its key was not set (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: FTOL_ABS reached after 51 evaluation(s): the loss changed by less than FTOL_ABS = 1e-07 (default) from one iteration to the next`). A Ceres ending without such a reason is named by Ceres' termination type and the head of its message, a fixed text too (below). NLopt keeps its result strings, ASA `ASA: <reason>`, a gradient that is not finite its own status and `RESTART` its stagnation status. Before, these drivers recorded none for most endings and `HPOLog::finish` wrote `COMPLETED`: `DLIB:BFGS` for a spent `MAXEVAL` (its only line was `DLIB:BFGS -- MAXEVAL=N reached; preserving best-so-far`, which stays its stop line) and for its convergence test, which left no line at all; `DLIB:GFS`, `TADAH:RANDOM` and `TADAH:GRID` for a spent `MAXEVAL` and the whole grid; the annealers for every stop, `STOPVAL` and `MAXTIME` included; the CERES driver for every ending but a rejected gradient's, a `FAILURE` such as `STEEPEST_DESCENT`'s `Numerical failure in line search` included. Per driver:
  - `DLIB:BFGS`: `MAXEVAL reached`, `FTOL_ABS reached` (dlib's `objective_delta_stop_strategy`: the loss changed by less than `FTOL_ABS`, 1e-7 by default, from one iteration to the next) or `tol_gradinf reached` (the largest component of the gradient below `PARAM tol_gradinf`, the BREAKING entry below).
  - CERES: Ceres' own reason when it converged, read from its message (`Function tolerance reached` → `FTOL_REL reached`, `Parameter tolerance reached` → `XTOL_REL reached`, `Gradient tolerance reached` → `tol_gradinf reached`; another message → `CONVERGENCE (<head>)`) or ran out of iterations (`max_iter reached`; another → `NO_CONVERGENCE (<head>)`); `MAXEVAL reached` when an evaluation refused for the budget made it fail (`GradientProvider::exhausted`, *Added*); `STOPPED -- CERES:<ALGO>: FAILURE (<head>)` for any other failure, the best standing, as after NLopt's `STOPPED -- nlopt roundoff-limited`. `<head>` is the head of Ceres' message, its text before the first `. `, `, ` or `: ` (`detail::ceres_message_head`): `STOPPED -- CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT: FAILURE (Numerical failure in line search)`, where the message goes on with the run's step size, cost and gradient. `USER_SUCCESS`, and `USER_FAILURE` without the host exception that is its only cause, are internal errors. The stop line (`CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT stopped: FTOL_REL reached after 30 evaluation(s): FTOL_REL = 1e-06 (default); Ceres: Function tolerance reached. ...`) gives Ceres' message for a converged solve too; it was logged only for one that did not converge.
  - `DLIB:GFS`: `MAXEVAL reached` for a spent `MAXEVAL`. A `MAXTIME` that ended dlib's run time with calls left records `MAXTIME exceeded` as before, now decided from the budget left and the `MAXTIME` set rather than by reading the clock again, and any other ending with calls left is an internal error.
  - `TADAH:RANDOM`: `MAXEVAL reached`; `TADAH:GRID`: `MAXEVAL reached` when it cut the grid short, `all grid points evaluated`.
  - `TADAH:ANNEAL`, `ANNEAL_PT`, `ANNEAL_PA`: from their stop line, on every MPI rank: `MAXEVAL reached` (also during the T0 probes, or before any proposal), `MAXTIME exceeded` (also during the probes), `STOPVAL reached` (also on a probe), `T_MIN reached`, `FTOL plateau` (the plateau and `ANNEAL_PT`'s best-stall rule), `no optimisable dimensions`; a signal records none (`SHUTDOWN`). Serial `ANNEAL`'s rung loop has no loop condition of its own any more: a `MAXTIME` that passed between its post-rung test and its loop test ended the search `loop completed normally` with no reason; it ends `MAXTIME exceeded`. With `REANNEAL` (the default) a `MAXTIME` that passed after the schedule's last post-rung test and so prevented a re-anneal that was due is `MAXTIME exceeded` too, in the stop line and the status: it was `T < T_MIN` and `T_MIN reached` (a spent `MAXEVAL` cannot end it there: the schedule ends on `T_MIN` only with budget left). With no re-anneal due (its start temperature `T_INIT * REANNEAL_SCALE^n` below `T_MIN / RT`) the schedule's end is the search's, `T_MIN reached`, whatever the time, as under `REANNEAL OFF`: the re-anneal driver tests that before `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME`. An end without a reason is an internal error.
  A normal end without a recorded status is an internal error (`drive_run`, right after a best potential that could not be saved, which outranks it, and before the no-usable-loss test, which it outranks): `std::logic_error`, the trailer `FAILED -- internal error: the optimiser ended without recording why it ended`, the queued rows written, exit status 1. `HPOLog::finish` without a status writes `(no status recorded)`, and `run_starts` adds the failed-starts note only to a recorded status (it made up `COMPLETED (N of M MULTISTART starts failed; see the WARNINGs)`). Under `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE` the trailer stays the last start's or the refinement's status: its `MAXEVAL reached` is that start's budget. A script that tells success from failure tests the exit status, not the status text. Tests: `test_backend_end_status.cpp` (the annealers', `DLIB:GFS`'s and the CERES driver's endings on stub hosts, the three Ceres convergence messages pinned), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`DLIB:BFGS records why it ended`, `drive_run refuses a normal end that recorded no status`, a normal end without a status among the endings that reach the cross-rank agreement once and before the final flush, and `End status: every registered backend records why it ended ...`: every backend of the caps registry under `PLAIN`, `MULTISTART`, `PRESTAGE` and `RESTART`), the annealers' stop-line tests, `test_policy_host_exceptions.cpp`, `tests/mpi/test_anneal_mpi.cpp` (every rank's status, and a normal end without a status on the last rank only, which ends that rank with the internal error while the others complete; `TADAH_HPO_MPI` builds, CTest `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix`).
- **`PC_FRMSE` and `PC_SRMSE_GPA` leave out the validation structures stored with priority 0** (force priority 0, `fweight`, for `PC_FRMSE`; stress priority 0, `sweight`, for `PC_SRMSE_GPA`), per dataset, in the metric and, for `PC_FRMSE`, in its analytical gradient (MLIP `Analytics`, `analytics_grad`); `tadah data convert` stores a structure without a stress tensor, and a `.castep` structure without forces, with that priority 0 and zeros in their place, which the metrics compared with the prediction. A validation dataset in which every structure has that priority 0 is not measured: its value and loss term nan in stats.tadah, loss.tadah and the best_pot.tadah result table, left out of `FRMSE[avg]`/`SRMSE_GPA[avg]` and of the gradient (`ValidationMetricTargets::set_measured()`: no loss term, slope 0, a global target the loss of the average over the measured datasets), and named by one journal line at startup. A run is refused at startup when no validation dataset has the data, or when an indexed `PC_FRMSE` or `PC_SRMSE_GPA` gives such a dataset a weight above 0, naming the metric, the file and the priority. Test: `test_validation_follows_metric_e2e.cpp` (cases 2a, 2b, 4a).
- **The energy metrics (`PC_EMAE`, `PC_ERMSE`, `PC_ErRMSE`, `PC_ERSQ`) leave out the validation structures stored with energy priority 0** (`eweight`), per dataset, in the metric and in its analytical gradient (MLIP `Analytics`, `analytics_grad`): such a structure has no energy of its own (its energy line, which the dataset format needs, holds a placeholder), which the metrics compared with the prediction. A validation dataset in which every structure has `eweight` 0 is not measured, as for `PC_FRMSE`: its values and loss terms nan, left out of `EMAE[avg]`, `ERMSE[avg]`, `ErRMSE[avg]` and `ERSQ[avg]` and of the gradient, and named by one journal line at startup. A run is refused at startup when no validation dataset has an energy, or when an indexed `PC_ERMSE` gives such a dataset a weight above 0, naming the metric, the file and the energy priority. The target-file reference gives the refusals of the energy metrics, of `PC_FRMSE` and of `PC_SRMSE_GPA` in the entries of the six metrics, each with a case on a dataset of priority 0. Test: `test_validation_follows_metric_e2e.cpp` (cases 2c, 4b).
- **Validation follows its metrics.** `FORCE` and `STRESS` (from `-c`, or `-F`/`-S`, which take precedence) decide only what training fits, as in `tadah train`; validation predicts forces whenever `PC_FRMSE` is set and stresses whenever `PC_SRMSE_GPA` is set, whatever the potential was fitted with. `best_pot.tadah` keeps the training `FORCE`/`STRESS`. The analytical gradient covers `PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` (the `PC_FRMSE needs FORCE true` refusal is gone). With `FORCE true` and no force metric, validation no longer predicts forces nobody reads (about 4× faster validation in the measured case); a validation energy may then differ in the last bit for `D2_LJ`, `D2_ZBL` and `D2_Blip5`, whose energy-only descriptor expressions are written differently.
- **BREAKING — the `--validation` file holds `DBFILE`, `EFILTER` and `FFILTER` (and `CONFIG`), and every other key is refused at its line** (also in a file it includes), by the run and by `--check`, as the run reads nothing else of it: `FORCE` and `STRESS` (validation predicts what its metrics measure), the keys that set a fit (`ENERGY`, `ENORM`, the error scales, `WDBFILE`: `Key WDBFILE on line 2 of config.val is not accepted here: the validation set is not fitted; WDBFILE, the priority of each training dataset in the fit, is set in the training configuration, and a validation structure without a label has eweight, fweight or sweight 0 in its data file`), the retired keys, and those of the model, of its fit and of the run (`LAMBDA`, `OALGO`, the descriptor keys, `VERBOSE`: `... is not accepted here: the validation configuration holds DBFILE, EFILTER and FFILTER (and CONFIG), the validation datasets and their filters; the training configuration sets the model, its fit and the run`). 1.5.0 accepted them and ignored them. Test: `test_hpo_error_scales.cpp` (`The validation configuration holds DBFILE, EFILTER and FFILTER only`).
- **POTENTIAL SETUP states what training fits and what validation predicts:** `Fit forces (FORCE) : no  (configuration file c.tadah)`, `yes  (command line -F/--force; overrides FORCE false in c.tadah)`, `(default)`; `Fit stresses (STRESS)`; `Validation predicts : energies, forces (PC_FRMSE)`. `hpo_run(ctx, provenance)` takes the task's `KeyProvenance`; `hpo_run(ctx)` journals `(source not recorded)` for a key the context holds.
- Framework version `1.6.0`, read from CORE's `blueprint/blueprint_tadah.toml` after the Tadah dependencies are fetched, so the module reports the right version in every layout (sibling checkout or FetchContent). A missing blueprint is now a configure error instead of a silent `0.0.0`.
- Tests take Catch2 and its test discovery from cmake-tools (`tadah_setup_tests()`, `include(Catch)`); the vendored `tests/catch2/catch.hpp`, `tests/Catch.cmake` and `tests/CatchAddTests.cmake` are removed. Every discovered CTest entry now selects exactly its own test case and passes `--warn NoTests`, so an entry that matches no test case fails instead of passing. New test sources are picked up without a manual re-configure (`CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`).
- The test `MultiStartPolicy: validate rejects NLopt GN_*` is renamed `... rejects the NLopt global GN_DIRECT` (what it checks). Catch cannot select a name that ends in `*` on its own (the `*` is a wildcard), and discovery now refuses such names.
- cmake-tools is fetched at `v1-dev` instead of `main`.
- The `ASA_SAVE` option hint says HPO runs are not resumable (it said HPO had its own checkpoints).
- The `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` error and the worker-process guard of the MPI build name `TADAH_HPO_MPI` only; `hpo_mpi.h` no longer mentions the legacy build.
- **A `SEED` in the `RESTART` block seeds its `METHOD` chain**: restart r draws its fresh points with `SEED + r`, so the restart points of a run are reproducible. The key was accepted and ignored, and every restart drew its points from `std::random_device`, as it still does without a `SEED` (one draw per run, which the journal names, and a stream of the restart points' own: the `RESTART` stream entry above). The journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line shows the seed first: `RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED 43, SEED 6`. It is a separate setting from the optimiser seeds `RESTART` derives for each round (`SEED + r·S`, `EXPLORE` `SEED + r`), and the restart points come from a stream of their own, not the optimisers' (the `TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` entry below), so the two may be equal. The first round, which starts from the `INIT` points, is unchanged. Like an `INIT` block's `SEED`, one that no `METHOD` of the chain draws from (only `CONFIG`/`WARM`, or no `METHOD` line) gets a startup warning.
- **A Sobol seed selects a whole block of K points, which starts at its own index** (`INIT` and `RESTART` `METHOD SOBOL K`): seed s draws block b = s mod 65536 (the seed's 16 low bits, as before), the points (b + 1)·K … (b + 1)·K + K − 1 of the sequence (point 0 is the low corner of the box, which no block holds), so seeds that differ modulo 65536 draw disjoint points — the seeds `SEED + r` of consecutive restart rounds, and an `INIT` and a `RESTART` block with the same `SEED` and K — and a block of K = 2^m points puts one point in each 1/K of the range of every search parameter, in the coordinates of the search (with K = 4 in two dimensions, one point in each quarter of each range). 1.5.0 skipped s points: seed s drew the points s + 1 … s + K, so the blocks of the seeds s and s + 1 shared K − 1 points (`METHOD SOBOL 3`: `SEED 42` drew points 43–45, `SEED 43` points 44–46), and a block was evenly spread only when s + 1 was a multiple of K (K = 4 in two dimensions: `SEED 0` drew (0.5, 0.5), (0.75, 0.25), (0.25, 0.75) and (0.375, 0.375), two points in one quarter of each range and none in another; `SEED 3` drew points 4–7, one in each quarter). `SOBOL 1` is unchanged: seed s draws point s mod 65536 + 1. **The points of `METHOD SOBOL K`, and the results of the runs that start from them, change for every K ≥ 2.** K is the number of points the method draws; `K_TOTAL` keeps the first of them.
- **`tadah refit` `MAXEVAL` counts objective evaluations exactly, for every NLopt and dlib algorithm** (each call of a stage's objective, for its value or its gradient, is one), and refit results change for three of them when `MAXEVAL` is set: EXPLORE `LIB DLIB ALGO GLOBAL` runs `MAXEVAL` evaluations (it took `max(500, MAXEVAL/20)` calls), dlib's `LBFGS` stops after `MAXEVAL` evaluations counted in its objective wrapper (it capped its L-BFGS iterations: at `MAXEVAL` in EXPLORE `INNER`, 50 × `MAXEVAL` in REFINE), and REFINE `LD_LBFGS` takes `MAXEVAL` evaluations (it took 50 × `MAXEVAL`). Ceres' `LM` and `DOGLEG` still count iterations (`max_num_iterations`), as `MAXITER` says. Without `MAXEVAL` the budgets of `GLOBAL` and of NLopt's REFINE `LD_LBFGS` are the old effective ones, 3000 evaluations (`CompactConfig::dlib_global_maxeval`) and 50000 (`lbfgs_refine_maxeval`); dlib's `LBFGS` keeps the numbers of its old iteration caps, 20000 in EXPLORE `INNER` and 50000 in REFINE, now counted in evaluations (at least two per L-BFGS iteration, as dlib asks for the value and the gradient in separate calls), so a default run whose cap binds stops sooner than before; the runs measured on `Fe_mm.eam.fs` ended on their tolerance long before it. `apply_stage_optimizers` moves next to the fitter (`compact_fitter.h`); the refit log gives each stage's budget in its unit and the evaluations each stage spent (`term=DLIB_GLOBAL:MAXEVAL_REACHED(3000ev)`), and `CompactBlipFitter::objective_evaluations()` counts them.
- **The order of the optimiser's dimensions is kept, and documented as the contract**: by `OPTIM` key (alphabetically, byte-wise), then by index within the key — for `FIXWEIGHT` by `FIXINDEX` slot — whatever the order of the `OPTIM` lines. It fixes the `params.tadah` / `best_params.tadah` columns, the `hpo_search_space.tsv` rows, the journal's `dim` numbers and the index of ASA's `PARAM <Name>[i]`, and it applies to the hpotargets `tadah refit` writes. Declaration order was considered for 1.6.0 and not adopted: it would have silently retargeted existing ASA `[i]` options and changed same-`SEED` trajectories of configurations whose lines are not in key order. The test `params.tadah columns: iteration then slot then OPTIM dimensions sorted by key and index` pins it.
- Optional-fixture tests are reported by CTest as Skipped, not Passed: `hpo_strategy_matrix` exits with 77 (its `SKIP_RETURN_CODE`) when `TADAH_HPO_INTEGRATION_BUNDLE` is unset, and the Corana 1987 oracle (`hpo_corana1987_protocol`) calls `TADAH_SKIP` (cmake-tools' TADAH-SKIP marker) while its reference table is empty. A 77 from the strategy matrix itself is reported as a failure. The hidden LAMMPS-driven benchmark cases (`bench_pair_tadah.cpp`, `test_hpo_adjoint_pilot.cpp`, `test_lammps_hessian.cpp`) also call `TADAH_SKIP` when there is no potential (`TADAH_BENCH_POT` unset and no default), instead of passing after a warning; a potential that `TADAH_BENCH_POT` names and that cannot be read, or any potential that does not parse or whose LAMMPS set-up fails, is now a failure, where the Hessian cases skipped. That exposed a set-up defect of these cases, now fixed: none initialised the periodic table, so building the potential failed (`Unknown symbol: Ta`) whenever one ran on its own.
- Building the tests requires Python 3 (`find_package(Python3 ... REQUIRED)`), as the umbrella build already did. The registry drift gate `hpo_anneal_registry_sync` was registered only when Python was found, so without it the gate silently did not exist.
- `optimizer_caps(lib, algo)` takes the names as `std::string_view` (the test helper `anneal_functions::by_name` too). The result refers to the static registry, but a call with a literal bound a temporary `std::string`, and GCC 13's `-Wdangling-reference` reported the reference bound to the result as possibly dangling at six sites (`test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` 340 and 372 among them). The build has no `-Wdangling-reference` warning left; no pragma.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE` columns are `L(<tag>)[k]` in `loss.tadah` and `<tag>[k]` in `outvar.tadah`, with `k` the 1-based ordinal of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` line** (`Observable::column_tag()`; `cohesive_energy`'s tag is `Ecoh`, so `L(Ecoh)[1]`, `Ecoh[1]`), and the journal names the term `cohesive_energy[k]` in the loss section (with its columns) and in the new-best `top:` breakdown. The index was 0-based, the loss column `L(cohesive_energy[0])` and the value column `cohesive_energy[0]`, which every `DIGITS` setting cut (at the default `DIGITS 6` to `L(cohesive*` and `cohesive_e*`; a second observable became `L(cohesiv~2` and `cohesive_~2`), and the `top:` breakdown named every term `cohesive_energy`. The labels fit every `DIGITS` for `k` up to 99; past that the tag is shortened and the index kept (`L(Eco*[100]`), the rule `params.tadah` uses (`header_detail::fit_keep_index`), and the journal's loss section names the columns as the files write them (the column geometry is `column_titles.h`, shared by the host and the journal). `kind()`, the warm-start cache key, is unchanged. Scripts that read these columns by name need the new names.
- **With `PC_OBSERVABLE`, an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolves to `ANALYTICAL`** at parse time in each block that runs an optimiser and obtains gradients (`obtains_gradients`, `phase_specs.h`: a gradient-based backend, or an MLSL/AUGLAG whose local search is one) — the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser and `EXPLORE` — and the journal's OPTIMISER section records it (`GRAD_STRATEGY : ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE)`; each such block now has a `GRAD_STRATEGY` line). It ran finite differences (`NUMERICAL`), which the same rule rejects when written out. An `INNER` block is not resolved: MLSL and AUGLAG hand their local search the gradient of their own objective, which the enclosing block's `GRAD_STRATEGY` obtains (`nlopt_adapter.h`), and an `INNER` block takes no `GRAD_STRATEGY` (next entry but one). Derivative-free blocks are left alone (ANNEAL and ASA reject any `GRAD_STRATEGY`), an explicit `HYBRID` or `ANALYTICAL` is kept, `NUMERICAL` is still rejected (the message no longer calls `HYBRID` and `ANALYTICAL` equivalent), and without `PC_OBSERVABLE` nothing changes. **Behaviour change:** such a run now needs the settings of the analytical gradient (next entry) or `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`.
- **`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` — written out, or resolved for `PC_OBSERVABLE` — is refused at startup on a setup the analytical gradient does not cover** (`validate_hpo_setup`, through `analytical_gradient_blocker()`, `analytical_gate.h`, which the run-time gate of `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` and `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer` now uses too): no training `DBFILE`, `FIXINDEX`, `STRESS true`, `NORM true`, `M_KRR` implementation 2 (`MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2`: the gradient differentiates the least-squares fit of the training rows, that of `M_BLR` and of implementation 1, and implementation 2 solves (K + `LAMBDA` sigma^2 I) w = t over its basis vectors; the message names implementation 1, and not `OALGO`, which the fit of implementation 2 does not read), `OALGO` other than 4, `LAMBDA` below 0 (`-1` included), a basis other than `BF_Linear` / `Kern_Linear`, or `PC_SRMSE_GPA` (`PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` was refused too until validation followed its metrics; see the entry above), and with an `OPTIM` line of an error scale of the fit (`ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA`, `SSIGMA`: `... cannot form: OPTIM ESIGMA searches an error scale of the fit, which the analytical gradient does not differentiate. ...`) or of `WDBFILE`, a priority of the fit, whose derivatives it does not form. It is also refused with a loss term that has no analytical derivative (`analytical_loss_term_blocker()`): a `PC_OBSERVABLE` line with `--unconverged force-scaled`, whose value at a relaxation that does not converge has none (`Observable::analytical_gradient_gap()`); `HYBRID` fills such a term with finite differences, so the run-time gate does not apply this check. The loss function of every term has a derivative with respect to its value, `MAPE` and `RMSLE` included. The rule checks the blocks that run an optimiser — the `STRATEGY`-level block and `EXPLORE` — not an `INNER` block, whose search takes the enclosing block's gradient. The message names the block, the setting or the `PC_OBSERVABLE` term, and `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID` (`OPTIMIZER block: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE: the block sets no GRAD_STRATEGY) needs the analytical gradient of the loss, which this setup cannot form: OALGO is 1 (it needs OALGO 4, the Cholesky normal equations). …`, `… cannot form: the PC_OBSERVABLE term cohesive_energy[1]: --unconverged force-scaled reports target + slope x fmax for a relaxation that does not converge, a value without an analytical derivative. …`). Tadah! 1.5.0 passed such a run at startup and stopped it at its first gradient with `no registered analytical derivative … register the missing analytical derivatives in dtheta_registry`; under `M_KRR` implementation 2 with `OALGO 4` it ran the search on the gradient of the least-squares fit of the training rows, a fit the run did not make. Under `HYBRID` the journal warns once with the reason (`No analytical gradient for this setup: …`) and every parameter uses finite differences, as before. The error of an `ANALYTICAL` gradient that cannot be formed at run time names its possible causes (a parameter without an analytical derivative, a candidate whose fit cannot be differentiated, a loss term without one at that point) and `HYBRID`; it pointed at a `dtheta_registry` that does not exist.
- **`GRAD_STRATEGY` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line**, like `SEED` there: `Error on line N: GRAD_STRATEGY is not accepted inside an INNER block: the enclosing block's GRAD_STRATEGY applies to its local search; the enclosing STRATEGY block takes GRAD_STRATEGY` (or `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` block, wherever the `INNER` block is). MLSL and AUGLAG hand their `INNER` search the gradient of their own objective, which the enclosing block's `GRAD_STRATEGY` obtains (`nlopt_adapter.h`), so a value written in the `INNER` block was accepted and never used. A configuration that sets one now fails to load; move the line to the enclosing block. `make_nlopt()` refuses an `INNER` spec that carries one (`validate_nlopt_spec`), the unknown-key error of an `INNER` block no longer lists `GRAD_STRATEGY`, and the `PC_OBSERVABLE` `NUMERICAL` message and the startup `ANALYTICAL` message no longer mention an `INNER` value.
- The `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` requirement of a finite `FAILSCORE` with room for the four bands is checked by `validate_hpo_setup`, so every caller of the startup validation applies it; it was checked by `hpo_run()` alone. The messages are unchanged.
- The README badges and links and the pointers to the umbrella's community files (`CITATION.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, …) point to the `v1-dev` line: GitLab `v1-dev`, `https://tadah.readthedocs.io/v1-dev/`. They pointed to `develop`.
- **`hpo_run()` returns `EXIT_FAILURE` (1) for a run that ends on its own without a usable loss**, and `tadah hpo` exits with 1: it returned 0. A job script that took 0 for success now sees such a run fail (*Fixed* below; *Configuration File*, *Stopping a run*).
- **`HPOLog::record_eval(std::size_t, double, EvalLoss)` replaces `record_eval(std::size_t, double, bool was_nan)`**: the third argument is the kind of the evaluation's loss (`EvalLoss::Usable`, `EvalLoss::Failed`, `EvalLoss::NanClamped`, `EvalLoss::Threw`), which the DIAGNOSTICS count of unusable losses needs. A caller passes `EvalLoss::NanClamped` where it passed `true` for a NaN/Inf loss, `EvalLoss::Threw` for an evaluation that raised an exception, and `EvalLoss::Usable` or `EvalLoss::Failed` where it passed `false`. New: `HPOLog::n_unusable()`, `HPOLog::n_threw()`, `HPOLog::set_rank_scope(rank, world)`, `HPO_Host::has_usable_loss()`.
- **`FAILSCORE` must be a positive finite number**, an error at its line otherwise (`Error on line 9: FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got '0'`). A `FAILSCORE` of 0 or below was accepted and left no failure band `[0.999 FS, FS)`: every finite loss, `FAILSCORE` itself included, counted as usable, so a run whose every evaluation failed ended with status 0, `COMPLETED`, `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss` and a `best_pot.tadah` that held a failed evaluation (`FAILSCORE 0`: `HPO_BEST_LOSS 0`), and without a skip cap the ceiling clipped every positive loss to `FAILSCORE`. Negative losses (negative-weight `--varloss` terms) stay usable under any positive `FAILSCORE`: failures sit above every loss. The message of a malformed value names the rule too (`FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got 'abc'`). No test configuration used such a value.
- **`PC_FAILPROBE --failscore` must be a positive finite number** (`Error on line N: PC_FAILPROBE --failscore must be a positive finite number, got '0'`). On the probe line it is a failure score: the probe's `S` reads it when the probe itself fails, and an `S` at or above it counts as a failed probe, so with 0 or below every probe read as failed (band D for every evaluation under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`). On a `PC_LAMMPS` line `--failscore` is the value substituted for the line's variables when its simulation fails, which goes through the loss against the target like a measured value: it may still be any finite number, 0 and negative values included.
- **DM_REAM `EMBF0`** (F(0) = 0 by construction, default ZERO): no change to the search, the evaluated numbers or the written rows is needed — each candidate's DM_REAM recomputes g_m(0) from its own CEMBFUNC/SEMBFUNC (fresh and cached-trainer paths), and every written potential carries the `EMBF0` line. Added: `OPTIM EMBF0` is rejected at startup (and in `validate_hpo_setup`); `hpo_run.log` POTENTIAL SETUP rows `Embedding F(0)` and the raw F(0) of the `-c` potential, and a RESULT line with the raw F(0) of the first and best evaluation (linear models only; diagnostic, never in any output row); a HINT when a DM_REAM model under ZERO is trained without BIAS/ESHIFT, naming `ESHIFT` and the run's `--eshift-atom`/`--eshift-dbatom` (a `-c` seed's own toggle line is not read); a WARNING when an INIT WARM seed declares a different `EMBF0` than the run.
- **Compact refit (`tadah refit`, REFIT_MODE compact) fits the zero-referenced embedding under `EMBF0 ZERO`**: `BlipFit`/`CurveCost`/`EnergyCost`/VARPRO carry an F-only `zero_ref` flag with the matching Jacobians (∂c = b - b0, ∂μ = -c(db - db0), ∂η = c[(x-μ)db - (0-μ)db0]/η), and the engine's energy/curve targets use F_tab - F_tab(0). FREE is textually unchanged. Compact ZERO results change for embedding bumps covering rho = 0 and knots below 0.
- **One rule for how a run ends** (`drive_run()`, `run_driver.h`, owns
  every ending, from the fresh-run reset of `best_pot.tadah` on): an
  error ends `FAILED -- <what>` (was `STOPPED with error: <what>`), a
  non-standard exception `FAILED -- non-standard exception` (was `STOPPED
  with unknown error`), both with exit status 1; a best potential that
  could not be saved ends `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <first
  recorded cause>`, exit status 1, also when the run then ended on a stop
  criterion, a signal or a stagnation stop that outranked or swallowed the
  error (`detail::rethrow_if_best_unsaved`, `durable_failure_cause`, now in
  `run_driver.h`); a signal ends `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)` with 128 + the
  signal number; a run without a usable loss exits 1. `hpo_run()` has no
  handler set of its own. NLopt's end is classified by
  `classify_nlopt_exception()` alone (by type: a subclass of
  `std::runtime_error` such as `durable_best::Error` is never NLopt's); it
  replaces `nlopt_self_terminated()`. A fresh-run reset of
  `best_pot.tadah` that fails (its lock cannot be taken, say) ends the run
  `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <cause>` too, with the RESULT
  section and its retries in DIAGNOSTICS: it was a startup error, and the
  journal ended with an `ERROR startup:` line and no trailer. Tests:
  `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`drive_run fails a run whose best potential
  could not be saved, however it ends`), `[durable_e2e]` (`durable best: a
  failed fresh-run reset fails the run`).
- RESTART with an NLopt inner optimiser now restarts on stagnation (the
  `StagnationStop` ended as a graceful "nlopt failure"). MAXEVAL applies
  per launch, so a RESTART run can use up to (MAX + 1) x MAXEVAL
  evaluations.
- SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGUSR2 during NLopt, a MULTISTART start, PT/PA
  calibration or NOISE_CALIBRATION ends as `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)` with exit
  status 128 + the signal number (*Fixed*, the signal entry). In a
  multi-rank `TADAH_HPO_MPI` PT/PA run every rank ends so (the *Fixed*
  entry on a signal one rank receives; the ranks other than 0 ended
  `FAILED -- ...: rank 0 aborted during ... calibration`, exit status 1).
- `NLOPT_ROUNDOFF_LIMITED` is a graceful stop (was FAILED); an error inside
  an NLopt gradient (e.g. a GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL wiring error) fails
  the run.
- A transient durable-best failure can hold the evaluations whose loss may
  become the new best for up to about 30 s (the others carry on).
- DIAGNOSTICS' transient-I/O note calls the best potential complete only
  when one was persisted. (Its `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable
  loss.` verdict is the count of unusable losses: *Fixed*.)
- `StagnationStop` moved to namespace scope (`tadah/hpo/stagnation_stop.h`;
  `HPO_Host<...>::StagnationStop` is an alias). New
  `host_analytical_grad_fn()` (`gradient_provider.h`).
- Numerics shift at the rounding level: a start point the plain map
  rounded is now evaluated exactly (3e-11 relative for a small weight in a
  wide box), and grids, LSCALE/ESHIFT and LAMMPS read-backs are unrounded,
  so first losses and possibly trajectories differ from 1.5.0 at that
  level; `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` runs are no longer byte-identical to earlier
  releases.
- **BREAKING — a DM_REAM `-c` configuration must declare `RHOFORM`** (see Tadah.MODELS): `HPOTargets` reads it right after the grids are expanded, before the OPTIM boxes and the WARM seeds that depend on it, and stops without it (`tadah hpo: DM_REAM: RHOFORM is required: ...`). `validate_hpo_setup` validates a bare DM_REAM density for its form (grids, the MONOTONE cutoff list, WATOM > 0 under the positive forms), and the startup probes (`derive_weight_count`, `derive_raw_embedding_f0`, the host's all-weights-fixed probe) rethrow `models::DensityFormError` instead of swallowing it, so an invalid density is a startup error, not a failure of every evaluation. Every potential the run writes carries the key. **Migration:** add `RHOFORM SIGNED` to a seed written before tadah 1.6.0; `SIGNED` runs evaluate bit for bit as before.
- **Non-negative boxes under `NONNEG`/`MONOTONE`.** `OPTIM AMPGRIDMB ... AUTO` with a linear rule clamps lo = max(0, x0 - d) before the LOG check and, without FLOOR/GROUP_FLOOR, takes an implicit GROUP_FLOOR 1e-3 (the refit writer's group floor), so a zero amplitude gets [0, 1e-3 max|x0|]; the new box origin `AutoLinearNonneg` prints as `AUTO FRAC>=0` (the SEARCH SPACE origin column widens when it occurs). `RATIO` needs every seed on the line > 0, a negative seed is an error, and an all-zero line without a floor is an error with a hint. An explicit `AMPGRIDMB` lo < 0, any `WATOM` lo <= 0 and, under `MONOTONE`, any `SGRIDMB` lo <= 0 (explicit or AUTO) are startup errors. The comment header of `hpo_search_space.tsv` gains two lines describing `AUTO FRAC>=0` (every run).
- **`OPTIM` on structure keys is refused** before `context.size(k)`, case-insensitively: `RHOFORM`, `TYPE2B`, `TYPEMB`, `RCTYPE2B`, `RCTYPEMB`, `KDEG2B`, `KDEGMB`, `KDEGEMB` ("OPTIM <KEY> is not a valid search target: ..."); `validate_hpo_setup` repeats the check.
- **WARM and the density form**: a WARM seed whose `RHOFORM` is absent, invalid or different from the run's is an ERROR when `AMPGRIDMB`, `CGRIDMB` or `SGRIDMB` has an `OPTIM` line and a WARNING otherwise (the seed's density keys are not read); independent of `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`. A `RESTART` refresh applies the rule to its `WARM` seeds as well (its `InitContext` carries the run's configuration), and its fresh points carry their origin (`optim_init_origin`).
- **Compact refit (`tadah refit`, `REFIT_MODE compact`) fits in the declared form** (`CompactConfig::rho_form`, `BlipFit::form`). `NONNEG` fits the bump columns and `MONOTONE` the integrated-bump step columns (dS/dr = -eta B(x)/A, A = 1.5 Blip, 20/11 Blip5, with the matching Jacobians) with every coefficient >= 0: `solve_coeffs` by `models::nnls` with the variable-projection gradient on the passive set, REFINE and FUSE with Ceres lower bounds at 0. The table pre-checks of the dense path run on the compact fit window; an `INIT RHO` anchor with eta <= 0 is an error under `MONOTONE`; the handoff to `EAMRefit` checks the form and every amplitude (>= 0), centre and width. `SIGNED` fitter numerics are pinned to the previous fitter by goldens.
- **OPTIM keys are upper-cased when the line is read**, as configuration keys are case-insensitive: every rule and lookup keyed on them applies in any case -- the `WEIGHTS`, `EMBF0` and structure-key refusals, the `FIXWEIGHT` slot mapping through `FIXINDEX`, `BUMPGATE`, the neighbour-list cutoff (`nn_safe_rcutmax`), the density gates' arming -- and the labels of `params.tadah`, the journal and the sidecars are upper-case (`OPTIM ampgridmb (1) 0 1` + `OPTIM CGRIDMB (2) 3 4` give `AMPGRIDMB[1]`, `CGRIDMB[2]`). A lower-case key was searched as a key of its own and missed every such rule, and `OPTIM cgridmb` + `OPTIM CGRIDMB` wrote `CGRIDMB` twice into the candidate context. Lines of one key that select other indices still add to it; an index an earlier line already selects is an error at the second line, naming the first (a second box replaced the first without a word). Errors about an `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` index name the model weight, not its slot. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **OPTIM on `DIMER`, `FIXINDEX`, `MODEL` and the first `OALGO` value is refused at its line** with the structure-key message (`HPOTargets::optim_structure_key_error`, `optim_discrete_index_error`): the rigid-dimer layout of the data (its bond length included), which weights are pinned, the model and its integer option, the training algorithm. A searched value was truncated by `Context::get<int>` (`OALGO 2.7` ran GELS), broke `FIXINDEX` or moved the data's bond length. `OPTIM OALGO (2)`, the rcond M of options 1 and 3, is continuous and stays searchable. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`.
- **A radial bump width must be > 0 in every `RHOFORM`**: `SGRID2B` of a `D2_Blip`/`D2_Blip5` pair term and `SGRIDMB` of a DM_REAM `Blip`/`Blip5` density. An OPTIM box whose low bound on such a width is <= 0, explicit or AUTO, is an error at its line, by `models::optim_low_bound_rule(key, ctx)` (the message names what sets the rule: `the D2_Blip pair basis requires every SGRID2B width > 0 ...`); the `-c` potential's widths are checked at startup, naming each key, bump and value (`SGRID2B (2) = -1: ...`); a `tadah refit` `INIT PHI`/`INIT RHO` MANUAL anchor with eta <= 0 is an error naming its channel, place and value (it was fitted as its mirror and written, or failed with "zero entry in SGRIDMB (internal error)"; only MONOTONE refused it). A bump is even in eta, so a negative width evaluated as its mirror while the bump-support checks, which computed the support edge c + w/eta, skipped it. The skips are gone: BUMPGATE's startup checks read the widths as they are, its table and the compact fitter's projection and spill guard throw `std::logic_error` on a width <= 0, and the per-candidate measure counts a width <= 0 (only an optimiser that leaves the box evaluates one) as 1 - eta rcut / w, the continuation of the overhang measure through eta = 0, and a NaN width as the top of band D. The widths > 0 also remove the +inf outer edge a pinned eta = 0 anchor had in FUSE. The NONNEG check that accepted `OPTIM SGRIDMB (2) 0 4` on a Blip5 density now expects the refusal. Tests: `test_bump_width.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp`, `test_compact_fitter.cpp`.
- **A `RHOGATE`/`RHOBARGATE` that no OPTIM line arms is refused at its line**, naming the keys that would arm it (`Error on line 12: RHOGATE cannot act: no OPTIM line searches a key that changes the density or its window (AMPGRIDMB, CGRIDMB, SGRIDMB or RCUTMB of a DM_REAM many-body term, or a DENSITY parameter <ID>.DENSITY.<parameter> of a DM_Embed component), ...`); it replaces the "not armed per candidate" INFO line and the reused `-c` verdict. The unarmed paths are gone (`RhoGateTable`/`RhoBarGateTable::armed`, `watom_armed`, `fixed`; the startup error of an unarmed failing `-c` density; `density_gate_scans_for_test`). Tests: `test_rho_gates.cpp`, `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp`.
- **Compact refit: FUSE's `MAXEVAL` bounds the whole stage**: the held re-run passes (the fallback when the linear re-solve after the bump-support projection leaves max |dE|/tol above 1) share the Ceres iterations with the joint solve (`FuseBudget`, `run_held_passes`; `joint_refine` reports the iterations it used). Each pass gets what is left; with nothing left the held re-run is skipped and the re-solve result kept (`FUSE held re-run skipped: MAXEVAL <n> spent` in `refit_opt.log`), and a pass that spends the rest ends the passes. The passes had the full `MAXEVAL` each, up to n_phi + n_rho more solves. Tests: `test_compact_fitter.cpp`.
- **Compact refit under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve`: the FUSE refine of each function runs with FUSE's own `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL`** (`fuse_curve_config`), so curve results change where REFINE's or FUSE's stop keys were set. It ran with REFINE's: REFINE's `MAXEVAL` (an L-BFGS REFINE's evaluation budget, read as Ceres iterations), `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL` set it, also with REFINE `ENABLED false`, whose warning said the REFINE optimiser was ignored, and FUSE's own keys had no effect (FUSE `MAXEVAL 1` and `500` wrote byte-identical potentials). Each of phi, rho and F gets FUSE's whole `MAXEVAL` (in energy mode it bounds the whole stage, above), and each `FUSE[phi|rho|F]` line of `refit_opt.log` logs its Ceres term (`FUSE[phi] curve score 0.0304 -> 0.0287 (NO_CONVERGENCE(501it, maxiter))`). **FUSE reads `GTOL`**, in both modes (`CompactConfig::joint_gtol`, default 1e-14, the energy-mode joint solve's constant before, so default results do not change); it was refused, and before 1.6.0 accepted and ignored. Without these keys a curve run changes only where a FUSE refine reached REFINE's default 1000 iterations (FUSE's default is 2000; the tolerances' defaults are equal). REFINE `ALGO NONE` with a stop criterion is now an error in curve mode too (`REFINE: MAXITER has no effect with ALGO NONE (the stage is skipped)`), and an L-BFGS REFINE `MAXEVAL` no longer sets Ceres' iteration cap. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp` (the end-to-end harness moves to `compact_refit_harness.h`).
- **`tadah refit` refuses a key of the other `REFIT_MODE`, and compact mode without Ceres** (MLIP's mode rule, `check_refit_mode_keys`, in `RefitEngine::validate()` and `run()`, which `HPORefitEngine` inherits; see Tadah.MLIP): under `REFIT_MODE compact` the dense fit's keys (`REFIT_LEVEL`, `N2B`, `NRHO_BASIS`, `NF_BASIS`, `REFIT_MAX_ESCALATIONS`, `REFIT_PLACEMENT`, `REFIT_CONSTRAIN_F0`) and, under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy`, `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING`, `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` and `REFIT_CURVE_DERIV_WEIGHT` (which `compact_fit` reads under `curve` only), and under `curve` with `REFIT_CURVE_WEIGHTING false` `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX` (the weighting's floor), are errors before any fitting; under `REFIT_MODE dense` the compact keys and the `INIT`/`EXPLORE`/`REFINE`/`FUSE` blocks are, so a dense run no longer accepts a stage block that `RefitParser` would refuse. They were ignored. A top-level `OPTIMIZER` block, which neither mode reads, is refused in either mode before the table is read, with the migration text `RefitParser::parse` gives it, now MLIP's `top_level_optimizer_refusal()`, which the parser takes; `validate()` accepted the block, and `run()` refused it in the parser after reading the table (dense mode ignored it). `HPORefitEngine::has_compact_fitter()` is true in a build with Ceres; without Ceres it is false, so `REFIT_MODE compact`, written or the default, is refused before the table is read, where `compact_fit()` returned false and the base fell back to the dense fit with a WARNING (that `compact_fit()` now throws `std::logic_error`). `compact_fit()` fits the table or throws: an `r_reach` below the table's `dr`, which the crystal gate of `run()` refuses, is an internal error, not a fallback to the dense fit (the entry at the top of *Changed*). `refit_curve_objective` is MLIP's `read_refit_curve_objective` (it inserted the blueprint default `energy` into the configuration). The migration text for a top-level `OPTIMIZER` block names the Refitting page (*Stage blocks*) for examples; it named `HPO_REFIT_2B_EAM/REDESIGN/configs`, which is not shipped. After a compact fit the F row of `refit_range_report.dat` says that `refit.eam` carries the original table above the fit window; it described the dense fit's uniform tail basis (MLIP). Its phi and rho rows' `fit_window_lo` is the compact fit's window, the `rwin_lo` that `compact_fit` hands to `EAMRefit::finalize_injected` (Tadah.MODELS: `fit_window_r_lo()`), and so are the curve files' `in_window` flags; it was 0.9 r_reach (MLIP). `tadah refit --edit` and `--retarget` refuse the keys of a refit of an EAM file, `HPORefitEngine` inheriting MLIP's rule. Without Ceres the `compact_fitter.h` header no longer says that the engine falls back to dense mode. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`, `test_compact_refit_rhoform.cpp` (the compact RHOFORM x `REFIT_BASIS` cases, moved from MLIP's tests, which ran them through the dense fallback), `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` (the migration text; a negative `FTOL_ABS`, refused already, now tested).
- **Compact refit: `REFIT_COMPACT_MERGE`, `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` and `REFIT_COMPACT_INSERT_TOPK` are refused under `REFIT_BASIS Blip` or `Blip5`** (MLIP's mode rule, which `HPORefitEngine` inherits; see Tadah.MLIP), before the table is read: the compact fitter applies them to the knot families only (`merge_close_knots` and the anti-coalescence factor, `opt_every`, `insert_topk`), and they were accepted with no effect. `compact_fit` sets `CompactConfig::merge_guard` for the knot families only, so the `schedule:` line of `refit_opt.log` says `merge_guard=off` for a Blip fit, which never applies the guard (under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy` it said `merge_guard=on`, the value of `auto`); the fits are unchanged. Tests: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`.
- **BREAKING for bounded gradient searches — no finite-difference point is evaluated outside the OPTIM box, with either step rule, CENTRAL or FORWARD, in NUMERICAL and HYBRID** (`fd_stencil.h`, `src/fd_stencil.cpp`, `gradient_provider.h`). Both step rules of `PARAM fd_kernel` -- `inhouse`, h = fd_step max(|u|, 1), and Ceres' rule, max(2^-26, |u| fd_step), which Tadah now evaluates itself (`fd_plan`, `fd_combine`: Ceres' `DynamicNumericDiffCostFunction` bit for bit inside the box, its points, order and arithmetic; it stays the default of `NUMERICAL` under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, and the Ceres library is off the finite-difference path, so `fd_kernel ceres` needs no `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES`) -- take the central difference inside the box and, within a step of a bound, the one-sided difference into it, (-3 f(u) + 4 f(u+s) - f(u+2s))/2s, second order (error -h^2 f'''/3 against h^2 f'''/6; noise 2.55 sigma/h against 0.71 sigma/h) at the same cost for `NUMERICAL` (1 + 2n evaluations: it evaluates the point itself first). `diff_method 1` (FORWARD) steps backward at the upper bound. A point outside the box -- only `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` proposes one (`LN_NEWUOA`, which ignored the bounds too, is refused: the BREAKING `LN_NEWUOA` entry above) -- is not differenced: it is not evaluated, and its gradient is the slope of its score (the BREAKING entry on points outside the OPTIM box, above). A coordinate proposed outside its box by no more than rounding is evaluated on the bound (`HPO_Host::snap_onto_box`, `SearchSpace::snapped_onto_box`): within `kSnapUlps` = 8 units in the last place of the bound, in real coordinates (of max(|lo|, |hi|) on a linear dimension, of the bound itself on a `LOG` or `ASINH` one) or in the optimiser's, recorded on the bound and counted in DIAGNOSTICS (`Snapped onto the box`) with no WARNING. Under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` NLopt's own arithmetic rounds points on a bound up to 2 ulps of the box's scale past it (`LN_COBYLA`, `LN_PRAXIS`, `LN_BOBYQA`, the unscaled DIRECT variants, `MLSL` and `AUGLAG` with such a local search: a survey of every NLopt algorithm, about 900k evaluations; none in the unit box of `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`), and these points were evaluated outside the box and warned about.
