The step is never shrunk: a box in which some point has no stencil is refused at startup (`validate_hpo_setup`, every phase that takes finite differences: `takes_fd_gradients`) and by the gradient before it evaluates anything, naming the dimension, a point without a stencil, the step's source (`PARAM`, the default, or the `NOISE` block's `AUTO`: `OptimizerSpec::fd_step_from_noise_auto`), the largest fd_step that fits (`fd_fit`, `fd_largest_fitting_step`, on the steps as computed, and sound; under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` fd_step <= 1/3 for CENTRAL and 1/2 for FORWARD with the in-house rule, 1/2 and 1 with Ceres' rule, exactly; under `NONE` over the whole box, bounding the computed step exactly for a power-of-two fd_step, else within an ulp of it, but with u +- h taken as exact reals, so that in a box only hundreds or thousands of ulps wide it can refuse a step every point of the box accepts, by up to a few per cent) and `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO` or a `LOG` box as alternatives. `HYBRID` takes the same stencil with its own `fd_kernel` (default `inhouse` under either scale, as before) and `diff_method`, and the loss the optimiser has just evaluated at the point as the centre of a one-sided or forward difference (`GradientProvider::gradient_at`: the NLopt objective, Ceres' `FirstOrderFunction`, and DLIB:BFGS, which asks for a gradient at its start, right after the loss there, and after each line search at the point the search ended on, the last point it evaluated; its line search evaluates the loss alone) -- a gradient asked for without that loss (`gradient()`, which no backend calls) evaluates it first, once per gradient; its kind decides the differences (the BREAKING entry on differences of one kind, above). Startup refusals: a box too narrow for the step; `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS); an invalid `fd_step`, `fd_kernel` or `diff_method`, with the same message on every backend (a number for `fd_kernel` too: `PARAM fd_kernel must be inhouse or ceres (got 1)`); these PARAMs where they never take effect -- an INNER block, `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL`, a derivative-free optimiser (they were accepted and ignored); the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` where the STRATEGY-level phase measures no slopes -- a derivative-free optimiser, ASA (its own probes), `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` written or resolved -- with `the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LN_SBPLX: NOISE AUTO has no effect: NLOPT:LN_SBPLX measures no slopes; remove AUTO from the NOISE block.` (a derivative-free run printed an `ignored` note on stderr), while it now derives `fd_step`, with its journal line, wherever that phase takes finite differences, an `MLSL` or `AUGLAG` whose local search takes gradients included, which it left without; the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` beside a `PARAM fd_step` written in the block, on the phase concerned (`NOISE AUTO has no effect: PARAM fd_step 0.0001 is set; remove one of them.`; the written step was taken, with a note on stderr), and a box too narrow for the derived step advises removing `AUTO` and setting `PARAM fd_step`; ASA settings whose tangent or curvature probes, as ASA computes them, cannot fit a box (`asa_probe_box_error`, also when ASA starts). The bundled ASA's exit curvature evaluates the points it tests (TADAH-PATCH 8): where both x +- Delta |x| lie in the box it evaluated (1 +- Delta) x, which can round an ulp beyond a bound at a point or two of about one box in six, whatever the Delta (`Curvature_0` 0 or -1 only; the default computes no curvature, and the golden gate is unchanged). `DLIB:BFGS` accepts `diff_method`. `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` gets one startup WARNING per run (it came at parse time, at startup and at each phase's start) saying that a point it proposes outside the box is not evaluated, is scored by its distance to the box and is never the best. The journal: an OPTIMISER line per phase that takes finite differences (`Finite differences : CENTRAL, h = 1e-06 of each box width (default fd_step; in-house rule); next to a bound: one-sided 3-point into the box, same h, same cost`), an INFO line at the first one-sided difference of each dimension, and DIAGNOSTICS counts printed only when not zero (`Snapped onto the box`, `Out-of-box points`, `One-sided differences` and `Mixed-kind stencils` per dimension). Runs whose gradients never come within a step of a bound are unchanged bit for bit, with either rule and with HYBRID (they gain the OPTIMISER line), and so are derivative-free, ANALYTICAL, annealing, ASA, GRID and RANDOM runs -- except where a run evaluates an end of a `LOG` or `ASINH` box (a `GRID` over one always does) or, rarely, of a `LIN` box under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`: that end is now exactly the `OPTIM` bound, where it could come out an ulp outside (*Fixed*); and where an NLopt search under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` proposes a point within rounding outside the box, now evaluated on the bound (*Fixed*). A search whose gradient reaches a bound, a NONNEG/MONOTONE run at a zero amplitude, a CERES run whose iterates leave the box, a LOG/ASINH run at a bound (ulp level, *Fixed*), a run with a formerly ignored `diff_method` or `fd_kernel`, a HYBRID run without an analytical derivative (*Fixed*), and an NLopt run under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` that rounded a point past a bound (evaluated on it now, *Fixed*) can take a different path. Tests: `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (either rule and scheme under AUTO and NONE on LIN and LOG boxes, the NONNEG slope, bit-for-bit guards against Ceres and against goldens of the d5457d0 provider, cost, budget and failures at a bound, HYBRID, the journal), `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_search_space.cpp`, `test_asa.cpp`, `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp`, `test_scale_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (every gradient backend from the upper bound of the straddle box; `LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 spends exactly `MAXEVAL`, 60: the NLopt `MAXEVAL` entry below).
- **BREAKING — `NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND` is refused** in every phase (the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, `MULTISTART`, the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, an `INNER` block), at `ENDOPTIMIZER` for the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser (whether the `STRATEGY` block, a `REFINE` block or the `OPTIMIZER` block itself names it), at `ENDEXPLORE` and at `ENDINNER`, with the phase and the replacements: `Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: NLOPT:LN_NEWUOA_BOUND is refused: NLopt 2.10 plans each LN_NEWUOA_BOUND step with a nested LD_MMA run that has no time limit and sees no stop request. Once a non-finite value reaches its model, each step takes about 10-60 s without evaluating the loss. ... Ctrl-C, SIGTERM, MAXTIME and RESTART wait that long. The recommended replacement is NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA, Powell's bound-constrained successor of NEWUOA (its STEP rescales each dimension instead of setting one radius)` (`LN_NEWUOA` is refused too: the BREAKING `LN_NEWUOA` entry above). It was accepted, and a run stalled once a non-finite value reached its model: the nested MMA subproblem then ran to its caps, about 10^8 model evaluations without an objective call, and nothing HPO does can interrupt it. Above all, every stop HPO makes through the objective -- a signal, a `RESTART` stagnation stop, an error that ends the run -- returns `HUGE_VAL`, NLopt's own convention for a forced stop (HPO keeps it), and from a run's (2n+1)-th evaluation on, the last of NEWUOA's starting points for n dimensions, it cost one such subproblem on any loss (a stop on one of the first 2n returned at once): with `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION 20` each stagnation stop was followed by 20 s without an evaluation, and Ctrl-C waited about 10 s. A flat loss without `XTOL` (300 evaluations in 5 s, then 20 in 590 s; `MAXTIME 20` ended after 31 s) and the approach to a minimum (with `XTOL_REL 1e-12` too) overflowed the model as well. `LN_BOBYQA` and `LN_NEWUOA` return at once after the same stop. The refusal is a table in the caps registry (`backend_refusal()`, `reject_refused_backend()`, `optimizer_caps.h`): the algorithm is not registered, so no list of alternatives offers it (the `MULTISTART` alternatives count one fewer), `optimizer_caps()` throws `LIB:ALGO is refused: <reason>`, `require_driver()`, `require_inner_driver()` and `require_inner_backend()` throw it with the phase (so `run_inner()` refuses a spec built at run time too), `validate_phase_backends()` reports it first, for its own phase and with `validate_run_policy()`'s text, in every `STRATEGY` shape (`reject_refused_backends()`, `strategy_rules.h`: a `MULTISTART` from `INIT` points and a `PRESTAGE` refinement of `TOPK` > 1 included, whose phases need the caps of their backend), and the `ALGO` help prints `REFUSED`. A study moved to `LN_BOBYQA` takes another trajectory: `STEP` rescales each dimension instead of giving one radius, BOBYQA moves a start closer to a bound than its step, and `STEP 0.5` is capped just below. The analysis and an NLopt-level repro, to re-run before an NLopt upgrade lifts the refusal: `docs/issues/nlopt_newuoa_bound_stall.md`.
- `BUMPGATE` rejects go through the shared pre-training reject path and the per-gate counters (`GateKind`, `HPOLog::record_gate_reject`; `record_bump_gate_reject` kept as a wrapper); its line, counter, numbering and loss are byte-identical.
- **BREAKING for NLopt gradient searches — NLopt's `MAXEVAL` counts every evaluation of its search**, as `DLIB:BFGS` and the CERES driver count: each loss, each finite-difference point and each analytical gradient (`ANALYTICAL`, `HYBRID`; one evaluation each), in one budget (`nlopt_adapter.h`, `run_nlopt_impl`: the objective counts its loss in the `GradientProvider`'s counter, refuses a call once the budget is spent without evaluating, and stops the search). An NLopt search makes at most its `MAXEVAL` evaluations, and a budget that ends a search ends it `MAXEVAL_REACHED` (a run can make more, as before: each `RESTART` launch has its own `MAXEVAL`, each `MULTISTART` start its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL` (ceil division), the `PRESTAGE` exploration its `EXPLORE` block's and each refinement its share, and the `NOISE` block's `CALIBRATION` repeats and `ANNEAL_PT`'s `INIT_JITTER AUTO` calibration are not charged to any search); the journal and stdout say `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 60 of MAXEVAL 60 evaluation(s)` for every `MAXEVAL` ending (a derivative-free search that NLopt's own count ends wrote `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted  fmin=<best>`). NLopt counted its objective calls, each a loss and a whole gradient, while the provider kept a budget of its own for the gradients' evaluations: once that was spent every gradient was refused (`GradientFailure::Budget`) and the objective returned `+inf` for points it had evaluated (a run's best could be a loss NLopt had been told was `+inf`). On Rosenbrock's valley in 2-D at `MAXEVAL 60` (`tests/test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`) `LD_LBFGS` made 94 evaluations, `LD_TNEWTON` 99, `LD_SLSQP`, `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and `GD_STOGO` 120 and `LD_AUGLAG` 121; `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR2` and `AUGLAG`/`G_MLSL_LDS` with an `INNER` `LD_LBFGS` ended `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged ...)`, `GD_STOGO` `SUCCESS -- generic success`, and `MAXEVAL 1` made 2 evaluations and ended `SUCCESS`. Derivative-free searches that passed NLopt's own `MAXEVAL` stop at it too: `GN_CRS2_LM` (61-64 for 60: it tests `MAXEVAL` only after a trial that does not beat its worst point), `GN_ORIG_DIRECT` (75) and `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L` (71; once per iteration), and `AUGLAG` with any local search (61: it evaluates the point its local search returned once more). NLopt keeps its own `MAXEVAL`, which never ends a search before the budget (it can when calls answered from the run's record, which NLopt counts among its calls, are no evaluations: the *Changed* entry on points a search asks for again) and is what ends `GD_STOGO`, which ignores a forced stop (the objective refuses its later calls without evaluating; `GD_STOGO` needs `MAXEVAL`, below). The same `MAXEVAL` gives an NLopt gradient search fewer steps, and it can end at a worse loss: raise `MAXEVAL` to keep the old totals; an NLopt `ANALYTICAL` run gets about half as many losses (`LD_LBFGS` at `MAXEVAL 30`: 15 losses and 15 analytical gradients; 28 and 28 before). An `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL` keeps NLopt's count, the objective calls of each local search, which HPO cannot tell apart: the journal's `Inner stop criteria` say so for a gradient-based `INNER` search (`hpo_log.cpp`). The masked-diagnostic warning gives the evaluations made (`... was set after N evaluation(s)`), not NLopt's count of calls. Tests: `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`; `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`LD_MMA` 120 and `LD_LBFGS` 101 evaluations → 60, `LD_SLSQP` from 1.8 120 → 60, `GD_STOGO` 120 and `G_MLSL_LDS` 104 → 60, each `MAXEVAL_REACHED`).
- **A finite-difference gradient takes the loss its caller has just evaluated at its point as its centre** (`GradientProvider::gradient_at`, `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` as `HYBRID` already did): the NLopt objective, Ceres' `FirstOrderFunction` and `DLIB:BFGS` (when the gradient's point is the last one its line search evaluated) evaluate the loss and then ask for the gradient there, and `NUMERICAL` evaluated the point a second time first — one evaluation per gradient, counted against `MAXEVAL` (when every call takes a gradient, a quarter of the evaluations of a 1-D search, a sixth in 2-D). A gradient now costs 2n evaluations (CENTRAL, and one-sided within a step of a bound) or n (FORWARD) after the loss; `gradient(x, g)`, without the caller's loss, still evaluates x first. Central differences are bit for bit the same; one-sided and forward ones use the loss the optimiser was given (under `--hotvar` a second evaluation of the same point can differ). A `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES or NLopt gradient search does more with the same `MAXEVAL`, and its path can change. Tests: `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp` (`gradient_at` evaluates 2n points and never x; NLopt and Ceres evaluate no point twice in a row); `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (the straddle cases: no point evaluated twice in a row).
- **`GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND` need `MAXEVAL`** (`OptimizerCaps::ignores_forced_stop`, `require_stop`; in its own block: an `INNER` block does not take StoGO, a global search, see the no-nested-global entry at the top of *Changed*): NLopt's StoGO tests only NLopt's evaluation count and clock, never the forced stop by which HPO ends a search (on a signal, an error, the `RESTART` stagnation stop, a spent budget, or a loss at or below `STOPVAL` that NLopt was not given). Once HPO has stopped such a search, StoGO calls on and the objective refuses each call without evaluating it, a few microseconds each, until NLopt's own `MAXEVAL` ends it. Without `MAXEVAL`, once a host exception, a signal or the stagnation stop had stopped it, a run with `STOPVAL` or a `RESTART` block as its only stop never ended (measured with a host exception), and one with `MAXTIME` waited out the rest of `MAXTIME` at 100% CPU before it reported; the withheld-`STOPVAL` rule of this release (*Fixed*) stops StoGO the same way (`MAXTIME 4`, `STOPVAL 2`, a withheld loss at `STOPVAL` after 5 evaluations: 4 s with no evaluation). `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or a `RESTART` block was accepted as its stop and is now a startup error, `NLOPT:GD_STOGO needs a stop: NLopt's StoGO has no convergence test of its own and ignores NLopt's forced stop, ...: it then calls on, each call refused, and only NLopt's own evaluation count ends it promptly (MAXTIME only once the rest of its time has passed); set MAXEVAL (it honours no tolerance)`; the `ALGO` help says `MAXEVAL is required (MAXTIME, STOPVAL and a RESTART block are not enough)`. Tests: `test_phase_specs.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp` (GD_STOGO with `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL` is refused before it evaluates; a withheld loss at `STOPVAL` ends a `GD_STOGO` search through NLopt's own `MAXEVAL`, in well under its `MAXTIME`).
- **The `DUMP` and `BDUMP` archive directories are checked when the target file is read and created right before the first evaluation**, once `best_pot.tadah` is prepared (its lock taken), not when the target file is parsed (`HPOTargets::check_archive_directories()` once the file is read, each error at its line; `HPOTargets::create_archive_directories()`, which `hpo_run()` calls after `HPO_Host::prepare_best_publication()` on every rank, an error on one rank being every rank's). A directory the run cannot create or write into is refused at its line, by the run and by `tadah hpo --check` alike, before any data is loaded: one in a directory that does not exist (`Error on line 10: DUMP directory 'nodir/pots' cannot be created: the directory '<dir>/nodir' does not exist (tadah hpo creates only the last directory of the path)`), in a file or in a directory without write permission, and a path that is a file, a directory without write permission or a symbolic link to no directory, `mkdir` creating no directory through a link (`... is a symbolic link to 'nowhere', which does not exist`; a trailing `/` or `/.` changes nothing to this). A trailing `/` or `/.` is taken as `mkdir` takes it: `pots/` is the directory `pots`, and `pots/.` is refused while `pots` does not exist and the other line does not create it (`... cannot be resolved: it walks '.' in '<dir>/pots', which does not exist`); the other line's directory, which the run creates first, is taken as a directory that exists (`DUMP 1 archive` with `BDUMP 1 archive/.` or `BDUMP 1 archive/./best`, in either order, is accepted). A directory on the way that the run cannot read is reported as such (`... cannot be resolved: '<dir>/locked/pots' cannot be read: Permission denied`). One directory inside the directory of the other line, which the run creates first (`DUMP 1 archive` with `BDUMP 1 archive/best`, or `BDUMP 1 archive` with `DUMP 1 archive/all`) is accepted whatever the order of the lines; the two paths are compared where the operating system puts them, symbolic links followed and `..` taken from the directory reached (`DUMP 1 ../archive` with `BDUMP 1 ../archive/best` is accepted; `BDUMP 1 archive/x/../best` is refused while `archive/x` does not exist, `... cannot be resolved: it walks '..' out of '<dir>/archive/x', which does not exist`), and the run creates the outer one first, also when one line reaches it through a symbolic link. A run that stops before its first evaluation, and `tadah hpo --check`, create none. In 1.5.0 the parse created each directory at its line: it accepted a `BDUMP` directory inside a `DUMP` directory that did not exist yet only after the `DUMP` line, stopped at a directory it could not create, and accepted a file or a directory without write permission, where no archive could be written. `HPO_Host` creates no directory: a caller of it creates them before the first evaluation. Tests: `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` (*Strict numbers: DUMP and BDUMP take a cadence and a directory*, *DUMP and BDUMP refuse a directory the run cannot create, at their line, and create nothing*, *DUMP and BDUMP refuse a directory the run cannot write into*, *DUMP and BDUMP: a directory inside the other's is accepted in either order and created after it*), `test_hpo_check.cpp`.
- **`tadah hpo` stops at startup when it cannot write its output files into the working directory**, before any data is loaded, and so does `tadah hpo --check`, with one message. Before the run opens (and truncates) its journal, `HPOTargets::check_run_outputs()` checks the working directory (`the working directory '<dir>' is not writable: tadah hpo writes its journal (hpo_run.log), its result files and best_pot.tadah there`) and the files the run writes there. A regular file or a device that exists is opened as the run opens it, without creating or truncating it, without waiting and without taking a controlling terminal, and closed (`/dev/null` is accepted); a socket is refused, as the run's open of it fails (`... cannot be opened: No such device or address (...)`); a named pipe is not opened (that would end the stream of its reader) but checked for permission, and is accepted as the journal or a search-space file, which the run opens once and whose reader it waits for, but refused as a result file, which the run reopens for each row (`'loss.tadah' ... is a named pipe: tadah hpo reopens the file for each row it writes, and a pipe would block it`: without the check the run would wait for ever after the header). The files are opened so: the journal and the result files to write, the lock of each best potential to read and write; the refusal is the open's own error (`'loss.tadah' in the working directory '<dir>' cannot be opened: Permission denied (tadah hpo rewrites it)`, `'best_pot.tadah.r1.lock' ... cannot be opened: Permission denied (tadah hpo opens it to lock best_pot.tadah.r1)`, `... cannot be opened: Too many levels of symbolic links (...)`), so every link is followed as the run follows it. One that does not exist is checked where the operating system would create it, every link followed and `..` taken from the directory reached: a name that ends in `/` or `/.` is no file name, and the file must be in a directory that exists and the run can write into, as the run creates the file and no directory (`'loss.tadah' in the working directory '<dir>' is a symbolic link to '<dir>/nowhere/loss', which cannot be created: the directory '<dir>/nowhere' does not exist`). `best_pot.tadah`, which the run replaces, is checked by its own name: it may be read-only, and a symbolic link there is replaced by the file and not followed, but a directory of that name stops the run (`... is a directory: tadah hpo writes a file of that name`). Once the target file is read, `HPOTargets::check_search_space_files()` checks `hpo_search_space.tsv` and, when a box comes from an `AUTO` rule (the only case in which the run writes it), `hpo_optim_resolved.hpo`, as the result files; the run records that error in its journal. On several MPI ranks each rank checks its own rank-suffixed files, and every rank stops, with the failing rank's message there and `the check of the output files failed on another MPI rank` on the others; the creation of the `DUMP` and `BDUMP` directories is agreed the same way. In 1.5.0 such a run started regardless: without its journal (`[HPOLog] Cannot open log file: hpo_run.log`), it stopped at the lock of `best_pot.tadah` when the working directory could not be written, and it lost the rows of a result file it could not write. Tests: `test_hpo_check.cpp` (*hpo --check: a working directory the run cannot write into stops the check and the run alike*, *hpo --check: an output file the run cannot write stops the check and the run alike, before the journal*, *hpo --check: best_pot.tadah as a directory and the symbolic links of the output files*, *hpo --check: hpo_optim_resolved.hpo is checked only when a box comes from an AUTO rule*, *hpo --check: the ranks agree on a startup step one rank fails*), `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` (*DUMP and BDUMP: a directory inside the other's is accepted in either order and created after it*), and, in the MPI build, `hpo_mpi_e2e_output_refusal` (on 2 ranks, a read-only `loss.tadah.r1` or `best_pot.tadah.r1.lock` stops every rank at once, with rank 1's message, `the check of the output files failed on another MPI rank` on rank 0, and no journal).
- The help of the annealing `PARAM` keys (the annealing reference pages, and `tadah explain` for `TADAH:ANNEAL`, `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` and `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA`) says what each key does:
  - `NEPS`: `ANNEAL` compares the loss at the end of a temperature step with the `NEPS` earlier ones and with the best loss; `ANNEAL_PA` applies that rule to the lowest loss of its population; `ANNEAL_PT` counts the diagnostics windows in a row without a significant improvement of the best loss.
  - `INIT_JITTER_MAXEVAL`: its `AUTO` value includes the `INIT_JITTER_AUDIT` probes.
  - `INIT_MODE` and the `INIT_JITTER_*` calibration keys: with `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO`, `ANNEAL_PA` uses the initial proposal scales as uniform bounds, without calibration and without a tolerance; the calibration keys are those of `ANNEAL_PT` (the BREAKING entry below).
  - `CORANA`, `RT`, `LADDER_SPAN` and `INIT_JITTER_LADDER` say what each of their values selects.
- **BREAKING — `STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART` needs `N_STARTS` 2 or more.** A `MULTISTART` block without `N_STARTS` is refused when it ends, at its `TYPE` line (`STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART needs N_STARTS 2 or more: without N_STARTS it runs one start, which is STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN`), and `N_STARTS 1` at its line (`N_STARTS must be an integer >= 2, got '1' (one start is STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN)`). 1.5.0 ran such a block as one start.
- **BREAKING — `STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART` refuses `DLIB:GFS`**, an optimiser that takes every `INIT` point in one run, at startup (`require_multistart_backend`): `STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART cannot run DLIB:GFS: it takes every INIT point in one run, so MULTISTART would run it once, whatever N_STARTS; STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN runs DLIB:GFS once from every INIT point.` 1.5.0 ran it once from every point and ignored `N_STARTS` and `BUDGET_PER_START`. A `PRESTAGE` refinement with `DLIB:GFS` takes every candidate of the exploration in its one run, as before. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — a `RESTART` block needs `AFTER_STAGNATION` and `MAX`, each 1 or more.** A value of 0 is refused at its line (`AFTER_STAGNATION must be an integer >= 1, got '0' (0 never restarts the search; a search without restarts has no RESTART block)`), and a block without one of the two when the `STRATEGY` block ends, at the last `ENDRESTART` line (`the RESTART block never restarts the search: a restart needs AFTER_STAGNATION and MAX, and the block has no MAX line; ...`). 1.5.0 accepted such a block, which restarted nothing, without a message.
- **BREAKING — `THREADS` in an `INNER` block, or in an `EXPLORE` block whose optimiser does not read it, is refused at startup.** The `THREADS` of the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser sets the workers of the run; `DLIB:GFS`, `ASA:ASA`, `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` and `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` also read the `THREADS` of their `EXPLORE` block (`NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA does not use THREADS in an INNER block: the THREADS of the STRATEGY-level optimiser sets the workers of the run; remove it`). 1.5.0 accepted both and they had no effect.
- **BREAKING — an NLopt algorithm takes the `PARAM` keys NLopt reads for it**, and, when it takes finite-difference gradients, the finite-difference keys: `tolg` for `LD_LBFGS`, the `LD_VAR` and `LD_TNEWTON` algorithms, `magic_eps` for the `GN_DIRECT` algorithms (and `magic_eps_abs`, `sigma_reltol` and `fglobal_reltol` for `GN_ORIG_DIRECT` and `GN_ORIG_DIRECT_L`), `t0_tol` for `LN_PRAXIS`, the settings of the inner and dual problems for `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ`. Any other name is refused at startup, naming the keys it takes (`NLOPT:LN_SBPLX: unknown PARAM 'magic_eps' (it takes none)`, `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: unknown PARAM 'fd_stp' (it takes tolg, fd_step, fd_kernel and diff_method)`); `tadah explain NLOPT:<ALGO>` (`the PARAM keys NLopt reads for it: tolg`) and the optimiser list name them. 1.5.0 passed any name to NLopt, which ignored a name the algorithm does not read.
- **BREAKING — a word given to a `PARAM` key of NLopt, `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES or `ASA:ASA` is refused at startup** (`NLOPT:LD_LBFGS: PARAM tolg takes a number (got 'tight')`; a word under a name the optimiser does not take gets the unknown-key message). 1.5.0 accepted it, and no optimiser read it. The finite-difference keys keep their own words (`fd_kernel inhouse`).
- **BREAKING — `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_NOSCAL` and `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L_RAND_NOSCAL` are refused**, in every phase, with the reason and the replacements: NLopt 2.10 runs both as `GN_DIRECT_L_NOSCAL`, the locally biased DIRECT-L, so `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT`, `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` and `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L_NOSCAL` are named.
- **BREAKING — `VECTOR_ARR` is refused for `NLOPT:LD_TNEWTON` and `NLOPT:LD_TNEWTON_RESTART`** (`NLOPT:LD_TNEWTON does not use VECTOR_ARR; remove it (it is read by NLOPT:LD_LBFGS, NLOPT:LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND, ...)`): of the truncated-Newton algorithms, only the `LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND` forms read it. 1.5.0 accepted it with no effect.
- **BREAKING — the `ASA:ASA` options `Include_Integer_Parameters` and `User_Initial_Parameters` are refused**, each with its reason: every `OPTIM` dimension takes real values, so ASA has no integer dimension to include; ASA starts at the first starting point of the `INIT` block, and `METHOD RANDOM 1` in the `INIT` block draws a random start from its `SEED`. 1.5.0 accepted `Include_Integer_Parameters`, which had no effect, and `User_Initial_Parameters 1`, its default, and refused `User_Initial_Parameters 0` beside the starting points of the run.
- **BREAKING — `DLIB:GFS` refuses `SEED`**, as `TADAH:GRID` does: `find_min_global` draws no random numbers (`DLIB:GFS does not use SEED: it draws no random numbers`), in the `STRATEGY` and `EXPLORE` blocks alike. The `SEED` of a `PRESTAGE` refinement that the exploration takes is accepted (the `PRESTAGE` entry under *Fixed*); one it does not take is refused with the reason, for every refinement that reads no `SEED` (`the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not use SEED: it draws no random numbers, and the exploration (TADAH:GRID) reads none`, or `... has a SEED of its own ...`). 1.5.0 accepted it and warned when the search started.
- **BREAKING — an NLopt algorithm that draws no random numbers refuses `SEED`**, as `DLIB:GFS` and `TADAH:GRID` do, at startup (`NLOPT:LN_SBPLX does not use SEED: it draws no random numbers`; `OptimizerCaps::reads_seed`, `reads_seed()`, `phase_specs.h`). The NLopt algorithms that draw random numbers read it: `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, `GN_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL`, `G_MLSL`, `LN_PRAXIS`, `GD_STOGO_RAND` and `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND`, the last in two `OPTIM` dimensions or more (in one it draws none: `NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L_RAND does not use SEED: in one OPTIM dimension it draws no random numbers (it draws them only to choose between equally long sides of a box)`). An `MLSL_LDS` or `AUGLAG` algorithm reads it when its local search draws random numbers (an `INNER` block with `LN_PRAXIS`). The `ALGO` help of an NLopt algorithm says whether it reads `SEED`, and so does the optimiser list (`optimizers_gen.toml`, its field `seed`). 1.5.0 accepted `SEED` for every NLopt algorithm, where it changed nothing for those that draw no random numbers. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_optimizer_help.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — the `best_*` histories head their first column `#IMPROVEMENT` and give the evaluation of each best in a second column, `EVAL`** (`best_loss.tadah`, `best_params.tadah`, `best_outvar.tadah`, `best_stats.tadah`; `HPO_Host::write_header_head`, `write_row_head`): `#IMPROVEMENT` numbers the improvements of the run from 1 (a row for each that `BOUTPUT` writes), and `EVAL` gives the evaluation that each is, the number `#ITERATION` gives that evaluation in `loss.tadah` and the other histories, which have its row when `OUTPUT` writes it (every evaluation under `OUTPUT 1`). The header of `best_pot.tadah` names both (`#  Evaluation 7, improvement 4  |  Loss: 1.2500e+00`), and so does that of a `BDUMP` archive (`# Tadah HPO -- improvement 4 (eval 7)  |  loss=...`). 1.5.0 headed the first column of every history `#ITERATION`, the improvement number in the `best_*` ones, which gave no evaluation, and wrote `Best iter 4  (eval 7)` and `best iter 4  (eval 7)`. Every column after the first moves one place to the right. Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`, `test_pot_header_search_space.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **BREAKING — `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` refuses the `INIT_JITTER_*` keys of the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration of `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT`** (`INIT_JITTER_MAX`, `_PROBES`, `_MAXEVAL`, `_SAMPLE`, `_AUDIT`, `_MAXTIME_FRAC`: `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA: PARAM INIT_JITTER_MAX is a key of TADAH:ANNEAL_PT`), **and the tolerance of `JITTER AUTO`**, which it does not read: `PARAM INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` takes no number under `ANNEAL_PA` (`PARAM INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO takes no tolerance (got '0.05'): TADAH:ANNEAL_PA measures no displacement and takes the first proposal scales as the bounds; ...`). 1.5.0 accepted the keys and the tolerance with no effect, and refused `JITTER AUTO` without a tolerance. `TADAH:ANNEAL` names `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` for these keys.
- **`K_TOTAL` caps the starting points of the `INIT` blocks of its optimiser (or of the `RESTART` blocks), a single `METHOD` line included**: the `INIT` blocks inside `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE` make one chain, those inside `EXPLORE` another and the `RESTART` blocks a third, and each chain keeps the first `K_TOTAL` points of its `METHOD` lines. 1.5.0 drew `K_TOTAL` points for a single `RANDOM`, `LHS` or `SOBOL` line in place of its `K`. **BREAKING:** a `K_TOTAL` that caps nothing, at or above the points the `METHOD` lines give (a `WARM` line counts as one), is refused when the file is read (`K_TOTAL 7 caps nothing: the METHOD lines of the INIT blocks inside OPTIMIZER, STRATEGY and REFINE give at most 3 starting points (RANDOM 3), and K_TOTAL keeps the first K_TOTAL starting points`), and so are `K_TOTAL 0` and `METHOD RANDOM 0`, as `LHS 0` and `SOBOL 0` are. An `INIT` block inside `EXPLORE` without a `METHOD` line gives the exploration the starting points of the `INIT` blocks inside `OPTIMIZER`, `STRATEGY` and `REFINE`, which its `K_TOTAL` does not cap, so a `K_TOTAL` there is refused, saying so; 1.5.0 accepted it with no effect.
- **BREAKING for `DLIB:BFGS` with `PARAM tol_gradinf` — the search ends once the largest component of the gradient falls below it**, as CERES tests its `tol_gradinf`; the stop line says so (`tol_gradinf reached after 5 evaluation(s): the largest component of the gradient fell below PARAM tol_gradinf = 4.5`). 1.5.0 tested the L2 norm of the gradient, which for n search parameters is up to sqrt(n) times the largest component, so a value ended a `DLIB:BFGS` search later than a CERES search.
- **`NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA` and `NLOPT:LN_COBYLA` keep the full first step of every search parameter, also next to a bound.** Both keep that step as the scale of the search parameter for the whole search; with `STEP` unset it is 0.25 of the width of its box. `LN_BOBYQA` moves a start within a step of a bound by at most half a step, and `LN_COBYLA` keeps its start. 1.5.0 shrank the step of a search parameter whose start lay closer to a bound than one step to 0.75 of that distance (`LN_BOBYQA`, and `LN_COBYLA` with `STEP` unset), which froze it. **The results of these searches from a start near a bound change**: from `RCUT2B 4.005` in `[4, 6]`, `LN_BOBYQA` now goes beyond 4.1 within 30 evaluations, where 1.5.0 stayed next to its start.
- **The journal names a start that `LN_BOBYQA` moves next to a bound**, with its old and new value (`NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA: RCUT2B starts at 4 instead of 4.005, on its lower bound: ...`). A `CONFIG` or `WARM` start that moves gets a WARNING, on stderr and in the journal: the search evaluates the moved point. A start on a bound keeps its place and its full step, which the journal's step table notes (`start on a bound (kept)`); 1.5.0 warned about such a start when `STEP` was set.
- **The local search of an MLSL or AUGLAG that is `LN_BOBYQA` or `LN_COBYLA` keeps its full first step as well**: an `INNER` block's, or the default `LN_COBYLA` of `GN_MLSL`, `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `LN_AUGLAG` and `LN_AUGLAG_EQ`. Its step is the `STEP` of the enclosing block, else that of the `INNER` block, else 0.25 of the width of each search parameter. AUGLAG's one local search starts at the start of the search, which `LN_BOBYQA` moves as above. With `STEP` unset in both blocks, 1.5.0 gave each such local search NLopt's default from its own start, which shrank its step next to a bound. **The results of these searches change** (`LN_AUGLAG` with an `INNER LN_BOBYQA` from `RCUT2B 4.005` now goes beyond 4.1 within 30 evaluations).
- **`DLIB:BFGS` runs on the loss divided by the length of its first gradient**, over the coordinates its first step can move, so its path does not depend on the unit of the loss. `FTOL_ABS` and `PARAM tol_gradinf` are divided by that length too and keep their meaning for the loss; the records keep the loss, and the journal gives the length. A failed trial of its line search is given the finite stand-in of NLopt and `DLIB:GFS` (below), and a trial step that is not finite ends the run with an error (`FAILED -- The line search generated a step that is not finite`). 1.5.0 ran dlib's loop on the loss itself and gave it `FAILSCORE` itself. **Every `DLIB:BFGS` result changes**: on the Ta test data (`RCUT2B 5.6` in `[4, 6]`, `SGRIDMB 0.2` in `[0.1, 0.4]`, `PC_ERMSE 0 w`, `FTOL_ABS 1e-7 w`) the runs for w = 1e-4, 1 and 1e6 now all reach the optimum at `RCUT2B 6`; 1.5.0 fell short of it for w = 1e6.
- **A `FIXWEIGHT` search parameter is labelled `FIXWEIGHT[n]`, n its model weight, also when `FIXWEIGHT` has one value**: in `params.tadah`, the journal and `hpo_search_space.tsv`. 1.5.0 labelled it `FIXWEIGHT`.
- **An empty `TADAH_OPTIM_SCALE` is ignored**, as an empty value of the other environment overrides is; 1.5.0 refused it (`TADAH_OPTIM_SCALE must be AUTO or NONE (got '')`). The refusals of `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING`, `TADAH_BUMPGATE` and `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION` name the variable and quote its value (`TADAH_PC_ISOLATION must be THREAD or PROCESS (got 'process')`; 1.5.0 said `PC_ISOLATION must be THREAD or PROCESS, got 'process'`, and the other two quoted no value).

### Removed
- **BREAKING — uncertainty output is removed** (Tadah.MLIP refuses `tadah predict --error`, `tadah properties pairwise --error` and `tadah train --uncertainty`: the errors of Tadah! 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 were not standard deviations, and no calibrated estimate replaces them). The potentials of a run have no weight covariance (the `OALGO 3` potentials of 1.5.0 had `SIGMA`), and a `-c` seed with an `UNCERTAINTY` line, such as a potential that `tadah train` of 1.0.0 to 1.5.0 wrote (whose `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT` lines a run refuses too), or with an `ERROR` line is refused at startup, naming the line as converted by deleting it, with the reason, until the line is deleted. `OPTIM UNCERTAINTY`, `WCOV`, `WNOISE` and `ERROR` are refused as retired keys, with the reason and the conversion. Tests: `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (`Each OALGO 3 evaluation writes the weights of its own fit`), `test_hpo_error_scales.cpp` (`Every retired key of the blueprint is refused as an OPTIM target`), `test_pair_tadah_notices.cpp` (a potential with `WCOV` and `WNOISE`, retired keys, gives one warning of retired keys per `pair_coeff`), Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo -c refuses the UNCERTAINTY line of a seed potential`.
- **The `examples/` directory**: the worked examples, their shared data and scripts (`common/`, `run.sh`, `run_all.sh`), the reference suite `run_reference_suite.sh` with its reference fingerprints and tolerance overrides, and the manual CI jobs `hpo_examples_fast` and `hpo_examples_full` that ran it. The data files of the 1.5.0 examples, `examples/common/data/eos_bcc.tadahdb` and `elastic.tadahdb`, store VASP's pressure-signed stress and the energy without entropy, and `elastic.tadahdb` the transposed cells of its 12 sheared structures (14-25). Copies of both with the free energy, the Cauchy stress (positive under tension) and the cells as rows, on the same +3.69807734 eV/atom energy reference, are in `tests/data/ta/common/data`; its `README.md` says what they store and gives their conversion from the VASP OUTCARs (`tadah data convert`, then `tadah data balance --eshift -3.69807734`).
- `reject_deferred_ceres_nlls()` (`ceres_adapter.h`): Ceres' least-squares solvers are entries of the refusal table (*Fixed*), whose text every caps lookup gives.
- The environment variable `TADAH_HPO_FORCE_REBUILD`: every evaluation builds its model (see Fixed), which is what it asked for.
- The journal's `PRESTAGE.EXPLORE.INNER init strategy:` line (`(default -- CONFIG only)` for every INNER block), and the INIT resolution and starting points of the PRESTAGE INNER spec: an INNER block takes no INIT block (the parser refuses one), NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG choose the INNER search's starting points, and the copy of the OUTER's points that `share_init_with_nested_phases()` gave the INNER spec was read only by a duplicate start-point check and the density gates' seed scan, which deduplicates. No result changes. Test: `test_init_strategies.cpp` (`PRESTAGE supports a nested INNER sub-algo (MLSL pattern)`).
- `STRATEGY TYPE BASINS` and `TYPE BASINSV2`. Both shipped in 1.3.0-beta.1 and 1.5.0 as experimental strategies that the user documentation never described (the examples README called them experimental). A configuration that uses them now fails: `TYPE` must be `PLAIN`, `MULTISTART` or `PRESTAGE`; their STRATEGY knobs (`BASIN_POOL_MAX`, `BASIN_DEDUP_DIST`, `STOP_NO_IMPROVE_ROUNDS`, `SEED_TOP_N`, `CLAMP_TO_NOISE_FLOOR`, `CHECKPOINT_EVERY` and the BASINSV2 scheduler knobs) and the per-stage `FTOL_LADDER` / `BUDGET_MIN_EVALS` / `BUDGET_MAX_EVALS` / `BUDGET_RATE_FACTOR` keys are unknown keys; and `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` are only valid with `TYPE PRESTAGE`.
- The `checkpoint.tadah` resume. The resume of long runs advertised in 1.3.0-beta.1, and the checkpoint resume of `best_pot.tadah` described in the 1.5.0 documentation, only ever worked through BASINS / BASINSV2, the only strategies that wrote or read checkpoints; it is withdrawn with them. The stagnation `RESTART` is not affected. HPO runs are not resumable: every run starts fresh and removes the previous run's `best_pot.tadah`, which is replaced atomically on each new best and so holds the incumbent at all times. The CORE `Checkpoint` library is kept.
- The modules that existed only for BASINS: the basin pools, the progress tracker and the tolerance ladder, `OptimizerCaps::ladder_ftol` / `apply_ladder_ftol`, `SearchSpace::fingerprint`, and `scripts/parse_hpo_log.py`.
- The "removed from the grammar" hints for pre-release keywords that only led to BASINS keys (`GLOBAL_LIB`, `GLOBAL_ALGO`, `LOCAL_LIB`, `LOCAL_ALGO`, `TOL_GLO_LADDER`, `TOL_LOC_LADDER`, `STAGE_RATE_FACTOR`, `STAGE_MIN_EVALS`, `STAGE_MAX_EVALS`, `EMERGENCY_NO_IMPROVE_ROUNDS`, `GLOBAL_SEED_TOP_N`, `CLAMP_LADDER_TO_NOISE_FLOOR`). No tagged release accepted them; they are reported as unknown keys. `LOCAL` / `ENDLOCAL` (v1.0.0, v1.1.1) still point at `INNER`, without the BASINS sentence.
- The BASINS and resume tests (85 cases), including the optional-fixture test `BASINS resume is idempotent` and its `TADAH_HPO_RESUME_*` variables (it also read `TADAH_BINARY`, which now names the `tadah` executable for the `[tadah_cli]` cases, *Added*). New parser contracts cover the rejections, and no parser error names BASINS. The fresh-run reset of the best comparator, which the resume tests also asserted, has its own host end-to-end case.
- The MPI build of 1.5.0 (`TADAH_BUILD_MPI`): `hpo_worker.{h,cpp}`, the host's MPI evaluation path (`run_lammps_mpi`, the `TrainerHost` cache), the MPI branch of `HPOEngine`, the ScaLAPACK link and the `tadah_mpi.hpo` library name. `TADAH_HPO_MPI` is unchanged and is now the only MPI build; cmake-tools rejects `-DTADAH_BUILD_MPI=ON` at configure time.
- `PC_LAMMPS --ncpu` (also on `PC_FAILPROBE`). A line that contains it is rejected at startup, with or without a value: `Error on line N: PC_LAMMPS --ncpu: a removed option: it set the MPI ranks of one script in an MPI build of Tadah! 1.5.0; THREADS sets the number of workers that run the scripts, and PC_ISOLATION whether they run in worker processes or inside the tadah process`. Only the MPI build of 1.5.0 (`TADAH_BUILD_MPI`) read it; elsewhere it logged a warning and reordered the `PC_LAMMPS` lines. `LammpsSim::ncpu`, `LammpsSim::greater`, the reordering of `HPOTargets::LS` and the `ncpu=` field of the potential header's `Sim[k]` lines are gone.
- `include/tadah/hpo/lammps_scripts.h` (`LammpsScripts`, the embedded LAMMPS scripts replaced by `PC_LAMMPS` custom scripts). Nothing had included it since then, it needed a `cmrc/cmrc.hpp` that no build provides, and it was still installed with the headers.
- **BREAKING — the `PARALLEL` key of the `STRATEGY` block** is refused at its line with the removed-key hint: `'PARALLEL' was removed from the grammar: the starts of STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART run one after another; THREADS sets the number of workers that run the work of the evaluations ...`. The removed-key hint of `MULTISTART_PARALLEL` says the same; in 1.5.0 it pointed to `PARALLEL`. 1.5.0 accepted `PARALLEL` and ran the starts one after another regardless.

### Fixed
- **A `PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` relaxation starts from the lattice constant at which the previous relaxation of the same replica of `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT`, walker of `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` or ASA trial-state slot (the n-th trial state of each generated batch, see `Gener_Block`) ended**, as a `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` value is handed on; every other optimiser hands the lattice constant on from each evaluation to the next, across `MULTISTART` starts and `PRESTAGE` phases. It was one value for the run, which evaluations running at the same time wrote without a lock, so a relaxation could start from the value of another replica's evaluation.
- **A point evaluated again whose loss differs from its earlier evaluation's is reported**: a `WARNING` in `hpo_run.log` (`eval 2 evaluated the point of eval 1 again and its loss differs: 1 then 2, because ...`, with the reason the target file gives, on the powers of two of its occurrences) and the DIAGNOSTICS row `Repeats with another loss`. The run's record of the point keeps the later loss, as before; the difference passed without a word.
- **The short LAMMPS options `-sc` and `-l` on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line are `-screen` and `-log`**: the scripts write the screen output and the log the line names. They were passed on as given, and the `-screen none` and `-log none` that HPO adds without `-screen` and `-log` replaced them.
- **BREAKING — `PC_ERSQ` is refused when the run starts if a measured validation dataset has energies per atom that are all equal** (a dataset of one structure, or of structures with the same energy per atom): its R^2 has no value (`tadah hpo: PC_ERSQ on the validation set: the energies per atom of validation dataset 1 (val.db) are all equal, so its R^2 has no value; PC_ERSQ needs two different energies per atom in every validation dataset it measures`). In 1.5.0 its R^2 was -inf, or about -1e32 when rounding left a spread of a few ulp, which the loss function of the term turned into a failure or a constant. A 1.5.0 file converts by dropping `PC_ERSQ`, or by giving each measured validation dataset two different energies per atom.
- **`tadah hpo` gives the critical-parameter warnings of the pre-flight audit under `AUDIT warn` and `error` alone, each once.** A preview of that audit ran in `HPOEngine::validate` before every run and `--check`, whatever `AUDIT` said (its default is off) and before the target file was read: it scanned the raw target file for `OPTIM` lines, read `OPTIMIZER` as a key `IZER`, warned about every fixed critical key of a search of `FIXWEIGHT` alone, and repeated each warning of `AUDIT warn`. The preview is removed; the audit of `AUDIT warn` and `error`, on the parsed search space and the training data, is the one that warns (Tadah.MLIP's `run_critical_optim_audit` flags the searched descriptors alone).
- **A chain of `METHOD` lines draws its points in a time that grows with their number, not with its square.** A point equal to one kept before it (within 1e-12 in each coordinate of the search) is dropped; each point was compared with every point kept, so a chain of `METHOD RANDOM 1000000` and `METHOD LHS 1000000` did not finish in 120 s. A point is compared with the kept points whose first coordinate lies near its own, and the chain keeps the same points; that chain is drawn in about 3 s. Test: `test_init_strategies.cpp`.
- **A bump centre beyond the low bound of an `OPTIM RCUT2B` or `OPTIM RCUTMB` box is warned about at startup** with a smooth cutoff (`CGRID2B bump 1 centre 4 lies beyond the OPTIM RCUT2B low bound 3.5: a candidate whose RCUT2B is below the centre keeps only the bump's inner shoulder (pathological) ...`). The check read the cutoff of the training configuration only, so a search that tried smaller cutoffs was not warned about.
- **The `TADAH:GRID` messages count the start point apart from the grid points**, and the banner names `1 dimension` for one `OPTIM` dimension. The first `INIT` point (the training configuration's point unless an `INIT` block draws others), evaluated first, is no grid point: `TADAH:GRID finished: the INIT point and all 11 grid points evaluated`, `TADAH:GRID stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 5 of 28 evaluations (the INIT point and 27 grid points)`. They said `1 dimensions.` and `all 12 grid points evaluated` for a grid of 11 points.
- **The `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` banner of `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` says what the start draw uses**: `INIT_MODE=JITTER AUTO (uniform bound; sigma=proposal sigma per dim, not calibrated)` (the initial V under `CORANA ON`). It said `sigma=AUTO per dim, tol=0.05`, a calibration that `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA` does not run.
- **With `PC_LAMMPS`, the refusal of a gradient-based optimiser names the optimisers that take no gradient and run as written**, among them `DLIB:GFS`, `ASA`, `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` and `TADAH:ANNEAL_PA`, and says that an NLopt MLSL or AUGLAG algorithm is accepted with a derivative-free `INNER` block. It named `NLOPT GN_*/LN_*` and `TADAH RANDOM/GRID/ANNEAL` only.
- **`tadah hpo` removes `best_pot.tadah.lock` when the run ends**, also when a signal or an error ends it, and a lock file it cannot remove is a WARNING in the journal; an empty `best_pot.tadah.lock` stayed in the run directory after every run. The file exists while the run publishes its best potential.
- **The `best_pot.tadah` header gives the search space in real bounds**, the box of each `OPTIM` line, as the journal does; it gave the optimiser's internal box, the unit box `[0, 1]` under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`. **The header and the journal write a numeric `PARAM` value as it reads**: `GRID_POINTS=11` and `STEP_FRAC=0.15`, where they wrote `1.1000e+01` and `1.5000e-01`.
- **`DLIB:GFS` refuses a negative `FTOL_ABS` at startup** (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser: DLIB:GFS: FTOL_ABS is the accuracy of dlib's solver and must be >= 0 (got -0.001)`). In 1.5.0 it passed startup, and the search failed inside dlib once the training data had been read (`Failing expression was 0 <= eps`).
- **A `PRESTAGE` exploration whose optimiser reads `SEED` and that has no seed of its own takes the `STRATEGY`-level `SEED`**, so a run with `SEED` in its `OPTIMIZER` or `STRATEGY` block repeats the optimisers of its exploration and its refinement. The exploration's own seeds come first: the `EXPLORE` block's `SEED` and, for NLopt, its `INIT` block's `SEED`. The refinement draws other random numbers from the same `SEED`, the journal names the exploration's seed (`EXPLORE SEED      : 7 (the STRATEGY-level SEED)`). A refinement whose optimiser reads no `SEED` (`DLIB:GFS`, `DLIB:BFGS`, `TADAH:GRID`, CERES) takes such a `SEED`, and a `DLIB:GFS` refinement says that it seeds the exploration; with an `EXPLORE` seed of its own, the refinement refuses it. Starting points that an `INIT` or `RESTART` block draws repeat only with that block's `SEED`. In 1.5.0 such an exploration was not seeded (NLopt's time seed, or a random device for `TADAH:RANDOM` and the annealers), so the run did not repeat although the journal named a `SEED`.
- **An NLopt search seeded only by its `INIT` block's `SEED` draws new random numbers in each `RESTART` round and in each `MULTISTART` start** (`OptimizerSpec::nlopt_seed_from_init()`, `derive_restart_seeds()`, `multistart_start_spec()`). NLopt takes the `INIT` block's `SEED` when its block has no `SEED` (`make_nlopt()`); the seeds that `RESTART` derives for round *r* (`SEED + r*S`, an `EXPLORE` block's `SEED + r`) and that `MULTISTART` and the `PRESTAGE` refinement give start *i* (`SEED + i`) are now derived from it the same way, so such a run evaluates the points of the same run with that `SEED` in its `STRATEGY` block. It was not derived: every round and every start seeded NLopt with round 0's seed, so a `RESTART` round of `GN_CRS2_LM` drew round 0's population again (every call after the round's start point answered from the run's record) and `GD_STOGO_RAND` or `GN_DIRECT_L_RAND` replayed round 0. The journal names the derived seed of a search that draws random numbers (`reads_seed`): `RESTART 1/2 -- fresh init from RANDOM; RESTART SEED 6, INIT SEED 8` (an `EXPLORE` block's `EXPLORE INIT SEED`) and `MULTISTART start 2/2 -- running inner optimiser (INIT seed=8)`, where it named none; the lines of `LN_SBPLX` or `LD_MMA`, which draw none, name no `INIT` seed. Round 0 and the first start are unchanged. Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp`, `test_phase_specs.cpp`.
- **DLIB:BFGS does not evaluate the point it stands on again, and ends a search that no step can move** (`dlib_adapter.h`: the adapter runs dlib's `find_min_box_constrained` loop itself, `detail::bfgs_box_search`, with dlib's parts, so every step that moves the point is dlib's, bit for bit). At a point where each coordinate with a step is at a bound its gradient points out of, dlib's step held to the box cannot move the point: its line search evaluated the point again -- up to 100 times while the loss came back above its first value by rounding, which the warm-started `PC_LAMMPS` relaxation does -- and BFGS took its gradient there again. A line search whose first trial is its own start is not run: the point, its loss and its gradient stand (`DLIB:BFGS: the step from RCUT2B = 6 cannot move the point within the OPTIM box: the line search is not run, ...`; DIAGNOSTICS `Line searches not run`), and under FTOL_ABS the search ends `DLIB:BFGS: FTOL_ABS reached`. Under `PARAM tol_gradinf` (the norm of the whole gradient, not small at a bound) or `FTOL_ABS 0` such a search went on until MAXEVAL, and without MAXEVAL it never ended (14,295 evaluations of 10 points in 40 s on the audit's 2-D problem). BFGS then falls back to its steepest-descent step from the same point; its step length grows tenfold (to at most the whole step) only after a line search accepted its first trial, which on the unchanged loss happens only while the decrease the Armijo test asks for is below the rounding of the loss, so no later line search starts beyond ten times the largest such step, or the whole step (`detail::stalled_reach`). When the step that far cannot move the point either -- a coordinate at a bound its gradient points out of, a gradient of zero, a step below the rounding of the point, as next to a jump in the loss (whose finite difference is steep while the loss is flat) or one ulp inside dlib's 1e-8 gap of a bound -- no later step can, and the search ends `DLIB:BFGS: no step within the OPTIM box`, a new status (`EndReason::NoStep`), its stop line naming the coordinates (`SGRID2B[2] at its lower bound 4, with a gradient that points out of the box`; `A = 0.499999999, whose step is below the rounding of its value`). When it can, the line search starts there, once (dlib got there, if at all, only through iterations that each evaluated the point again), and if it comes back to the point the search ends with the same status (`from A = 0.5 the line search from the farthest step BFGS takes there (5.551115122e-14 of the steepest-descent step) found no lower loss and came back to the point`). So no two iterations in a row ask for no loss, and an iteration that asks for none checks the stop flag (SIGINT, SIGTERM) as an evaluation does. Every iteration that moves the point is dlib's. The termination tests' DLIB:BFGS pins follow (51 to 44 evaluations, 9 to 6 under FAILSCORE_GRADING, a PRESTAGE refinement FTOL_ABS where it spent MAXEVAL 20). Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp` (DLIB:BFGS cases: at a bound, next to a jump, in the gap of a bound, the line search from the farthest step, and `hpo_shutdown_bfgs_not_run`, label `shutdown`), `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **DLIB:BFGS ends a search that an iteration without an evaluation took back to a point where it stood** (`dlib_adapter.h`, `detail::bfgs_box_search` (e)): `DLIB:BFGS: back at an earlier point`, a new status (`EndReason::Revisit`), with the stop line `DLIB:BFGS stopped: back at an earlier point after 111 evaluation(s): the iteration from A = 0.89349363928454106 (the point of eval 109) made no evaluation: it asked only for points the run had evaluated, and took the search back to A = 0.89349363928412684 (the point of eval 98), where it had stood with the same loss and gradient`. The gradients the record holds are those the search formed where it stood, so such an iteration came back to a point where the search had stood, with the loss it had there: the iterations since left the loss where it was. On a plateau of the loss's rounding (every step passes the Armijo test, its decrease lost in the rounding of the loss) under a stop test that does not end a search whose loss stays -- `FTOL_ABS 0`, or `PARAM tol_gradinf` with a coordinate held at a bound -- BFGS goes round points it has: with the repeats answered from the record (above) it made no evaluation and never ended, MAXEVAL counting evaluations (a fuzz of DLIB:BFGS over random losses on [0, 1] and [0, 1]^2: 14 of 3000 searches, among them a smooth bowl with a coordinate held at a bound under `tol_gradinf`); without the record each lap was evaluated again, until MAXEVAL, and without MAXEVAL without end. dlib's own stop test comes first (under `FTOL_ABS` > 0 such a search ends `FTOL_ABS reached` there), and every iteration that evaluates is dlib's. It is a stop rule, not a proof that the search could not go on: of the fuzz's 3000 searches it ends 22 otherwise -- the 14 that spun, 7 that ran to MAXEVAL 400 on such a plateau (now after 18 to 113 evaluations, the same best) and 1 that ended `no step within the OPTIM box` after 144 evaluations (now 79, its best 2 ulp higher) -- and the other 2978 keep their evaluation counts, best (bit for bit) and status; 20 of them count fewer reused calls in DIAGNOSTICS (a gradient right after a reused loss is no second reuse, above). An NLopt search whose calls are answered from the record is bounded by NLopt's own `MAXEVAL`, which counts calls (`LD_MMA` held at the lower corner of [0, 1]^2: 20 evaluations, 56 of its 60 calls answered); with tolerances alone (`XTOL_REL` at u = 0) one that only asks again still does not end (NLopt's state is not visible to HPO; open). A gradient centred on a recorded loss evaluates its stencil, so it counts in MAXEVAL. Tests: `test_repeat_points.cpp` (`[revisit]`), `test_repeat_record.cpp` (`Run record: DLIB:BFGS's loop ends an iteration that went back to a point without an evaluation`).
- **Ceres' least-squares solvers (`CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT`, `DOGLEG`, `SUBSPACE_DOGLEG`) are refused at the line that closes their block, with their reason**: entries of the refusal table, so every caps lookup gives `CERES:<ALGO> is refused: tadah hpo does not run Ceres' least-squares solver (...): it minimises a sum of squared residuals with their derivatives, and tadah hpo gives an optimiser one scalar loss, over which these methods degenerate. The recommended replacements are CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT, Ceres' line-search solver of a scalar loss that tadah hpo runs, and the bound-constrained gradient searches NLOPT:LD_LBFGS and DLIB:BFGS`, and the parser names the phase and the line. The run stopped with `HPOEngine error: unknown optimizer CERES:LEVENBERG_MARQUARDT (not registered in optimizer_caps)`, no line and no alternatives: the HPOTargets constructor looked the backend up (resolving the `INIT` chain) before validate_backend() and run_inner() could give their deferral text. Tests: `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp`, `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp`, `test_hpo.cpp`, `test_phase_specs.cpp`.
- **An unknown `LIB:ALGO` is an error at the line that closes its block**, naming what is wrong (`require_registered_backend`, which `require_driver`, `require_inner_driver` and `reject_refused_backends` call with the phase): ``Error on line 10: the STRATEGY-level optimiser: unknown optimiser NLOPT:LD_FOO: LIB NLOPT has no ALGO LD_FOO; `tadah explain ALGO` lists the optimisers``, or `there is no LIB FOO (LIB is ASA, CERES, DLIB, NLOPT or TADAH)`; `optimizer_caps()` throws the same text with no phase. It threw `unknown optimizer NLOPT:LD_FOO (not registered in optimizer_caps)` from the HPOTargets constructor, with no line, and `reject_refused_backends` accepted an unknown backend, which `validate_phase_backends` then reported with no phase.
- **Every variable at fault in a `PC_LAMMPS` task is journalled**, in its one journal line, joined with `; `, in one order -- the line's variables (`--varloss`, the `PC_FAILPROBE`'s `S`), then the `--hotvar`, then the `--failvar` variables, each group in the line's order --, each with the LAMMPS error its read-back raised or why its value has no number: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (rb.in, occurrence 1): reading --varloss E after the script: its value 'abc' is not a number; reading --failvar F1 after the script: ERROR on proc 0: Variable F1: Divide by 0 in variable formula ...; reading --failvar F2 after the script: its value 'xyz' is not a number`. Only the first was named. The worker no longer fails a task for a `--hotvar` or `--failvar` read-back error: it hands on every value, each error beside its variable (`hotvar_errors`, `failvar_errors`, as `outvar_errors`), and the parent's judgement (`apply_task_result`, `hpo_host_impl.hpp`), the one place every variable is judged, fails the task as before (the variables failscored, the evaluation aborted, band B graded by the readable failvars). Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp` (the worker's per-variable errors, the task OK) and `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (the line's clauses and their order under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD`, and through the built `tadah` under `THREAD` and `PROCESS`).
- The ALGO help describes the CERES driver, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` (`print_caps`): the solver, the stops that end a run, `PARAM tol_gradinf`, `max_iter`, `max_ls_iter` and the finite differences, that `STEP` is rejected, and that it ignores the box (a point it proposes outside it is not evaluated). It printed `(no info)`. A refused backend of any library prints its refusal (it was NLopt's alone).
- **A build with `TADAH_ENABLE_CERES=OFF` compiles** (`CMakeLists.txt`). Eigen (3.4.0) is fetched and linked `PUBLIC` to `tadah.hpo` whatever the option: the public headers `adjoint_solver.h`, `lammps_hessian.h`, `observable_registry.h` and `sims_grad.h` (the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint gradient, the LAMMPS Hessian, the observables) include `<Eigen/Dense>` in every build. Eigen was fetched only in the Ceres block and linked only when its target existed, so without Ceres the configure step said nothing and 13 sources of `tadah.hpo` stopped at `Eigen/Dense: No such file or directory` (so since the adjoint kernels, in 1.3.0-beta.1 too); a missing Eigen is now a configure error. Without Ceres, `LIB CERES` is refused at startup and `tadah refit` refuses `REFIT_MODE compact` (Tadah.MLIP).
- **The `PC_LAMMPS` analytical chain adds the direct θ term** (`compute_sims_grad_dWchain`, `compute_sims_grad_for_script`; `sims_grad.h`). A θ that is a descriptor parameter changes the energy and the forces at the same weights. By the implicit function theorem dO/dθ of a relaxed LAMMPS observable is the chain through the weights, (dg/dW + λ·dF/dW)·dW/dθ, plus dg/dθ + λ·dF/dθ at fixed x* and W. The chain had only the first part, as the `PC_OBSERVABLE` chain had before 1.6.0. `SimsGradScriptInput` gains the fields of the second part (`dg_dtheta`, `dF_dtheta`, `direct_theta`), which a provider fills when it can compute them, and the chain adds them with each observable's λ. A script with observables whose input does not carry them sends every θ to finite differences, like an unknown expression. On an analytic stub (`test_sims_grad.cpp`: two atoms and an iso box DOF, θ in the descriptors) the chain now matches central differences of the loss to 2e-10 relative; through W alone it was 0.28 to 3.5 relative off. The path is not reachable: `PC_LAMMPS` refuses every `GRAD_STRATEGY` at startup. `HPO_Host`'s provider computes no direct term, as pair_tadah's adjoint snapshot has no θ derivatives, so that path now takes finite differences for every θ, where it took the chain through W alone. Tests: `test_sims_grad.cpp` (`[direct_theta]`), `test_hpo_analytical.cpp` (`[sims_grad_provider]`), `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`hpo_host_e2e`: the provider on a `PC_LAMMPS` script, every θ to finite differences).
- **`LammpsHessianAssembler`'s box block comes from the pair virial** (`lammps_hessian.cpp`; the `PC_LAMMPS` adjoint chain, not reachable: `PC_LAMMPS` refuses every `GRAD_STRATEGY`). Each entry is the central difference of dE/ds_α = −tr(d_α F⁻¹ W), read from the virial W of each strained state, along the deformation the assembler applies (F and d_α from the strained boxes as LAMMPS holds them). The diagonal was the energy's second difference over h_strain² = 1e-10, which multiplies the energy's round-off by 1e10. On the 4-atom Ta cell of the tests the iso entry was 2.6e-6 and the xx entry 1.6e-5 off; on a 16-atom cell with the `HPO_test` potential the iso entry was 1.5e-4 off, and so was the lattice-constant chain dlx/dW against its finite differences (1.1e-4 at the case's step, against its 1e-4 tolerance). The off-diagonal took −dvirial/dε in the Voigt convention, the same for two normal modes. The chain's box part now matches the finite differences to 5e-9 with the shipped potential and 4e-7 with the `HPO_test` one. On an orthogonal box, whose tilts LAMMPS ignores, the shear rows are now exactly 0 (they were the energy's round-off). The rest of the chain's error, 1.2e-5 (iso) and 2.4e-5 (Voigt-6) with the shipped potential, is its atom part. That is λ's atom entries, round-off at the relaxed crystal, times pair_tadah's adjoint snapshot dF/dW, which is not the derivative of the forces (Tadah.LAMMPS, not changed here). Before this fix that part was partly offset by the diagonal's error: 6.0e-6 and 3.3e-6. Tests: `test_lammps_hessian.cpp` (the box block against the central difference of LAMMPS' pressure), `test_hpo_adjoint_pilot.cpp` (the box part of the iso and Voigt-6 lattice-constant chains against the finite differences' plateau).
- **Startup refuses `MODEL M_KRR Kern_Linear 2` without an `SBASIS` it takes** (Tadah.MLIP `checked_sbasis`, through `model_weight_count`): a missing `SBASIS`, or one below the intercept vectors plus one, is refused as for a non-linear kernel, naming the model (`the -c configuration does not build a model: M_KRR Kern_Linear 2: SBASIS 1: the fit needs at least 2 basis vectors ...`). Implementation 2 draws its basis vectors for its solve with every kernel. Startup passed it (the weight count, `DSIZE`, was given before any `SBASIS` check; 1.5.0 had no `SBASIS` check at startup for any kernel), and every evaluation threw (`Evaluations that threw: 1 (100.0%)`, `no evaluation produced a usable loss`). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp` (`startup validation: SBASIS of M_KRR Kern_Linear 2 is checked`).
- **Every evaluation trains a model built from its own candidate.** `HPO_Host` builds a `Trainer` from the candidate context (the `-c` configuration with the candidate's `OPTIM` values) for every evaluation. A thread used to keep the `Trainer` of its first evaluation, rewrite only the `OPTIM` keys of its context and call `Trainer::reinit()` (MLIP), which re-applied the descriptor parameters only, so whatever the model, its kernel or basis function and its design matrix read when they were built kept the first evaluation's value, and what a fit wrote into the context stayed for the next. Consequences, now gone:
  - `OPTIM EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT` and `SWEIGHT` of 1.5.0 (the design matrix's row weights, every model; the error scales `OPTIM ESIGMA`, `FSIGMA` and `SSIGMA` replace them) were not applied after a thread's first evaluation: every later evaluation trained the first one's weights, while the potentials recorded the candidate's values;
  - `OPTIM MPARAMS` (the kernel of M_KRR) kept the first value, while every potential recorded the candidate's: a `best_pot.tadah` predicted otherwise than the loss it was reported with;
  - `OALGO 3` potentials gained one `SIGMA` per evaluation of a thread (10, 20, 30 values for 3 weights), and `tadah predict --error` on such a `best_pot.tadah` corrupted the heap (Tadah.MODELS removes the writing of `SIGMA`);
  - an M_KRR `-c` potential with `WEIGHTS` failed the first evaluation of each thread ("This object is already trained!"; Tadah.MODELS fixes the flag; every evaluation would fail now that each builds its model);
  - a second run in one process (a program that calls `hpo_run()` twice) trained the first run's model, with the first run's `LAMBDA`, error scales, `FORCE` and every other key it does not search (and started its `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` values where the first run ended: see below).
  The per-thread slot keeps the trainer of the thread's latest evaluation for `analytical_gradient` and the observables, stamped with its host: `analytical_gradient` of a host whose evaluation did not build the slot's trainer routes every parameter to finite differences (it differentiated another host's model). Building a `Trainer` costs little against a fit (0.023 ms against 0.40 ms for D2_BP with 3 functions on 80 atoms with forces); the setfl file of D2_EAM and DM_EAM is parsed once while its contents are unchanged, and the descriptors' configuration warnings are logged once per process (Tadah.MODELS). The line a kernel prints when it is built at `VERBOSE` > 0 (`Kern_RBF | sigma: ...`) is printed once per run, not for the model of every evaluation (see Changed). Other searches give the losses they gave (a search over a setfl cutoff now stops at startup, see Added). With `LAMBDA -1` the evidence approximation starts every evaluation from the configured `ALPHA` and `BETA`, not from the previous evaluation's. Potentials from earlier runs that searched `EWEIGHT`, `FWEIGHT`, `SWEIGHT` or `MPARAMS` report an optimum that was never applied: re-run those searches (of the error scales, for the first three). Tests: `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (CTest `hpo_trainer_e2e`), Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: a second hpo run in the process evaluates its own configuration`.
- **Every run starts its `PC_LAMMPS --hotvar` values from their declared initial value, or from WARM's seed.** The per-chain cache of the values each evaluation hands to the next (`warmstart_cache.h`) is cleared when a run starts (`HPO_Host::prepare_best_publication`, `warmstart::reset_for_fresh_run`); it lived as long as the process, so the second run in one process started each hot script from the value the first run's last evaluation converged to, and did not reproduce the same run made alone (`tests/data/ta/target_files/dlib_bfgs.hpo` at `MAXEVAL 3`, `DIGITS 15`: `TOTAL_LOSS/N` 1.043183703093665e-01 against 1.043183703095260e-01; now the second run's `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah` and `best_pot.tadah` are those of the run alone, under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`). WARM's seeds, primed before the run, are kept (an earlier run's WARM seeds are not: `warmstart::begin_run`, the *Fixed* entry on the journal of a second run). The evaluation counter still continues across runs, so the second run's `ITERATION` numbers and `DUMP` names follow the first run's (4-6 after a run of 3). Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`A second run in the same process starts its --hotvar values from their declared start or the WARM seed`), `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (CTest `hpo_hotvar_e2e`).
- The pc-pool test `killing the worker process is contained and respawned` takes the worker's pid from `worker_pid` and has no skip path. It found the pid with `ps --ppid | awk`, and where that found none (no procps `ps` or no `awk`, as in a minimal container) it warned and passed after the first round trip, while the two cases that need the same pid failed there; the `ps`/`awk` helper is gone.
- Two durable-best cases of `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`hpo_durable_e2e`) have no `WARN`-and-return path: `RESULT distinguishes the last saved best from the unsaved one` requires its `FAILED` ending, which its search reaches every time (`NLOPT:LN_SBPLX` draws no random numbers; the second new best, eval 3, is the unsaved one, eval 1 the last saved), and `durable best: hpo_run ends FAILED after storage recovers` checks both outcomes of its four threads, a candidate saved after the failure or none (then `best_pot.tadah` and RESULT keep the last best saved before it): in either, `best_pot.tadah`'s `HPO_SOURCE_EVAL`, `Best potential saved` and RESULT's `Last saved best` or `Unsaved candidate` line name the eval of the journal's last new-best line, which is written once a best is saved. A new case, `... ends FAILED when storage does not recover, keeping the last best saved before the failure` (the lock fault `count=always`), takes the outcome without a save after the failure every time. Each returned with a warning, checking nothing more, when its run took the other path.
- `hpo_host_impl.hpp` includes `hpo_host.h`, whose `HPO_Host` templates it defines: included before `hpo_host.h` it did not compile (`expected initializer before '<'`). Including `hpo_host.h`, as the code does, was not affected.
- **The per-evaluation health monitor (`HEALTH_LOG`) checks the weights of every evaluation.** It read the column layout of `Trainer::dm` (Tadah.MLIP), which nothing builds -- M_BLR and M_KRR fit their own design matrix, and the fast path of a `FIXINDEX` that pins every weight builds none -- and skipped an empty layout, so it never recorded an evaluation: none of its warnings, the NaN/Inf one included, was printed since it was added. `HPO_Host` now passes the weights the model holds (fitted, or provisioned from `FIXWEIGHT`) and the layout `DesignMatrix::build` gives the model's design matrix, `fb->phi_layout(dc.layout())` (bias, D2 and DM blocks for `BF_Linear` and `Kern_Linear`; one block over all columns for a non-linear function, a kernel's over its basis vectors). Defects of the monitor that its first records showed:
  - `compute_iter_health` checked for NaN/Inf only the weights inside the layout's blocks; it checks every weight;
  - a layout with more columns than weights was taken for NaN/Inf weights (a sentinel) and the block it could not read for a block of zero weights; a layout that does not describe the weights is reported as such, `iter_health: eval <n> -- the model has <N> weights but its column layout has <M> columns: the per-block checks are skipped.` (`... the column layout of the model's <N> weights is not contiguous from column 0: ...` for blocks that do not partition as many columns as weights), rate-limited like the other warnings, and the per-block checks are skipped;
  - a NaN weight counted as an exact zero (the comparisons `|w| > max` and `|w| > 1e-15` are false for NaN), so a block of NaN weights, the case of a diverged fit, was reported as a block of zero weights, `iter_health: eval <n> block[<b>/<label>] max_abs -- all weights in this block are exactly zero -- the descriptor block contributed nothing to the fit.`, after the NaN/Inf line (and in `summary` mode that warning's 100-evaluation cooldown hid a block that was zero in the next evaluations), and a block of mostly NaN weights as one with `fewer than 5% of weights in this block exceed 1e-15.`; a block that holds a NaN or infinite weight is flagged (`IterHealthRecord::block_has_nan_inf`), gets no aggregates and no block check, and the NaN/Inf warning describes it;
  - `IterHealthMonitor` changed its maps without a lock (its header asked callers to funnel records through one thread), while `HPO_Host`'s evaluations record concurrently (`ANNEAL_PT`/`ANNEAL_PA` replicas, `DLIB:GFS` batches): it is thread-safe (8 threads recording 400 records each counted 2730 to 3021 of the 3200 warnings).
  The warnings are printed on standard output (core Logger at WARNING), not in `hpo_run.log`. The `DM-skip fast path` test checked that `Trainer::dm` had an empty layout, which held for every evaluation; it checks that no MLIP parallel loop runs over the training structures on the fast path (one does when a weight is fitted). Tests: `test_iter_health.cpp` (a non-finite weight outside the blocks, a layout that does not describe the weights, a block of NaN or mostly NaN weights, concurrent records), `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`[fixweight_e2e]`: the fast path and a fit with a block of zero weights, a fit whose weights are not finite, a fitted block of NaN weights).
- **Every run has worker pools of its own and stops them however it ends** (`RunPools`, `hpo.cpp`). `hpo_run()` builds the script executor of the run's `THREADS` and installs MLIP's parallel backend where it sizes its pools; on every way out -- a return, a signal stop, an error, also one at startup after the pools were built -- it removes the backend, stops the `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker processes and joins the executor's workers, whose thread-local `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` LAMMPS instances and potentials end with them (they did at process exit). Before:
  - a run that threw (at startup after the worker processes were spawned, or an error `drive_run()` rethrows) left them running until process exit, so the next `hpo_run()` of the process failed `pc_worker_pool_init: pool already initialized` (a program that calls `hpo_run()` again);
  - the executor was a function-local static built at its first use, so every later run of the process (a program's next `hpo_run()`) ran on the first run's `THREADS` while its journal reported its own, and under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` a script of the same name and line options ran on the LAMMPS instance the first run had cached, with the script text that instance had rendered from the first run's file (a script rewritten between the runs ran as the first run had read it); under `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` a later run with a smaller `THREADS` handed tasks to worker ids its pool did not have (`PcWorkerPool::execute: bad slot`: in each of 8 runs of the test every evaluation failed, `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`);
  - MLIP's parallel backend outlived the run, so a later training in the process (a program that trains after `hpo_run()`) ran its kernels on the HPO pool instead of OpenMP.
  The evaluation counter still carries over (the second run's `ITERATION` numbers continue the first's); an earlier run's WARM hot values and the journal's tallies no longer do (the *Fixed* entry on the journal of a second run in one process). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (two runs in one process: the first failing after its worker processes started; `THREADS` 2 then 3; `THREADS` 3 then 1 under `PROCESS`; under `THREAD` each run's LAMMPS instance ends with the run, its log closed with LAMMPS's `Total wall time` line, and the second run runs its rewritten script), `test_script_executor.cpp` (`init_script_executor` gives the executor the width it asks for).
