- **`FIXINDEX` is read strictly**, with CORE's `parse_fixindex`, as the trainers read it, by `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT`'s slot mapping and labels (`HPOTargets`, without the weight count), `validate_hpo_setup` (through MLIP's `classify_weight_source`, with it) and `derive_raw_embedding_f0`. `FIXINDEX 13 14` (two values) maps `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT (14)` to `FIXWEIGHT` slot 2, labelled `FIXWEIGHT[14]`, as `FIXINDEX 13,14` does; the lenient CORE `parse_indices` dropped the joined values, and the line was refused with the self-contradicting `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT (14): weight 14 is not pinned by FIXINDEX (13 14)` (`validate_hpo_setup` said `FIXINDEX names 0 weights`, and `derive_raw_embedding_f0` did not see the weights pinned). A descending range (`14-13`, which gave the same message) is refused as such, and a weight named twice is refused (**BREAKING**): `13,13,14` with two `FIXWEIGHT` values was deduplicated and the run went ahead. `validate_hpo_setup`'s branch for a model without weights (K = 0), with its own lone-key and count checks, is removed: `probe_model` refuses such a model first (`The descriptor size is 0`), so it was not reached. Tests: `test_optim_key_rules.cpp`, `test_startup_validation.cpp` (whose `size mismatch` section pins `FIXINDEX 1,2` for one value: its `1,2,3` also named weight 3 of the 2-weight model, which is refused first).
- **`tadah hpo` refuses `FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT` with M_KRR implementation 2 at startup** (`validate_hpo_setup`, through MLIP's `classify_weight_source`): `MODEL M_KRR <kernel> 2 cannot pin weights: ...`, its solve fitting every weight at once. The setup passed, and every evaluation trained with the pins ignored (an `OPTIM FIXWEIGHT` line searched values no fit used). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp`.
- **A signal that one rank of a multi-rank `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` run (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) receives ends every rank as a signal stop.** The ranks agree on how each phase ended (`agree_on_rank_stop`, `run_stop.h`): rank 0's seed/T0 calibration, ANNEAL_PT's calibration pass 2, every `ReplicaPool` epoch (population initialization, the automatic step warm-up and pilot, replica rungs, walker sweeps) and the `INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO` calibration. The most severe exception of any rank decides, as `ExceptionStash` decides among threads (an error before a signal stop before a stagnation stop): a rank whose own exception is of that kind rethrows it, another throws the error or `PeerShutdown`, a `core::ShutdownRequested` that names the signal and the lowest rank that stopped on one. `drive_run` ends such a rank with `SHUTDOWN (<signal> on MPI rank <r>)` and 128 + the signal; a rank that received the signal itself still reports `SHUTDOWN (<signal>)`. A rank that had not seen the signal ended with an error, status 1 (`FAILED -- TADAH:ANNEAL_PT: rank 0 aborted during seed/T0 calibration`, `... during calibration pass 2`, `... worker failed on another MPI rank during <phase>`). A jitter-calibration probe that threw on one rank (a signal or an error) hung the job: that rank left the calibration before its gathers and waited in `drive_run`'s collective, the others waited in the gathers, no rank returned to `tadah`'s exit handshake, and the job ran until it was killed from outside; such an error now ends every rank with status 1 (`... the INIT_MODE JITTER AUTO calibration failed on another MPI rank` on the others). `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, which every rank runs on its own before the driver, has no such agreement: a signal or an error there that only some ranks see now ends the job through `tadah`'s exit handshake, which aborts it 30 s after that rank's run ends (its `drive_run` collective meets the others' first agreement, and they then wait in the next one); before, that collective met the others' seed/T0 broadcast, and Open MPI 5 aborted the job at once (`MPI_ERR_TRUNCATE` in `MPI_Allreduce`; collectives that do not match are an error in MPI, so another MPI library need not report it). Tests: `hpo_mpi_signal_*`, each scenario checking the phase of the evaluation the signal fell in.
- **The LAMMPS instance that reads a `PC_LAMMPS` script's variables at startup writes the line's `-log` / `-screen` file with the rank's `.r<rank>` suffix in a multi-rank run** (`lmp.log.r0`, `lmp.log.r1`; unchanged on one rank), as every rank-local output does; every rank wrote the one file the line names. Test: `hpo_mpi_e2e_eamfast`.
- The MPI build journals a `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` request (`PC_ISOLATION THREAD: the MPI build of tadah runs the scripts inside the tadah process`): its journal had no `PC_ISOLATION` line then (its default already had one). Test: `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in that build.
- **`tadah hpo` refuses at startup a training `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice** (`validate_hpo_setup` calls Tadah.MLIP's `require_distinct_training_datasets`), naming both entries: the same path, two spellings of it or a link to it. Every evaluation fitted the file's structures twice (an `M_BLR` potential written before records its `DBFILE`, so one used as the `-c` configuration with the `DBFILE` added listed it twice). Test: `test_startup_validation_dbfile.cpp`.
- **A validation `DBFILE` that lists one dataset twice is refused** (`require_distinct_validation_datasets`, called by `HPO_Host` when it reads the `--validation` file, before any validation dataset is read; the rule of the training `DBFILE`, Tadah.MLIP `repeated_dataset`): `the --validation file config.val: DBFILE lists one dataset twice: entry 1 (...) and entry 3 (...) are one file, ...`, naming the same path twice, two spellings of it or a link to it, and the indexed `PC_ERMSE (i)` / `PC_FRMSE (i)` lines as the way to weight datasets. Every validation metric averages over the validation datasets, and the duplicate counted twice in it (on the Ta test datasets `elastic` and `eos_bcc` with `elastic` listed again, the first evaluation's `ERMSE[avg]` was 1320.9 meV/atom, the mean of 881.3, 2200.3 and 881.3). Tests: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp`, Tadah.MLIP `Subcommand: hpo refuses a validation DBFILE that lists one dataset twice`.
- **`--validation` takes one file** (`validation_config_file`, at startup): a `VALIDATION` of two or more values (`--validation a.val b.val`) is refused naming them; `hpo_run()` read the first, and the others were not read. The validation datasets are the `DBFILE` lines of that one file. Test: Tadah.MLIP `Subcommand: hpo --validation takes one file`.
- The refusal of a run without a training `DBFILE` and without `ATOM` ends with the advice of `tadah train`'s refusal of a configuration without `DBFILE` (Tadah.MLIP `potential_has_no_datasets_advice`: a potential has no `DBFILE` line; add a `DBFILE` line to a copy of it), as do the startup refusals of `classify_weight_source` (Tadah.MLIP), which `tadah hpo -c best_pot.tadah` without a `DBFILE` line meets. `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` without a training `DBFILE`, which startup lets through only with every weight pinned, is still refused advising `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`, without that advice: with a `DBFILE` line the pins are refused next (`FIXINDEX pins model weights`). Test: `test_startup_validation.cpp` (a pin).
- **The journal's OPTIMISER section describes the starting points and the RESTART block as configured.** The run policy prints the RESTART block like an INIT block, under `RESTART init strategy:`: its `METHOD` chain, or `(default -- CONFIG only)` without one (a restart then starts from the CONFIG point), and its `K_TOTAL`, `SEED` and `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`, which appeared nowhere (`RESTART ... K_TOTAL 2 / SEED 77 / WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` printed only `[1] LHS 3`, and nothing without `METHOD`). An INIT, EXPLORE or RESTART block's `K_TOTAL` and `SEED` print under those names; they read `INIT_K` and `INIT_SEED`, the retired keys the parser refuses. `Starting point:` counts the INIT chain's points by origin (`Starting point: 3 points from the INIT chain (3 LHS)`; it read `user-supplied (3 seeds)` for sampled points), and reads `none (the INIT chain produced no point; the optimiser starts from its own default)` when the chain produced none (it read `random (uniform within bounds)`, which the ANNEAL family, starting at the box centre, does not do). An EXPLORE block without an INIT chain of its own reads `(the OUTER INIT points, shared)`, where it read `(default -- CONFIG only)` though the exploration starts from the OUTER's points. Tests: `test_init_strategies.cpp`.
- **A RESTART block's `WARM_ON_MISMATCH` governs the WARM seeds of its `METHOD` chain** (`build_init_strategy` binds a block's policy to the WARM entries of its chain), at startup, where the chain's seeds are resolved, and at each restart: `error` makes a seed that misses an OPTIM key or lies outside the box a startup error, and `skip` drops it, as in an INIT block. A WARM entry took the policy of the INIT block of the spec it is drawn with, which for the RESTART chain is the OUTER spec: the RESTART block's was accepted and ignored (`RESTART ... METHOD WARM seed.pot / WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` with a seed outside the box ran with the seed clamped and a warning, while the journal's run policy printed `WARM_ON_MISMATCH : error` under it), and an INIT block's `WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` stopped the run on the RESTART chain's seed. A RESTART block without the key takes `warn`, the default, whatever the INIT block says. A restart whose chain produces no point (its WARM seed dropped under `skip`, `RANDOM 0`) warns `RESTART r/MAX: the METHOD chain (WARM) produced no starting point; the round starts from the previous round's starting points`, which it did without a word. Tests: `test_init_strategies.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **ASA's stop line names the stop that ended the run.** An exception that leaves an evaluation read `ASA stopped: exception raised during evaluation`, whatever it was; the line now names it: `shutdown requested (SIGINT)` (the signal, when one was received), `RESTART stagnation stop (no new best within AFTER_STAGNATION evaluations)`, or `an evaluation raised an exception (<message>)`. The count `after N host eval(s)` includes the evaluation that raised the stagnation stop or an error, which completed and is in `loss.tadah`: it was one short (16 rows of a run's `loss.tadah` read `after 15 host eval(s)`). A `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL` or signal stop that falls in the reanneal tangents read `(asa exit: INVALID_COST_FUNCTION_DERIV)`, in the summary (`exit=`) too, the code of a failed evaluation during the tangents, for which the documentation advises `Reanneal_Parameters 0`; it reads `IMMEDIATE_EXIT requested during the reanneal tangents (asa_out: INVALID_COST_FUNCTION_DERIV)`: the pending stop's invalid states are a failed derivative to ASA, so asa() returns that code (`asa_out` keeps it). A host failure in the tangents keeps its text. Statuses are unchanged. New: `ExceptionStash::peek()`. Tests: `test_asa.cpp` (the case `ASA host failure during reanneal tangents aborts gracefully` sets `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus 10`: with the default its run ended at `P_TEMP_TOO_SMALL` and never computed the tangents), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (CTest `hpo_shutdown_asa`, `hpo_shutdown_asa_block`).
- **The journal's new-best breakdown names an indexed `PC_ERMSE` or `PC_FRMSE` per dataset** (for example `top: ERMSE[2]=1.78e-03(56.3%)  ERMSE[1]=1.38e-03(43.7%)`): `ERMSE[i]` / `FRMSE[i]`, 1-based as in `PC_ERMSE (i)` and the `L(ERMSE)[i]` column (a dataset the metric does not measure has none). Each is the dataset's part of the metric's loss, `L(ERMSE)[i]` / datasets (the parts add up to `L(ERMSE)[avg]`), shown like every part of the line as its contribution to the run's `loss=`, the mean over the loss terms: `L(ERMSE)[i]` / datasets / loss terms. So the parts of all the terms add up to `loss=` (the line lists the five largest), and each is its share of `L(ERMSE)[avg]` only when the indexed metric is the one loss term, as in that line. It labelled the average of the dataset losses `ERMSE_global` / `FRMSE_global`, the name of the global form, the loss of the average metric, which keeps it. Losses and result files are unchanged. Test: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`The new-best breakdown names indexed PC_ERMSE and PC_FRMSE terms per dataset`).
- **`NLOPT:NLOPT_LD_LBFGS_NOCEDAL` is refused at startup**, in every phase, naming `NLOPT:LD_LBFGS` (the refusal table of the caps registry, as `LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`). NLopt 2.10 names it but does not implement it: `nlopt_optimize()` returns `NLOPT_INVALID_ARGS` for it, so every run of it ended `FAILED -- nlopt invalid argument` once its setup was done. The registry-wide end-status test found it; `test_optimizer_caps_step.cpp` runs NLopt on it. Docs: Tadah.MLIP's *Configuration File* (the `ALGO` row, the `NLOPT` row of *Algorithms by library*, `MULTISTART`) and *Optimizer*.
- **`DLIB:GFS` takes a `MAXTIME` of any length.** dlib counts its run time in nanoseconds, which hold about 292 years, and the run time a longer `MAXTIME` left (the parser takes any positive number of seconds) did not convert (a negative count on x86-64): dlib's deadline had passed when it started, so the search ended after its `INIT` points with calls left, and the journal said `DLIB:GFS stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 1 evaluation(s)`. dlib now gets at most its own no-limit, `FOREVER` (200 years). Test: `test_backend_end_status.cpp`.
- **The CERES driver refuses at startup the settings Ceres cannot take**, naming the value: `FTOL_REL` or `XTOL_REL` below 0, `PARAM max_iter` not an integer from 0 to 2147483647, `max_ls_iter` not one from 1 (`CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT requires PARAM max_ls_iter to be an integer from 1 to 2147483647 (Ceres' max_num_line_search_step_size_iterations), got 0`). Ceres refused the negative values and `max_ls_iter 0` when the search started, before any evaluation, with the `FAILURE` `Invalid configuration. ...`, which the run took for the search's ending (the trailer `COMPLETED` when another phase had a usable loss, the no-usable-loss failure otherwise); a fraction was cut to an int (`max_ls_iter 2.5` ran as 2) and a value past an int converted to an undefined one. Options Ceres refuses all the same are an internal error before any evaluation (`std::logic_error`; `Options::IsValid` before the solve). Docs: Tadah.MLIP's *Configuration File* (the CERES `PARAM` keys). Tests: `test_hpo.cpp` (`CERES validators refuse the tolerances and integer PARAMs Ceres cannot take`), `test_backend_end_status.cpp`.
- **The `ALGO` help of `DLIB:BFGS` says what its stops test**: `FTOL_ABS` the change of the loss from one iteration to the next, `PARAM tol_gradinf` the largest component of the gradient (the BREAKING entry under *Changed*).
- **`tadah hpo` with a non-linear model** (M_KRR with `Kern_RBF`, `Kern_Polynomial`, `Kern_Quadratic`, `Kern_LQ` or `Kern_Sigmoid`, M_BLR with `BF_Polynomial2`): every evaluation's training was refused by MLIP's design-matrix check, so every evaluation got FAILSCORE and no best potential was written; with that check alone fixed, the cached trainer refitted a growing, corrupted basis from the second evaluation on and its random basis made the loss at a fixed point random (MODELS and MLIP 1.6.0). Each evaluation now trains on the same basis draw for the same descriptors, and the potential written is the model evaluated. Test: Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo trains a non-linear KRR model in every evaluation`, `test_pair_tadah_nonlinear.cpp` (`pair_style tadah` evaluates each trained non-linear model as the model predicts, with `BIAS false`, and with `BIAS true` on one species in two LAMMPS instances one after the other, as `PC_LAMMPS` evaluations create them: Tadah.LAMMPS 1.6.0 sets the bias component only for the atoms LAMMPS has). A kernel reads `MPARAMS` only when it is built, and the cached trainer of a worker kept its first one: with `OPTIM MPARAMS` every evaluation after a worker's first fitted with that `MPARAMS`, while `best_pot.tadah` recorded the candidate's value (every evaluation builds its own model now: the *Fixed* entry on evaluations that train a model built from their own candidate). With `BIAS true` on several species, a trained non-linear potential has one intercept component per species, as the descriptor of `pair_style tadah` (see the BIAS entry), and `pair_style tadah` loads it.
- **`FIXINDEX`/`FIXWEIGHT` with a non-linear M_KRR kernel are checked at startup.** The weight count of the `-c` model (`derive_weight_count`, `probe_model`) was its design-matrix width before training, 0 for a kernel without a basis, so `FIXINDEX` was not range-checked (an index past `SBASIS` failed every evaluation) and a run without a training `DBFILE` was accepted. It is now `SBASIS` (`mlip::model_weight_count`; `SBASIS` below 2 is refused at startup), a run without a training `DBFILE` is refused (the basis vectors, which the weights belong to, are drawn from the training data), and when `FIXINDEX` pins every weight such a model is still trained each evaluation, not provisioned from `FIXWEIGHT`. Tests: `test_startup_validation.cpp`, `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp` (`fixweight all-fixed with a non-linear M_KRR kernel: every evaluation trains the model`).
- **A LAMMPS error while HPO reads a `PC_LAMMPS` variable back no longer ends the run.** Reading a variable declared with `--varloss`, `--outvar`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` evaluates its formula in LAMMPS after the script, and that can raise a LAMMPS error: a thermo keyword the script's `thermo_style` does not compute (`variable Pend equal press` under `thermo_style custom step pe`), a divide by zero for one candidate. For `--varloss` and `--outvar` the error escaped the task. Under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` (the only mode of the MPI build) `tadah hpo` then died with SIGSEGV at the next write of the per-evaluation files: the evaluation's catch submitted its partial result, and the row writer read its losses out of bounds. Under `PROCESS` the worker failed the whole script -- a diagnostic `--outvar` took the script's `--varloss` variables with it -- without reading its failvars, kept the errored LAMMPS instance, and journalled `pc-worker task failed (...)` without naming the variable. A `--hotvar` or `--failvar` whose read raised an error was taken as absent, in both modes: no range check and no warm start for the hot value, whose cold scripts ran without it (or on an earlier evaluation's), and a failvar counted as no violation. Now, alike in both modes, each variable is read on its own: an unreadable `--varloss`, `--hotvar` or `--failvar` fails its simulation as a failed run does (its variables take `--failscore`, the failvars still grade band B under `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, a hot script's cold scripts do not run), journalled as `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): reading --varloss E after the script: <LAMMPS message>`; so does the `S` of the `PC_FAILPROBE` line, which grades every failure (`reading PC_FAILPROBE --outvar S after the script: ...`; the evaluation is aborted and, under `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, graded band D, as when the probe's script fails); a variable declared only with `--outvar` (a diagnostic) that cannot be read is `NaN` in `outvar.tadah` and the evaluation keeps its loss, journalled as `PC_LAMMPS --outvar is NaN (<script>, occurrence n): reading --outvar Pend after the script: <LAMMPS message>`. A variable that reads one that cannot be read (`variable Pdiff equal v_Pend-1.0`) raises that one's LAMMPS message: `LammpsRunner::get_variable` first clears the in-progress marks of LAMMPS's variables (`Variable::clear_in_progress`, as `clear` does), which a LAMMPS error leaves set and LAMMPS reports as `Variable Pend: has a circular dependency` at the next read that reaches them. Both lines are rate-limited per run (occurrences 1, 2, 4, ...). The LAMMPS instance that raised the error is discarded and created afresh for the next task, as after an error inside the script. `PcTaskResult::outvar_errors` carries the outvars' read-back errors to the parent, which knows the roles; the worker protocol is version 3. Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp` and `test_lammps_variable_readback.cpp` (`[readback]`), `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (new hidden group `pc_readback_e2e`, CTest `hpo_pc_readback_e2e`, and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback`, which runs the built `tadah` under both isolation modes, `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION` left out of its environment, with and without `FAILSCORE_GRADING`).
- **The LAMMPS message of an error inside a `PC_LAMMPS` script is journalled**, in both isolation modes and rate-limited as above: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (bad.in, occurrence 1): ERROR: Unknown command: bogus_command 1 2 (...)`. Such an evaluation failed with nothing in the journal: `LammpsRunner::run_script` dropped the message (it now hands it to a new optional `error` out-parameter of both `ScriptFile` overloads, with the potential's construction error for the `Context` overload). A timeout still has no message. The line read `pc-worker task failed (...)` and came only from the failures that carried a message (a potential that could not be built, an exception in a worker process); it is now `PC_LAMMPS task failed (...)`, as it is not specific to the worker processes (`THREAD` mode writes it too). An exception inside a task that is not a LAMMPS error (no such case is known; `execute_script_task` no longer throws) fails that task with its message and discards its instance in both modes; under `THREAD` it ended the whole evaluation, counted as a NaN clamp.
- **A hot value LAMMPS refuses to inject before a `PC_LAMMPS` script fails that script**, alike under `PC_ISOLATION THREAD` and `PROCESS`, journalled with the LAMMPS message on the rate-limited `PC_LAMMPS task failed` line: ``PC_LAMMPS task failed (cold.in, occurrence 1): injecting `variable hot_a0 equal 3.25` before the script: ERROR: Cannot redefine variable hot_a0 with a different style (...)``. HPO injects each hot value as `variable <name> equal <value>` before the scripts that read it (and the warm-start seed before its hot script), and LAMMPS refuses that when the name is already defined with another style -- `--invar hot_a0 3.3` or `-var hot_a0 3.3` on the line makes it an index-style variable when the instance is created; on a cached instance, the script deleted the name and redefined it as a string an evaluation earlier -- or is not a LAMMPS name (a target file that defines the name on the line of its `--hotvar`, or on a line whose script reads it, is refused when it is read: see Changed). The error was swallowed and the script ran on the value it had (here 3.3, in every evaluation, with a feasible loss and nothing in the journal). Now the script does not run, its variables take `--failscore` and the evaluation is aborted as for a failed run; nothing is read back from the instance, whose variables are an earlier evaluation's (under `FAILSCORE_GRADING` the failure is graded by the failed fraction), and the instance is discarded. A cold script is injected the hot values it reads -- `${name}`, `v_name` as a whole word (`v_a0b` does not read `a0`) or, for a one-character name, `$name` in its text, `include` files expanded, the test that puts it in the wave after the hot scripts and that the refusal of a line defining such a name makes -- and no other, so a value it does not read cannot fail it (`--invar hot_b 7` on a line that reads only `${hot_a0}`); every hot value went to every such script, which went unnoticed while a refused injection was ignored. A cold script that reads a hot value its hot script did not produce (that script could not start, which does not abort the evaluation) is not run and its variables take `--failscore`; it ran without the value, or in a cached instance on an earlier evaluation's. Tests: `[inject]` in `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `[pc_readback_e2e]` (also two hot scripts and a cold one that reads one of them; a hot script whose instance cannot be created) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` variable read back with no number for its value fails its simulation as one that cannot be read does**, alike in both isolation modes. A variable can be read and still have no number: it is not defined after the script (deleted, or defined in a branch the script did not take), has no single value, or is text (redefined as a string). A `--varloss` variable, the `PC_FAILPROBE` `S`, a `--hotvar` and a `--failvar` (one that is inf or nan too, whatever `FAILSCORE_GRADING`) now fail the simulation -- its variables take `--failscore`, the evaluation is aborted and scores `FAILSCORE` (see the entry on failed simulations below), the failvars grade it under `FAILSCORE_GRADING` -- journalled as `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): reading --varloss F after the script: no value (the variable is not defined, or has no single value)` (or `its value 'abc' is not a number`, `its value 'inf' is not a finite number`); a diagnostic `--outvar` is `NaN` with a `PC_LAMMPS --outvar is NaN (...)` line, and the evaluation keeps its loss. Before, a `--varloss` value's `FAIL_SCORE` term was averaged with the evaluation's other terms: with two other terms (`PC_ERMSE` and another variable) the evaluation scored about `FAILSCORE/3`, below `0.999*FAILSCORE`, and counted as feasible -- it could become the best. A hot value was skipped (no range check, no warm start; the cold scripts ran without it, or on an earlier evaluation's in a cached instance), a failvar counted as no violation under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` (under `OFF` a failvar's value grades nothing and a value above 0 does not fail the simulation, then and now), and a diagnostic was `NaN` with nothing in the journal. Hot and fail values are parsed as whole numbers, as `--varloss` values already were (a trailing text after a number was ignored: a hot value `'3.25abc'` was 3.25, in its window, and warm-started the next evaluation). Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF` the startup warning of a `PC_FAILPROBE` line without `--gate` says what `OFF` changes: `PC_FAILPROBE on line N has no --gate and FAILSCORE_GRADING is OFF, so its --outvar S grades no evaluation; a probe that fails (its script fails, or S cannot be read, is no number, or is negative, not finite or at or above the line's --failscore) fails its evaluation, as any PC_LAMMPS simulation does.` It said `the probe runs as a plain diagnostic sim; no grading, no gate.`, but a probe whose script failed aborted its evaluation under `OFF` too. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` (`--varloss`, `--hotvar`, `--failvar`, the `PC_FAILPROBE` `S` and a diagnostic; text after a number) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` (with the warning) in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` task that fails before its script runs says why in the journal**, alike in both isolation modes and on the same rate-limited line: `PC_LAMMPS task failed (<script>, occurrence n): creating the LAMMPS instance: <LAMMPS message>` for an instance LAMMPS cannot create (an option it refuses), `... rendering the script to a temporary file: <reason>` for a script that cannot be written to its temporary file (`TMPDIR` naming no directory, a full disk). `LammpsRunner::create_instance` dropped LAMMPS' message (it now hands it to a new optional `error` out-parameter), and the parent's `CREATE_FAIL` path returned before the journal line, so such a run failed every evaluation with nothing in the journal. The failure stays an infrastructure one: the script's variables take `--failscore`, the evaluation is not aborted and not graded (it scores `FAILSCORE`, see the entry on failed simulations below), and the creation is retried by the next task. Tests: `[create]` in `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `[pc_readback_e2e]` and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **A single-dash LAMMPS option LAMMPS refuses on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, with LAMMPS' own message: `Error on line 9: PC_LAMMPS: LAMMPS cannot start with this line's command-line options: ERROR: Invalid command-line argument: -sff (...)` (`PC_FAILPROBE: ...` on a probe line). The LAMMPS instance that the startup check of the script's variables (`index_defined_variables`) creates with the line's arguments judges them; its failure was dropped -- the check fell back to reading the script as text -- so the line was accepted and every evaluation failed to create its instance, with nothing in the journal. HPO keeps no list of LAMMPS's options. An option this LAMMPS build cannot honour is refused the same way (`-sf omp` without the `OPENMP` package: `ERROR: Using suffix omp without OPENMP package installed`). Tests: `test_lammps_sim_options.cpp`, `test_hpo_targets_order.cpp`; the option-forwarding pins there and in `test_hpo_parser_contracts.cpp` use `-echo none` and `-nocite`, which every build starts with, in place of `-sf omp` and `-pk omp 4 neigh no`.
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation scores `FAILSCORE` whatever loss its variables are measured with** (`FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`; under `ON` it is graded, as before). A failed simulation -- an error inside the script, a timeout, a variable that cannot be read back or has no number, a hot value LAMMPS refuses to inject or that lands outside its window, a failed `PC_FAILPROBE`, an instance that cannot be created, a script that cannot be rendered -- gives its line's variables their `--failscore`, and the evaluation's loss was the mean of its terms, that line's loss of the `--failscore` value among them. The mean reached `0.999*FAILSCORE` only when the line's loss carried the value past `FAILSCORE`, as `L2` does with the default `--failscore`. Under `L1` or the bounded `TUKEY`, or with a small `--failscore`, the failed evaluation scored below it -- with `PC_ERMSE` and one `L1` `--varloss`, about `FAILSCORE/2`; with a `TUKEY` one, about `(L(ERMSE) + 1)/2` --, counted as feasible and could become the best (`best_pot.tadah`), and a run all of whose evaluations failed ended as a completed one (exit 0, `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`). Now `run_lammps` marks such an evaluation (`EvalResult::sims_failed`: aborted, or a task that could not start) and `sims_loss` scores it `FAILSCORE`; the line's terms in `loss.tadah` are still its loss of the `--failscore` value, and `outvar.tadah` has the value. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` (`L1` and `TUKEY`: a `--varloss` with no value, a script error, a script that cannot be rendered, a hot value outside its window) and `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_readback` (a script error under `L1`, both modes) in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is a failed evaluation**: it scores `FAILSCORE` under `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, and under `ON` a graded band-B failure measured by the failed fraction of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms (`graded_failscore(FS, B, failed/observables, 1)`; the band table of `pc_fail_grading.h` puts observable failures in band B). A term that cannot be computed -- a relaxation whose lattice parameter leaves its `--hotvar a0` window, a value or a loss that is not finite, an observable that throws -- is `FAILSCORE`, and the evaluation's loss was the mean of it and the usable terms, about `FAILSCORE/n` for one failed term of n >= 2: below `0.999*FAILSCORE`, so the evaluation was usable under `OFF` and `ON` alike (no band was recorded) to everything that classifies a loss -- the journal's census (`[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`), the best potential, `STOPVAL`, the annealers' T0 calibration and the gradient provider. Under the default `FAILSCORE` (the largest double) the usable terms vanished in it: the loss was `FAILSCORE/2` all over the region where the term failed, and every finite-difference gradient there was 0. With `PC_ERMSE 0 1` and a cohesive energy whose relaxed lattice parameter leaves its window `[3.0, 3.35]` above `RCUT2B` = 4.94 (`OPTIM RCUT2B (1) 4.0 5.5`, a fixed potential, `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`, `PARAM fd_step 0.05`, from 5.2), before this change: `DLIB:BFGS` ended `FTOL_ABS reached` after 6 evaluations, `LD_LBFGS` `SUCCESS -- generic success` after 3, `LD_SLSQP` roundoff-limited after 3, `CERES:LBFGS` `tol_gradinf reached` after 3, `LD_MMA` spent 45 evaluations there, each with status 0 and `best_pot.tadah` at 8.988e+307; under `FAILSCORE 1e16` the start scored 5.0000000004e+15 and was published as the best, a `TADAH:RANDOM` run inside the region exited 0 with such a best, and `STOPVAL 6e15` was reached at it. (An evaluation with no usable term -- a target whose only term is the failed observable -- or one whose failed terms summed past the largest double, two under the default `FAILSCORE`, scored `FAILSCORE` already.) Now `obs_loss` marks the evaluation (`EvalResult::terms_failed`), and one `assemble_loss` ends every loss assembly (`fit_loss`, `obs_loss`, `sims_loss`): the mean of the terms clipped to `FAILSCORE`, `FAILSCORE` for a failed simulation or term, the graded loss for a classified failure. Such a gradient search stops at its start (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at RCUT2B = 5.2 is 1.797693135e+308)`, status 1, no `best_pot.tadah`; under `ON`, where its finite differences are taken, after the start and its two stencil points, `... every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`, since the failed fraction is constant there; under `ANALYTICAL`, the default with `PC_OBSERVABLE`, and `HYBRID` at a graded failure see the next entry), as at any failed point -- under `FAILSCORE 1e16` it used to follow the gradient of the usable terms out of the region, treating its points as usable, graded or not (`FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`: `DLIB:BFGS` 9 evaluations, `FTOL_ABS reached`, the best 7.7476e+04 at the bound 4.0; `LD_LBFGS` 6, `SUCCESS`; `CERES:LBFGS` 36, `XTOL_REL reached` outside the box; status 0 and a `best_pot.tadah` each; now 3 evaluations and status 1) --, the random run ends without a usable loss, and `STOPVAL` is reached by the next usable loss. The term stays `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` and the value `NaN` in `outvar.tadah`; the journal's band-B count reads `B=... (script or observable failed)` (it said `script failed`). A `--varloss` term without a value in `sims_loss` (the branch for a task whose result was never applied, which no run reaches) fails its evaluation too; it was averaged under `OFF`. `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` remain mutually exclusive, so no simulation follows an observable. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp`.
- **`refit_opt.log`'s curve-mode note advises the keys the fit's basis family reads** (`HPORefitEngine`): for a costly Ceres `REFINE` at high `NMAX` it said `cap its MAXEVAL / use REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` for every basis, and a Blip family refuses `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY`; it names `REFINE`'s `MAXEVAL` and `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` there now, and `REFIT_COMPACT_OPT_EVERY` for the knot families. Test: `test_compact_refit_stages.cpp`.
- **The journal of a second run in one process is that run's alone** (a program that calls `hpo_run` twice). `HPOLog::open` reset only the status, the start tally, the EMBF0, density and occupancy reports, the per-run notes and the finite-difference counters; the census (`Total evaluations`, `NaN/Inf clampings`, `Evaluations that threw`, `Unusable losses` and the verdict, `Graded failures` and the gate counts), the I/O retries, the best and its metrics, the timing and fan-out statistics and the milestones were reset only by `reset_for_test`. A second run that failed 4 of 4 evaluations after a run of 3 journalled `Total evaluations : 7`, `Unusable losses : 4 (57.1%)`, `[!] The majority of evaluations failed`, the first run's evaluations as its fastest and slowest, and in RESULT the first run's best (`Best total loss : 1.0000e+02 (eval 2)`, `Best ERMSE`, `Best potential saved : best_pot.tadah`, which it never wrote); a second run with a worse best had no `BEST` line (`log_new_best` dropped every loss not below the first run's best) and named the first run's evaluation. `open()` now makes the same reset (`reset_run_locked`), and the milestones count from the run's first evaluation (`HPOLog::set_eval_base`, which `hpo_run` gives `HPO_Host`'s evaluation counter), while the evaluation numbers go on from the first run, as before. The hot-variable `rejecting` WARNING is rate-limited by the run's count (`HPOLog::count_in_run`); `warmstart::reject_count`, the process's, is removed: a second run's first reject was, say, the process's 6th, and was not journalled. And `hpo_run` clears every hot-variable seed before `HPOTargets` is built (`warmstart::begin_run`), before the run's `WARM` primes its own: `warm_prime_map`, which a chain's first evaluation falls back to, outlived its run, so a run without `WARM` after one that `WARM`-started from `# HOT` lines started its `--hotvar` values from that seed (`A 42` against its `<initial>` 5). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`[two_runs]`: a second run with no usable loss and one with a worse best against the same run alone, the second run's rejects, a run without `WARM` after a `WARM` run).
- **An evaluation with a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation or a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is never counted as a NaN/Inf clamping** (`HPO_Host::assemble_loss`, the census's `loss_kind`). The mean of its terms came first, and the failure substitutes in it overflow under the default `FAILSCORE` (the largest double): the `L2` of a failed line's `--failscore` value, or two failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms. That set `was_nan_clamped` before the recorded failure made the evaluation `FAILSCORE`, so with `LOSS L2` and no `FAILSCORE` line every failed simulation -- a crashed script, an unreadable `--varloss` (the entries above) -- and every evaluation with two failed observables was journalled `evaluation produced NaN/Inf -- loss clamped to FAIL_SCORE` with the parameter and bound hints, and counted under `NaN/Inf clampings` (`[!] 100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss that was clamped.`); under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` with a `FAILSCORE` whose square or sum overflows (`1e200`, `1e308`) it was counted both there and under `Graded failures`, two counters the DIAGNOSTICS call disjoint. Now the recorded failure decides: the evaluation is a failure (`FAILSCORE`, or its graded loss), counted under `Unusable losses` and its band, with its own journal line (`PC_LAMMPS task failed (...)`, `PC_OBSERVABLE term failed (...)`) and no NaN warning. (One failed observable, or a finite `FAILSCORE` such as `1e16`, did not overflow and was classified correctly.) Tests: `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp` (an unreadable `--varloss` at the default `FAILSCORE`, and under `ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e200`), `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (two failed observables, and under `ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e308`).
- **At a graded failure the optimiser is given no analytical gradient** (`GradientProvider`, under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON`). A graded failure's loss is set by its band's measure (`pc_fail_grading.h`: the failed fraction of the `PC_OBSERVABLE` terms, a skip cap's or a gate's excess), not by the loss terms, and the analytical gradient there is that of the terms that did not fail (the observable chain leaves a failed term out) or of a skip cap's uncapped loss. `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` kept it there. Now `ANALYTICAL` fails the gradient at the caller's graded loss before its analytical pass, nothing evaluated or counted: `DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point (the loss at RCUT2B = 5.2 is 9.99375e+15: a graded failure, whose grading the analytical gradient does not see; GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID takes finite differences there)` after one evaluation, status 1; `HYBRID` runs no analytical pass there and differences every parameter, so the graded slope applies, or the flat-region rule (`... every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`, after the point and its two stencil points). A candidate a gate rejects before training, a graded failure under `ON`, now gets this cause at the caller's loss (it got the host's reason, `the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, which `gradient()` without the loss still gives), and its analytical pass no longer counts an evaluation: with the bump-gate fixture of `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`MAXEVAL 20`) `LD_LBFGS` and `LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND_RESTART` end after 15 evaluations right after a rejected candidate (`NLopt ended right after it (nlopt failure)`), where each rejected candidate counted twice and `MAXEVAL` came first. A gradient asked for without the loss at its point (`gradient()`, which no backend calls: each passes the loss it has just evaluated) still cannot tell a graded failure under `ANALYTICAL`. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (a trained model: the default strategy and `HYBRID`, `DLIB:BFGS`, `LD_LBFGS`, `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT`), `test_gradient_failure.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- **A failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term is journalled with its observable and why it failed**, as a failed `PC_LAMMPS` task is: a `WARNING` `PC_OBSERVABLE term failed (cohesive_energy[1], eval 2, occurrence 1): the relaxed lattice parameter a0 = 3.3532 is outside its --hotvar window [3, 3.35]` at the first, second, fourth, eighth, ... failed term of the run (`HPOLog::count_in_run`). The reason is the observable's (`ObservableEvalResult::failure`, new: `cohesive_energy`'s relaxed a0 outside its window, or an unconverged relaxation whose `--unconverged force-scaled` value is not finite, `... and its --unconverged force-scaled value, target + slope x fmax = <t> + <s> x <fmax> = <v>, is not finite`: forces that were not, or a product that overflowed; a parsed `--unconverged const` value is always finite), a value or loss that is not finite (`its value is not finite (nan)`, `its loss is not finite (value 1e+300, --loss L2)`), or the message of an evaluation that threw (`its evaluation threw: <what>`). A failed term left only `FAILSCORE` in `loss.tadah` and `NaN` in `outvar.tadah`. Tests: `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` (`a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term is journalled with its observable and why it failed`, `a failed PC_OBSERVABLE term's journal line says why: ...`, `an unconverged cohesive_energy relaxation whose --unconverged value is not finite names its strategy`).
- **With `PC_LAMMPS`, an NLopt MLSL or AUGLAG is judged by its local search**: `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` with a derivative-free `INNER` block are accepted (`reject_pc_lammps_grad` asks `obtains_gradients`, as the startup rules for `GRAD_STRATEGY` and the finite-difference `PARAM`s do). They were refused for the `D` of their name (`OPTIMIZER's optimiser NLOPT:LD_AUGLAG is gradient-based`), although MLSL and AUGLAG evaluate their own points without a gradient and such an `INNER` block takes none. Without an `INNER` block they run NLopt's default `LD_MMA` and are still refused, now naming it (`... NLOPT:GD_MLSL takes a gradient: NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take one for their local search, and NLopt's default local optimiser, NLOPT:LD_MMA, is gradient-based; an INNER block with a derivative-free one takes none.`); a gradient-based `INNER` block is refused at that block, as before. Tests: `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp` (`[pc_lammps_grad_drop]`), `hpo_tadah_cli_pc_lammps_local_search` (new CTest entry: `LD_AUGLAG` + `LN_COBYLA` and `GD_MLSL_LDS` + `LN_SBPLX` run with `PC_LAMMPS`).
- **Every row of `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah`, `stats.tadah` and their `best_*` twins has its header's columns.** An evaluation stopped before its simulations (`--skip-above`/`--skip-rel`, or a loss already at `FAILSCORE`), rejected before training by a feasibility gate (`BUMPGATE`, `RHOGATE`, `RHOBARGATE`), or ended by an exception wrote only the cells it had: `TOTAL_LOSS/N` fell under an `L(<variable>)` column of `loss.tadah`, `outvar.tadah` lacked the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` columns, and a gate reject's metric columns held a computed-looking `0`. A cell the evaluation did not compute is now NaN, in the file's own spelling: `nan` in `loss.tadah` and `stats.tadah` (as for a dataset that is not measured), `NaN` in `outvar.tadah` (as for a value that cannot be read). The rows of a best are complete, so `best_*` files do not change. A reader that parses rows with `istream >> double` stops at either spelling; `strtod` and `numpy.loadtxt` read both. Tests: `[pc_readback_e2e]` in `test_hpo_pc_readback_e2e.cpp`.
- **HPO builds with GCC 15.** ASA's headers include `<omp.h>` inside their `extern "C"` blocks (with `ASA_PARALLEL` and OpenMP), and GCC 15's `omp.h` declares C++ templates, which C linkage forbids: `algorithms_asa.cpp` and `tests/test_asa.cpp` stopped with "template with C linkage". Both now include `<omp.h>` first, at C++ linkage, so the include inside is a no-op; the vendored ASA headers are unchanged.
- **`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy` hands on the relaxed a0 as a0 × the Iso scale factor** (`NativeRelaxer::Result::scale_factor`), the quantity its early-reject gate uses. It read `cell(0,0) / nx`, one ulp off for `--supercell 3 3 3` (30 of the 121 a0 values 2.70 … 3.90 Å moved) and wrong for a cell whose first row is not a0 × nx along x; power-of-two supercells (the default 4 4 4) give the same bits as before. Test: `test_cohesive_energy_observable.cpp`.
- **A training or validation structure with two atoms at the same position stops `tadah hpo` at startup**, naming the structure and the atoms (MLIP `NNFinder`): in the training set every evaluation failed with a LAPACK error naming no structure, and in the validation set every loss was nan (clamped to FAILSCORE), so the whole budget was spent before the run gave up. Test: `test_hpo_host_e2e.cpp` (`[coincident]`), and `pair_style tadah` in `test_pair_tadah_coincident.cpp`.
- **HPO builds without a system Boost.** `src/init_strategies.cpp` (the SOBOL init strategy) includes `boost/random/sobol.hpp` and `uniform_real_distribution.hpp`, which only a system Boost supplied; they are now in the Boost MLIP vendors, on HPO's include path through `tadah.mlip`. A drift gate, CTest `hpo_vendored_boost_includes` (`tests/check_vendored_boost.py --cxx <compiler>`), checks that every Boost header HPO includes is vendored and, following the includes as the compiler resolves them in the environment the test runs in (`CPATH` included), that every Boost header they pull in comes from the vendored tree; CTest `hpo_vendored_boost_gate_tool` tests the gate on fake trees. A test pins the first SOBOL points (the Sobol sequence, unchanged).
- **`ErRMSE` carried a wrong unit** in the journal (LOSS FUNCTION, the progress line, RESULT), the `best_pot.tadah` and archive headers and the `--skip-above` message: `ErRMSE`, a relative error times 1000, said `meV/atom`. It says `per mille`; the values are unchanged. (`SRMSE`, in the dataset's eV/Å³, said `GPa` too: its metric is `PC_SRMSE_GPA`, in GPa, see *Changed*.)
- **`PC_FRMSE` with `FORCE false` (or no `FORCE` key) stopped the run with a segmentation fault** at the first evaluation, with every optimiser and gradient strategy and in refit polish runs: the validation prediction took FORCE/STRESS from the trained model's context, predicted no forces, and `Analytics::calc_f_rmse` read past an empty vector. `MODEL M_KRR`, whose saved context dropped FORCE/STRESS, crashed even with `FORCE true`, and `PC_SRMSE` (which `PC_SRMSE_GPA` replaces) compared the reference stresses with zero, a constant RMS(reference) for every candidate (with `--skip-above` it could reject every candidate), with `STRESS false` and, for `MODEL M_KRR`, also with `STRESS true`. Validation now predicts what its metrics measure (see *Changed*). `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` advised `HYBRID` for this setup, which then crashed too.
- **`tadah.hpo` linked NLopt `PRIVATE`** although its public headers `nlopt_adapter.h` and `run_policy_base.h` include `<nlopt.hpp>`: `test_hpo` compiled only when `CPATH` happened to name an installed `nlopt.hpp`. NLopt is now linked `PUBLIC`.
- **Finite-difference points outside the OPTIM box**, with either step rule, CENTRAL or FORWARD: the central stencil evaluated one point outside within a step of a bound (in 1.5.0, `DLIB:BFGS` searches over `RCUT2B` in [4, 6] on the Ta data recorded their best at RCUT2B = 6.000002); Ceres' rule, the default under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`, and `fd_kernel ceres` ran inside Ceres, which has no bounds; FORWARD stepped above the box at its upper bound; RIDDERS started 0.32 max(|u|, 1) away. See *Changed*.
- **Settings that did nothing:** `PARAM diff_method` with the in-house kernel, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` under `HYBRID` (honoured now), an invalid `fd_kernel` under `HYBRID`, and `fd_step`/`fd_kernel`/`diff_method` in an INNER block, on a derivative-free optimiser or under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` (refused now). `diff_method 2` (RIDDERS) ignored `fd_step` (refused now). The `NOISE` block's `AUTO` on a derivative-free optimiser or ASA, or beside a written `PARAM fd_step` (refused now), and on an `MLSL` or `AUGLAG` whose local search takes gradients (it derives `fd_step` there now). `PARAM fd_kernel 1` on `DLIB:BFGS` was "not supported" by a message that listed it as supported, and the `CERES` list left it out.
- **`HYBRID` returned a zero gradient when no parameter had an analytical derivative** (a setup the analytical gradient does not cover, e.g. `OALGO 1`, a candidate rejected before training, a failed `PC_LAMMPS` chain), although the journal said every parameter used finite differences: `DLIB:BFGS` ended `COMPLETED` at its start after two evaluations (`tests/data/ta/target_files/dlib_bfgs.hpo` without its `PC_LAMMPS` line, with `GRAD_STRATEGY HYBRID`). It differences every parameter now. The same `HYBRID` evaluated the loss at the point again for a one-sided difference right after the optimiser had (an evaluation more, a duplicate row, and with `--hotvar` warm starts a value the optimiser had not seen); it takes the optimiser's own now.
- **NLopt points a rounding error outside the OPTIM box under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`** were evaluated there, counted and warned about as points outside the box: `LN_COBYLA` pressed against `CGRID2B[2] <= 1.7` asked for 1.7000000000000002 in 29 of 60 evaluations. They are evaluated on the bound (see *Changed*). Tests: `test_hpo_rhoform.cpp` (`[snap_e2e]`: HPO_Host at 1, 8 and 16 ulps past each bound, AUTO and a `NONE` `LOG` box), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`LN_COBYLA` under `NONE`).
- **`LOG` and `ASINH` box ends** came back one ulp outside the box (`SearchSpace::to_real`: `OPTIM X 0.01 10 LOG` at its upper bound recorded X = 10.000000000000002; a linear AUTO box's upper end in about 2% of boxes): the ends of the internal box map to exactly lo and hi, and a point inside it into [lo, hi].
- The journal said `PC_ISOLATION: PROCESS requested but no PC_LAMMPS sims are configured -- nothing to isolate` when `PROCESS` was only the serial build's default, such as in a run with a `PC_OBSERVABLE` line and no `PC_LAMMPS` line; the line says `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS (the default): the target file has no PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE line, so no worker process starts`, without `(the default)` for a `PROCESS` set by `PC_ISOLATION` or `TADAH_PC_ISOLATION`. Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp`.
- Five tests whose names contain `;` never ran under CTest: the vendored Catch discovery script split each into two entries, which matched no test case and still passed. They cover the NLopt step plan, `PcFailState` band grading, the validation filter and ESHIFT forwarding, the volume observable on anisotropic cells, and the removed `LADDER_ADAPT_UPDATES` key. All five now run and pass.
- The explicit `hpo_anneal_statistical` and `hpo_corana1987_protocol` CTest entries pass `--warn NoTests`, so a hidden tag that matches no test case fails instead of passing.
- A bare root `LOSS HUBER` / `LOSS TUKEY` used a zero parameter (Huber then scored every potential as 0). `LossFunction::resolve_params` is now the single place for defaults (Huber 50, Tukey 4.685) and validation, shared by root `LOSS`, `PC_LAMMPS --varloss` and `PC_OBSERVABLE --loss`; non-positive, non-numeric and surplus parameters are rejected.
- `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` and `TADAH_PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` are parsed strictly (finite, >= 0); a malformed environment value was silently ignored.
- Error messages: MULTISTART / PRESTAGE-TOPK rejection of single-seed global backends states the real constraint; PRESTAGE without `EXPLORE` names the current grammar (it referenced the removed `PRESTAGE ... ENDPRESTAGE` block); the CERES `STEP` hint no longer names a `PARAM` the adapter rejects; INIT errors name the `METHOD` (or `STRATEGY`) line as written.
- A configuration without any `OPTIM` line is rejected at startup for NLOPT, DLIB and CERES (it previously failed inside the optimiser after the whole setup had run); the TADAH-native optimisers still evaluate the start point once.
- Negative numbers after `PC_LAMMPS` options (`--varloss` targets/weights, loss parameters, `--hotvar` bounds) are values, not options.
- `PC_LAMMPS` lines keep their configuration order, so `outvar`/loss columns and the potential header no longer come out permuted with more than 16 lines. The `--ncpu` sort that permuted them is removed with `--ncpu` (see Removed); `failprobe_idx` is the probe's configuration position.
- **A run stopped by `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` or `SIGUSR2` ends the same way with every backend and strategy, and `hpo_run()` returns 128 + the signal number (130, 143, 140 on Linux).** It returned 0, and `HPOEngine` ignored its result anyway. NLopt reported the signal as `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged ...)`; `MULTISTART` logged it as a failed start and ran the remaining starts, each failing at once, before ending `COMPLETED`; `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` turned a signal during the seed evaluation or a T0 calibration pass into an error (`STOPPED with error: shutdown requested ...`); and a signal during `NOISE_CALIBRATION` escaped `hpo_run()` without writing the queued history rows or the journal trailer. `drive_run()` (`run_driver.h`) now owns every ending of the evaluating part of `hpo_run()`, calibration included: it drains the result queue, writes one trailer (`SHUTDOWN (<signal>)`, `FAILED -- <message>`, `FAILED -- non-standard exception`, `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <error>`, or the status of the optimiser run that ended the run; *Changed*, the ending rule) and rethrows errors after it; `HPOEngine` returns the status and reports a non-standard exception as a failure instead of terminating. `ctest -L shutdown` raises a real signal against `hpo_run()` for DLIB BFGS, DLIB GFS (four threads), NLopt SBPLX, CERES STEEPEST_DESCENT, ASA (serial and block-parallel), ANNEAL_PT (two threads), ANNEAL_PA (serial and four threads), MULTISTART, PRESTAGE (during the exploration) and `NOISE_CALIBRATION`, one process each, and checks that `best_pot.tadah` holds the lowest `loss.tadah` row and that `loss.tadah` has one row per evaluation, numbered from 1. TADAH `RANDOM`, `GRID` and serial `ANNEAL` and the NLopt global algorithms have no signal test of their own; `tadah_hpo_exit_status_*` signals the `tadah` binary running TADAH `RANDOM`.
- **`DLIB:GFS` with `THREADS` > 1 no longer aborts the process** (exit 134, core dump, no trailer) when a signal or a `RESTART` stagnation stop reaches its concurrent evaluations. dlib's thread pool rethrew the first exception while the other evaluations were still running on `find_min_global`'s already-destroyed frame, and called `abort()` when a second one had thrown; so `RESTART` never got past its first restart. The adapter now keeps the exception, stops `find_min_global` through its stop condition (later evaluations do not run), lets the running ones finish, and rethrows the exception once it has returned (the NLopt and ASA pattern).
- When several concurrent evaluations throw before the optimiser stops (the `ANNEAL_PT` / `ANNEAL_PA` worker pool, ASA's block pool, `DLIB:GFS`), the run ends with the exception that matters most: an error, then a signal stop, then a stagnation stop (`ExceptionStash`, `run_stop.h`). The pools kept the first one, so a failure to save `best_pot.tadah` that raced a signal or a stagnation stop was dropped: the run ended as a shutdown (exit 130) or restarted. `HPO_Host<>::StagnationStop` is now one type for every host (`tadah::hpo::StagnationStop`), so the pools can rank it.
- **`RESTART` fires with NLopt backends (including the `INNER` search of MLSL/AUGLAG), with `DLIB:GFS` at `THREADS` > 1 (above), under `MULTISTART`, and under `PRESTAGE` with `TOPK` > 1**; it did not (`PLAIN` runs of most other backends, and a `TOPK 1` `PRESTAGE` refinement, did restart). The NLopt adapter keeps the first exception its objective raises, force-stops NLopt and rethrows it after `optimize()` (the ASA driver's pattern), instead of letting NLopt's C++ wrapper turn it into `nlopt failure`; `MULTISTART` lets the signal stop and `durable_best::Error` through and reports a stagnating start to `RESTART`, instead of treating them as a failed start. **Behaviour change:** a restart re-runs the whole strategy from the fresh points — the `MULTISTART` from its first start, with `min(N_STARTS, K)` starts for the *K* points the RESTART chain draws, or both `PRESTAGE` phases. Each `MULTISTART` start and each `PRESTAGE` refinement seed has a stagnation window of its own; after the last start or seed, a batch in which any of them stagnated restarts the whole strategy, whether or not it improved the global best, as a `PLAIN` run that improved and then stagnated does (the entry "MULTISTART, PRESTAGE and RESTART" under *Fixed*). Test: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`under PRESTAGE, a refinement that improved the global best and then stagnated restarts the PRESTAGE`). A `PRESTAGE` exploration without an `INIT` block of its own now restarts from the fresh points: `RestartPolicy` replaced only the outer spec's points, so every restart replayed the exploration from the original ones and never used the RESTART points (`OptimizerSpec::share_init_with_nested_phases()` is now the one rule for the parser and `RestartPolicy`). Under `PRESTAGE`, a stagnating exploration still hands its top-K to the refinement, which now starts with a stagnation window of its own (it inherited the exploration's, so its first evaluation already crossed the threshold). The stagnation watchdog is disarmed however `RestartPolicy` ends.
- **Each `RESTART` round draws new random numbers.** Restart *r* (1 … `MAX`) runs every phase that has a `SEED` with a seed derived from it: the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser with `SEED + r*S`, where *S* is the number of starts the strategy runs from `SEED` (`OptimizerSpec::seeded_starts()`: `N_STARTS` under `MULTISTART`, `TOPK` under `PRESTAGE`, whose refinement runs candidate *i* with `SEED + i`, and 1 under `PLAIN`), so the starts of round *r* use `SEED + r*S + i` and never another round's seeds (`SEED 7`, `TOPK 3`, `MAX 2`: 7–9, 10–12, 13–15); and an `EXPLORE` optimiser with `SEED + r` (`OptimizerSpec::derive_restart_seeds()`, applied by `RestartPolicy` next to `share_init_with_nested_phases()`). A restart kept every `SEED`, so a seeded stochastic optimiser or exploration (`TADAH:RANDOM`, the annealers, ASA, NLopt's stochastic globals) replayed round 0's random sequence from its new start point and largely found the same points again. Round 0 keeps `SEED`, so a run is unchanged up to its first restart. The journal's `RESTART r/MAX` line lists the round's seeds.
- **NLopt takes its random seed from the `SEED` of the phase it runs, and from the phase's `INIT`-block `SEED` only when the phase has no `SEED`** (`make_nlopt()`). The `INIT` `SEED` took precedence, so in an NLopt phase that set both keys neither the start seeds `SEED + i` of `MULTISTART` and of the `PRESTAGE` refinement nor the `RESTART` seeds reached NLopt: every start and every round drew the same random numbers (for example the same `GN_CRS2_LM` population). The `INIT` `SEED` still draws the `INIT` points. Round 0 changes only for an NLopt phase that sets both keys. The startup warning for an `INIT` `SEED` that no `INIT` method uses says it seeds NLopt only when the optimiser has no `SEED`.
- **`SEED` inside an `INNER` block is a startup error that names the line**: `Error on line N: SEED is not accepted inside an INNER block: NLopt draws the INNER search from the enclosing run's random stream; the enclosing STRATEGY block takes SEED` (or `EXPLORE` / `REFINE` block, wherever the `INNER` block is). MLSL and AUGLAG run their `INNER` search inside the enclosing block's NLopt run, and NLopt has one random stream per run; `make_nlopt()` seeded it for the enclosing block and then again for an `INNER` `SEED`, which therefore replaced the enclosing block's seed. Neither the `MULTISTART` and `PRESTAGE`-refinement start seeds `SEED + i` nor the `RESTART` seeds reached NLopt: with `SEED 10` on a `MULTISTART` `AUGLAG` and `SEED 20` on its `GN_CRS2_LM` `INNER` (a global search, which an `INNER` block no longer takes: *Changed*), all three starts drew the population of seed 20. A configuration that sets `SEED` in an `INNER` block now fails to load; with the `SEED` in the enclosing block, the starts draw from 10, 11 and 12. `make_nlopt()` seeds NLopt once, for the block that runs it, and refuses an `INNER` spec that carries a seed (`SEED` or `INIT` `SEED`; the grammar has no `INIT` block inside `INNER`); `derive_restart_seeds()` and the `RESTART r/MAX` journal line no longer handle `INNER` seeds, and the unknown-key error of an `INNER` block no longer lists `SEED`.
- **A launch on more than one MPI rank (`TADAH_HPO_MPI`) with a `RESTART` block fails at startup**, before any evaluation: `a run on 2 MPI ranks takes no RESTART block (AFTER_STAGNATION …, MAX …): each rank counts its own evaluations without a new best, so one rank would restart without the others; a run with a RESTART block runs on one rank`. It started, and at the first stagnation the rank that stagnated restarted and blocked in the driver's next collective while its peers exited with `worker failed on another MPI rank`, so the job hung until the scheduler killed it. `hpo_mpi_dispatch_refusal()` is the one rule behind `hpo_mpi_dispatch_supported()` and `hpo_run()`'s error, and `RestartSpec::active()` the one "RESTART is on" rule (both `AFTER_STAGNATION` and `MAX` positive). One rank keeps `RESTART`.
- **The journal trailer of a run that ends by itself is the status of the optimiser run that ended it** (under `MULTISTART` the last start, under `PRESTAGE` the refinement; every driver records one, *Changed*: why each search ended); when the `RESTART` budget ends the run it is `STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after R restart(s)`. Every optimiser run now starts without a status (`HPOLog::clear_status()` in `run_inner`), and ASA no longer records one for a run that an exception ended. The trailer repeated whatever an earlier phase had left: `MAXEVAL_REACHED` from an NLopt exploration under a DLIB refinement, or `ASA: exception raised during evaluation` from the ASA run the last stagnation stop had ended.
- **A `PRESTAGE` refinement starts only from the exploration's usable losses.** `HPO_Host`'s top-K buffer took every completed evaluation's loss, a failed one included (`FAILSCORE`, a graded failure), and kept it while it held fewer than `TOPK` entries, so while the exploration had fewer than `TOPK` distinct usable losses a refinement seed started at a failed point, and an exploration whose every evaluation failed so was refined from failed points only. (A candidate a gate rejected before training, `BUMPGATE`, `RHOGATE` or `RHOBARGATE`, and an evaluation that threw never reached the buffer.) The buffer takes a usable loss only (`loss_is_feasible`, the rule of best publication). With fewer candidates than `TOPK` the refinement runs from those and the journal says so, `PRESTAGE: 4 usable candidate(s) for TOPK 6 (the exploration made 6 evaluation(s), 2 without a usable loss); refining 4`. With none, the refinement runs from the outer spec's own points (the OPTIMIZER-level `INIT` points, or a `RESTART` round's fresh ones; after a stagnation stop of the exploration too), as it did for an exploration without a candidate, and the `WARNING` says why: `PRESTAGE: no evaluation of the exploration produced a usable loss (9 evaluation(s)); refining from the OPTIMIZER-level INIT point(s) instead` (it read `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`; an exploration that made no evaluation is an error, below). A run that finds no usable loss there either ends as one (`STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`, status 1). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`PRESTAGE: the refinement starts only from usable losses, fewer than TOPK when fewer exist`, `PRESTAGE: an exploration without a usable loss leaves the refinement to the OPTIMIZER-level INIT points`, `PRESTAGE under RESTART: an exploration that stagnates without a usable loss hands over to a refinement from the INIT points`, `PRESTAGE: an exploration whose every candidate the bump gate rejects is refined from the OPTIMIZER-level INIT point`).
- **A `PRESTAGE` exploration that asked for no point is an error** (`StagedPolicy`: `std::logic_error`, `PRESTAGE: the exploration (EXPLORE block) TADAH:RANDOM returned without evaluating a point, so it has no candidate to refine (it asked for no point: no call was answered from the run's record either, and every exploration a configuration sets asks for at least one point; its driver, or a spec built outside the parser, asked for none)`; the run ends `FAILED`, status 1). The refinement ran from the outer spec's own points after a journal `WARNING`, `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`. No configuration the parser and startup accept gets there: every exploration driver asks for at least one point (`MAXEVAL` is at least 1, and every driver makes its first call whatever `MAXTIME`; `ASA` and a `DLIB:GFS` search without an `INIT` point made none when `MAXTIME` had passed before it, the next entry), every evaluation is counted (a throwing one included), and a stagnation stop is raised at an evaluation or at a call answered from the run's record (`count_reused_repeat`); so it is a driver that returned without asking, or a spec built outside the parser that asks for nothing, which the refinement hid. An exploration whose every call the run's record answers (the *Changed* entry on points a search asks for again: one record for the run) makes no evaluation and is no error: its recorded points are its candidates (a `RESTART` round retracing round 0; under `NOISE CALIBRATION` an `EXPLORE` of `MAXEVAL 1` whose one call is the calibrated seed point: `NLopt finished: MAXEVAL_REACHED -- maximum evaluations exhausted after 0 of MAXEVAL 1 evaluation(s); ...`, then the refinement). This entry said that every exploration evaluates at least one point and that a stagnation stop is raised inside a counted evaluation, which the run's record made false, and so did the error's parenthesis (`every exploration a configuration sets evaluates at least one point: ...`). An exploration whose every evaluation failed is refined from the `INIT` points as before (the entry above). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`PRESTAGE: an exploration that made no evaluation is an error, not a refinement from the INIT points`, `PRESTAGE: an exploration whose every call the run's record answers makes no evaluation and hands on its candidate`).
- **A search makes its first evaluation whatever `MAXTIME`**; `MAXTIME` keeps the later ones from starting, as it did. `ASA` read `MAXTIME` before every evaluation, its first included, and `DLIB:GFS` without an `INIT` point did not start `find_min_global` once `MAXTIME` had passed (and dlib reads its run time before every call), so a `MAXTIME` that had passed before the search's first evaluation (one shorter than the driver's setup, `MAXTIME 1e-9`) left it without one: a `PLAIN` run ended with status 1, `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`, and a `PRESTAGE` exploration had nothing to refine (the refinement ran from the `INIT` points after the `WARNING` `PRESTAGE: produced no candidates -- running outer optimiser as-is`). The TADAH drivers, which read `MAXTIME` after an evaluation, NLopt and `DLIB:GFS`'s `INIT` points made their first evaluation already. `ASA` reads `MAXTIME` once the search has made an evaluation; a `DLIB:GFS` search that made none after `find_min_global` calls it once more for one call without a run-time limit, which evaluates the point dlib starts every search with (`DLIB:GFS stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 1 evaluation(s)`, `ASA stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 1 host eval(s)`). Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`MAXTIME: a search makes its first evaluation when MAXTIME has passed before it`).
- A failure to save `best_pot.tadah` (`durable_best::Error`) ends NLopt and `MULTISTART` runs as an error, like every other backend; NLopt reported it as convergence and `MULTISTART` moved on to the next start.
- NLopt: an error raised by the objective or the gradient provider is reported as an error, not as the algorithm's `nlopt failure` self-termination; and `NLOPT_ROUNDOFF_LIMITED` is a graceful stop (`STOPPED -- nlopt roundoff-limited (...)`) — the adapter compared the message with `roundoff-limited`, which NLopt never produces, and failed the run. The unused `HPO_Host::nlopt_adapter_cpp`, which bypassed this handling, is removed.
- The final flush (`HPO_Host::flush_remaining`) writes results stranded behind a missing iteration, in iteration order, with a journal warning (`N result(s) stranded behind missing iteration k`); it stopped at the gap and dropped them silently. A row whose write fails is still reported (`dropping iter=N`).
- A new journal does not inherit the termination status of a previous run in the same process (`HPOLog::open` clears it), and it is written even when that run left its journal open (it failed during its setup, before its trailer): `HPOLog::open` opened the new file on the still-open stream, which failed silently, so nothing of the new run reached its journal. `HPO_Host::reset_globals_for_test()` also resets the result writer and the stagnation watchdog, so an in-process test no longer receives another test's unflushed results.
- `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` workers ignore `SIGUSR2` as well as `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` (`kPcWorkerIgnoredSignals`, which a test checks against the signals `core::SignalHandler` handles). A `SIGUSR2` sent to the whole process group or job step killed the workers, and the evaluations they were running became FAILSCOREs.
- **`STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART` with a global optimiser that takes one starting point, and `TYPE PRESTAGE` with a configured `TOPK` > 1 and such a refinement optimiser, are startup errors** (`validate_hpo_setup`), raised before the host is built or any data is loaded; so is `TYPE PRESTAGE` without an `EXPLORE` block. `validate_hpo_setup` now runs as soon as the configuration is read, before the worker pool is sized and before any `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker is started (it ran after both), and the journal records a startup error on an `ERROR` line (`ERROR     startup: <message>`): it went to stderr only, and the journal ended without a word. The policies found out only when they ran: after the host had loaded the data, and under `PRESTAGE` after the whole exploration, only when it had harvested more than one candidate (and not at all when it had harvested one). `PRESTAGE` has its own message: `STRATEGY TYPE PRESTAGE with TOPK 3 cannot refine with TADAH:ANNEAL: ... A local or multi-seed refinement optimiser (...) refines each candidate, and TOPK 1 refines the best candidate once.` The rules live once in `strategy_rules.{h,cpp}` (`validate_run_policy`, `require_multistart_backend`, `require_prestage_explore`, `require_prestage_refinement`); `MultiStartPolicy::validate` and `StagedPolicy::validate` forward to them and still run when the policies do. The alternatives the messages list are sorted (`format_caps_list`); they came in hash order.
- **An optimiser without `LIB` or `ALGO` is a parse error that names its block**, at the line that closes it: the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser (under `PRESTAGE` the refinement, `STRATEGY` level or `REFINE` block) at `ENDOPTIMIZER`, an `EXPLORE` block at `ENDEXPLORE`, and a file without an `OPTIMIZER` block at its end: `Error on line 8: the OPTIMIZER block names no optimiser: LIB and ALGO are not set; LIB and ALGO in its STRATEGY block (or directly inside OPTIMIZER) name it`. It failed at run time with `unknown optimizer NOT SET:NOT SET`. A block the file leaves open is refused as open (see *Changed*). An `INNER` block needs both too, checked at its `ENDINNER` line, naming the block it sits in: `Error on line 10: the INNER block inside STRATEGY names no optimiser: ALGO is not set; LIB and ALGO inside the INNER block name the local optimiser that NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG run`; one without `ALGO` failed inside NLopt once the run had started, and one without `LIB` skipped the `STEP` checks. The parser's checks that only tolerated a missing `LIB`/`ALGO` are gone. This is the rule of the hpotarget grammar, and of a `tadah refit` stage block that runs an optimiser (below).
- **An `INNER` block needs an optimiser that runs one, and `LIB NLOPT`**: only NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG algorithms run an `INNER` search, and they run it with NLopt. Both are startup errors naming the optimiser the block belongs to (`strategy_rules`: `require_inner_backend`, called by `validate_run_policy` and again by `run_inner`): `DLIB:BFGS takes no INNER block (the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser): ...`, `the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser names DLIB:BFGS, but NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS runs its INNER search with NLopt: ...`. The first was reported only once the host had loaded the data; with another `LIB` the run failed inside NLopt once it had started (`wrong algorithm string`).
- **The `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` gradient rules apply to the `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` block and its `INNER` block**, as they did to the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser and its `INNER` block: with `PC_LAMMPS`, a gradient-based optimiser and any `GRAD_STRATEGY` are parse errors there, and so is `GRAD_STRATEGY NUMERICAL` with `PC_OBSERVABLE` (`--hpotarget defines PC_LAMMPS but EXPLORE's optimiser NLOPT:LD_LBFGS is gradient-based. A PC_LAMMPS or PC_FAILPROBE line requires a gradient-free optimiser ...`). `NLOPT LD_LBFGS` in `EXPLORE` failed at once inside NLopt (`nlopt failure`) and the run reported success after a wasted exploration; a `GRAD_STRATEGY` there failed only after the first evaluation.
- **`N_STARTS` and `BUDGET_PER_START` outside `TYPE MULTISTART`, and `TOPK` outside `TYPE PRESTAGE`, are rejected at their own line** (`N_STARTS is not accepted with STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN: only TYPE MULTISTART runs N_STARTS starts, so it would have no effect; N_STARTS takes effect under STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART`). They were parsed under every `TYPE` and silently ignored. The check runs when the `STRATEGY` block ends, so `TYPE` may come before or after the key. A `TYPE` line after an `EXPLORE` or `REFINE` block, which in 1.5.0 took it out of `PRESTAGE` (the run silently became `PLAIN`, without the exploration), is refused as a second `TYPE` line (a key of the target file that takes one value is set once), and a second `STRATEGY` block is an error at its line (`STRATEGY block given more than once (the first opens on line 4); ...`): it reopened the first, and its `TYPE` replaced the one the first block's keys had been checked against.
- **An optimiser key inside a `NOISE` block is refused at its line**: `Error on line 7: unknown key 'LIB' inside the NOISE block opened on line 6 (only AUTO and CALIBRATION are valid; ENDNOISE closes it)`. A `NOISE` block takes `AUTO` and `CALIBRATION` only, and the message names them for every other key too. 1.5.0 applied an optimiser key there (`LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL`, `SEED` and the others) to the main search, as if it stood directly inside `OPTIMIZER`; such a line belongs outside the `NOISE` block.
- **Numbers in the hpotarget file are parsed strictly** (`strict_parse.{h,cpp}`: `parse_uint`, `parse_finite`, `single_value`, `parse_lammps_timespec`, `parse_script_timeout`). Every numeric key takes one whole token that represents what it holds, else it is an error at its own line: `Error on line 6: MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '1e5'`, `FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got 'abc'`, `N_STARTS requires a value`, `QFLUSH takes one value (unexpected '7')`. `iss >> v` and `std::stod` read a prefix (`5x` as 5, `MAXEVAL 1e5` as 1 and then complained about `e5`), stored 0 for a non-number (`FAILSCORE abc` scored every failure 0, `MAX abc` switched `RESTART` off), wrapped a negative into a huge unsigned value (`N_STARTS -2`, `MAXEVAL -1`), read an uninitialised variable for a missing `N_STARTS`/`TOPK`/`MAX`/`AFTER_STAGNATION`, and ignored extra tokens. Covered: `FAILSCORE`, `QFLUSH` (≥ 0), `DIGITS` (1–15), `OUTPUT`/`BOUTPUT` (≥ 1), `DUMP`/`BDUMP` (the directory is created only once the line is valid), `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` (now a single value), `PC_FAILPROBE --gate/--s0` (`--s0` a positive number), `N_STARTS` (≥ 2), `TOPK` (≥ 1), `BUDGET_PER_START` (1 to 2147483647), `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION`/`MAX` (≥ 1), `INIT K_TOTAL`/`SEED` and `METHOD <K>` (now checked at the `METHOD` line; `RANDOM`, `LHS` and `SOBOL` take `K` from 1, in the same message: `K 0` was `INIT METHOD LHS K must be > 0`), `NOISE CALIBRATION`/`AUTO`, `LOG_HP`, the optimiser attributes (`MAXEVAL`, `SEED`, `POPULATION`, `VECTOR_ARR`, `THREADS`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `FTOL_*`, `XTOL_*`; `LIB`/`ALGO` need a value; `MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block (below), the `int` NLopt counts evaluations in, as are `BUDGET_PER_START`, each start's `MAXEVAL`, and `tadah refit`'s `MAXITER`, the budgets the refit hands NLopt and Ceres as an `int`: the NLopt adapter converted `MAXEVAL` implicitly, so `MAXEVAL 4294967299` ran 3 evaluations and `2147483648` became a negative budget, which NLopt reads as no limit, and `make_nlopt` now refuses such a value in a spec built another way; `THREADS` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block: `THREADS 0` was accepted, and at the `OPTIMIZER`/`STRATEGY` level the pool quietly ran one worker; `ScriptExecutor`, `init_script_executor` and `PcWorkerPool` now reject a width below 1 instead of raising it to 1; `MAXTIME` is a positive number of seconds (`parse_positive`: `MAXTIME must be a positive number of seconds, got '-5'`), left out for no limit: 0 or a negative value meant no limit to NLopt and `TADAH` and a startup error to ASA and the annealers), `STEP` (the block-wide fraction and an override's must be finite numbers: `STEP inf` failed only when the run started, and `STEP 5x` was taken for an override key; an override's index list has the strict grammar of the `OPTIM` lines, where `STEP CGRID2B(3x) 0.3` dropped `3x` with a message on stderr and applied to every index), numeric `PARAM` values (`PARAM fd_step inf` failed only when the optimiser ran, and `PARAM fd_step 5x` was kept as a word no backend read), the single-value keys (`TYPE`, `PC_ISOLATION`, `FAILSCORE_GRADING`, `BUMPGATE`, `OPTIM_SCALE`, `WARM_ON_MISMATCH`), the explicit `OPTIM` bounds (`0.1x` was read as 0.1), and the `PC_LAMMPS` options `--varloss` target/weight/loss parameter (a negative weight stays allowed), `--failscore`, `--hotvar` min/max/initial and `--failvar` scale: an optional `--hotvar`/`--failvar` value that did not parse used to be passed on to LAMMPS as a command-line argument. A configuration that relied on the lenient reading now fails at startup with the line.
