Every real number has one grammar (`parse_number`), the decimal one LAMMPS reads (`utils::is_double`: an optional sign, digits with at most one decimal point, an optional exponent), converted with `std::from_chars` (locale-independent): `parse_finite`, `parse_lammps_timespec`, `PC_<METRIC>` (`parse_metric_number`), `LOSS`, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`, the `OPTIM` numbers, `STEP`, `PARAM` (the `INIT_MODE JITTER` extras too), the `PC_LAMMPS` and `PC_OBSERVABLE` options and the `tadah refit` keys all use it, and `is_number_shaped` decides which optional token is meant as a number. `std::stod` also read hexadecimal floats: `FAILSCORE 0x1p40` loaded as 1.0995e+12 and `STEP 0x1p-3` as 0.125, while `PC_<METRIC>` rejected them; they are errors (`FAILSCORE must be a positive finite number, got '0x1p40'`). A number below the smallest normal double is rejected and says so (`... got '1e-320' (too small: ...)`): a subnormal (`1e-320`), or one that underflows to 0 (`1e-400`). It was reported as not a finite number, and `PC_<METRIC>` accepted it; LAMMPS accepts it too, so this is HPO's own rule. The test is on the bits of the double, like the finiteness test, so it holds with denormals-are-zero on (an executable linked with `-ffast-math`), where a subnormal compares equal to 0. The `PC_<METRIC>` errors name the key and the value (`PC_ERMSE <weight> must be a finite number, got '1x'`; it was `Expected a finite number, got '1x'`), and the `OPTIM` and optimiser-attribute errors name their line once (`Error on line 9: OPTIM WATOM: <high> must be ...`; it was `Error on line 9: OPTIM WATOM on line 9: ...`). A value in the grammar but outside its key's range is named too: `PC_EMAE <weight> must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`, `PC_EMAE --skip-above must be a positive number, got '-1'`, `PC_FAILPROBE --s0 must be a positive number, got '-1'`, `--failvar F <scale> must be a positive number, got '0'` (they were `PC_EMAE weight must be nonnegative`, `... requires a positive number`, `PC_FAILPROBE --s0 must be finite and > 0`, `--failvar F: <scale> must be > 0`).
- **A `PC_LAMMPS` option takes only its own values.** A token after an HPO option's values (`--maxtime 1:30 00`, a typo for `1:30:00`; `--failscore 100 5`) was passed to LAMMPS as a command-line argument, so every evaluation failed on it (`Invalid command-line argument: 00`) and the run reported no best loss. It is an error at the line naming the option (`PC_LAMMPS --maxtime takes one value (unexpected '00')`; a token after the value of `PC_FAILPROBE --gate` or `--s0` is named with that option), and so is a token before any option (`PC_LAMMPS: unexpected 'junk': every word after PC_LAMMPS is an option or a value of one ...`); a LAMMPS option (`-var x 1 2`, `-pk omp 4 neigh no`) keeps every value that follows it. The LAMMPS variable `maxtime` is reserved for the script timeout: `-var maxtime`, `-v maxtime` and `--invar maxtime` are errors. Such a value replaced the `timer timeout ${maxtime}` limit unchecked while the journal reported `PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` and its deadline, and any `-var` whose value was the word `maxtime` suppressed the limit.
- **`tadah refit` `EXPLORE MAX_ADD` is an integer from 0 to 2147483647** (`parse_uint`); `std::stoi` read `+3` as 3, and a negative value silently meant no cap. Leaving `MAX_ADD` out means no cap. The cap arithmetic no longer overflows for a large `MAX_ADD`.
- **The compact `tadah refit` reads its configuration numbers with MLIP's strict reader** (`read_refit_numbers`, before the table is read; see Tadah.MLIP): `cget`/`iget`/`bget` and the `std::stoi` of `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` (`+3` was 3) are gone, `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED` is read unsigned, 0 to 4294967295 (3000000000 failed converting as an `int`), and `REFIT_CURVE_RELAX`, which `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` reads, is range-checked by the same reader (under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE energy` the mode rule refuses the key before its value is read: see the `REFIT_MODE` entry under *Changed*). A `REFIT_COMPACT_NMAX` whose curve sample grid, at least 4 × NMAX points per function, does not fit an `int` is refused by that reader, before the table is read (`4 * NMAX` overflowed above 536870911). `CompactBlipFitter` refuses an `opt_every` or `insert_topk` below 1, which `std::max` made 1 silently, and a `jitters` outside 0 to `INT_MAX` - 1, whose candidate loop overflowed.
- **`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT`, `MAXTIME` and `--maxtime` are LAMMPS time specs** (`off`, `unlimited` or `[[HH:]MM:]SS`, one to three non-empty parts of the number grammar: a strict subset of what `timer timeout` reads, to the same seconds; LAMMPS also takes an empty or a fourth part and `std::stod`'s forms), checked at their line; they were passed to LAMMPS unchecked. A limit of 0 or less is rejected too (`parse_script_timeout`): `Error on line 9: PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT must be a positive time, 'off' or 'unlimited' (a limit <= 0 stops every script), got '0'`. LAMMPS accepts it, but 0 stops a script at its first timer check, and with 0 or a negative limit `$(timeremain)` is 0, so the section guard stopped every multi-section script and the evaluations scored `FAILSCORE`. `off` and `unlimited` are the ways to write no limit. A spec whose parts add up to an infinite time (`1e307:0:0`) is rejected as well. `unlimited` now means no limit, like `off`: it was injected as `timer timeout unlimited`, which leaves `$(timeremain)` at 0, so the section guard failed every multi-section script as timed out. The PROCESS worker's hard deadline (`pc_worker_deadline_ms()`, now a free function) is computed from the parsed seconds: the old `strtod` read `2:00:00` as 2 s, so the worker was killed after 122 s, and `00:05:00` as 0, so there was no deadline. A limit <= 0 reaching it is an `std::invalid_argument`, no longer "no deadline". The journal prints the parsed limit and the derived deadline. The worker's wait (`pcwire::readn`) polls a deadline above `INT_MAX` ms in slices: `static_cast<int>` wrapped it, so a script timeout above about 715828 s (derived deadline) or a `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` above about 2147484 s killed the worker at once (`PC_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT 1431656`: after 704 ms) or gave it no deadline, while the journal reported the deadline. A timeout whose deadline does not fit in 64-bit milliseconds (a script timeout above about 3.07e15 s, `PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` or `TADAH_PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT` above about 9.2e15 s) is an error at its line (`... is too large for the worker deadline, got '4e15'`); its conversion was undefined behaviour.
- **A `tadah refit` stage's `OPTIMIZER` block rejects the keys the stage does not use** (`read_optimizer_block` takes a key check, `stage_key_check`), naming the keys it reads: `REFINE: THREADS is not used by a tadah refit stage (it reads LIB, ALGO, MAXEVAL/MAXITER, FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, GTOL)`. EXPLORE reads `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL` and `INNER`, its `INNER` block `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `XTOL_REL`, REFINE `LIB`, `ALGO`, `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `GTOL`, and FUSE the same without `FTOL_ABS` (its `GTOL`, accepted and ignored before, is read now: see the curve-mode FUSE entry); within these, a key the block's `ALGO` does not read is an error too (below). Every other optimiser attribute (`THREADS`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `POPULATION`, `STEP`, `PARAM`, …) and an `INNER` block in REFINE or FUSE was accepted and did nothing. `SEED` is rejected with a pointer to `REFIT_COMPACT_SEED`, which seeds the refit, and `FTOL_ABS` with a Ceres `ALGO` with a pointer to `FTOL_REL`, as before, now at the key. Every stage `OPTIMIZER` block and `INNER` block must set `ALGO` (`EXPLORE INNER: ALGO is not set; the INNER block of a tadah refit stage names its ALGO (NONE for no local search)`): an `INNER` block without `ALGO` silently ran no local search, while an `EXPLORE` optimiser without an `INNER` block runs `LN_SBPLX`; the other blocks failed with `unsupported optimiser LIB '...' ALGO '(unset)'`. `GTOL`, `MAXITER` and the `INIT MANUAL` operands are strict there too. Every error of a stage's `OPTIMIZER` block names the block, the `INNER` block's as `EXPLORE INNER` (`REFINE: FTOL_REL must be a finite number, got '0x1p-40'`, `EXPLORE INNER: unknown key 'FTOLREL' in the INNER block`); a malformed number named no block.
- `PC_OBSERVABLE` values must be finite numbers, and its integer flags take decimal digits only, from 1 (`--supercell`) or 0 (`--maxiter`, `--maxeval`) to 2147483647, in the message of every integer key (`PC_OBSERVABLE cohesive_energy: --maxiter must be an integer from 0 to 2147483647, got '-5'`): a narrowing cast turned `--supercell 4294967300` into 4, a sign was read (`--maxiter -0` was 0), and a negative value got a range error that did not name it; an ASA `PARAM` index too large for a `long` names the `PARAM` (the error was `stol`).
- **`MAXEVAL` is an integer from 1 to 2147483647 in every optimiser block** (short form, `STRATEGY`, `EXPLORE`, `REFINE`, `INNER`), and so are `tadah refit`'s `MAXEVAL` and `MAXITER`: `MAXEVAL 0` is an error at its line (`MAXEVAL must be an integer from 1 to 2147483647, got '0'`). It meant something different to every backend: no limit to NLopt, no evaluation to `TADAH:RANDOM`, the annealers and ASA, the first starting point to `DLIB:GFS`, an error when `DLIB:BFGS` and `CERES` started, nothing to `TADAH:GRID`, and in `tadah refit` no Ceres iteration. Leave the key out for no evaluation limit; `BUDGET_PER_START` was already from 1.
- **A backend with no convergence test and no finite schedule needs a stop** at startup: `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, a tolerance it honours set above 0, or a `RESTART` block (`GD_STOGO` and `GD_STOGO_RAND`: `MAXEVAL`, see *Changed*), or an error names the backend, why and the stops it honours (`NLOPT:GN_DIRECT needs a stop: NLopt's global search has no convergence test of its own and runs until it is interrupted; set MAXEVAL, MAXTIME, STOPVAL or a positive tolerance it honours (FTOL_REL, FTOL_ABS, XTOL_REL, XTOL_ABS); DIRECT's XTOL_REL and XTOL_ABS end it only once every rectangle is that small, after roughly (1/XTOL_REL)^n evaluations in n dimensions`), in every phase. A tolerance of 0 or below is no stop: NLopt switches it off and `ANNEAL_PT`'s best-stall rule arms only above 0, so `FTOL_REL 0` ran until it was killed. A `RESTART` block is a stop of every phase it wraps (but not the stop of a `GD_STOGO` or `GD_STOGO_RAND` search): once its `MAX` restarts are used, the next `AFTER_STAGNATION` evaluations without a new best end the run (`STOPPED -- stagnation: no new best in W evals after MAX restart(s)`), so `GN_DIRECT_L` without a stop of its own under `RESTART AFTER_STAGNATION 60 MAX 1` runs as before (699 evaluations on the Ta test data) — at startup (`restart_stop_of`) and when a driver checks its spec again at run time, the `PRESTAGE` exploration included (`restart_stop_armed`: `RestartPolicy` has armed the stagnation watchdog); the journal says so (`(none set -- the RESTART stagnation stop ends NLOPT:GN_DIRECT_L: once its MAX 1 restart(s) are used, AFTER_STAGNATION 60 evaluations without a new best)`). Measured by running each backend without a stop, through `hpo_run` and on synthetic objectives: NLopt's DIRECT family, `GN_CRS2_LM` and `GN_ISRES` (every tolerance ends them), `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS`, StoGO and the MLSL family (no tolerance does; MLSL's reach only its local searches), `LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ` and `LD_SLSQP` (the tolerances are their only convergence test), `TADAH:ANNEAL_PT` (`FTOL_REL`/`FTOL_ABS`, its best-stall rule), `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` (no tolerance; `GFS`'s `FTOL_ABS` is its solver epsilon) ran until they were killed; the original DIRECT (`GN_ORIG_DIRECT`, `_L`) stops itself after about 100000 evaluations (its cap is checked once per iteration: 100011 to 100071 in the probes) and the other local algorithms converge. DIRECT's `XTOL` does end it, but only once every rectangle is below it, since DIRECT divides the largest rectangle in every sweep: roughly (1/`XTOL_REL`)^n evaluations for n dimensions (`XTOL_REL 1e-3` in two dimensions ran past 20000), which the message says (`OptimizerCaps::tolerance_note`). They were accepted and ran until interrupted, and `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` needed `MAXEVAL` itself. The caps field `OptimizerCaps::needs_stop` (with `stop_note`; `require_stop`, `backend_rules.h`, which takes whether the `RESTART` stagnation stop bounds the run, `RestartStop`) replaces `needs_maxeval`, and the `supports_*` flags now say which criteria end a run. NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG hand their local search (an `INNER` block, or NLopt's default local optimiser) what is left of the enclosing `MAXEVAL` and `MAXTIME`, and the enclosing `STOPVAL` in place of the `INNER` block's (`inner_run_spec`, `phase_specs.h`; `STOPVAL` inside `INNER` is therefore an error at its line, below): an `INNER` search that needs one under `AUGLAG` (`LD_MMA`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_SLSQP`; a global `INNER` search is refused, *Changed*), or `LD_AUGLAG` without an `INNER` block (NLopt's `LD_MMA`), needs a stop from either block (`require_inner_run_stop`). The journal's stop criteria no longer say that an optimiser without any "may run indefinitely": it ends on its own (`(none set -- TADAH:GRID ends on its own: …)`), under `MULTISTART` each start has its `BUDGET_PER_START`, or the `RESTART` stagnation stop ends it.
- **`TADAH:GRID` honours `MAXEVAL`**: it stops after `MAXEVAL` points in the order it visits them (the `INIT` point, then the grid nodes, the first dimension of the documented dimension order varying fastest), and the journal says so: `GRID: MAXEVAL 5 < 28 grid points (the INIT point, then 27 = 3^3 grid nodes); evaluating the first 5 in grid order`. It ignored `MAXEVAL` without a word (`OptimizerCaps::supports_maxeval` was false and never enforced). Without `MAXEVAL` the whole grid runs, as before.
- **Each backend's attribute and `PARAM` checks run at startup, on the settings each phase runs with** (`validate_phase_backends`, `backend_rules.h`, called by `validate_hpo_setup` after the `STRATEGY` rules): the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, each `MULTISTART` start (its `BUDGET_PER_START` or its share of `MAXEVAL`), the `PRESTAGE` exploration and refinement, and every `INNER` block. The message names the phase: `the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME`. They ran only when that optimiser started: after the data were loaded, for the `PRESTAGE` refinement after the whole exploration, and under `MULTISTART` each start failed with a warning, so the run ended without an evaluation. The drivers still apply their own checks when they start. The spec of each phase comes from the new `phase_specs.h` (`plan_multistart`, `multistart_start_spec`, `prestage_refinement_spec`, `strategy_phase_specs`), which `MultiStartPolicy` and `StagedPolicy` now run with, so the startup checks see what the run runs. `validate_backend` dispatches to each backend's validator: the annealers' `Knobs::parse` and the `ANNEAL_PT` replica count (`anneal::pt_replica_count`, factored out of the driver), ASA's options, `DLIB:BFGS`, the CERES validators, and `TADAH:RANDOM`, `DLIB:GFS`, `TADAH:GRID` (moved out of `run_grid`; a word-valued `PARAM` is now an error too) and NLopt (`validate_nlopt_spec`, `make_nlopt`'s checks). The annealers, ASA, `DLIB:BFGS`, CERES and `TADAH:GRID` reject a `PARAM` key they do not read; `TADAH:RANDOM` and `DLIB:GFS` reject every `PARAM` (below), and NLopt refuses a `PARAM` key its algorithm does not read (the BREAKING entry under *Changed*). The validators journal nothing: the `BOUNDARY_MODE REDRAW` hint and ASA's `Queue_Resolution` hint are written when the run starts (`anneal::announce_knobs`, `announce_asa_options`). No test configuration is rejected by the new checks.
- **A refusal of the `OPTIMIZER` block's settings at startup names the line of its block**, as the parser's refusals do (`Error on line 17: the PRESTAGE refinement: DLIB:BFGS does not support MAXTIME` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER`): every backend's checks (`validate_phase_backends`: the CERES validators, `DLIB:BFGS`, the annealers, `TADAH:RANDOM`/`GRID`, `DLIB:GFS`, ASA, NLopt, a backend that needs a stop), the per-phase checks of `validate_hpo_setup` (finite differences, `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase that takes no gradient, `NOISE AUTO`, ASA's probes) and the `STRATEGY` rules (`validate_run_policy`: `STRATEGY TYPE MULTISTART cannot run ...`, the `INNER` rules). The line is where `HPOTargets::read()` checks the phase's optimiser (`OptimizerSpec::block_line`, `block_line_text`): `ENDOPTIMIZER` for the `STRATEGY`-level optimiser, a `MULTISTART` start and the `PRESTAGE` refinement (a `REFINE` block's keys included), `ENDEXPLORE` for the exploration, `ENDINNER` for an `INNER` block, and a block's own line for NLopt's default local search or a missing `INNER` block. They named the phase and no line; the caps-registry refusals (`LN_NEWUOA_BOUND`) and every parse error already named it. One form for both, `config_line_error()` (`strict_parse.h`), and `with_block_line()` (`phase_specs.h`); a spec built outside the parser has no line, and its message is unchanged. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp` (`a phase's settings refused at startup name the line that closes its block`; `judge()` checks the line of every refusal that names a phase).
- **More refusals of a block's settings name the line of the block**, in the same form (`with_block_line`): the check of the `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` setup (`validate_hpo_setup`: `Error on line 20: OPTIMIZER block: GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL (resolved for PC_OBSERVABLE: ...) needs the analytical gradient of the loss, ...` and `Line: ENDOPTIMIZER`; `EXPLORE block: ...` at `ENDEXPLORE`), `STEP <KEY>` overrides that cover only some dimensions without a block-wide `STEP` (`validate_optimizer_scaling`, at parse time and at startup; the refusal of one `STEP` line names that line: the key-line entry) and `hpo_mpi_dispatch_refusal` of a parsed spec (`a run on 2 MPI ranks takes STRATEGY TYPE PLAIN with TADAH:ANNEAL_PT or TADAH:ANNEAL_PA only, ...`, `a run on 2 MPI ranks takes no RESTART block ...`). **The rule that NLOPT, DLIB and CERES need an `OPTIM` line holds for every phase** (`validate_phase_backends`, before the backend's own checks): `Error on line 14: the PRESTAGE exploration (EXPLORE block): NLOPT:GN_DIRECT cannot optimise a zero-dimensional search space (there is no OPTIM line; ...). Add at least one OPTIM line, or use a TADAH-native optimiser, which evaluates the INIT start point once.` and `Line:     ENDEXPLORE`. `validate_hpo_setup` checked the `STRATEGY`-level `LIB` only (`No OPTIM line: the search space has zero dimensions, and LIB NLOPT cannot optimise a zero-dimensional space ...`, no line), so an `EXPLORE` block's NLopt optimiser or `DLIB:GFS` passed startup and failed in the run (`NULL args to nlopt_optimize_`, dlib's `The vectors can't be empty`), and an `EXPLORE` block's `DLIB:BFGS` was refused as `... DLIB:BFGS requires non-empty, same-sized low/high bounds`. A spec built outside the parser keeps its message. Tests: `test_startup_validation_strategy.cpp` (`the GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL check, the zero-dimension rule and the multi-rank refusal name the line that closes their block`, `the STEP rules name the STEP line, or the line that closes the block for overrides that cover too little`; `judge()` knows `OPTIMIZER block` and `EXPLORE block`), `test_startup_validation.cpp`, `test_pc_observable_parser.cpp`.
- **A refusal of an `INIT` or `RESTART` block's `METHOD` entry when its chain is drawn names the `METHOD` line** (`InitStrategySpec::Entry::line` and `line_text`, which the parser records; `build_init_strategy` wraps such an entry): `Error on line 3: WARM: cannot open seed file 'missing.tadah'. ...` and `Line:     METHOD WARM missing.tadah`, likewise an unreadable `WARM` file, a `WARM` seed whose `RHOFORM` the run refuses, a `WARM` seed that `WARM_ON_MISMATCH error` refuses (`WARM: 1 parameter(s) not found in seed.tadah: RCUT2B`) and a `SOBOL` block beyond the sequence. The chains are drawn after the file has been read (the `INIT` chains and the `RESTART` chain's seeds at startup, a restart round's points when it starts), and these refusals named no line. An entry built outside the parser keeps its message. Refusals that still name no line of the file: a starting point outside its `OPTIM` box or not finite (`the starting point 1 of the INIT block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser: ...`), `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` without a finite positive `FAILSCORE`, `BUMPGATE` (its refusals name their keys), and two refusals of `RHOGATE` and `RHOBARGATE`: a CONFIG or WARM starting point that fails them under `SEED ERROR`, and a `RHOBARGATE` `f_lo` that puts the largest reachable `RCUTMB` beyond 20 lattice constants. Tests: `test_restart_seeds.cpp` (`A METHOD chain's refusal names the line of its METHOD`).
- **A `tadah refit` stage block that runs an optimiser names the library of its `ALGO` in `LIB`**, as `tadah hpo` requires `LIB` and `ALGO` in every block: `NLOPT` for the NLopt algorithms and their short names, `DLIB` for `GLOBAL`/`DLIB_GLOBAL` and `LBFGS`/`DLIB_LBFGS`, `CERES` for `LM`/`DOGLEG`. A missing `LIB` (`REFINE: LIB is not set; ALGO LD_LBFGS is an NLopt algorithm, whose LIB is NLOPT`) and a `LIB` that does not match (`EXPLORE: LIB CERES does not match ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS, an NLopt algorithm, whose LIB is NLOPT`) are errors; `LIB` could be left out where the `ALGO` named the optimiser and was ignored when it did not match. When the stage has algorithms of the `LIB` given, the message names them too, since that `LIB` may be the one meant: `REFINE: LIB NLOPT does not match ALGO LBFGS, a dlib algorithm, whose LIB is DLIB; LIB NLOPT takes ALGO LD_LBFGS`. The `ALGO` names are the refit's own, not those of `tadah hpo`: its dlib global search is `GLOBAL` (`tadah hpo`'s `GFS`), and `LBFGS` is dlib's L-BFGS (NLopt's is `LD_LBFGS`; in `tadah hpo` `LBFGS` is a CERES algorithm). `ALGO NONE` runs no optimiser, so a `LIB` there is an error (`EXPLORE INNER: LIB has no effect with ALGO NONE (no local search runs)`). `UNIFORM`, the refit's own uniform start, runs no optimiser either, so a `LIB` with it is an error too (below).
- **`tadah refit`'s EXPLORE optimiser refuses `ALGO GN_MLSL`, `GN_DIRECT` and `GN_CRS2`**, whatever the `LIB`, naming what each is and the algorithm the refit runs: `EXPLORE: ALGO GN_MLSL is not run by tadah refit (NLopt's MLSL with pseudo-random starts); its MLSL is GN_MLSL_LDS`, likewise `GN_DIRECT` (NLopt's DIRECT without the local bias of DIRECT_L) for `GN_DIRECT_L` and `GN_CRS2` (not an NLopt algorithm) for `GN_CRS2_LM`. They were aliases: `GN_MLSL` ran `GN_MLSL_LDS`, `GN_DIRECT` ran `GN_DIRECT_L` and `GN_CRS2` ran `GN_CRS2_LM`, byte-identical results, while `tadah hpo` runs `GN_MLSL` and `GN_DIRECT` as named. The refit's short names `MLSL`, `DIRECT` and `CRS2` stay.
- The journal prints a `PRESTAGE` `EXPLORE` or refinement block without `MAXEVAL` as `MAXEVAL=none` (the *Run policy* lines of the OPTIMISER section, `PRESTAGE EXPLORE  : TADAH:GRID  MAXEVAL=none  TOPK=2`) and `(MAXEVAL=none: no evaluation limit)` (the exploration-phase line); it printed `MAXEVAL=0`.
- `tadah refit`'s L-BFGS `REFINE` computed its budget, 50 per `MAXEVAL`, in an `int`: signed overflow, undefined behaviour, for `MAXEVAL` above 42949672 (in practice a negative budget, which NLopt reads as no limit). The budget is now `MAXEVAL` evaluations itself (see *Changed*), and `lbfgs_refine_budget` is gone.
- **`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID` and `DLIB:GFS` honour `MAXTIME` and `STOPVAL`**, which they accepted and ignored: no evaluation starts once `MAXTIME` has passed, and the first feasible loss at or below `STOPVAL` ends the search (`GRID` in grid order; `GFS` through dlib's run-time limit and stop condition, the points queued after a `STOPVAL` hit returning without running). The journal says which stop ended the search (`TADAH:RANDOM stopped: STOPVAL reached after 7 evaluation(s)`, `TADAH:GRID stopped: MAXTIME exceeded after 812 of 1000001 grid points`), and the stop is the run's status (`TADAH:RANDOM: STOPVAL reached`); one that spends `MAXEVAL` or the grid ends on that (`TADAH:RANDOM stopped: MAXEVAL reached after 30 evaluation(s)`, the status `TADAH:RANDOM: MAXEVAL reached` or `TADAH:GRID: all grid points evaluated`; *Changed*: why each search ended), also when `MAXTIME` has passed by its last evaluation, which starts no other: `MAXTIME` is tested only while another evaluation would start. `RANDOM` and `GFS` run on `MAXTIME` or `STOPVAL` alone.
- **`TADAH:RANDOM`, `TADAH:GRID` and `DLIB:GFS` reject a `PARAM` they do not read**, naming what they take (`TADAH:RANDOM: unknown PARAM 'FOO' (it takes none)`, `TADAH:GRID: unknown PARAM 'FOO' (it takes GRID_POINTS)`); `RANDOM` and `GFS` accepted any. `DLIB:GFS` rejects `FTOL_REL`, `XTOL_REL` and `XTOL_ABS`, which it cannot apply. The `ALGO` help text of the library (`OptimizerSpec::print_caps`, which no `tadah` command prints) lists each backend's stops from the caps registry, DIRECT's `XTOL` note, and that a backend that needs a stop takes one of them (a tolerance above 0) or a `RESTART` block; `GFS`'s listed the `PARAM`s `SOLVER_EPS` and `REL_NOISE`, which it never read, and `BFGS`'s called `MAXEVAL` an iteration limit.
- **Optimiser keys a backend never reads are rejected at startup**, naming the phase, the backend and the stops it honours or the backends that read the key: `RANDOM` and `GRID` refuse `FTOL_*`/`XTOL_*`, `POPULATION` and `VECTOR_ARR` (`GRID` also `SEED`); `GFS` refuses `POPULATION`, `VECTOR_ARR` and `SEED` (the BREAKING entry under *Changed*); NLopt refuses a tolerance its search never tests (every one of `GN_ESCH`, `GN_AGS` and `GD_STOGO*`, all but `XTOL_REL` of `GN_ORIG_DIRECT*`, and every one of MLSL and AUGLAG with an `INNER` block, which NLopt hands only to the default local optimiser it creates without one), and `POPULATION` or `VECTOR_ARR` on an algorithm that has none (`POPULATION`: `GN_CRS2_LM`, `GN_ISRES`, `GN_ESCH`, the MLSL forms, `GD_STOGO_RAND`; `VECTOR_ARR`: `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_VAR*`, `LD_TNEWTON_PRECOND*`); `CERES` refuses `SEED`; `STOPVAL` inside an `INNER` block is an error at its line (NLopt replaces it with the enclosing block's); `GRAD_STRATEGY` on a phase whose optimiser takes no gradient (a derivative-free NLopt algorithm, `RANDOM`, `GRID`, `GFS`, and an MLSL or AUGLAG whose local search takes none: see the next entry) is refused (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:LN_SBPLX: GRAD_STRATEGY never takes effect: NLOPT:LN_SBPLX takes no gradient; remove it.`). E.g. `TADAH:RANDOM does not support FTOL_REL: uniform random search has no convergence test to apply it to; stop it with MAXEVAL, MAXTIME or STOPVAL`, `NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS does not support FTOL_REL with an INNER block: ...; set it in the INNER block, whose local search tests it`, `NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA does not use POPULATION; remove it (it is read by NLOPT:GD_MLSL, ..., TADAH:ANNEAL_PA)`. They were accepted and did nothing: the journal listed them as if they applied (`ftol_rel` among `RANDOM`'s stop criteria, `Random seed` for `GRID`, `Inner stop criteria: stopval`), and an AUGLAG tolerance with an `INNER` block relabelled a run that spent its `MAXEVAL` `XTOL_REACHED`. One NLopt behaviour follows: an MLSL block's `XTOL_ABS` with an `INNER` block that set none of `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS` and `XTOL_REL` switched off the tolerances NLopt gives such an `INNER` search (`FTOL_REL 1e-15`, `XTOL_REL 1e-7`), which it now always gets. An NLopt algorithm refuses `SEED` only when it draws no random numbers (the BREAKING entry under *Changed*). `OptimizerCaps::is_population` (unused, and wrong for `ANNEAL_PT` and `GD_STOGO_RAND`) is replaced by `reads_population`, `reads_vector_storage` and `reads_seed`; `validate_backend()` and `validate_nlopt_spec()` take the `SpecRole` of the spec (an `INNER` search's message says where to set its stop).
- **NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only when their local search does** (`obtains_gradients()`, `phase_specs.h`): `mlsl.c` and `auglag.c` evaluate their own points without one, and the `D` of `GD_MLSL`, `GD_MLSL_LDS`, `LD_AUGLAG` and `LD_AUGLAG_EQ` only picks NLopt's default local optimiser, `LD_MMA`, which an `INNER` block replaces. With a derivative-free `INNER` block (`LN_BOBYQA`, …) they were treated as gradient-based: `GRAD_STRATEGY`, `PARAM fd_step`, `fd_kernel` and `diff_method` were accepted and never used (the losses were byte-identical without them), the `NOISE` block's `AUTO` derived an `fd_step` nothing used, the journal printed `GRAD_STRATEGY` and the finite-difference settings, and with `PC_OBSERVABLE` an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` resolved to `ANALYTICAL`, so startup required the analytical gradient for a run that takes none. Now such a phase is derivative-free, as `GN_MLSL_LDS` with the same `INNER` block was: those settings are refused at startup in every phase, the message naming the search that takes no gradient (`the STRATEGY-level optimiser NLOPT:GD_MLSL_LDS: GRAD_STRATEGY never takes effect: NLOPT:GD_MLSL_LDS takes no gradient: NLopt's MLSL and AUGLAG take a gradient only for their local search, and the INNER block's NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA takes none; remove it.`), an unset `GRAD_STRATEGY` stays unset under `PC_OBSERVABLE`, and the journal lists no gradient settings. The refusals on `GN_MLSL*` and `LN_AUGLAG*`, which take none through NLopt's default `LN_COBYLA`, name that search too.
- **NLopt `AUGLAG`, `AUGLAG_EQ`, `G_MLSL` and `G_MLSL_LDS` without an `INNER` block, or named in an `INNER` block, are refused at startup** (`NLOPT:AUGLAG needs an INNER block (the PRESTAGE refinement): NLopt has no default local optimiser for it; an INNER block with LIB NLOPT and a local ALGO, such as LN_BOBYQA, names one, or NLOPT:GD_MLSL, ... run LD_MMA or LN_COBYLA without one`; `the INNER block of the STRATEGY-level optimiser names NLOPT:G_MLSL_LDS, which needs an INNER block of its own; INNER blocks do not nest, and the INNER block takes a local algorithm, such as LN_BOBYQA`), in every phase, and again by `run_inner()` for a spec built at run time. NLopt refused them once the run had started, after the data were loaded and after a `PRESTAGE` exploration had run in full (`local optimizer must be specified for AUGLAG`), or, inside `INNER`, after some evaluations with only `nlopt invalid argument`.
- **An unknown `--` option on a `PC_LAMMPS` or `PC_FAILPROBE` line is an error at its line**, naming the options the line takes and the closest of them within two edits (`PC_LAMMPS: unknown option '--hotvars' (closest option: '--hotvar'): a PC_LAMMPS line takes the options --script, --varloss, --invar, --outvar, --failscore, --maxtime, --hotvar and --failvar, and LAMMPS command-line options, which start with a single '-'`; a `PC_FAILPROBE` line's are `--script`, `--invar`, `--outvar`, `--failscore`, `--maxtime`, `--gate` and `--s0`, without the `--varloss`, `--hotvar` and `--failvar` it refuses; `PC_FAILPROBE`'s `--gate` and `--s0` on a `PC_LAMMPS` line are named as `PC_FAILPROBE` options). LAMMPS has no `--` option, so it was passed to LAMMPS with its values, every evaluation failed (`Invalid command-line argument: --hotvars`) and the run spent its whole budget before ending with no usable loss. Single-dash options still go to LAMMPS unchanged.
- **`tadah refit`: `ALGO UNIFORM` runs no optimiser, as `NONE`**, so a `LIB` with it is an error (`EXPLORE: LIB has no effect with ALGO UNIFORM (no global search runs)`); it accepted any `LIB`, `LIB FOO` included. **A key that has no effect in a block with `ALGO NONE` or `UNIFORM` is an error**, named as written (`REFINE: MAXITER has no effect with ALGO NONE (the stage is skipped)`); they were accepted and ignored. An EXPLORE `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` stay accepted, because they also set the local searches of the EXPLORE optimiser `GN_MLSL_LDS` (`refit_curve_objective` is now the one parser of `REFIT_OBJECTIVE`).
- **A `tadah refit` stop criterion that its block's `ALGO` does not read is an error**, named as written with the `ALGO` and the keys it reads (`reject_keys_not_read`, per-`ALGO` read sets): `REFINE: GTOL has no effect with ALGO LD_LBFGS (it reads MAXEVAL, FTOL_REL)`. The keys were checked per stage, so `GTOL` with REFINE `LD_LBFGS` or `LBFGS`, `XTOL_REL` with an EXPLORE `INNER` dlib `LBFGS`, and `MAXITER` with an L-BFGS REFINE were accepted: that `XTOL_REL` had no effect (but under `GN_MLSL_LDS`, whose local searches it set), that `GTOL` none on REFINE, but under `REFIT_OBJECTIVE curve` it was the gradient tolerance of the FUSE curve refine (`fuse_one` copied REFINE's settings; see the curve-mode FUSE entry), and that `MAXITER` set the L-BFGS evaluation budget. The NLopt searches read `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` (REFINE `LD_LBFGS` `MAXEVAL` and `FTOL_REL`), Ceres' `LM` and `DOGLEG` `MAXEVAL`/`MAXITER`, `FTOL_REL` and `GTOL`, the global searches `MAXEVAL`. **`MAXITER`**, Ceres' iterations, is read by `LM` and `DOGLEG` only (`REFINE: MAXITER counts Ceres iterations; ALGO LD_LBFGS counts objective evaluations, which MAXEVAL limits`). **dlib's `LBFGS` (EXPLORE `INNER`, REFINE) reads `MAXEVAL` and `FTOL_ABS`**, the absolute change of the objective it stops at (`objective_delta_stop_strategy`), as `tadah hpo`'s `DLIB:BFGS` calls it; it took `FTOL_REL` as that absolute change, and `FTOL_REL` there is now an error naming `FTOL_ABS` (`REFINE: FTOL_REL has no effect with ALGO LBFGS: dlib's L-BFGS stops on an absolute change of the objective, which FTOL_ABS sets`). The defaults are unchanged (1e-13 in `INNER`, 1e-15 in REFINE: `CompactConfig::dlib_inner_ftol_abs`, `dlib_refine_ftol_abs`). The one key set another stage reads stays accepted: under the EXPLORE optimiser `GN_MLSL_LDS` an `INNER` block's `MAXEVAL`, `FTOL_REL` and `XTOL_REL` also set MLSL's local `LN_SBPLX` searches, whatever its `ALGO`, and a key neither reads is an error naming those searches as the readers of these (`EXPLORE INNER: FTOL_ABS has no effect with ALGO NONE (no local search runs; under ALGO GN_MLSL_LDS the block sets MLSL's local LN_SBPLX searches, which read MAXEVAL, FTOL_REL, XTOL_REL)`); with an `INNER` dlib `LBFGS` the `FTOL_REL` now sets only those, so such a run without `FTOL_ABS` gives different results. **REFINE or FUSE with `ENABLED false` and an `OPTIMIZER` block is an error** (`REFINE: ENABLED false skips the stage, so its OPTIMIZER block has no effect; ENABLED false without the block, or ALGO NONE in the block without ENABLED false, switches the stage off`); the block was ignored with a WARNING. The `optimiser:` line of `refit_opt.log` gives each stage's settings as its `ALGO` reads them (no `gtol` for an L-BFGS REFINE), the MLSL seed with its local searches' (`EXPLORE.seed{algo=MLSL maxeval=60000 local=LN_SBPLX{maxeval=20000 ftol_rel=1e-13 xtol_rel=1e-11}}`). Every tolerance is a finite number >= 0 (*Fixed*).
- **A negative tolerance in a `tadah refit` stage block is an error** at its key, naming the block and the token as written (`REFINE: GTOL must be a number >= 0, got '-1'`): `FTOL_REL`, `FTOL_ABS`, `XTOL_REL` and `GTOL` are finite numbers >= 0 in REFINE, FUSE and the EXPLORE `INNER` block, whatever the `ALGO`; 0 switches the test off. A negative one was accepted: Ceres refuses it in its option check and returned `FAILURE` without iterating, which the refit logged as the stop term (`refine=FAILURE(0it, Invalid configuration...)`, `term=FAILURE(0it, Invalid configuration, cost ...)`) and went on, so REFINE and FUSE did nothing and the run exited 0; NLopt switches a negative tolerance off, and dlib's L-BFGS requires one >= 0. A malformed tolerance keeps its message (`must be a finite number`). `CompactBlipFitter::refine`, `refine_edges` and `joint_refine` throw `std::invalid_argument` naming Ceres' reason when Ceres refuses their options (`compact_fitter refine: Ceres refuses the solver options (Invalid configuration. ...)`), before they change the fit: a refused solve leaves its amplitudes, centres and widths, and its EMBF0 reference and RHOFORM, as the caller passed them (it does not lift a negative NONNEG amplitude or a width below the floor first). Only a caller that sets `CompactConfig` itself can reach this now. `read_optimizer_block` offers each key to the command's hook (`ExtraAttrFn`) before `dispatch_opt_attr`, so a command reads a standard attribute by its own rule.
- The `tadah refit` EXPLORE `INNER` search with `LD_LBFGS` ran through a dangling pointer: its NLopt objective context was a local of the branch that set it and was gone when `optimize()` ran. Both contexts now live until it returns. The test `compact fitter: each EXPLORE INNER local search lowers the rung's objective` runs that search; built with AddressSanitizer it reported a stack-use-after-scope in the objective on the old code (the Release build does not show the defect).
- **An ASA run no longer stalls when a periodic test follows a new best**, deaf to `MAXEVAL`, `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` and `SIGUSR2` until `SIGKILL`. ASA's periodic test (every `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus` acceptances, 100 by default) resizes its block of generated states to the average, over the last `Gener_Mov_Avr` tests, of the number of states generated since the last new best (or since a periodic test that found the accepted-to-generated ratio below `Accepted_To_Generated_Ratio`), rounded down and capped at `Gener_Block_Max`. That number restarts at 0 at every new best, so a test right after one averaged `{1, 1, 0}` to a block of 0 states, and `asa()` then looped at full CPU without generating or evaluating a state; with the block hook installed (`THREADS` > 1 or `Gener_Block_Max` > 1) only `MAXTIME` could still end it. About one run in five at the default options stalled at the first periodic test (6 of 30 seeds on the termination-test fixture; the Ta test data with `OPTIM RCUT2B (1)` and `SGRIDMB (1)` and `SEED 1` after 210 evaluations). TADAH-PATCH 5 in `external/asa/asa.c` keeps the block at one state or more, as upstream's own option check requires (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`); a run that did not stall keeps its trajectory, and at the default `Gener_Block_Max` 1 the block stays at one state, which is canonical serial ASA. The `Gener_Block_Max` description no longer claims that setting it equal to `Gener_Block` freezes the block size: the resizing keeps the block between 1 and the cap, and can shrink it below `Gener_Block`. Tests: an objective whose every state is a new best (serial, block-parallel, and the golden gate against the pristine build), and `hpo_shutdown_asa_periodic`, which raises `SIGTERM` past the point where an ASA run stalled. A real-signal case whose run stops evaluating now fails with a message instead of at the CTest timeout (300 s): once three evaluations have started, the signal is raised after 20 s without an evaluation, and the case fails 60 s later if the run has not returned (about 80 s in all); before the third evaluation it fails after the 20 s, without a signal. A run that has not returned 60 s after its signal, or that has neither ended nor reached its signal within 4 minutes, fails the same way.
- **A stop ends an ASA run inside ASA's sampling loops.** Outside its main loop ASA draws states until one is valid, bounded only by `Limit_Invalid_Generated_States`, in three places: the `Number_Cost_Samples` samples of the initial cost temperature, the random start (without an `INIT` point), and the resampling at a periodic test with `Reanneal_Cost` above 1 or below -1. Once a stop was pending (`MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME`, `STOPVAL`, `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGUSR2`, an evaluation's exception), every draw returned an invalid state without evaluating, and upstream ignores its exit request while it samples the cost temperature: the loop ran on for the 1000 draws of the default limit and ended with `TOO_MANY_INVALID_STATES`, and with the documented `PARAM Limit_Invalid_Generated_States -1` it spun at full CPU for ever, deaf to further signals, until `SIGKILL` (for example `MAXEVAL 3`, or a Ctrl-C during the cost-temperature samples). TADAH-PATCH 6 in `external/asa/asa.c` ends each loop at the stop with `IMMEDIATE_EXIT`, as a stop in the main loop does (`TADAH-PATCHES.md`); a run evaluates nothing more after the stop either way, so only how it ends changes. Tests: each loop and stop with the limit off and at its default, and `hpo_shutdown_asa_sampling`, a `SIGINT` among 1000000 cost-temperature samples with the limit off.
- **PC_LAMMPS scripts no longer leave `tadah_pc_<pid>_<n>.in` files in the temporary directory.** Each script was rendered to a named file that only the destructor removed, so a process that ended without running destructors left it behind: a `PC_ISOLATION PROCESS` worker killed at its hard deadline (`PC_WORKER_TIMEOUT`), crashing inside LAMMPS, or killed with its parent; the worker-pool tests left one per full ctest run. The rendering is now created under a random name (`mkstemp`-style, `tadah_pc_<pid>_<random>.in`, so stale files of an earlier process with the same pid neither block it nor get overwritten or removed), unlinked as soon as it is created and read by LAMMPS through `/proc/self/fd/<fd>`, so the kernel frees it however the process ends. Identical scripts share one rendering while one is alive (one descriptor per distinct script, not per `THREADS` slot). Tests: `test_pc_script_file_executor.cpp`, `test_pc_worker_pool.cpp` (count the files in a scratch `TMPDIR`).
- **The analytical gradient of a `PC_OBSERVABLE` run matches the loss** (`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` / `HYBRID`; `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer`, `compute_observables_grad_dWchain`). It chained each observable only through the refitted weights (dO/dW · dW/dθ) and left out the observable's own dependence on the descriptor parameters at fixed weights — for a cohesive energy the descriptors of the relaxed crystal and of the isolated atom, a term that largely cancels the first — and it summed the observable terms instead of averaging them with the validation metrics as the host loss does. On the Ta test model it gave dL/dRCUT2B = 61.8 where central differences of the loss give −0.0097, so `DLIB:BFGS` stepped away from the optimum and stalled at the start point. An observable now reports the energies its value is linear in (`ObservableEvalResult::energy_terms`), which the gradient differentiates with the training rows' descriptor walker, and every chained term is divided by the host's term count; the gradient agrees with central differences to about 0.1 % (new test on the Ta test model, with and without a validation metric). An unconverged relaxation's constant penalty contributes no gradient (`value_is_constant`) instead of routing every parameter to finite differences through a size-mismatch exception, and a value without an analytical derivative (the force-scaled penalty) routes them there explicitly. Tests: `test_sims_grad.cpp` (`compute_observables_grad_dWchain`: a constant value, a value without dO/dW, the explicit dO/dθ term and its size check). A `PC_OBSERVABLE` run without `--validation` also has an analytical gradient now: an empty validation set no longer sends every parameter to finite differences (`phi_val missing`).
- **`DLIB:GFS` no longer crashes (SIGSEGV) when its evaluations fail.** dlib's trust-region step takes as best sample the first whose negated loss exceeds −1e300; handed the default `FAILSCORE`, the largest double, for every failure, a run whose first *dims* + 2 evaluations all failed left it without one, and it indexed an empty vector — whether or not later evaluations would have produced usable losses (CLI example 2 with its old `OALGO 3` crashed at `MAXEVAL` 12). `tadah hpo` now gives dlib a finite stand-in for a failed evaluation (NLopt and `DLIB:BFGS` are given the same stand-in, below): the worst usable loss of the search so far plus a margin (the larger of the usable spread and the worst loss's magnitude; twice that for an ungraded failure, between one and two times for a graded one by its place in the graded-failure band), or, before any usable loss, `FAILSCORE` capped at 1e100. A stand-in is fixed when dlib gets it, and each search (a `RESTART` round is a new one) starts afresh, so graded failures keep their order only among the failures handed over in the same state (all before the first usable loss, or while the same worst and best usable losses hold). No value dlib sees exceeds 1e100 in magnitude. `loss.tadah`, the journal and `best_pot.tadah` keep the loss the evaluation reported, and the journal notes the stand-in once per search (`DLIB:GFS: dlib is given a finite stand-in for each failed evaluation of this search …`).
- **NLopt is given a finite stand-in for a failed evaluation, as `DLIB:GFS` is**: the worst usable loss of the search so far plus a margin, or, before any usable loss, `FAILSCORE` capped at 1e100. A loss NLopt asks for together with its gradient is given the stand-in only for a graded failure with a usable gradient; a failed point whose gradient fails is rejected, as *Gradients and finite differences* (*Cost and unusable gradients*) describes. `loss.tadah`, the journal and `best_pot.tadah` keep the loss the evaluation reported, and the journal notes the stand-in once per search (`NLOPT:LN_BOBYQA: NLopt is given a finite stand-in for each failed evaluation of this search ...`). Handed the default `FAILSCORE`, the largest double, 1.5.0's `LN_BOBYQA` evaluated points again, and its `LN_COBYLA` asked for points whose coordinates were not numbers or ended after a few evaluations. **The results of NLopt searches with failed evaluations change**: on the Ta test data from `RCUT2B 5.6` with `PC_ERMSE 0 0.0001 --skip-above 5.5`, `LN_BOBYQA` and `LN_COBYLA` (`MAXEVAL 60`) now make all 60 evaluations, none of them twice, and the same ones under the default `FAILSCORE`, 1e300 and 1000.
- **`ASA:ASA` in block mode (blocks of more than one state: `Gener_Block` above 1, or `Gener_Block_Max` above 1 with adaptive resizing) runs a periodic test after every block in which the count of accepted states reaches a multiple of `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus`, or the count of generated states a multiple of `Generated_Frequency_Modulus`**, as serial ASA runs one after that state. 1.5.0 tested the counts after the block's last state only: a block whose acceptance reached a multiple before its last state, or whose last state was rejected, ran no test, so a block run made few of its periodic tests (with blocks of 4 states and `Acceptance_Frequency_Modulus 5`, none in 96 acceptances), and blocks of 4 states tested `Generated_Frequency_Modulus 42` only every 84 generated states. Serial runs (`Gener_Block_Max 1`, the default) are unchanged. **The results of block-mode runs change.**
- **A run in which no evaluation produced a usable loss exits with status 1** (`hpo_run()` returns `EXIT_FAILURE`), with an `ERROR` journal line and a line on stderr (`[HPO] ERROR: no evaluation produced a usable loss; no best_pot.tadah was written`) and the trailer `STOPPED -- no evaluation produced a usable loss`. It returned 0 with `COMPLETED`, although no `best_pot.tadah` was written. `drive_run()` decides it after an ordinary end (a normal end or a stagnation stop) from `HPO_Host::has_usable_loss()`, over every MPI rank; a signal (128 + N) and an error keep their own status and trailer. Runs with a usable loss end as before. The question over the ranks is one collective that every ending reaches exactly once, before the final flush, so a rank whose flush fails, or whose run an error or a signal ends after the drivers' last collective, leaves no other rank waiting in it (`tests/mpi/test_anneal_mpi.cpp`, `hpo_anneal_mpi_matrix`, runs `drive_run()` on 1, 2 and 4 ranks).
- **The journal's DIAGNOSTICS count every evaluation without a usable loss** (`Unusable losses : N (x%)`: NaN/Inf clampings, evaluations that threw, `FAILSCORE` from a skip cap or a failed script, and graded failures), and the verdict reads that count: `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss.`, `[!] No evaluation produced a usable loss.` or the majority warning. The verdict read the graded failures alone, so the journal said `[ok] Every evaluation produced a usable loss` right after `100.0% of evaluations produced a non-finite loss`, and for runs whose every loss was a positive `FAILSCORE`. (A `FAILSCORE` of 0 or below, which left no failure band, is refused at startup: *Changed*.) Each rank of a multi-rank run keeps its own journal, which counts that rank's evaluations: its verdict names the rank (`[!] No evaluation on MPI rank 1 (of 2) produced a usable loss.`), since whether the run produced a usable loss is decided over every rank (`HPOLog::set_rank_scope`).
- **A search that a point without a usable gradient ended says so** (`STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil`: under a finite `FAILSCORE` the difference with a failed evaluation is finite, and it fails the gradient all the same, the next entry), where it reported success or a spent `MAXEVAL`. With the default `FAILSCORE`, the largest double, a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil made the difference with a usable loss overflow (for any step below 1/2 in the optimiser's internal coordinates: half the box width under `OPTIM_SCALE AUTO`), and such an evaluation now fails the gradient whatever `FAILSCORE` is (next entry). In one dimension with `PARAM fd_step 0.2`, `MAXEVAL 60` and a skip cap that fails the stencil point 1.4 of the start point 1.6, every gradient-based backend stopped after the same four evaluations and reported `MAXEVAL=60 reached` and `COMPLETED` (`DLIB:BFGS`), `SUCCESS -- generic success` (NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR1`, `LD_VAR2`), `roundoff-limited` (`LD_SLSQP`) or `COMPLETED` (every CERES driver); `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` asked for the same point until NLopt's `MAXEVAL` (120 evaluations of three points), and so did `LD_AUGLAG` (its default local search `LD_MMA`) and an `INNER` `LD_MMA` or `LD_CCSAQ`, while `AUGLAG` with an `INNER` `LD_LBFGS` or `LD_TNEWTON` reported `FTOL_REACHED` after the same evaluations and one more, AUGLAG's evaluation of the point its local search returned. The adapters now read why the gradient failed (`GradientProvider::failure`, *Added*). `DLIB:BFGS` cannot reject a point: such a gradient ends the search (`detail::GradientNotFinite`). NLopt and Ceres reject the point (`+inf`; a failed evaluation) and step back, as before; a local NLopt gradient search that has had no usable gradient yet is stopped at that rejection (`force_stop`: the same evaluations, except the repeats) — an `LD_*` algorithm without an `INNER` block, or an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one (`single_local_search`, *Added*; `force_stop` reaches that search). An NLopt search that ends right after a rejection ended because of it: no evaluation after the rejection but `AUGLAG`'s evaluation of the point its local search returned (`detail::ended_on_rejection`), and neither `MAXEVAL`, `MAXTIME` nor `STOPVAL` met, nor the `MAXEVAL` or `MAXTIME` of `AUGLAG`'s local search, which `AUGLAG` reports as `FTOL_REACHED`; a tolerance (`XTOL_REACHED`, `FTOL_REACHED`) reached there is reported as the rejection's ending. So does a Ceres solve that ends with `FAILURE` right after one. `MLSL` and the global `GD_*` searches are not stopped at a rejection. The journal has a warning that names the backend, the point and its failed loss or its stencil points that failed (`DLIB:BFGS stopped: no usable gradient: a failed evaluation in the finite-difference stencil (the loss at CGRID2B[2] = 1.25 is 1.797693135e+308); best-so-far preserved`; a stencil point that fails on one side only is not differenced, and the gradient takes the other side: the BREAKING entry on differences of one kind, *Changed*), and the status is the trailer of a run the search ends. A gradient that is not finite for another reason ends it the same way with its own cause (`the finite-difference quotient overflowed`, `the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, `… is not finite at this point`). Such a run ends with status 0 when an evaluation produced a usable loss (the stencil's other points normally did), and with status 1 and the no-usable-loss trailer otherwise; a signal or an error still decides the ending first (`run_driver.h`). Nothing stands in for the failed evaluation and the search does not go on past the point where it ends. Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` ("Gradient not finite: …", per backend, `AUGLAG` and its local search's `MAXEVAL`, `MLSL` and `GD_STOGO`, `MULTISTART`, and for a later point), `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (the endings right after a rejection, a tolerance and the local search's `MAXEVAL` on an outage stub: "No usable gradient: an NLopt search …", "… a Ceres search …"), `test_phase_specs.cpp` (`single_local_search`), `test_gradient_failure.cpp`.
- **A failed evaluation fails a gradient, whatever `FAILSCORE` is** (`GradientProvider`, for every gradient backend: `DLIB:BFGS`, NLopt's `LD_*`, the local search of `MLSL` and `AUGLAG`, the CERES driver; every `GRAD_STRATEGY`). Only a loss that was not finite, or a difference that overflowed, failed a finite-difference gradient, and the analytical strategies did not read the loss at all, but `HPO_Host` clamps every failure to a finite `FAILSCORE`: between two failures the difference was an exact 0, and the analytical gradient at a failed point is that of the loss before the failure (a skip cap's uncapped loss), small but not 0, so a start inside the failure region looked converged; under a finite `FAILSCORE` a failed stencil point gave a slope as steep as the jump to it, which the searches followed. On a one-dimensional problem (`OPTIM CGRID2B (2) 1.0 2.0`, a skip cap failing the box below about 1.47, `PARAM fd_step 0.2`, `MAXEVAL 60`), from 1.25, before this change, when each finite-difference gradient also evaluated its point again: `DLIB:BFGS` made 8 evaluations (1.25, then 1.25, 1.45 and 1.05 for its gradient, twice) and ended as converged, NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_TNEWTON*` and `LD_VAR2` 4 (`SUCCESS -- generic success fmin=1.79769e+308`), `LD_SLSQP` ended roundoff-limited, `LD_MMA` and `LD_CCSAQ` asked for the same point and its stencil until `MAXEVAL` (120 evaluations for `MAXEVAL 60`, when NLopt counted only its own calls), every CERES driver ended `CONVERGENCE` at iteration 0; a `MULTISTART` `DLIB:BFGS` start drawn there (`METHOD RANDOM`, SEED 3: 1.206) made 8 evaluations without a word and the trailer read `COMPLETED`; under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` (the default under `PC_OBSERVABLE`; `OALGO 4`), or `HYBRID` with nothing differenced, `DLIB:BFGS` and such a `MULTISTART` start ended as converged, Ceres `CONVERGENCE` at a cost of 1.79769e+308 and `LD_LBFGS` `nlopt failure -- algorithm converged`, every loss `FAILSCORE`; under `FAILSCORE 1e9`, from 1.6, `DLIB:BFGS` evaluated the bound 2 28 times in a row and ran to 60 evaluations, `LD_LBFGS` ended `SUCCESS` after 8, `LD_SLSQP` `bug: more than iter SQP iterations`, `LD_MMA` ran to 120, `CERES:LBFGS` left the box (2.6). The loss at the point decides now (`StencilReport::classify`): at a usable loss a failed stencil evaluation (not finite, or at least 0.999 `FAILSCORE`, `loss_is_feasible`) is not differenced -- the gradient takes the other side, and fails when no side is usable (the BREAKING entry on differences of one kind, *Changed*); at a failed loss — `FAILSCORE_GRADING OFF`, a loss equal to `FAILSCORE`, or a loss that is not finite — the gradient fails at once, before any stencil point; at a graded failure under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` (`OptimizerSpec::failscore_grading`, *Added*) the differences take graded failures only (the same entry), the graded slope out of the failure region, and the gradient fails when every differenced component is 0 (`… and every difference at it is 0: a flat failure region`). `HYBRID` classifies the caller's loss (`gradient_at`) the same way, and without it (`gradient()`, which no backend calls) evaluates the point first. `ANALYTICAL`, and `HYBRID` before its analytical pass, read the caller's loss too: a failed one fails the gradient at once, `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: a failed evaluation at the point` (`kGradientFailedPointCause`), with no analytical pass; a graded failure under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` fails it too under `ANALYTICAL`, and `HYBRID` differences every parameter there (the entry on a graded failure's analytical gradient). The searches then end or step back as for any point without a usable gradient (previous entry): from 1.25 a local search — `DLIB:BFGS`, an `LD_*` algorithm or an `AUGLAG` whose local search is one, a CERES driver — stops after one evaluation under every `GRAD_STRATEGY`, its stop line naming the loss (`(the loss at CGRID2B[2] = 1.25 is 1.797693135e+308)`), and the run ends without a usable loss (status 1), while `MLSL` and the global `GD_*` searches reject the start and go on to usable points; such a `MULTISTART` start makes one evaluation and, as the last start, its stop is the trailer; under `FAILSCORE 1e9` from 1.6 every backend takes the one-sided difference of 1.8 and 2.0, whose losses are usable, as under the default (the same entry). Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` with `FAILSCORE 1e9` from 1.25 the graded searches are unchanged: `DLIB:BFGS` reaches the bound (95.25) at its 4th evaluation, `LD_LBFGS` after 6. A search that touched a failure from a usable point no longer takes that difference: with the ERMSE target 10.5, `LD_SLSQP` from 1.72 and the CERES solvers from 1.8, which were handed `FAILSCORE` with a zero gradient at a point inside the region (1.074, 1.0586) and ended right after a later rejection (13 and 12 evaluations before this change), reject that point and go on, `LD_SLSQP` to `MAXEVAL` (with `XTOL_REL 1e-3` and `FTOL_REL 1e-6` to `XTOL_REACHED` after 50), `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` to the edge of the failure region (54 evaluations); `AUGLAG` with an `INNER` `LD_SLSQP` of `MAXEVAL 4` ends after 8 evaluations (14 before). Under `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` a failed point costs no analytical gradient, and NLopt and Ceres are given a rejection (`+inf`, a failed evaluation) for it, not `FAILSCORE` with the analytical gradient, so a search that meets failures takes another path. Limitations: a usable point whose stencil reaches a failure on both sides has no gradient (`DLIB:BFGS` stops there, NLopt and Ceres reject the point); an evaluation whose loss mixed failed and usable terms (the mean of terms of which one is `FAILSCORE`, below 0.999 `FAILSCORE`: a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation, a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term) was a usable loss to this rule -- it is now a failed evaluation (the entries on a failed `PC_LAMMPS` simulation and a failed `PC_OBSERVABLE` term); at a graded failure next to a usable point the difference takes the graded side only (the BREAKING entry on differences of one kind, *Changed*): under `FAILSCORE 1e9` with `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` from 1.4 `DLIB:BFGS` reaches the bound 2 in 6 evaluations, `FTOL_ABS reached` (its line search asks for the bound again, answered from the run's record: the entry on points a search asks for again under *Changed*; no line search runs from the bound, where the box holds the step: the `DLIB:BFGS` entry under *Fixed*), `LD_SLSQP` after 6, roundoff-limited, and `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` ends `FTOL_REL reached` after 30 at 1.9854; and a gradient asked for without the loss at its point (`gradient()`, which no backend calls: each passes the loss it has just evaluated) cannot tell a failed point under `ANALYTICAL`. Tests: `test_gradient_failure.cpp` (the provider rules, `HYBRID` and its flat graded region and lazily evaluated `FAILSCORE` centre, `ANALYTICAL`, the status text), `test_fd_stencil.cpp`, `test_fd_bounds.cpp` (the NLopt and Ceres adapters on stub hosts: a start inside the failure region, `MLSL` and `GD_*`, grading), `test_hpo_termination.cpp` ("No usable gradient: …": every finite-difference backend from 1.25 and under `FAILSCORE 1e9`, `MULTISTART`, grading and `TADAH_FAILSCORE_GRADING=ON`, `ANALYTICAL` and `HYBRID` from 1.25 and a `MULTISTART` start under either; "Finite differences: under FAILSCORE_GRADING a graded start next to the usable side …": `DLIB:BFGS`, `LD_SLSQP` and `CERES:STEEPEST_DESCENT` from 1.4), `test_failgrade_parse.cpp` (the flag through the parser, the override and every spec copy), `test_nlopt_maxeval.cpp`.
- **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a candidate rejected before training is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** Under `FAILSCORE_GRADING ON` a candidate the bump-support gate (`BUMPGATE CANDIDATE`) or a density gate (`RHOGATE`, `RHOBARGATE`) rejects scores a graded failure, so the analytical pass ran there; `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` handed every parameter to finite differences, its trainer slot holding no fit of the point, and `ANALYTICAL` took that for a parameter without an analytical derivative: `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for 2 parameters at this point (indices: [0, 1]) ...`, exit 1, at the first rejected candidate a step reached (NLopt `LD_LBFGS`, `LD_MMA`, `LD_SLSQP`, `LD_CCSAQ`, `LD_TNEWTON*`, `LD_VAR2`, `CERES:LBFGS`, `WOLFE`, `NLCG`, after 5-9 evaluations of a D2_Blip search under `Cut_Dummy`). `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient_gap()` says why the slot holds no fit of the point (the gate's reject note, a training that did not complete), and the `ANALYTICAL` provider asks it when its pass leaves parameters to finite differences: such a point has no usable gradient (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`, its detail naming the point, the reason and `HYBRID`), which NLopt and Ceres reject and step back from, as from any point without a usable gradient, and `DLIB:BFGS` (which asks no gradient at a point its line search rejects) would stop at, `STOPPED -- no usable gradient: the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`. A parameter or a loss term without an analytical derivative stays an error, which no longer names rejected candidates among its causes. `HYBRID` takes finite differences at such a point, as before. Tests: `test_gradient_failure.cpp`, `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL: a candidate the bump gate rejects before training ...`).
- **Under `GRAD_STRATEGY ANALYTICAL` a point whose fit the analytical gradient cannot differentiate is a point without a usable gradient, not an error.** `compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer` factorises the normal equations of the fit, Phi^T Phi + `LAMBDA` I over the training rows, for its implicit-function solve; where they were not positive definite (the trainer's LAPACK factorisation of the same matrix succeeded: the two differ at rounding level on a numerically singular one) it returned with every parameter handed to finite differences and nothing said why (the one-time gradient diagnostic named a parameter without a derivative), and a throw from it (an inconsistency between the trainer and its lists or descriptors) was written to the journal and to stderr at every gradient. `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient_gap()` was empty there, so `ANALYTICAL` took the list for parameters without a derivative and ended the run `FAILED -- GRAD_STRATEGY=ANALYTICAL: no analytical gradient for N parameters at this point ...`. The helper throws at such a point, naming the solve, the matrix and `LAMBDA` (`the implicit-function solve has no solution at this point: the normal equations of the fit (Phi^T Phi + LAMBDA I over the training rows, LAMBDA = 0) are not positive definite in the gradient's Cholesky factorisation`); `HPO_Host::analytical_gradient` makes any failure of the helper a point without an analytical gradient, `analytical_gradient_gap()` says why (`its analytical gradient could not be formed: ...`), and `ANALYTICAL` fails the gradient there (`the analytical gradient could not be formed at this point`), as at a candidate rejected before training (above); `HYBRID` takes finite differences for every parameter there, as before. The journal has a `WARNING` at occurrences 1, 2, 4, ... of the run (`Analytical gradient could not be formed at this point (occurrence 1): ...`). The `ANALYTICAL` error for a parameter without an analytical derivative no longer names an ill-conditioned fit among its causes. Not reproduced end to end: no portable fixture makes the two factorisations disagree; the host's part is tested with the helper made to throw (`testing::ScopedAnalyticalHelperFailure`, *Added*). Tests: `test_hpo_analytical.cpp` (`compute_analytical_gradient_from_trainer: normal equations that are not positive definite fail the gradient at the point`), `test_hpo_fresh_trainer_e2e.cpp` (`A gradient helper that fails at a trained point leaves it without an analytical gradient, and the host says why`, `hpo_trainer_e2e`).
- **`TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` draw a stream of their own for their `SEED`, not the generator of the starting-point methods `RANDOM` and `LHS` for the same seed.** Both seeded `std::mt19937_64` with the integer `SEED`, as `RANDOM` and `LHS` (`init_strategies.cpp`) seed theirs, and read it through `uniform(0, 1)`, as `RANDOM` does, so equal seeds of two keys gave the same numbers: with `INIT { METHOD RANDOM 1, SEED 2 }` and `TADAH:RANDOM SEED 2` evaluations 1 and 2 were the same point, and so were the exploration's first two with `EXPLORE INIT { METHOD RANDOM 2, SEED 2 }`; with `SEED 5` in the `RESTART` block (its `SEED` is new in this release) and on `TADAH:RANDOM` under `PLAIN` (round r seeds both with `5 + r`) every round's first draw was the round's fresh point, evaluated twice (documented, with the advice to give the two keys different values); `ASA` with `SEED s` drew the `RANDOM` method's numbers for `s`. `seed_stream.h` (*Added*) has the seeded stream of every optimiser's random consumer, `seed_stream(seed, salt, idx)` (a `std::seed_seq` of the seed, the consumer's salt and an index; `anneal::make_stream` forwards to it, so the `ANNEAL` family draws as before), and the registry of the salts (`seed_streams`: the `ANNEAL` family's chains, probes, swap coins, warm-up and resampler, `kRandomSearch`, `kAsa`), checked distinct at compile time; `TADAH:RANDOM` and `ASA` draw `seed_stream(SEED, kRandomSearch)` and `seed_stream(SEED, kAsa)`. The `METHOD` chains draw as before (an `INIT` block's points and a `RESTART` round's are unchanged by this entry), and two consumers of one kind given the same seed still draw the same numbers (two `INIT` chains of one phase; an `INIT` block's `SEED s` and a `RESTART` round whose `RESTART SEED + r` is `s` did until a `RESTART` round's `RANDOM` and `LHS` got a stream of their own: the `RESTART` stream entry under *Changed*); the `PRESTAGE` refinement draws its own (the next entry). A `TADAH:RANDOM` or `ASA` run with a given `SEED` takes another path. Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Termination: TADAH:RANDOM does not draw the points of a RANDOM method with the same seed`, `Termination: a RESTART round's fresh RANDOM point is not the seeded TADAH:RANDOM's first draw of the round`), `test_asa.cpp` (`ASA draws random numbers of its own and not the RANDOM method's for the same seed`); `test_hpo_partial_failure.cpp` pins SEED 3's new draws.
- **A `PRESTAGE` refinement draws random numbers of its own, not its exploration's** (`OptimizerSpec::stream_phase`, `phase_stream_seed`, *Added*). An optimiser drew the same streams for a `SEED` in the refinement as in the exploration, so a refinement with the exploration's optimiser drew the exploration's numbers whenever the two seeds were equal: with `TADAH:RANDOM` and `SEED 7` in the `STRATEGY` and `EXPLORE` blocks the refinement evaluated the exploration's draws again (evaluations 7-10 were 2-5). The seeds `RESTART` derives for a round met for `SEED`s that differ: the refinement runs round r with `SEED + r·S` and the exploration with `EXPLORE SEED + r`, so with `SEED 7` and `EXPLORE SEED 8` (`TOPK 1`) round 1's refinement ran with round 0's exploration seed and drew its points again. The refinement now seeds every stream of its optimiser (`TADAH:RANDOM`, `ASA`, the `ANNEAL` family, `nlopt::srand`) with a key derived from its `SEED`; the journal still names the `SEED`. A search draws as before (`PLAIN`, `MULTISTART`, the exploration); a refinement with a seeded `TADAH:RANDOM`, `ASA`, annealer or stochastic NLopt algorithm takes another path. Tests: `test_hpo_termination.cpp` (`Termination: a PRESTAGE refinement does not draw its exploration's random numbers`), `test_phase_streams.cpp`.
- **`PARAM fd_kernel inhouse` selects the in-house finite-difference kernel under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE`**, as documented; it was accepted and ignored there, and the Ceres kernel, `NONE`'s default, ran. Test: `test_gradient_space.cpp`.
- **Potential files are exact and never carry `NUMERIC`.** Every HPO
  potential writer (`best_pot.tadah` and its `.r<rank>` mirrors, `DUMP`
  `pot_<N>.tadah`, `BDUMP` `best_pot_<N>.tadah`) goes through
  `Context::write_potential`: every non-integer number with NUMERIC
  significant digits, default 18, so a best potential re-parses to exactly
  the doubles the evaluation used, but for the cutoffs and radial grids
  (`RCUT2B`, `RCUTMB`, `CGRID2B`, `CGRIDMB`, `SGRID2B`, `SGRIDMB`: Tadah.CORE's
  blueprint field `exact_in_potential`), which are written exactly whatever
  `NUMERIC` is, below 17 as the shortest text that reads back as the same
  value, because a load checks them exactly (Tadah.MODELS' setfl-cutoff
  check of `D2_EAM` and `DM_EAM`, the startup bump-support gate); the
  `NUMERIC` WARNING (`hpo_run.log` and standard error) names those keys.
  The old writer default-inserted and
  wrote `NUMERIC 12` into every 1.5.0 potential; such a potential used as
  the `-c` configuration does not pass the line on: no parse stores a
  potential's `NUMERIC` (Tadah.CORE), so the run writes exact potentials
  and its WARNING names the potential's line among those not read (the
  BREAKING entry of the `HPOTARGET`, `VALIDATION` and `VERBOSE` key lines).
  A `NUMERIC` below 17 of the run's own configuration is honoured with the
  `NUMERIC` WARNING, which says to delete the line from the configuration
  that set it. The `# HPO_BEST_*` and `# HOT`
  header lines are unchanged. Tests: `test_hpo_pot_digits.cpp` (also: the
  WARNING names the keys written exactly, and the configuration's line),
  `[fixweight_e2e]`, and
  Tadah.MLIP's `Subcommand: hpo with NUMERIC below 17 writes potentials
  whose EAM cutoff predict accepts` and `Subcommand: a Cut_Dummy bump that
  train writes with NUMERIC below 17 stays inside the cutoff for predict
  and hpo`.
- **Values read back from LAMMPS are exact.** `LammpsRunner::get_variable`
  evaluates equal-style variables with `compute_equal` and renders the
  double losslessly; LAMMPS' `retrieve()` used `%.15g`, so every PC_LAMMPS
  outvar, hot value, failvar and gradient observable lost up to ~2
  significant digits. Other styles are returned verbatim. Inside a script
  LAMMPS still expands `${name}` and `v_name` of equal-style variables with
  15 digits; `nfp_configuration.rst` ("Precision of values passed to and read
  from LAMMPS") gives the lossless idioms.
  Test: `test_lammps_variable_readback.cpp`.
- **Optimiser start points are evaluated at exactly their loaded values.**
  The real → internal → real map of the OPTIM dimensions is not the
  identity (in a `[-300, 300]` box a small weight came back 3e-11 relative
  off and `1.2e-14` as `0`), so even a `MAXEVAL 1` WARM run did not
  evaluate the input potential. Every optimiser run now anchors its start
  points (`anchor_seed_points`, `SearchSpace::with_seed_anchors`): CONFIG,
  WARM and INIT points, MULTISTART starts, RESTART refreshes, PRESTAGE
  top-K points map back to their loaded doubles in every
  adapter, and a coordinate the optimiser has not moved keeps its seed
  value. NLopt BOBYQA/COBYLA/PRAXIS round the start before evaluating it,
  so any u within 4 ulp of the internal box of a seed maps to the seed
  (capped at 2^-27, below the finite-difference steps with the default
  `fd_step`, but never below 2 ulp of the seed). One exception remains:
  PRAXIS under `OPTIM_SCALE NONE` on a LIN box reaching 2^24 (1.7e7) can
  start a few ulp of the box off a seed of smaller magnitude than the box
  (`nfp_configuration.rst` gives the measured range). Tests: `test_search_space.cpp`,
  `test_seed_anchor.cpp`, `[seed_anchor]` in `test_hpo_fixweight_e2e.cpp`.
- **Knot-channel RCUT is re-derived exactly every evaluation.** A value
  within 1e-12 relative of the largest knot was kept, so the cutoff
  depended on what the thread evaluated before. Test:
  `test_rcut_autoderive.cpp`.
- Configuration and seed readers accept subnormal values (`1e-310`, which
  the lossless writers produce): the WARM reader skipped such a token and
  gave every later dimension of the key with an OPTIM line its neighbour's value.
  Test: `test_init_strategies.cpp`.
- FAILSCORE clamps in outvar slots are exact (`failscore_token()`):
  `std::to_string` wrote 6 decimals, so `FAILSCORE 1.0000004` read back
  below the failscore and a failed `PC_FAILPROBE` passed as a real S(θ).
  Test: `test_failscore_token.cpp`.
- Validation ESHIFT is forwarded exactly (it had 6 significant digits), and
  `hpo_optim_resolved.hpo` writes its bounds exactly (10 digits), so the
  pasted block pins the box the run searched.
- **A fatal error fails the run, with a non-zero exit code.** NLopt's C++
  wrapper turns any exception from the objective into `NLOPT_FAILURE`,
  which the adapter treated as convergence: a best potential that could not
  be saved ended as `STOPPED -- nlopt failure (algorithm converged; ...)`,
  exit code 0, with a stale `best_pot.tadah`. The NLopt objective and
  gradient and Ceres' `Evaluate` now capture the host exception, stop the
  solver and rethrow it unchanged afterwards; NLopt's own exceptions are
  classified by type (`classify_nlopt_exception`). MULTISTART
  (`detail::run_start_guarded`) and the ANNEAL_PT/PA seed and T0
  calibration no longer swallow or retype run-level exceptions.
  `drive_run()` also covers the fresh-run reset and NOISE_CALIBRATION, and
  closes the journal with `FAILED -- best-potential persistence: <error>` (other
  errors `FAILED -- <error>`) before rethrowing. Tests:
  `test_nlopt_host_exceptions.cpp`, `test_policy_host_exceptions.cpp`,
  `[durable_e2e]`; `tadah` exit status: `test_tadah_cli_exit_status.cpp`
  (Tadah.MLIP).
- **Parallel optimisers keep the most severe of concurrent exceptions.**
  The ANNEAL_PT/PA replica pool and ASA's block evaluations kept the first,
  so a stagnation stop or shutdown request caught first masked a concurrent
  persistence failure (the run could end COMPLETED, exit code 0). Errors
  now outrank a shutdown request, which outranks a stagnation stop
  (`ExceptionStash`, `run_stop.h`). `DLIB:GFS` with THREADS > 1 no longer aborts
  (exit 134, journal left open) when two evaluations throw together.
- **RESULT reports a best potential that could not be saved.** It said
  `(none -- no evaluation produced a usable loss)` or named the older best.
  Now: `Best total loss : <loss> (eval N) -- NOT saved (best-potential
  persistence failed)`, `Last saved best : ...` when there is one, and
  `Best potential saved : not confirmed -- best_pot.tadah may hold eval N
  (write not synced) ...` only when the candidate's rename may have
  happened (not after e.g. EROFS). A lower best that another chain saved
  after the failure is the result, the lost candidate listed as `Unsaved
  candidate : ... superseded by eval M`. The record also backs the error
  up: the run fails on it even where a policy swallowed the exception, and
  a PRESTAGE or MULTISTART stage that stops on stagnation ends the
  run at once.
- **After a fatal best-potential failure, later candidates get a single
  attempt.** Every new best of another chain queued behind the failure
  spent its own retry budget (a `DLIB:GFS` THREADS 8 run took 8 x 30 s to
  end). Once a publication has failed for good (`durable_best::FailLatch`,
  `Options::no_backoff`), later ones make one attempt without backoff: a
  transient or permanent error fails at once, and only a rename reporting
  ENOENT gets one immediate re-attempt. A failure is journalled as
  `best-potential persistence failure at eval K (no backoff after the
  failure at eval N): <error>`, the error ending in `(gave up after 1
  attempt over 0 s)` after a transient errno. Storage that has recovered
  still saves a later best (`best potential of eval K saved after the
  persistence failure at eval N (no backoff); the run still ends FAILED`),
  but the run ends FAILED with a non-zero exit and the first failure as its
  status (`detail::durable_failure_cause`). The GFS run above now ends
  after one budget when failing calls fail fast; with a soft-mount timeout
  of T s each of the up to THREADS - 1 queued candidates adds about T.
- **Evaluations that cannot become the new best no longer wait on best
  publication** (`HPO_Host::may_become_best`, a lock-free pre-check), so a
  publication's fsync or backoff holds up only the evaluations that may
  win; a stagnation check that would fire first waits for every new-best
  decision counted before it began (`detail::throw_if_stagnated`). The
  rank-local mirror of an MPI run (`best_pot.tadah.r<rank>`) retries for at
  most 1 s, then makes single attempts until one succeeds.
- **MULTISTART, PRESTAGE and RESTART.** A MULTISTART whose every start
  fails now fails the run (it ended COMPLETED, exit code 0), and failed
  starts are noted in the status (`(N of M MULTISTART starts failed; see
  the WARNINGs)`, counted over the whole run). Under RESTART each
  MULTISTART start and each PRESTAGE refinement seed gets its own
  stagnation window (a start used to stop at its first evaluation after
  the first stagnation); after the last start or seed, a batch in which
  any stagnated restarts the whole strategy -- MULTISTART relaunches all K
  starts, PRESTAGE re-runs both phases -- whether or not the batch improved
  the best. An exhausted RESTART ends `STOPPED -- stagnation: no
  new best in W evals after R restart(s)` (was COMPLETED or a stale
  per-start status). `hpo_run()`
  starts with the stagnation window and top-K tracking disarmed: a policy
  that ended by an exception left them armed for a later run in the same
  process.
- **Compact refit: the energy-mode joint refinement (FUSE) keeps every bump inside the hard cutoff, in every density form.** It optimises the radial bumps (phi and rho) in their outer support edges e = mu + w/eta, bounded at the cutoff (`EdgeParamCost`, test hook `make_edge_param_cost`), and bounds every free radial width at the ladder's floor 0.03 eta0. Before, only the centres were bounded: a bump could end past the cutoff, or walk its width through 0 (the projection then skipped it and `tadah hpo` refused the written pot), and the post-solve projection's curve-only re-solve could undo the energy-volume fit. Synthetic fcc table, 8 bumps, small budgets: `SIGNED` went from a failed crystal self-test (16-40 meV/atom) to 3.3 / 8.3 meV/atom with the density within 4e-3 of the table; Ravelo Ta setfl, `NONNEG` seed 12345: the projection no longer moves anything (it moved one phi bump and needed a re-run). A projection that still moves a bump (a pinned `INIT` anchor, FUSE ALGO NONE) keeps the fallback: when the channel's re-solve leaves max |dE|/tol > 1 the joint refinement is re-run with the moved bumps held until nothing spills, keeping the better result. This changes compact `SIGNED` refits where the joint refinement had left a bump past the cutoff; dense refits are unaffected.
